    Kate was two hours into the journey to the Cape in her black Range Rover, windows down and music blasting at high volume.

    She’d spent the better part of the past day– after packing– pouring over the information she’d been given. Memorization wasn’t her strong suit; not because she couldn’t do it but because she absolutely loathed it. It could be argued that eighty percent of the reason she was so impulsive was because planning was boring.

    By pacing a path into the safehouse floor, drinking copious amounts of coffee, and staying up until almost two in the morning, Kate found herself more prepared for this mission than she had been for almost anything else in her life.

    She’d crashed on the sofa around two fifteen with Lucky tucked into her side and six hours later she’d been on the road.

    A sense of calm had enveloped her the closer she got to her destination and she chalked it up to her growing confidence. If they had given her this job, they at least believed she could do it. Even Clint and Laura must know that she was capable or they would have put a stop to it before Kate left New York.

    She was ready and she would succeed in this mission as the first of many; she’d follow in Clint’s footsteps until she was fighting side by side with the Avengers. With a grin, Kate turned up her music, bass pounding through her body as miles of road passed underneath her.

    As Kate drove up to the resort, she lifted her brows, impressed. It was almost quaint; instead of sleek metal and endless, mirrored windows, she was staring at exposed brick and stone accented with white decor. The front of the building was symmetrically decorated in shrubbery and florals that lined either side of a brick pathway.

    Kate slowed to a stop in front of a valet station, leaning over in her car to try and get a better view of the expansive building. It seemed to wrap in a slight arc and she followed it from end to end with a low whistle.

    Glancing out on her driver’s side could see a valet waiting patiently for her gaping to come to a close.

    Kate scolded herself internally even as she nodded and the valet opened her door for her. You’re the product of wealth, dumbass. Act like it.

    She slipped the valet a twenty as he greeted her and took her keys.

    “Your luggage will be delivered to your room immediately. Inside you will be provided with your keys and offered a tour of the resort. If you have any questions at all, don’t hesitate to ask. We’re here to make your stay as effortless as possible.”

    Kate smiled at him, gave a quiet thanks, and started her walk up the brick path. The pink and white flowers were in full bloom yet the fragrance was soft enough to remain pleasant without overpowering the senses. Twisting trees were placed distantly behind the bushes and soft yellow flowers bloomed against starkly green leaves. A sign made of light stone stood proudly amongst the nature engraved with the words “Madiera Herdade,” which Kate had learned from the files was Galatian.

    As she approached the front of the resort Kate saw the double doors of the entrance swing open, two doormen standing aside so that she could enter.

    The lobby was stunning, starkly white and filled with natural sunlight and lanterns that hung from the high ceiling. Pillars lined the wide, wooden walkway and on either side of it was antique chairs and couches tucked around antique tables. A few people milled about the lobby, a woman working at a table with headphones pressed in her ear, a man and woman standing at the front desk speaking to the concierge.

    A throat cleared beside Kate and she turned to find that another concierge had appeared at her left. She smiled brightly.

    “Hello! I hear I’m supposed to get a tour?”

    Her smile was returned plastically, the man’s face unwrinkled either from a life without emotions or due to botox.

    “Of course, right this way! We’ll start down here, take a step outside to visit the pool and talk about the beach amenities, and eventually I will walk you to your cottage.” Normally Kate would relieve the concierge of their duty and make her own way to her room, but a tour was a perfect way to start exploring the premises. Nerves and excitement warred in her as she trailed after the concierge in his loose-fitting white button-up and pale blue slacks. It was similar to the other employees of the hotel and Kate had to hand it to whomever decorated not only the place but the people: they knew exactly how to create an atmosphere of serenity.

    Kate was led through a restaurant that probably served fancier food than she’d had in two years; pizza being a habit she couldn’t break even if she had stopped feeding it to Lucky. From there, she was taken to a bar that sat both indoors and outdoors, an overhang shielding the half vulnerable to the weather. It looked out onto a massive, amorphous swimming pool that was filled with other guests of the resort. Lounge chairs held sunbathers that were glistening under the heat.

    Past the pool was the vast expanse of the ocean, and Kate took several moments to take it in, her eyes tracking the bright sand and the crashing waves. A part of her was tempted to sprint out and throw herself into the water but she maintained her dignity long enough to be led to her room.

    She was taken down toward the beach where there were rows of cottages that lined it like a personal little haven. By the time they reached it, Kate was sweating though she only had on loose khaki shorts and a white tee. Somehow the concierge in his suit remained perfectly unbothered by the sun beating down on them.

    They reached the front of her cottage, luckily only two away from the main resort where she’d need to be spending most of her time, and the concierge held out a silk pouch that presumably held her keys.

    “Your luggage has already been brought to your room. Please don’t hesitate to call us should you find any issue with your cottage.”

    They were standing on a small deck, just big enough to place a railing around and a small table and chairs within.

    “Thank you.”

    The concierge moved off just as quietly as he arrived, and Kate opened her door, pushing into the air conditioned space. She sighed, the door closing behind her as her head fell back and her eyes closed for a moment. It smelled like fresh flowers and sandalwood. The space itself was as white as the lobby, the sun pouring into the room from large bay windows that faced towards the endless waters of the harbor. More windows covered the other three walls and Kate walked through the lounging area to take in the remainder of the interior.

    The cottage held three divided rooms though they weren’t separated by doors. She entered the bedroom and saw a king-sized bed with crisp white sheets and one accented pillow for decoration. To her left, separated by a half wall that was used as a bar, Kate could see a very simple kitchen with a microwave, small oven and fridge, and a few appliances. Her luggage had been set by the closet and the deep purple color looked gaudy in such a neutral place. She was glad that they hadn’t been vetoed by Eben.

    With a release of breath, Kate walked toward the bed and threw herself on it, body sinking into the down comforter and eyes immediately slipping shut.

    She would give herself ten minutes and then she would get to work.

    On her second day, Kate was exhausted.

    She'd had a little excursion along the trellises of the buildings that had sapped a significant amount of her energy. It was meant to be a simple recon mission to see if there was an easy path to the hacker's room.

    Kate had donned her white sweatpants and sweatshirt, tucking a beanie into her pocket so that she could cover her dark hair.

    She had a tactical suit that– according to Eben– could change colors. But Kate wasn’t interested in trying to play off wearing leather in eighty-degree weather as she walked about the resort.

    She’d made her way inside the first floor just as the sun was setting and had taken the elevator to the fourth floor. Tucked around a corner, out of sight from most of the guests, Kate had found what she was looking for.

    The door of the service stairwell to the roof was locked, so she’d taken a moment to pick it with the set Clint had got her for her birthday. It took far longer than she was comfortable with, her skills still not at expert levels, but Kate had eventually heard the telltale click of victory.

    She’d slipped into the stairwell and taken the steps two at a time, her white sneakers squeaking on the rubber steps. This was probably the only part of the building that didn’t look pristine.

    Kate reached the end of the stairs and pushed the door open to the roof, making sure to leave a doorstop in case it tried to lock her out.

    She’d walked to the beachside of the resort and peered over the edge, her eyes traveling until she counted to the correct balcony.

    Climbing down had been exhilarating and only mostly terrifying– heights still weren’t her thing– and it was during this exercise that she’d found her excessive training had paid off. The drop from the fourth floor balcony to the third was only somewhat strenuous, her muscles protesting mildly as she swung a little too far away from safety. She’d then jumped from one balcony trellis to the next, diverting her path if she saw that anyone’s doors were open.

    It had taken only four minutes to make her way to the correct one.

    As she was hanging from Stražar’s balcony, she heard a clatter below and her head jerked down, her eyes wide as she looked for the commotion. Just in sight she could see that a delivery pallet had tipped and dropped several crates across the path to the entrance. Kate let out a breath, heart pounding in her throat, as a much quieter sound popped behind her head. Brick particles splattered her neck and Kate jerked her head again, this time almost losing her grip on the balcony. She cursed, the hand that had slipped clinging back to the trellis before she risked looking for the cause of the small sound.

    Seeing nothing, she’d rolled her eyes.

    The archaic bones of the resort are groaning at you for trespassing. Just focus on the job at hand.

    Kate had steadied her breathing and returned her attention to the balcony. There had been no obvious sign of any kind of sensors or cameras set up, and she'd sidled along the trellis to get a better look into the room.

    The curtains on the balcony doors were closed but Kate was able to press herself against the building and get a decent look through a space in the curtain.

    The room looked just as white and just as pleasant as her own, though more compact and without its own kitchenette. Nothing else looked amiss, and disappointment flooded her as she realized this entire jaunt along the balconies had been for naught.

    By the time Kate made it back to the cottage, she was coated in sweat and her hands were beet red from her climbing. The particles that had landed on her were now smeared into her neck and she felt grimy from head to toe.

    Kate slumped into the air conditioning and stripped off her sweatshirt, tossing it on the floor to be picked up later. She poured herself a glass of water and tried to settle her nerves. The bay window outlooking the ocean drew her in and she watched the night unfolding for the rest of the guests.

    Fires were being raised by the poolside and out on the beach as people gathered about them. She thought about joining them as she kicked off her shoes and tugged her sweats down her legs.

    She was proud of herself. Day two of her stay and she’d already done a sweep of the resort and begun tracking guests. There was one man that she’d run into a few times that seemed a bit out of place. He had all the charm of a wealthy resort-goer, his voice deep and inviting as he spoke into his headphones or the the staff, but he paid far too much attention to his surroundings and he was built like an absolute tank.

    It was not the false muscle of a gym bro but the bulk of somebody that prided power over speed and probably did quite alright by it.

    He had an almost unhealthy looking tan and bright, blue eyes that stood out starkly against his black hair and beard. Kate had given him a nod at one point when she’d caught him staring and she’d received a flirtatious grin back. He was definitely somebody to keep an eye on.

    Finishing her water, Kate made her way to the bathroom and started the shower, her mind racing with the information she’d been able to gather so far. Considering the fact that the hacker wasn’t in their room, she believed that they truly were attempting to enjoy some form of vacation in the process of selling their code.

    It was the ideal situation and she planned to make it down to the resort bar to do a bit more reconnaissance; maybe start a list of guests that could possibly be Stražar. Kate stepped into the shower and let the salty sweat rinse from her body. She took her time, scrubbing shampoo and conditioner through her hair and turning the heat up until it was almost scalding her. After twenty minutes, she shut off the water.

    She toweled off and pulled on her clothing that she’d set on the counter of her bathroom; a nice pair of gray slacks, a simple black t-shirt, and a pair of white shoes. She looked good. Kate slid her fingers through her hair, tussling the natural waves. She ran a blowdryer over them so they’d stick and she'd look the part of a spoiled heiress as she reconned the resort guests.

    It almost didn’t feel real. In fact, it was almost comical that all she’d needed to do was nod her head and suddenly she was on a mission hours from home.

    Yes. One word and Kate found herself at a resort in Cape Cod on SWORD’s dime. Kate pocketed the Ronin sword and took a moment to stash her retractable bow– another gift from Clint– that she’d left resting against the wall.

    Kate patted her pockets to ensure she had everything and turned to leave when she felt a body pressed against her back, a sharp blade pricking her ribs. Before any kind of panic could sink in, her attacker spoke.

    “Kate Bishop, again you are in my way?”

    Kate felt like she’d been drenched in ice water and subsequently stuck in a sauna as that voice snarled in her ear.

    “Yelena. Hey. Hi. How are you? Long time no–”

    Kate cut off as the blade pressed deeper, definitely drawing blood through her t-shirt. She gasped out and tried to lean her body away. Yelena didn’t allow it.

    “Quiet.” Yelena’s voice was sharp, her breath ghosting over Kate’s ear. Kate tried and failed to ignore the goosebumps it raised on her skin. “You are a wound in my ass.” Kate grimaced at herself even as her mouth opened.

    “Pain.”

    There was silence for several moments.

    “What?”

    From her voice, Kate could tell Yelena did not care about the answer. Unfortunately she was never one to make the right call when it came to self-preservation.

    “It’s pain. Pain in my ass.”

    Yelena didn’t respond, instead she stepped around Kate and slammed her back into the wall, her hand against Kate’s chest, almost around her throat.

    “You want to make jokes now, Kate Bishop?”

    It was jarring, seeing Yelena after so much time. Her eyes were cold, and despite her harsh words, there was little expression on her face. Kate couldn’t help the feeling that she was being seen and found lacking.

    Yelena’s hair was pulled back into an intricate braid not dissimilar to the one from Christmas Eve. Her body was covered in a black tactical suit and her Widow Bites sat proudly on her wrists.

    Kate could no longer feel the blade but she was under no impression that it had been removed from the equation.

    She decided to play on her hunch; the same one she’d had from that first time their eyes locked on the roof of that building over a year ago. She let out a slow breath. “And if I do?”

    Yelena’s eyes studied her, the intensity branding Kate’s skin in a way she’d never be able to forget. She smelled like expensive perfume and warmth and Kate was drowning in it. The coldness seeped between them when Yelena stepped away from her.

    “You do not belong here. You will get yourself killed and that is not my problem if you die because you are stupid.”

    Yelena turned and walked toward the door of Kate’s cottage, careless, like she had no qualms about a counter-attack; like nothing Kate could do would ever pose a threat to her. Kate felt embarrassment and anger pool in her chest and she acted without thinking. As always.

    Kate lunged.

    She aimed to hit Yelena in the back and tackle her into the door; a plan that definitely should have worked if Yelena wasn’t Yelena.

    Yelena's movements were slight, perfectly intentional, and Kate felt herself pass by her target as a hand shoved her roughly to the ground. Before she could reorient herself the same hand grabbed the back of her jacket and pulled her up just to slam her into the wall. Kate's brow hit first and she felt the split before the blood started dripping.

    “Do that again and I will cut off your hands, Kate Bishop.”

    By the time Kate was able to turn around Yelena was already gone, the door shutting with a quiet click.

    Kate’s breath shuddered out as she wiped furiously at the new, throbbing cut on her forehead. “Asshole.”

    She growled as the blood began slinking down the side of her face. Moving to the bathroom, she grabbed one of the towels and pressed it against her brow.

    Any sense of accomplishment, of pride, it was ripped away and dragged out the door with Yelena.

    This raised so many questions and most of them were one’s Kate was incapable of answering. Why was Yelena here? Was she after the code herself? Why did she seem more inclined to hurt Kate now than when they were actual enemies?

    That one stung. To see Yelena so indifferent toward her instead of her general inclination towards exasperation at Kate was enough to set her on edge.

    She didn’t know whether to call this new revelation into Eben or if she should wait to find out more.

    Despite her embarrassment at being manhandled like a small child, Kate found herself straightening up and cleaning her face. She closed the cut with a liquid bandage and applied a light layer of makeup to make it less recognizable.

    Fuck Yelena and fuck waiting for Eben to tell her what to do. With her luck, she’d get pulled out before she could prove herself.

    Kate checked the prick the blade made in her back and found that the black shirt hid the stain rather well. Instead of changing, she pressed an alcohol-soaked cotton ball to the wound to clean it and then washed her hands in the sink.

    Yelena’s shocking arrival and demeaning show meant nothing.

    Kate found herself at the main resort fifteen minutes later, forcing a pleasant look on her face as she kept her eyes peeled for trouble: ie, blonde assassins that could and apparently would throw her around.

    She entered the bar that looked out toward the pool and the water, protected by a roof but otherwise open to the soft breeze that was passing through. Plenty of other guests were lounging about the space; a family with two young kids, a group of friends that seemed to be taking up at least three tables, and the same man that had flirted with Kate previously. She definitely wasn’t against using his interest to see if he was somebody to watch out for, but she had a feeling he liked the chase. Instead of looking at him when she felt his eyes on her, Kate walked to the bar.

    It was smooth, stained wood carefully taken care of and lacking any kind of sticky residue or glass marks. The bartender glanced up at her, her dark eyes framed by perfectly curled brunette hair, and gave her a practiced smile.

    “What can I get for you, Miss Bishop?”

    Kate had been startled the first several times she’d been addressed that way, but it seemed that the resort management was very particular in showing attentiveness to guests with wealth. It left an uncomfortable pit in her stomach as it always did when she realized people could know her and her mother in just a few seconds on Google.

    She smiled and slid onto a stool.

    “Give me something with Gin. Your choice.”

    The bartender immediately set to work, not bothering to ask what shelf of gin Kate wanted. Kate watched her muddle an orange followed by a lime in a highball glass; grenadine, Patron Citronge, and Oxley Cold Distilled London Dry Gin were poured expertly into a shaker. The liquid drained out onto the muddled fruits with a bright orange color and Kate was actually looking forward to more than the prospect of getting buzzed.

    The bartender added garnishes and slid the drink into Kate’s expecting hand. “Enjoy.”

    Kate had also learned that while tips could be given in cash, any purchased items would be added to her bill automatically. She dropped a ten on the bartop and turned toward the water just in time to see the tan man approaching her.

    He sat beside her with a confidence that likely worked on many, his blue eyes already watching her and the smile on his lips revealing perfect teeth.

    He held out his hand.

    “Hechal.”

    Kate clasped his hand, giving him a controlled smile.

    “Kate.”

    Kate had to admit, he wasn’t terrible to speak with. Hechel seemed well-traveled and relatively intelligent. He wasn’t exceptional at comedic timing, his jokes usually falling flat somewhere between the hook and the distant delivery of the punchline. She let him lead the conversation for several minutes, answering his questions politely and with enough detail that it wouldn’t seem suspicious.

    “Why would you travel out here alone? No man to keep you company even looking like you do?” Classic. “How long to you plan on staying?” Easy. “We should take a dip later, I hear the water is very beautiful in the moonlight.” Gross.

    Kate let each one pass by, never showing too much interest and waiting to ask her own questions in return.

    Eventually, a lull occurred where she was able to order another drink– the same as before- as a gentle buzz warmed up her bones. She tilted the drink left, then right, before turning a curious eye on Hechel.

    “So what are you doing here, Hechel?”

    She made sure the inflection in her voice was flirtatious even as her stomach roiled in discomfort. Gag. Talking like she didn’t have a million words trying to pour out of her mouth was exhausting. This is why she didn’t like to think before she spoke. Hechel seemed pleased that she was inquiring, the smug look on his face showing that he believed he was winning her over.

    Why couldn’t Yelena be so easy to read? She could literally be choking Kate out against a wall and somehow she’d look like she was simply engaging in boring small talk. Kate’s mind replayed the scene; Yelena’s hazel eyes: cold and sharp and just as deadly as her hands. It was irritating that somebody so small could wield so much power with her body and her words. Actually, it was fucking rude. Yelena was fucking rude.

    “...and so boom, that’s how I got here. Fucking work, you know?”

    Kate swore internally as she barely caught the tail end of the story. How the hell was she going to get information when she wasn’t listening as it was proffered. Hiding the fact that she had no idea what Hechel had just said, Kate hummed and nodded. “Well, it’s a good place to get stuck with work at least, right?”

    Hechel grinned, hand lifting his own glass– straight whiskey: pretentious– out toward Kate. “I will drink to that.”

    Their glasses clinked and Kate took a long sip of hers, trying to will away the still-bubbling feelings that her encounter with Yelena had stirred up. Taking a risk considering Kate had no idea what the former part of his story help, she asked her next question. “What do you do for work?”

    Hechel’s expression didn’t change in any way that seemed to suggest he’d already shared the information. It did shutter slightly and she recognized the face of a man that was about to lie to her.

    “Consulting mostly. My partner is here somewhere as well but,” Hechel leaned toward her conspiratorially, “she’s a bit of a bitch.”

    Kate couldn’t quite hide the distaste that she felt at his words and he held out his hands to quickly protest.

    “Okay, wait, I am not a woman-hater or whatever you are worried about. I love women.” He winked and Kate barely avoided gagging. “But she is so mean. Has never said a nice thing to me even though this is our fifth time working together. It’s bullshit.” He seemed to be getting lost in his tirade. “I mean, just today, she threatened to sho– to hurt my balls.” He waved his arms. “I need my balls.”

    Kate opened her mouth to respond, intent on trying to pry into his emotional response and get more information. Before she could make a sound, the same voice that had growled out threats in her room spoke with a syrupy, mocking tone directly behind Kate’s back. “Hechel. I cannot imagine why you would be talking about your small genitals in public. That is terrible way to pick up women.”

    It truly was so unfair how just Yelena’s voice could give Kate goosebumps. Kate attested it to the fear that also spiked up her spine, her body eager to avoid a repeat of their last encounter. Kate watched Hechel’s face shift into a deep scowl, reddening in an unpleasant way. He cleared his throat and attempted to brush Yelena off.

    “Kate, this is the wonderful co-worker I was telling you about. She’s a peach.” Yelena was still out of Kate’s sight and she knew it was weird she hadn’t looked at her yet. If Hechel wasn’t so caught up in his wounded pride he may have noticed it. Instead, his eyes were solely focused on glaring at Yelena.

    Yelena, who was speaking again.

    “Dominica would like to speak to you.” Hechel didn’t move for a moment, and Yelena snapped her fingers twice. “Chop chop.”

    Hechel cleared his throat again and Kate could see that he was beyond irritated. Still, by the time he looked at her he’d schooled some of his features.

    “Kate, it was lovely to get to know you. I’m sure I’ll be seeing you around, but would you mind if I got your number?”

    No part of Kate wanted to give this man her number, but something was niggling at the back of her brain, something petty and unpleasant that she let herself listen to. Yelena clearly didn’t want her talking to Hechel; so in turn, that is all Kate wanted to do. She pulled out the burner phone that was provided to her and passed it over to Hechel with the new contact page already up.

    “Sure, it was lovely chatting with you as well.”

    A pleased grin overtook his features as he filled in his information and Kate could feel Yelena’s eyes burning into the side of her head.

    Hechel handed her phone back and leaned in, pressing a kiss to Kate’s cheek in a mildly charming way. Kate in turn had to shift her head to give him space, and she took in Yelena for the first time since she’d arrived in the bar.

    Her stomach dropped.

    Yelena had a white, cropped tank that she was wearing under a too-big, light, long-sleeved button up that hung open. The shirt had bright sunflowers on it and was french tucked into a pair of loose jeans. She looked like she was ready to relax with a fruity drink and yet no part of Kate was convinced that this woman couldn’t kill her with her hands tied behind her back. That could be partially due to the dangerous look clouding Yelena’s expression but Kate wasn’t going to dwell on it.

    It wasn’t the same indifference that Kate had seen in the hotel room. While she couldn’t read its meaning, she could see the hard line of Yelena’s mouth and the tightness of her jaw. Hechel leaned back and gave Kate a smile, Yelena a deep grimace, before excusing himself and heading out of the bar.

    Yelena tilted her head toward the ocean.

    “Walk with me, Kate Bishop.”

    There was a threat in her words; like Kate would be foolish to say no to her. Kate scoffed, eyes rolling as she finished the rest of her drink and set it down on the bartop louder than necessary. The bartender glanced over and Kate tilted it in a request. She chanced another look at Yelena whose face was still neutral. At least her jaw muscles were less prominent against her skin. Maybe that flexion had been for Hechel and not for herself. “Why? So you can slam my face into a wall again? No thanks.”

    Yelena’s eyes flicked to Kate’s brow where she’d done a decent job of covering the cut. “Ridiculous. There are no walls on the beach.”

    Her drink was set in front of her and Kate grabbed it eagerly before realizing that being so obviously uncomfortable probably wouldn’t help her case. She attempted a casual sip. How was it possible that Kate could feign interest in Hechel for twenty minutes and the moment Yelena was within a foot she became an open book of anxiety?

    “You don’t need a wall to hurt me.”

    Yelena was silent for a few moments and when she answered, her voice was more sincere than Kate expected.

    “I do not want to hurt you.”

    Kate wanted to continue her tactical avoidance but there was a part of her that was desperate for answers as to why Yelena just happened to be here, at this resort at the same time Kate was working a case. Obviously it was all connected, and she also knew that she had slim to no chance of beating Yelena in a fair fight. Especially not if Yelena was here with an entire team to. . . what exactly? Buy the code? Swap it? Kill the hacker?”

    Kate sighed and slid from her stool, finishing her drink in two long pulls. She set it down more gently this time and dropped a twenty dollar tip on the bar.

    “Fine. But if you kill me I’m going to be fucking pissed off.”

    She heard a slight huff of amusement from Yelena and felt a jolt of pride at getting anything other than sharp words and aggression from her.

    They walked side by side out past the pool where there were several families still swimming, the water glowing a soft blue from the lights underneath. A kid stumbled past them to launch himself into the water and Kate watched until he resurfaced laughing.

    Yelena steered them out of the resort’s white gates and down the pathway that led to the beach. She pressed her hands into her pockets and the movement was so casual that Kate found herself mirroring it. She caught herself and flexed her hands, irritation crawling up her throat.

    “What do you want, Yelena?”

    Yelena didn’t answer, simply continuing to walk even as the sidewalk disappeared and their shoes hit the soft, shifting sand. Kate stopped without warning and reached down to tug off her shoes and socks, tucking them together and holding the heels with two fingers. Yelena seemed unbothered by the sand currently pouring into the sides of her sandals as she forged ahead and Kate refused to rush to catch up.

    Luckily, her legs were longer and she was walking side by side with Yelena after a few strides. They avoided the gathered groups, walking away from the fire pits and lanterns and coming to a stop near where the water was rushing up on the sand.

    The ocean sounds were soothing enough that Kate felt her muscles relaxing, the impatience she was feeling dripping off of her.

    “Did you bring me out here to stand in silence or were you just trying to spend time with me?”

    Okay, so maybe she hadn’t quite mastered letting impatience drip from her yet. Yelena leaned her head back toward the sky, the sharp edges of her jawline prominent in the low light. She looked almost wistful and Kate had to remind herself that Yelena had smashed her face in not an hour before.

    Yelena’s upturned face shifted until she could look at Kate from the corner of her eye. “You are in my way. You need to leave.”

    Kate threw up her hands and turned to head back to the resort.

    “I’m not going to waste my time. Kick my ass to next week but don’t think I’m going to hightail it out of here. I’m not going anywhere.”

    As she took her first step, a hand was on her arm faster than Kate could pull it away. She tensed, expecting to be thrown on the ground. Instead, the pressure was tight but bearable. Glancing at Yelena, she saw the same harshness from the hotel room staring back at her. “If you stay you will die.”

    Kate tried to dislodge her arm but it was like twisting flesh in a vice; far too painful and futile. She felt her anger pooling higher, sick of being manhandled by a five foot four, sunflower-wearing assassin.

    She wanted to lash out but refrained, knowing it would be met with a quick and likely painful counter. Instead, she relaxed her arm in Yelena’s grip.

    “So you've said. Are you going to be the one killing me?”

    Yelena’s jaw twitched so quickly Kate was sure she’d just manifested a hallucination of her wishful thinking.

    “If I was going to kill you I–”

    Kate waved her free hand dismissively.

    “Right. Of course. You would have already.” Taking a different approach, her chest tight from what might happen if she pushed Yelena, Kate stepped into her space. “So tell me: why should I care about your threats if you’re never going to do anything about them–” Yelena was on her in the space of a heartbeat, so quick that Kate could barely finish her last word. She found herself on her back, sand sinking into her slacks and shirt. Her head hit hard enough to jar her and she barely blinked before Yelena was standing over her. Yelena opened her mouth to speak but Kate was done.

    With the desperation that came with being outmatched, Kate’s hand dug into the sand and flung it as hard as she could into Yelena’s face.

    Yelena jerked backward, eyes blinking rapidly to attempt to disperse the sand. “ты чё, сука, охуел, бля?”

    A thrill shot up Kate’s spine as she realized she’d actually caught Yelena by surprise. She swung a leg out, catching Yelena’s foot and dragging it sharply. As Yelena tried to jump over, Kate kicked directly into Yelena’s standing leg.

    Yelena hit the sand hard but was pushing herself up in seconds, still practically blinded. With a vicious lunge, Kate shoved at Yelena again.

    Despite one of Yelena’s hands catching hers, Kate was able to dodge the other and her body’s force did the rest.

    Kate ended up sprawled across her, scrambling for purchase as she grabbed at Yelena’s hands and slammed them into the sand.

    If Kate hadn’t been so lost in her own ego she would have realized that Yelena stopped fighting, that she was letting Kate force her into submission. Instead, she grinned manically at Yelena.

    “I’m better than I was before.”

    Tears from the irritation the sand caused streaked down Yelena’s cheeks and yet she somehow looked far more unbothered than Kate felt.

    Yelena stared at Kate, her eyes barely visible in the low light.

    “You believe putting your body near a Black Widow is considered a win for you?” A shiver raced along Kate’s arm at the promise of violence and she didn’t have time to lurch away before knees slammed into her back and lurched her forward.     Kate turned and walked out of the bar, her stomach roiling unpleasantly but her mind alight with ideas for the next day. Hechel worked with six others including Yelena but somebody else was in charge. He’d discussed his schedule with her, stating when he was free and when he’d be busy. He’d also said that he was staying on the third floor, the same floor as the hacker. That had been good and bad news as it confirmed the hacker’s location but also confirmed that her task was going to be far more difficult than she expected. Lastly, Hechel had let slip that the hacker was a woman and Kate planned on tracking the one with the buzzed head and piercings starting tomorrow.

    Kate grinned to herself as she pushed her way into her cottage well after midnight, prepared to crash onto her bed with minimal fanfare.

    She tugged off the shirt dress and tossed it on a lounge chair before pulling her belt free of the loops.

    She’d just untucked her shirt when Yelena spoke.

    “I did not expect you to whore yourself for information.”

    Kate froze, heart leaping a single time in her throat before settling back down. She whirled around and found Yelena leaning against the kitchen bar, still in her clothing from earlier. “Excuse me?”

    Yelena lifted a brow, her eyes burning with masked emotion and her lips turned down in disgust.

    “You want me to say it again?”

    Kate’s teeth clenched together and she took a step toward Yelena.

    “You have most definitely done worse.”

    Yelena’s jaw tightened and she pushed off of the counter to stand upright. “Not by choice.”

    The words slammed into Kate’s chest but she forced herself to bypass them for now. She could apologize for the low blow (that she didn’t know was low) when Yelena wasn’t banking on her to do just that.

    “Why are you in my room again?”

    Yelena scoffed, her eyes boring into Kate’s.

    “I think the answer is very obvious. You cannot fuck Hechel for information.” Kate definitely wasn’t going to but Yelena didn’t need to know that.

    “Why not? He’s not ugly and I know he’ll tell me everything I want to know. Maybe it’ll even be fun.”

    Yelena bristled, her eyes glaring daggers at Kate as she prowled closer. The line of her shoulders was loose but Kate knew it wouldn't take much effort for the assassin to put her on her back. She tensed.

    Yelena came to a stop a step too close to her.

    “He is a pig. He does not even know how to touch a woman.”

    The force behind her words carried through the room, settling in the rising tension and floating there.

    Kate’s throat was dry and she couldn’t look away from Yelena because that would mean she’d lost. There was something about the way Yelena challenged her that had her nerves flaring and heart racing. The heat licked along her spine and began to settle low. Something to ignore now and freak out about later.

    “That’s what you’re worried about? That I might not enjoy myself?”

    Yelena was just a foot from her now, eyes searching as she took in all Kate had to offer. After several very heavy moments, Yelena relaxed entirely.

    “You are not going to sleep with him.”

    It was proffered as a statement of fact; said without question. Kate almost pushed it further but she knew Yelena could see through whatever facade she had put in place. Rolling her eyes, Kate huffed.

    “Of course I’m not going to sleep with him.”

    The tension in the room lessened significantly but Kate was still very aware of Yelena’s presence in front of her.

    The assassin seemed to have little issue with direct eye contact even as everything in Kate was screaming at her to look away.

    She broke the silence.

    “I can’t believe you pushed me in the pool.”

    Yelena’s lips twitched and she finally took a step back, giving Kate some room to relax. “Your face was so funny.” Yelena shook her head. “You were very mad.”

    “I’m still mad!”

    Yelena waved her off and glanced around the cottage like she hadn’t already broken in three separate times.

    “SWORD got you the nice room. Tell me why they have interest in Stražar.” The change in subject threw Kate for a loop but she attempted to look confused. “I don–”

    Yelena shook her head and interrupted.

    “No, no, Kate Bishop. Do not pretend, it does not look cool.”

    Somehow, in shorts and a t-shirt, Yelena still seemed morelike the professional of the two of them. She existed in every space like she owned it, even when it was Kate’s. “I’m working alone.” Yelena rolled her eyes but Kate forged on. “And Stražar is trying to sell a code that will get people killed. Why are you protecting her?”

    If Yelena was surprised by Kate’s procured information she didn’t show it. “I do what I’m paid to do. Stražar has lots of money.”

    Kate tried to parse through that but Yelena was giving her nothing.

    “Aren’t you tired of being a weapon for other people to weild?”

    Wrong thing to say. Very, very wrong thing. Yelena’s expression darkened significantly, her lips thinning as the room flooded with the familiar air of an oncoming fight. “Kate Bishop, you are too bold for somebody that can be squished like bug.” Kate knew her next words could very easily have her decked. She would be far more visible to the resort guests with a broken nose and she could not afford that. Mentally scrambling, she ran through what she’d learned tonight.

    “Hechel said there are seven of you.”

    Yelena’s posture didn’t change.

    “You think the man has not learned to lie to women to bring them to his bed?” Kate envied Yelena’s ability to lie without a single tell. Well, she hoped she was lying. Hechel’s information was all she had to go on.

    “I’m not going to let you convince me that I didn’t score big tonight. You’re working with an idiot.”

    Yelena finally shifted, her hands thrown up in the air and a groan leaving her lips. “This is what I have been saying. You do not listen to me. He is the worst.” Yelena sang out the last two words, her accent wrapping out them, and Kate had to fight a smile. It was so fucking hard to be mad at Yelena.

    No, strike that. It was impossibly easy to be mad at Yelena. But maintaining that anger was proving to be about as difficult as landing a hit on the assassin.

    “You know, me doing my job won’t stop you from doing yours. I am not here to hurt the hacker. I just want to swap the code.”

    Yelena nodded, frown of concentration on her face as her hand dug into her pocket. Kate’s chest tightened as she saw the drive she’d hidden in the lining of her suitcase sitting pretty in Yelena’s hand.

    Yelena looked at it curiously.

    “I know why you are here. Did you expect that my job did not also require me to protect the code?”

    Kate clenched her jaw as she considered her options. One, fight Yelena. That would go just as poorly as the last times. From the way Yelena had lifted a brow Kate could see that she knew what she was thinking. Her only option was getting Yelena to give it back. Before she could speak, Yelena’s hand motioned at her, fingers coming far too close. “Do you get tired of telling everything you think with your face?”

    The amount of times Yelena brought Kate to the point of wanting to stomp her foot in frustration like a toddler was becoming absurd. Instead, she held out her hand. “If you know what I’m going to say, why don’t you just give me the drive?” Yelena laughed, the sound bright and honestly melodic and Kate wanted to strangle her. “You have not thought anything worth listening to.”

    Yelena’s wrist flicked and the drive was flung toward the halfwall that led to the kitchen. Kate went to lunge for it when she saw a glint of metal flash out in front of her. A thunk and two small clatters later and Kate was staring at a knife in her wall and pieces of her drive on the ground.

    Without thinking, Kate shoved at Yelena, her irritation getting the better of her. Yelena didn’t move and she felt her palms meet the fabric of the t-shirt, slamming against incredibly strong shoulders.

    Yelena barely budged, rooted in place and eyes daring Kate to do more. Kate pulled her hands back toward herself before Yelena thought to make good on her threat. “It doesn’t matter. I can get another one.”

    Yelena walked toward her knife and pulled it from the wall, the blade disappearing from sight before Kate could tell where it came from.

    “And I will destroy that too.” Yelena walked back to her, expression devoid of any playfulness or humor. She tilted her head at Kate. “You must understand: you are going to lose. It is time for you to go home or you will hate me by the end of this. That would be pretty lame.”

    Kate crossed her arms over her chest, her pride wounded and her emotions running rampant, stomping across her chest.

    “I’m better than you think I am.”

    Yelena nodded, patting Kate’s arm and dodging the swat that followed.

    “I am sure you believe that.” She turned toward the door. “Have a good night, Kate Bishop. Be gone in the morning.”

    Chapter End Notes Let me know what you think! I'm not sure if this is the kind of story people are looking for with these two but I'm enjoying writing the back and forth.

    Chapter 4

    Chapter Notes This week has been shiiiit but at least I had Bishova to fall back on lol. Reminder, this isn't edited!

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Kate had been determined before.

    Her willpower could fluctuate in many areas of her life but when it came to getting something done that she really wanted to get done, she was virtually unstoppable. Or she had been in the past. Yelena was presenting quite an obstacle.

    But, it was either win or accept that Yelena was right; that she wasn’t going to be good enough for this life.

    That wasn’t an option.

    Kate stared out at the rolling green plains that led to a sandy beach and the open ocean, her body thrumming with dour agitation. She’d memorized this path, had memorized the surrounding area should she need to change course or lose a tail.

    Glancing behind her, she could only see an empty road and a blazing sun pouring out and blinding her when it caught the right angle in her mirror.

    Kate had spent twenty minutes parked at a gas station close to the resort searching her car for bugs and trackers and had come up with nothing. She’d taken apart her phone and put it back together once she’d been certain it was also clean.

    But Yelena had gotten in her head. If the assassin had planted trackers, there was a chance Kate wouldn’t be able to find them no matter how hard she looked.

    The idea rankled her and she pressed the gas pedal down further in hopes that breaking the speed limit by twenty would give her something else to worry about.

    She drove for another ten minutes before finding herself in a cozy town with historical buildings that had been refurbished and shops that were rebranded with tourism in mind. It was still early, around eight, but Kate could see the open signs were already turned outward in several businesses. She paid for a day of parking and found the first clothing store she could, paranoia driving her to change and leave anything that Yelena could have gotten her hands on in the car and away from her team.

    She found herself a navy, long-sleeved shirt, a tan baseball cap, and gray sweatpants with different iterations of Sandwich branding them and carried them to the desk. A young cashier–Grant by his nametag– stood at the counter and Kate dropped her things, her eyes landing on a rack of flip flops a few feet away. She shuffled over, found a purple pair in her size– fuck color coordination– and tossed them on top of the pile. She was almost sad they had no identifying ‘Sandwich Mass’ markings.

    The teen began scanning, his eyes tired and shoulders slouched as he bagged them up. Kate motioned toward the items.

    “Y’all should think about rebranding. I mean, Sandwich? The possibilities, the puns! They’re endless.”

    The kid rolled his eyes but Kate caught the lift of his lips even though his response was a simple, “I’ll let them know.”

    Kate nodded, eyes traveling around the bland shop.

    “I’m just saying, ‘Sandwish you had been here?’ would be a better t-shirt than just the name and some oars, don’t you think?”

    Grant huffed out a laugh and finally met her eyes.

    “I told the sandwich shop down the road that they should sell items with, ‘Have a Sandwich in Sandwich, they taste better’ written on all their merch and packaging. They didn’t bite.” Kate grinned.

    “Town’s filled with a bunch of bummers, isn’t it?”

    Grant sighed wistfully.

    “You have no idea.” He finished packaging her items and set them on the counter. “That’ll be seventy-two-sixy-seven.”

    Kate passed over her card and waited for her receipt that she shoved into the bag. She snapped her fingers.

    “I was born and bread in the town of Sandwich. You could sell it to the locals.” Grant outright laughed.

    “I think the stooges would run me out and I need to finish high school before I think about being turning everyone against me.”

    Kate shook her head in faux sadness, a grin twisting her lips.

    “I wish you the best of luck bringing puns to the town, kid. Have a good one.” Kate waved goodbye and returned to her car to change. She felt almost ridiculous as she shucked her clothes and dressed in her new purchases. She kicked her Vans off her feet and shuffled into the purple flip-flops.

    She looked like an idiot. If ever found out that Yelena didn’t plant anything in her shit she would definitely feel like one as well. As it was, she had no way to tell. She left everything but her keys– which she’d also checked– and started the thirty-minute jaunt toward the SWORD safehouse.

    She received a few odd looks on her path there but arrived outside without issue. It was a cozy-looking place, a true cottage, with stones of differing sizes making up the exterior and a chimney puffing smoke built up the side. White shutters sat on either side of the windows and drapes hung inside to cover them.

    As she walked up the gravel path to the porch, the door opened.

    Eben watched Kate with raised brows, her blond hair in a messy pony and her body decked out in a pair of pajama pants and a too-big sweater.

    Kate felt a smidgen of relief as she took in her friend.

    Maybe I should be concerned that so many of my friends are in their forties. She nodded her head at Eben.

    “Looks like the agent dress code is pretty lax around here.”

    Eben chuckled and pushed open the screen door for Kate.

    “It’s not a private school. We don’t have uniforms. Anyway,” She looked Kate up and down. “I think your current wardrobe should be our conversational focus.”

    Kate stepped into the house and let her shoulders drop, head leaning back as she groaned dramatically.

    “I have a lot to tell you.”

    Kate had asked Eben to let her finish before she said anything but she could see the deeply worried tilt of her brows and the downturn of her lips.

    Kate had jumped into it rather quickly, knowing the more time she spent in Sandwich the longer she wasn’t doing her actual job.

    She could hear muted conversation in the room beside the kitchen where she was currently tucked into a bay window looking out over a nice backyard.

    Eben said that Jack and Akilah had set up in the living room and practically overtaken the space with their computers and equipment.

    Kate dug her finger into the surface of the table, picking at loose, white paint on the warped wood. She had a half-empty mug of hot chocolate– her request–and a scone that Jack had made uneaten in front of her.

    “So yeah, this is going to be a lot more difficult than I thought but I can do it. I don’t want to be pulled out.”

    Eben took a sip of her tea, eyes never leaving Kate. She set it back down and pursed her lips. “Kate, a Black Widow on a mission is a massive red-flag for any agent. In the past even a whiff of one nearby required an entirely new approach and additional agents. It’s far too dangerous for you to be pursuing this on your own; especially when the Widow has a team with her. It’s not a knock on your skill. This would be the case for me as well. For Amil, Jack, Akilah, None of us are to the level of an assassin trained from the moment they could walk.”

    Kate nodded along, waiting for her moment to counter.

    “Yes, but I know this Widow. She was the one tasked with killing Clint before. She doesn’t want to hurt me.”

    Eben looked pointedly at the cut on Kate’s face.

    “She already has. I can’t risk your life for a hunch.”

    Kate shook her head sharply and pulled out the note Yelena had left her. She pushed it over to Eben to read.

    “It’s not a hunch. I know she doesn’t want to hurt me. The problem is, she’s absolutely convinced that I can’t beat her– which, yeah obviously not physically– but there has to be a way I can get this job done anyway.”

    Eben took the note, her face shifting from concern to vague amusement as she pondered Yelena’s words. She passed the note back after a moment.

    “How well do you know her?”

    Kate considered whether she should lie but stuck to the truth.

    “In all honesty, hardly at all. But she’s had multiple chances to kill me and she hasn’t. She didn’t go through with killing Clint and she told me about what my mom was doing.” Kate hesitated on her next words, heart clenching and a sense of guilt filling her as she added the next part. It felt almost disrespectful to utter the words without Yelena’s permission. “She’s also Natasha Romanoff’s sister.”

    Eben’s eyes widened and Kate watched her reprocess everything that had been said with new understanding. She finished the last of her tea as she considered the updates and risks and finally gave Kate a slight nod.

    “Fine. I’m giving you seventy-two hours to make some headway. If anything happens during that time that I deem too dangerous, I’m pulling you. Akilah will be ready to extract if I give her the word.”

    Kate grinned, falling back in her chair and tossing her hands up.

    “Fuck yes!”

    Eben rolled her eyes.

    “I’m serious, Kate. If you get yourself killed when I’m supposed to be watching your back I’m going to be mad.”

    Kate laughed, taking her first bite of the scone that looked to have blueberries baked inside. It was surprisingly good.

    She spoke with her mouth full.

    “I promise I won’t die. Any advice on that whole making progress thing? Yelena already destroyed my drive and she’s been one step ahead of me the entire time.” Eben gave her a look.

    “It’s because she knows you, Kate. Even if you don’t know her. She might not know every secret that you have, but she has created a profile of you in her mind and it is likely, and unfortunately, rather accurate. Despite that, I do believe she’s making a mistake when it comes to boxing in your morality.”

    Kate frowned.

    “What does that mean?”

    “It means that she’s decided she knows what you are and aren’t willing to do for this job. You have the capability of surprising her. Doing so will just put you in more uncomfortable positions than you might be ready for.”

    Kate grimaced.

    “You think I should sleep with Hechel?”

    Eben’s aghast face was answer enough even before she opened her mouth to rebuke it. “Absolutely not. I’m talking about looking at your limitations and pushing your own boundaries. It is risky, but being unpredictable is what makes a good agent. Yelena is only human despite her skill. She can’t read your mind and she doesn’t have all the answers.” Kate bobbed her head in thought for several moments, her mind swirling with ideas. After a moment, she nodded.

    “Can we start with making two drives? I have an idea.”

    Kate stood in front of Jack looking curiously at the two new pieces of technology she’d never truly understand. They were tiny, easily hidden, and she had a pretty gnarly plan. “Will blood damage them?”

    Jack’s brow furrowed as they shifted on their feet, green eyes studying Kate. “Uh– Um. No. It won’t. Why?”

    Kate smiled excitedly and received a bewildered one in return.

    “Can you get me a blade and a suture kit?”

    Jack’s brows shot high as they pieced together what Kate wanted to do. They stared in surprise for a time and then nodded reluctantly.

    “Sure.”

    They dipped from the room leaving her alone with Akilah, a woman built like a tank with the kindest face Kate had ever seen. She had light brown skin and long braids that stretched down her back.

    She was watching Kate with lifted brows though she looked far less disturbed than Jack. “Are you going to hide it on your person?”

    Kate nodded, lifting her arms in front of her and debating where it might be best hidden. “Yes.” She held up her left arm. “Where do you think I should put it?” Akilah patted a meaty spot on her own thigh.

    “In the leg, not the arm. Easier to hide with clothing and more space to work with.” She grinned. “Just don’t hit the artery.”

    Jack returned with a medical bag and set it on the only open space of the coffee table. The rest was cluttered with gadgets and pieces of equipment Kate didn’t recognize. “Are you wanting to do it or would you like me to?”

    Kate was glad she wasn’t being looked at like a crazy person. She glanced down at her thigh and gave a hopeful smile.

    “I could probably figure it out…”

    Akilah pushed herself from the couch and clapped her hands together.

    “I’ll do it. Let’s go to the kitchen so no blood falls into the carpet.” As she passed Jack, she pressed a hand to their back. “You run the face of the woman Kate believe’s is Stražar and see what you find.”

    It wasn’t a command, but Kate watched curiously as Jack blushed and ducked back toward their screens.

    Akilah prodded Kate into the kitchen and pushed out a seat for her.

    “Would you like a pair of shorts or do you mind just removing your sweats? Kate answered by tugging her pants halfway down her thighs and taking a seat in the chair. She felt a little clammy at what was coming but knew that it was likely the least of her upcoming trials.

    A cup of cleaning solution was set beside her and Kate tossed one of the drives into it before watching Akilah wash her hands thoroughly and don a pair of gloves.

    She was proficient and clinical with her movements as she wrapped a towel around Kate’s thigh.

    “Hold this.”

    Kate gripped either side of the towel and watched Akilah clean the area with bated breath, her eyes glued to her leg.

    Akilah glanced at her.

    “Don’t be too worried now. I will numb the area. You won’t have that luxury when you need to cut it from your leg. Pay very close attention because if you cut too deeply or in the wrong place, you will damage your muscle.”

    Akilah gave her a small shot to the same area and Kate felt her leg numb over the next forty seconds.

    She watched curiously as Akilah pulled a scalpel and began cutting at a steep angle, less so carving in and more lifting up a layer of Kate’s skin. Blood pooled and slid around Kate’s thigh but she felt none of it as Akilah pulled the drive from the cleaning solution and dried it thoroughly on a sterile cloth.

    Once she was certain it was dry, Akilah pressed the drive into the pocket she’d created in Kate’s skin and settled it into place.

    Kate found herself both intrigued and disturbed by the scene playing out and she stared as Akilah began to stitch the wound closed. It took only a couple minutes for Kate to be cleaned up and ready to go.

    Akilah patted Kate’s knee kindly.

    “Alright, good to go. It’s going to be very annoying when the local wears off. If you itch and prod at it, Yelena will catch on. It’s a very good test of your willpower.” She stood and motioned back toward the living room.

    “Jack probably has the information we’re looking for. Come on.”

    When she pushed through the swinging door, Kate took in Jack in their element, their eyes focused on the large screen before them as they scrolled through what looked oddly similar to Bishop Security’s dismantled database.

    They paused and zoomed in on a photo positioned beside a screen grab Jack had been able to snag from the resort’s security.

    “This her?”

    Kate nodded immediately, the picture a dead ringer besides the lack of a septum and hair that came to her chin.

    “She was staring at Stražar’s room a bunch and she was in the group Yelena kicked me out of.”

    Jack was nodding, eyes never leaving the screen.

    “Yeah, I bet. Her name is Sifiso. She has connections to the Black Dahlias. That’s not the worst news, but I’m sure that Yelena already tagged her as a threat.”

    “Black Dahlias?”

    Jack pulled up a few more documents; some with pictures others with gray spaces. “They work out of Los Angeles. Good mission. They’re practically vigilantes but they ride the line of crime pretty hard. Crazy mother fuckers. Won’t kill unless they have to. I can imagine they want the code but are unwilling or unable to pay the price.” Kate stared at the photos of the other members on the screen.

    “There’s not many of them.”

    Jack chuckled.

    “There are plenty more than just these. The organization has grown pretty rapidly over the years and they’ve managed to protect the identities of most of their newer members. Sifiso isn’t directly connected, possibly due to them covering her tracks or because she is an independent contractor that they hire out for anonymity.”

    Kate sat on the edge of the couch and tried to lean closer to the computer, her mind abuzz as it started connecting the new pieces to the puzzle. A pool of dread panged in her stomach. “Can you search for any reports of dead bodies found in the area?”

    Jack nodded, not even questioning her line of thinking. They may have come to the same conclusion Kate had.

    If Yelena already marked her, there’s a chance she hadn’t made it through the night. Kate’s heart felt heavy in her chest, disappointment at both herself and Yelena. It was silly, she knew that. Yelena was an assassin. This was her job. But Yelena hadn’t gone through with Clint’s death and for some reason Kate had thought that maybe she was veering away from that path.

    It was naivety and nothing more.

    After a few tense moments, Jack shook their head.

    “Nothing so far. I wouldn’t be shocked if we don’t find any trace of her. I can’t imagine Yelena is underfunded.”

    Kate sighed, already itching to get back. She wanted to finish this as soon as possible so that nobody else ended up dead; so that Yelena didn’t have to add anyone else to the list of people she’d killed.

    She wasn’t sure why that part mattered to her so much but it did.

    “Alright.” She nodded her head absently. “Okay. I need to get back. While I talk to Eben will you try and get information on the other two?”

    Kate had switched into her own clothing and she returned to her car feeling like she wasn’t going to be able to sit still for the upcoming drive. Her leg was burning where they’d inserted the tracker and she couldn’t get over the reality that Sifiso could be dead. The next near-hour was near torture, traffic into the peninsula slowing her down and keeping her on edge. She slammed the steering wheel with her palm as another vehicle cut in front of her, causing her to miss the next light.

    Breathe, Kate. Fucking breathe.

    She leaned her head against the seat and took several deep breaths. She knew who Yelena was before showing up. It wasn’t new information. Kate didn’t know that Sifiso was in danger and there was no way she could have saved her.

    She thought about the other two that had caught her interest. Both pedestrians, business people that had come to the resort to work through their vacations. Both normal citizens that would leave Cape Cod and find their way back to their lives.

    The light turned green and Kate waited for her moment to jump into the right lane and speed around the vehicle that had cut her off.

    She drove the next five minutes like she was being chased and finally pulled into the valet circle at slightly too fast a pace.

    Kate didn’t wait for the valet to open the door as she jumped out and tossed her keys at the woman. She scrambled for a tip and stuffed it into her hand, walking quickly toward the lobby and ignoring the valet’s standard greeting.

    She slowed as she reached the entrance and allowed the doors to open for her. Her eyes roved the lobby and the pool as she passed through them but didn’t see anyone that she recognized. It was disheartening, but she knew she needed to gather herself. People died all the time in the life she wanted to pursue. Getting torn up about it wasn’t something she could afford. That didn’t so much help as it allowed her to shove the things she was feeling down to be processed later.

    When she made it back to her room, she retrieved a knife she’d brought in her suitcase and walked to the living room, the only part of her cottage that had carpet. She’d been thinking about where to hide the second drive, wanting to ensure it looked like she tried extremely hard to keep it from Yelena.

    Kate knelt beside the couch and pushed it to the side. She carved a small slit in the carpet in the midst of the fluffy fibers and slipped the drive into it. When she pushed the seam back down it looked like it had never been disturbed.

    A grin split her face and she was careful to slide the sofa back to the exact place that it had been before.

    Her inspiration was her own skin pocket – a term the team attempted to veto– and she was pretty proud of the placement.

    Something told her Yelena would find it, but at least it wouldn’t look like she’d half-assed it. A spark of annoyance flared up as she thought about Yelena’s condescending laugh. She’d probably say something rude like, ”Oh Kate Bishop, it was a good effort. It took me over four minutes to locate this.” Kate made a face and stood, her voice doing a poor mimic of Yelena’s accent. “Kate Bishop, you are very bad at hiding. Did you never play the seek and hide? So sad.” She huffed, her voice coming out normal if a bit petulant as she continued. “We’ll see how dumb you feel when you realize I tricked you. Asshole.”

    Realizing that engaging with the Yelena in her head was probably bordering on creepy, Kate refocused.

    Kate tucked the knife back into her suitcase after a thorough cleaning of the blade and collapsed on the bed to think. It was only two in the afternoon and she had three days to try and make something happen.

    Her only lead on Stražar had been a dead end and Kate was afraid that she’d have to start from the beginning.

    No, not the beginning. She knew that Stražar was a woman and that she was supposedly staying on the third floor.

    Something about that bugged Kate. Her instincts were chittering about it but they weren’t speaking loud enough for her to put together why it felt wrong.

    The information had been gathered from an infrared camera picking up on a significant source of heat from the software running within the room. It made sense. Stražar was probably running tons of programs to protect herself and facilitate the sale. Her room likely would be emanating heat like crazy.

    But Stražar would know that too. And she seemed too intelligent and careful to have missed this one thing.

    Her instincts kicked up.

    Stražar had taunted them by removing the money in one of the only accounts they had access to. They wanted anyone looking to know where they were going.

    Kate bent her knees, feet pressed into the unmade comforter as she cracked her knuckles one by one.

    Hechel was on the third floor. Yelena had seen her climbing on the balconies. Sifiso knew the location of the room. There was a team of seven players and they had shifts. Kate sat up so quickly her head spun. She launched out of bed and sprinted to put on her shoes.

    Stražar wasn’t at the resort.

    It was a red herring and everyone was falling for it.

    She tied the laces of her tennis shoes and threw her hair into a ponytail. She began a series of quick stretches to loosen up her limbs even as her impatience drove her to leave immediately. She would run along the beach in the opposite direction of her last trip. That path had taken her past small shops and homes. While it was possible the hacker was staying somewhere within them, it would be safer for them to reside in a larger hotel or resort where they could blend in. There were five within five miles of this resort. One was just down the road. Kate stuck her headphones over her ears but left the music off and headed for her door. She’d barely opened it when she saw the blonde tresses of her least favorite assassin; a bright smile lighting up her face.

    “Kate Bishop, where are you going in such a hurry?”

    Kate screeched to a stop before she slammed into Yelena who hadn’t budged or flinched at the close call.

    She drew back and opened her mouth to answer only to find herself at a loss. Seeing Yelena now, knowing that she’d probably killed Sifiso, had her slipping toward her earlier feelings again and she apparently failed to keep it from her face.

    Yelena tilted her head as she watched it play out on and her smile slipped to neutral. “You are mad at me.”

    Kate tried to swallow the feelings, cursing Yelena’s dumb, amazing perceptiveness. “I don’t know why you’re surprised by that considering the bullshit you’ve pulled the last several days.”

    Yelena shook her head, dismissing Kate’s excuse.

    “No, this is not that kind of angry. That is okay angry.” She paused, eyes searching. “I do not like this angry.”

    Kate rolled her eyes, a reluctant fondness filling her chest at Yelena’s annoyed look. “Angry is angry.”

    Yelena let out an exasperated huff and she bodied Kate to the side to enter her living room. “Stop being difficult. It is very childish.”

    Fondness gone.

    Kate closed the door behind them, the previous plan on hold until she could pursue it without Yelena’s eyes on her.

    “Lacking proper manners is also childish, Yelena–”

    She cut herself off in her mocking as she realized she didn’t know the assassin’s last name. Yelena picked up on this immediately, her expression turning into glee. She motioned impatiently toward Kate.

    “What is wrong?”

    Kate stared anywhere but at Yelena for several moments as she tried to decide the best course of action. The assassin knowing about Sifiso wouldn’t give Yelena more information or tip Kate’s hand. Yelena probably knew she’d been a person of interest for Kate. Kate didn’t try as hard as she should to find a reason to hold her tongue. The desire to know the truth was so intense and she could feel the question pressing out of her lungs and into her mouth before she was entirely decided on speaking it.

    “Did you kill Sifiso?”

    Yelena’s face settled into neutrality again, mind calculating and very quickly coming to the right conclusion.

    “You are disappointed.”

    “That’s not an answer.”

    Yelena’s eyes hardened and Kate hated how she was capable of turning from playful and annoying into a killer in less than a breath.

    “You do not want to hear it.”

    Kate’s heart dropped and she felt her disappointment shift to anger.

    “Did you even bother figuring out who she was before you ended her life or does that just not matter to you?”

    Yelena approached slowly as her eyes screamed out a challenge, a reprimand. “You are ignorant of this world that you try to play in with your costume and your sticks and string. You should not even have to ask the question.”

    Kate felt the hairs on her arms rise up as her instincts screamed ‘danger,’ fear settled like a stone in her chest. She pushed at it viciously. She would not be afraid of Yelena. “I’m not dead.”

    Yelena stepped far too close.

    “Push me and see how long that will last.”

    It felt real. The threat carved across Kate’s chest with the force of an actual blade but she was far more stubborn than Yelena gave her credit for. With every part of her brain screaming at her to stay still and stay silent, Kate listened to her gut.

    She moved into the small space between them and even though she was taller she felt the opposite as Yelena’s eyes darkened.

    “I’ve already pushed you. I fought you. I ignored your warnings and your threats. I’m still here. Do something about it.”

    It was probably the riskiest thing Kate had done since arriving; actively taunting Yelena, basically inviting her to kill her.

    Yelena’s emotions were shrouded by the coldness of her expression but Kate could see the twitch of the muscles in her jaw and could almost hear her teeth grind with how hard they must be clenching.

    Kate waited for the strike, waited for anything really.

    When Yelena moved Kate tensed. It took her a moment to realize that no attack was coming. She blinked and Yelena was bypassing her and opening the door.

    Kate stood there in surprise for several moments before allowing her shoulders to fall and her breath to stream out audibly.

    A hint of pride bloomed in her chest and she collapsed back on the sofa; it felt like a win. Kate glanced toward the door, debating whether to try and follow Yelena. That thought was swiftly placed in the category: a way to actually lose her life.

    Instead, she let her adrenaline from the encounter propel her to complete her previous plan. She needed to get a better lay of the land and checking the surrounding hotels was her best bet.

    Kate zipped her phone into the pocket of her shorts and gave Yelena a ten minute buffer before leaving.

    Kate ran for five miles, eyes heavy on the hotels and resorts that she passed. She stuck to the pavement this time in order to get a better look into the spaces.

    Her real interest was the hotel almost adjacent to the resort she was staying in. On her way back, she’d slowed to a stop beside it, feigning exhaustion (was it really fake if she was actually gasping air into her lungs?) and sitting in the patch of grass outside. She did her cooldown there, her eyes tracking the windows and counting the levels. She walked along the side of the hotel that faced the resort, swinging her arms in circles and seeking out Stražar’s supposed room.

    Her eyes locked onto it with ease and Kate’s pulse jumped in excitement as she tilted her head back toward the hotel. It was the perfect angle: not direct but close enough to watch from an east-facing balcony. Her eyes tracked up and her grin widened. Or from the roof. Kate thought about entering and taking a look around but an idea was beginning to form in her mind as she finished her stretches.

    With a final glance at the roof, Kate started the walk back to her little cottage, pleased at her findings and giving herself a mental pat on the back.

    She slowed as neared it, her eyes landing on the open door and a man in the resort uniform standing outside looking in. She could hear murmuring voices and she halted her approach. What the…

    Kate knew standing and watching would get her no answers so she jogged the remainder of the distance and caught the attention of the guy outside.

    “What’s going on?”

    He startled slightly and then straightened his shoulders back.

    “Um, Miss Bishop–”

    He was interrupted by a woman stepping out in a suit, her eyes sharp and angry. “Miss Bishop, maybe you can explain to us why you thought it acceptable to destroy resort property?”

    Kate’s eyes widened.

    “What?” She tried to get closer to look but the woman blocked her path. Kate looked at her harshly enough that she saw the woman swallow. Kate glanced down at her name tag. “Brenda. I don’t know what you’re talking about. If you could move I might be able to shed some light.”

    Brenda glared but moved out of her way and Kate was quick to slip inside. She froze. Things were quite literally turned upside down. The coffee table in the living room was broken like a body had fallen on it and the lamps in the room were both shattered on the ground. The television had a circular dent in it and the stand it was on had its doors ripped from its hinges. There was a hole in the wall by the couch and Kate could barely see through the archway but the bedroom was clearly much the same.

    Rage flooded her as she took in the almost perfect way everything was destroyed. There were broken liquor bottles on the ground, stains of varying colors all over the carpet, and white powder dusted on the side of the coffee table that hadn’t been flattened. It all made it look like she and a herd of massive gorillas had gone on a bender, snorted lines of cocaine, and destroyed everything in sight.

    “Holy shit.”

    Brenda spoke right into her ear and Kate had to refrain from punching her. “That’s right, Miss Bishop. This is unacceptable.”

    Kate didn’t look at her.

    “I didn’t do this.”

    Brenda scoffed.

    “We received a tip from another guest at the resort that there were party sounds coming from your cottage all the way into the morning.”

    Kate whirled on her, causing Brenda to take a step back.

    “Take in the context clues! You think I’d be capable of getting this blitzed and then be conscious enough to go on a run several hours later? Check with your valet drivers. I left today and didn’t get back until the afternoon!”

    “Miss Bishop, being rude isn’t going to help you in this situation. I’m sorry but we’re going to need you to remove your items and leave the resort. You will be charged for the damage to the cottage unless it can be proven that you did not cause it. We’ll keep the payment for the remainder of your stay as partial payment and charge you the rest.”

    Kate’s blood was boiling but it had nothing to do with Brenda– the woman was just doing her job and Kate would definitely be pissed in her shoes– and everything to do with a miniature blonde menace that literally couldn’t let Kate win for more than an hour. She’d only been gone an hour!

    Kate threw up her hands and stormed further into the room. She found her own belongings entirely untouched and quickly threw her dirty clothes into the suitcases. It took a total of four minutes to fully pack and drag them to the front door.

    The man there, who hadn’t said anything for the duration of this terrible encounter, shuffled forward.

    “I can go ahead and take these to the front. Your car will be brought around.” Kate nodded absently and turned to look at the space. Her eyes fell to the couch in the living room and she sighed.

    “I just need to do one more sweep.”

    Brenda watched like a hawk as Kate walked through the rooms again. As her last stop, she knelt down and reached under the couch to feel around. Her hand quickly found the pocket of carpet and Kate wasn’t surprised to find it empty.

    She huffed and pushed to her feet, her vision tinted red but the reality of her situation taking place. She walked up to Brenda as calmly as she could.

    Her expression must not have been all that friendly because Brenda watched her warily. Reaching into her backpack, the only thing she’d kept on her person after the suitcases were taken away, Kate pulled out her wallet.

    “Look, Brenda. I didn’t do this but there’s no way to convince you of that. Money isn’t an issue so charge away at the card on file.” Kate pulled out a wad of one hundred dollar bills and held them out. She saw Brenda’s eyes land on them with poorly concealed interest. “Keep this under the radar, would you? My name does not need to be involved. In fact, if it happened to come across that the cottage was ransacked and the guest that was staying here left early…” Kate shrugged. “that would just solve all of our problems.” Brenda stared at what had to be over a thousand dollars. She slowly reached out and Kate tugged it back to get her attention.

    “But, and I mean this, if my name is attached to this, you can’t imagine the kind of pain I can cause to this business. It doesn’t need to come to anything nasty, but I really can’t have any more bad press. Understood?”

    Brenda seemed irritated by the threat but she nodded her head anyway.
