    “Understood. There are a bunch of hooligans on this beach.”

    Kate’s lips twitched into a grin that she didn’t feel.

    “There’s the spirit.”

    She dropped the cash into Brenda’s hand and walked past her into the warm night air. Kate could hardly feel anything as she took one step after another toward the main building of the resort. Her chest was painfully tight and her hands shook at her sides. As she reached the pool, she slowed to a stop.

    Of course. Yelena.

    She stood in the courtyard by the pool only lit up by the lights of the resort. There was a look of finality on her face and Kate wanted to sprint at her. She wanted to tackle her to the ground and shake the living hell out of her.

    When Yelena’s fingers pressed to her forehead in a mock salute practically claiming responsibility for the current events, Kate’s teeth clenched.

    She didn’t approach, didn’t give into the sway of her body as it ripped at her and clawed toward Yelena with all its rage. She had to be smarter. She had to be unpredictable and Kate knew that Yelena wanted her pissed off and fed up.

    Kate allowed a look of defeat to overcome her face and she turned away from Yelena to march the rest of the way to the entrance.

    Her car was waiting for her and the valet handed her her keys with pep, like he hadn’t heard of the chaos and the subsequent banning from the resort. Maybe he hadn’t. Brenda seemed like she was shrewd enough to get things done. It probably wasn’t the first time a lot of money had been offered to buy her silence. The woman probably expected it from people that were able to afford shirking responsibility for their actions.

    It rankled Kate to be one of them, but she had bigger problems to take care of. Kate climbed into her car, ensured her bags were in the back, and waited until she’d driven away to let out the scream of frustration that had been building in her chest. She slammed the steering wheel multiple times and hardly felt the bursts of pain in her palm through the haze. Sucking in deep lungfuls of air, Kate’s mind tried to slough through the anger and find her next move.

    As she drove further from the resort her eyes landed on the hotel that she’d been casing earlier. A half-formed idea planted itself into her mind and grew, pulling an almost manic grin to her face.

    Yelena had just given her an in. She’d given her a reason to move locations. Kate let out a breathy laugh as she pulled to a stop just three minutes later, her eyes on the tall building that Stražar was likely squirreled within.

    If they were playing a game of chess; Yelena had taken a lot of her pieces. But this felt like check.

    “Room for one. One of the top floors if possible.”

    The clerk behind the counter smiled at her.

    “Of course. How long will you be staying and will there be other guests?” Kate leaned forward and gave her best charming grin.

    “Three days. And no, it’ll just be me.”

    The clerk typed on her keyboard in silence for several seconds before looking back up. “Our top two floors are full currently but I’ve got you a room just below on the eighth floor! Would you prefer a deluxe or standard suite?”

    “Deluxe is fine.”

    The clerk smiled again.

    “Great! We have a King remaining that I can get you booked in. It’ll be six hundred and forty four dollar plus an additional fifty for the walk-in-booking. If you could just give me your ID and a credit card I can go ahead and get you set up.”

    Kate hesitated. If she was right and Stražar was in this hotel, Yelena easily could have had alerts tagged to her name even if she hadn’t told the rest of her team about Kate’s presence. But it wasn’t like she had another option. She’d given most of her emergency cash to buy Brenda’s silence and what she had left wouldn’t cover the hotel and a bribe. Kate sucked up the risk and passed her ID and card over.

    Within fifteen minutes she was able to get her key card and get to the elevator, hoping she looked like a weary last-minute-guest should anybody be watching the security cameras. Her room was decent and clean, the furniture brand new and the view out toward the rest of the island was beautiful. Kate tossed her two suitcases onto the ground and slipped her backpack off her shoulders with a sigh.

    She knew that there was a serious chance Yelena was tracking her but she had a plan to take care of that. Hopefully it would look like she’d just picked a hotel to stay at for the night before booking it out of town the next day.

    Kate crawled into her bed and went boneless, the fury that had built up not totally subsiding but at least taking a back seat to the exhaustion she felt. She didn’t think she’d ever been so thoroughly outplayed in her life and it seemed like Yelena wasn’t even trying. Yeah, she’d incidentally come out on top– hopefully– but it had taken every bit of brainpower to stay even remotely close.

    She knew the next two days wouldn’t be any easier and decided going to sleep would be a better idea than trying to muddle through in her current state.

    Her plan was simple. She was going to drive back to Sandwich, call Akilah to trade vehicles with her, and buy enough clothing to get her through at least a few days. She had Akilah give her a cloned phone– she had a text from Hechel from fifteen hours before that she really needed to respond to– and she’d also taken another drive with the false code just in case she needed one more decoy.

    That left her with the task of checking her weapons for bugs. Her bow was easy. She knew the weight and feel like the back of her hand. It was likely Yelena knew that and she wasn’t surprised to find it clean.

    Her Ronin sword was an easy check as well.

    That just left her quiver.

    When Kate removed her arrows she used the scantron device Akilah had brought with her to do a first sweep of the equipment. It revealed nothing. Not trusting it, Kate removed the organizing screen and shoved her hand into the opening to feel around. As her fingertips brushed the bottom, she felt a small ridge. Kate flicked it hard enough to dislodge it and crowed in triumph as she tipped a small black device onto the ground Akilah whistled.

    “If her trackers are cloaked from even our technology I’m no longer assuming this act of yours is paranoia.”

    Kate glanced over to where Akilah was standing by the front of the vehicle. “Yelena covers her bases. Smart to put it on my quiver. I won’t do anything too involved without it. I’m sure there’s one somewhere in the car as well. Maybe the brand new wardrobe was a stretch but I have been on the losing side of this battle far too much to risk it.” Kate stuck her new– similarly colored– suitcases with two sets of clothing and a new tactical suit from Akilah into the front of the navy sedan. One suitcase was empty but Kate wasn’t about to show up differently than she’d left.

    She tossed her collapsible bow, sword, and quiver into a backpack similar to her last one and stood there for a moment to make sure she hadn’t forgotten anything.

    Akilah watched patiently and raised her eyebrows when Kate nodded absently. “Ready to go?”

    Kate spun her new set of keys in her hand and pocketed her wallet.

    “Yeah. I know it’s a pain in the ass for you to drive this car back toward New York. Hopefully the handoff happens sooner rather than later.”

    Akilah waved away her concern.

    “No, this is a good plan. Have the trackers working against Yelena and make her think you’re leaving. I do wonder if we should give you a new ID and credit card.”

    Kate had dismissed that idea already and she explained her reasoning.

    “I’m banking on the fact that Yelena hasn’t told her team or Stražar about me. She’s been relying on her own methods to keep track of me so it’s likely she’s not going to be looking at the hotel records. I’m certain Stražar would notice if somebody with a similar face to another guest entered and paid with a different name. He’s probably watching the lobby very closely. I think the only true risk I took was leaving with my suitcases today.” Akilah nodded, a curious and impressed look in her eyes as she listened to Kate’s thought processes.

    “I wish you good luck, as does the team. But you should know that you are doing very well. Agents have failed first assignments that were far easier than this one. You are cut out for this, Kate. Don’t let her get in your head.”

    Kate ignored the swell of emotions those words brought her and gave Akilah a grateful smile. “Thank you. I’m going to get this done. I’ll head your way as soon as possible.” Akilah called out as Kate went to open her own door.

    “Remember, if you need to, just destroy the code. That’s better than nothing if replacing it isn’t possible.”

    “Got it. I’ll keep that in mind.”

    Kate waved and popped open the sedan door. She took a deep breath.

    This was it. She had to stay off of Yelena’s radar as well as Stražar’s for this to work. Kate’s expression was grimly determined as she lowered herself into the sedan. She wasn’t above things getting messy if that turned out to be her only option.

    Chapter End Notes Any comments are appreciated and make me feel all gooey inside. Thanks for reading!

    Chapter 5

    Chapter Notes Several things: -My cat peed on my bed for the first time ever and that is the only reason you're getting this chapter tonight. I couldn't sleep until I washed everything. I usually wait for the kudos to hit 100 times the chapter number but that's just a general guideline. -I was watching RuPaul's Drag Race as I finished this so like, oops if it is subpar lol. -I struggled immensely to put images of Kate's notes/charts (you'll see what I mean) in the chapter and I couldn't figure it out. I should have taken it out entirely but fuuuck rewriting like hundreds of words to fix it. Sorry if the descriptions aren't clear. I might go back in the future rework it. Probably not.

    -This is unedited.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Kate’s return to the hotel had been nerve-wracking at best and borderline panic-inducing at worst.

    She’d acted careless; her eyes on her phone as she moved across the lobby. Internally her heart was pounding and she was waiting for Yelena’s voice to crop up in another ‘gotcha’ moment that probably would have Kate trying to tackle her again.

    Instead, she was able to make it to her room with no interruptions.

    She checked just below the handle for the tape she’d laid over the seam between frame and door. It was unbroken, and she tugged it off and folded it for the trash. She entered and closed the door behind her with a relieved sigh.

    Kate set her new items on her bed and pulled out the tactical suit she hadn’t had a chance to try out yet.

    For the next ten minutes she acted like a child with a new toy as she changed the colors of the nano-tech leather. It had several pockets tucked along its seams that contained various chemical agents .

    There were little marble-sized balls she could activate and toss to create smoke. Another was filled with capsules that contained a sleeping agent.

    The last three were different methods of incapacitating a large group of people: flash-bang, tear gas, electricity.

    Honestly Kate was a little scared to put it on her body but Eben had promised her that she would be entirely safe. It required human touch and a throwing force to trigger. It seemed a little redundant considering what her arrows could do, but she wasn’t going to turn her nose up at something that could come in hand in a pinch.

    The suit was also bullet-proof which Kate was thrilled about.

    She loved her Hawkeye outfit but its utility was underwhelming when compared to SWORD’s technology. Maybe she could request a customized suit once she had a net positive of completed missions.

    Her phone buzzed in her hand and Kate lifted it to see another message from Hechel. She looked at both of the texts that she had yet to read.

    Friday, 6:33pm: Hechel: my horrible partner is being a bigger bitch than ever. i could definitely drink tonight. . . maybe we can have it a little more comfortable in my room? Today, 1:48pm: Hechel: kate I am beginning to worry that i offended you. my apologies. i do not assume you are easy if that is what your worried about. usually i have women in my bed by the first night ;)

    Kate groaned audibly, glad she didn’t have to jump on the offer to get up to the third floor of the resort.

    She quickly typed out the response she’d crafted on the drive back.

    Today, 1:51pm: Kate: Hechel! I’m sorry for the late text but things have been crazy! I had to head out right after my early meeting yesterday and I’ve been traveling since. It was lovely to meet you but it seems it wasn’t meant to be.

    After a moment she grinned and sent a second message.

    Today, 1:51pm: Kate: If you are serious about your last text, you shouldn’t have any problem finding somebody else to warm your sheets tonight.

    It was hardly four minutes before she received a response.

    Today, 1:55pm: Hechel: my heart is broken! i will remain alone tonight and spend it thinking of you.

    She had no intention of responding again, but something bitter and petty rose in her and guided her fingers across the keys.

    Today, 2:06pm: Kate: You know, I have been thinking about your partner. I think she is so rude to you because she likes you. She’s jealous of the attention you give other women.

    She knew she might as well be signing Hechel’s death warrant but the idea of him coming onto Yelena with any kind of confidence was so ludicrous and comical to her and she wouldn’t miss the opportunity to make that happen.

    Today, 2:07pm: Hechel: do you really think so? imagine us! two very beautiful lovers. i will not lie i have had many dreams about it.

    Kate was both offended and delighted that he’d picked up her suggestion so quickly. She was also verging into the territory of becoming a confidant to another enemy and Clint had told her numerous times that that was a terrible idea.

    Still, she wanted to drive home the belief so she offered Hechel one more piece of advice. Today, 2:11pm: Kate: I do. Maybe a grand romantic gesture would get her to admit her feelings! I wish you the best of luck. Since you are moving on, I’ll be deleting your number so I can also find my soulmate.

    Kate was full-on cackling as she stood in the middle of her hotel room. It was unbecoming of an agent but she would pay such good money to see the look on Yelena’s face when Hechel did whatever idiot thing he was going to do.

    She tossed her phone on the bed and pulled on a baseball cap to round out her outfit. She was wearing a simple pair of jean shorts and a nice blouse: not her style in any way but it fit into the vibe of the Cape and she wanted to blend in as much as possible for her next step in her scraped-together plan.

    It was going to be risky, but Kate was under the impression that every step from there on out would be make or break.

    She left her hotel room, a piece of tape placed under the door handle again, and sauntered down the hallway to the stairwell. It had less cameras and she hoped that Yelena and her team would prefer the easy ride up ten flights on the elevator.

    There was a chance that Stražar was on a different floor, but Kate had a feeling that the reason the top two floors were entirely booked out was due to a desire to maintain a perimeter.

    A fuck-ton of the plan is based off of your feelings and assumptions.

    Doubt crept into Kate’s mind as she descended the steps but she shoved it down. Akilah said she was good at this. Her instincts were good.

    Kate reached the first floor and pushed out a side door into the parking lot, eyes peeled mostly for a grumpy, blonde asshole. She walked casually to her vehicle and climbed inside. Kate drove to the closest dispensary– still about thirty minutes away– and bought several pre- rolled joints and tucked them into her backpack.

    She’d have it on her from now on, needing to be able to access her weapons at any moment. She meandered for an hour to kill time and ensure it looked like she was enjoying her stay on the Cape, not just lounging about in her hotel.

    By the time she returned to the hotel it was nearing five and Kate found a parking spot that allowed her to keep eyes on two out of the three entrances while keeping her tucked between other vehicles She rolled down the tinted windows slightly and opened the sun roof before reclining her chair enough that it would look like she was fully lounging. Her sunglasses covered her eyes and her hair was tied in a bun at the back of her head and pulled through the gap above the closure of her hat. She lit one of the blunts and leaned back.

    For the next twenty minutes, Kate watched. She’d calculated the possible shift times based off of the five to nine time frame and Hechel revealing he was only free in the night and early mornings due to his work. She also only ever saw Yelena around the resort around one to five with the exception of the night before. Either the shifts were random or Yelena had broken the pattern to get Kate kicked out.

    She looked at the chart she’d made. Kate had decided that she could risk having a paper trail since her presence was now—hopefully— unknown.

    It was a simple seven by seven table with six shifts vertically and Yelena, Hechel, and BG— bad guy— 1, 2, 3, & 4 filling the boxes across the top. Kate had only filled in a few slots for both Yelena and Hechel, everything was else a massive question mark.

    Written beneath her table wasThree Locations? Kate had circled the two words several times over although it got her no closer to deciphering the complicated pattern problem she’d created for herself.

    Below that, she’d circled: Seventh BG?

    As five o’clock rolled around, Kate zeroed in on the entrances. Her blunt that she pretended to smoke was nearing its end and filling the car, seeping through the roof and windows. She thanked her years in college that allowed her to accustom herself to the smell and potency of weed. She didn’t smoke anymore but the motion was practiced and easy to mimic. She was going to reek when she got out of the car but at least it would sell the cover. Despite expecting it, her body tensed when—through two rows of cars— Yelena’s blonde hair appeared in her view, the angles of her face accentuated by the midday sun as she walked across the parking lot toward the hotel. She looked stunning. She always looked so put together and untouchable and Kate’s first thought strayed from the mission and veered into strange territory.

    In that moment Kate wanted nothing more than to muss up Yelena’s picture perfect persona and have her looking like she always made Kate feel: disheveled, irritated, and entirely off- balance.

    She thought about their past fights and how the only time Yelena had even looked close to unkempt was when Kate launched sand into her eyes. That had only lasted about four seconds before she was maneuvering Kate into an even worse position.

    Kate received flashes of the night in her room after her ‘date’ with Hechel and she remembered the warmth that had pooled low in her abdomen.

    Kate cleared her throat as Yelena disappeared inside the hotel, a strange guilt overcoming her at the direction her thoughts had taken. Her head was buzzing like she’d actually partaken in the bud and Kate opened her passenger window a little more.

    She adjusted in her seat and refocused, hoping she’d get another piece of the puzzle. Five minutes after Yelena entered the building, Kate saw Hechel departing the same door. A grin pressed at her lips as she realized that at least a part of her chart was correct. Due to the paths the team had to take from hotel to resort, Kate would probably be able to see them from her hotel room.

    She waited several minutes, letting the smoke settle and disperse before she pushed her car door open and moved lethargically out of her vehicle. She walked like a stoner until she was safely back in her room eager to see if her assumptions would prove true. Kate sat on her bed and took a deep breath. She was determined. She was smart. And Yelena had pissed her off enough that every synapse in her brain was alive and angry and ready for revenge.

    Kate found herself slipping onto her own balcony around one o’clock in the morning to try and see if she could spot any kind of movement. She wasn’t located perfectly: the side entrance Yelena had entered faced East and Kate’s hotel room was facing toward the North, but the resort was located Northeast of her hotel and she hoped she’d be able to spot some movement before the agents were out of sight.

    Her body buzzed with excitement as she watched a lone, tall figure, their movements lithe and graceful, stride toward the hotel. Kate wished she’d thought to get a scope of some kind as she peered toward them and failed to make out any significant features. There were streetlights filling the hotel parking lot and Kate held her breath as they walked under them as if that would help her see. Light brown, almost golden skin, dark waving hair that reached their shoulders, boobs? Kate rolled her eyes at herself and tried to find other defining features as the figure neared the corner that would take them out of Kate’s vision. She got nothing.

    Kate growled in frustration and pulled out her chart, crossing out BG and writing Tall One. She marked her chart and looked back toward the parking lot. If she was correct, somebody would be heading for the resort next.

    Like clockwork, Kate’s eyes honed in on a stocky figure that appeared at the corner, muscles bulging and tattoos littering his body. His hair was shaved close to his head similar to a military cut. Kate huffed out a laugh.

    That was easy.

    She crossed out a different BG and wrote Tatt Man.

    Kate’s alarm blared just three hours after she’d forced herself to nap and she fought her body’s desire to sink into the warmth of her bed. She flung her comforter off and scrambled to the balcony hoping to find that her pattern completed itself.

    At four-fifty-nine in the morning, in sleep shorts and a scratchy, new t-shirt, Kate crossed out the third BG and wrote Blonde Dude.

    It was the first break in the pattern, the Blonde Dude leaving the hotel and heading toward the resort before somebody had come back from it.

    To her disappointment, there were no other starring appearances from members of Yelena’s team.

    Kate looked at her chart, brow furrowed. She felt like she was back in math class trying to wrap her brain around the word problems that were always about fruits. She started to mark the sheet with guesses like she was playing sudoku. If the shifts repeated, she could use Yelena and Hechels’ partnership to map out parts of the others. Kate hummed as she looked at her partially filled chart. It provided full coverage for the Resort and the Hotel but left a third shift up in the air for everyone. She thought about removing the third shift before the pieces form into a different answer. Of course. Somebody watching from the roof or balcony, somebody at the resort…. …somebody had to be with Stražar. Right?

    If Blonde Man’s partner didn’t come from the resort, they had to be at the hotel already. She looked between BG 4 and Hechels’ schedule with a grin, pride filling her as she added two more shifts on the chart.

    She could work with this.

    Now came the harder part.

    She needed to figure out which room Stražar was staying in.

    It was almost ten o’clock and Kate had run through ideas about how she might get to the top floor without alerting Stražar or her protection, but each one had too high a risk attached to it. Kate was sitting on the beach, her body clad in a similar outfit to the previous day as she pressed her hand to her eyes to block the sun.

    She was irritable and weary due to the minimal sleep she’d gotten and the current predicament she was attempting to work through.

    She could simply arrive on the top floor and sneak around. The cameras in the building were few and far between, only at the end of the hallways and entrances to the stairwells. But Kate had little doubt that Stražar had provided extra eyes and ears on the top two floors. She could try and use her new grapple arrows to climb up the side of the building but it was unlikely she’d be able to make it up without being seen. She could attempt it at night and probably get to the roof, but that left dealing with whomever was on shift there. While Kate was improving, she still had a lot to learn when it came to being quiet and moving stealthily. She knew she couldn’t reveal her presence here until she had the location of the hacker.

    A shout of laughter to her left had her heart shooting into her throat at the volume and she moved her arm to peer at the noise’s source.

    Two boys were wrestling, their eyes bright with amusement as one attempted to get a volleyball from the other. They stumbled a little too close to an old man and he started to yell at them.

    Neither of the teens seemed bothered and they laughed their way out of the cursing man’s space, parting with two middle fingers.

    Kate’s lips turned up and a half-formed plan began worming its way into her brain. Before she could stop herself, she was calling them over.

    “You two!”

    Kate waved them toward her and they made faces at her, clearly hesitant to engage with somebody directly despite their blase attitudes. They studied her for a moment and seemed to deem her a low threat as they walked over.

    The taller boy seemed the more forward of the two. He was tan and scruff was just beginning to define his jaw and cheekbones.

    “Yeah?”

    Kate motioned at both of them.

    “You two siblings?”

    The taller teen shook his head.

    “Just friends.”

    Kate studied the two as they stood over her, the confidence that came with being young and likely wealthy emanating from them. They were perfect.

    “Are you here on vacation?”

    She tried to ignore how fucking creepy she sounded and waited for their response. The taller boy answered again, brows pressing together.

    “Why?”

    Kate figured she might as well explain before she lost her chance.

    “Listen, this is going to sound weird as hell and you don’t have to do it. But I’m trying to get some info.” Kate pointed toward her hotel that was several hundred yards away, her brain shuffling through explanations and settling on an easy one. “My ex is staying on the top floor of that hotel and I don’t want her to know I’m looking. She stole a bunch of my shit and I just need to know which room to send the cops to.”

    The kids were listening with interest, their young minds probably coming up with wild stories and bullshit that could have happened between her and her ex.

    Kate hid her amusement and continued.

    “If you two can go to the top floor and walk around, report back if you see anyone exit a room, and keep your mouths shut about me, I’ll give you each two hundred dollars.” It was incredibly risky and she felt a little bad about sending up two kids that were probably no older than seventeen, but she also knew there was no way she could make it up there herself.

    The two teens’ eyes lit up and they shuffled forward. This was the first time the shorter boy spoke. He had almost bleached hair that waved and swooped near his round chin. “How do we know you’re going to pay us?”

    Kate tugged her backpack forward and found her remaining emergency fund. She pulled out two hundred and split it to hold out toward them.

    “The rest when you get back, yeah? And if nothing happens on the top floor, try the one right below it. Don’t overstay if you’re asked to leave. If somebody asks what you’re doing there, just make something up. Wander around the other floors for a few minutes before coming back here.”

    Kate didn’t want to give them too much to memorize or too many instructions to follow. She figured the kids looking suspicious because they had no explanation was better than them looking suspicious because they were trying to remember and relay a story. The teens stared at the money and the tall one smirked, arms crossing and probably trying to show off his meager muscles.

    “I make more than that in my allowance.”

    Kate could tell he was attempting to bait her into giving more but she wasn't about to be played by a kid that would likely be entering a frat in two years. She shrugged and pulled her hands back, pretending to put the cash back.

    “If you don’t want to do it you can just say that. I’ll find somebody else.” He backpedaled, head shaking as he reached for the money.

    “We’ll be right back.”

    The shorter boy followed suit and they both turned to sprint toward the hotel. Kate’s stomach clenched as she watched them leave, chest twisting unpleasantly. It felt wrong.

    What if the kids were hurt? Yelena wouldn’t hurt a kid, right? What if they’re stopped at the entrance? What if they tell Yelena’s team that somebody paid them to spy? Stražar’s team would have no reason to hurt kids. Something a little something like this had probably already happened once or twice if Kate’s own childhood was anything to go by. She remembered running through hotels with her teammates anytime they traveled for a competition. The amount of times other hotel guests had complained had given her mother a complex.

    Despite reassuring herself over and over, Kate didn’t relax.

    The reality of the situation was crashing down on her and she realized that she’d just put two innocent kids in danger because she wasn’t able to come up with a solution. She’d been so focused on winning that she was willing to put others at risk? Is that who she was? Yelena has put you in a box and that’s how you’re going to win. She’d never think you’d put kids in harm’s way for a job. That’s so fucked up. This is going to work It didn’t make her feel better. She felt dirty.

    Many ideas flooded her head at why Stražar and her protection would possibly hurt the kids and each scenario grew worse than the last.

    Her anxiety built as minutes passed: five turning to ten turning to fifteen turning to twenty. She cursed her first mission being one that prevented her from using all the stupidly fancy tech SWORD had access to. A fucking hacker was the worst targ– “Got it!”

    Kate almost jumped out of her skin as she shot up from her towel, sand flying everywhere. The two teens were standing in front of her, their faces red– probably from exhilaration and exertion equally– their eyes bright and enthused.

    Kate let her heart settle.

    “You got it?”

    She wasn’t exactly sure what they meant but she had to resist looking too eager. The shorter boy frowned and his head tilted to the side as he thought. “Well, there were four people.”

    Kate’s heart jumped.

    “Four? Same rooms?”

    The teens’ brows scrunched up in thought. The tall boy answered with a head shake. “No, most of them were like, near the end of the hallway.”

    Kate hid her impatience.

    “Left or right of the elevator? Close to the stairs?”

    “Left. We didn’t make it all the way to the end before the first door opened. I think the stairs were past it? This dude came out. He was like, super tan. He talked to us like we were babies. I think your ex was with him. She’s like, really old, dude.” They both looked grossed out. “She told us to leave the floor.”

    The shorter boy interrupted.

    “Maybe her ex was the hot one.”

    Kate’s throat tightened.

    “The hot one?”

    He nodded.

    “Yeah, when the old lady was telling us to go, another door opened and this hottie came out. She looked kind of scary.”

    Kate heard the awe in the way the kid’s voice softened and his eyes went a little wider. “Was she blonde?”

    Both of the teens nodded and the taller boy picked up the story.

    “Yeah. We told them we just wanted to look out the windows and turned around. We tried to go the other way but there was a woman blocking our way. She told us to get on the elevator. Kate frowned.

    “What did she look like?

    He shrugged.

    “She was really tall.”

    Kate wanted to laugh but she dug into her bag and pulled out the remaining money. “Before I give this to you, I have two more questions.” She waited for them to meet her eyes. “One, what did you do when you got on the elevator?”

    “We did what you said. We checked the next floor and looked out the windows. Then we stopped like one floor down and walked around.”

    Kate felt relieved.

    “Final question: did you talk to them at all?”

    They both shook their heads and Kate smiled as she held out the money. “Great, good job. You earned it. Never talk about this again and maybe don’t go back to the hotel. Have a good life.”

    Kate collapsed back onto her towel as the money left her hands. She let out a slow breath, letting her success sweep over her. After a moment, she realized she hadn’t heard the shifting of leaving feet and she glanced at the teens still watching her.

    “What?”

    The taller one crossed his arms.

    “Well what are you going to do? Which one is your ex? Aren’t you going to get your stuff back?”

    Kate rolled her eyes.

    “You two have four hundred dollars to spend. This is adult shit. Go find your jet skis.” His voice was near a whine when he responded.

    “That’s really lame.”

    Kate pushed her sunglasses up her nose and closed her eyes.

    “Being an adult is really lame.”

    Kate had a plan. It wasn’t a great plan. It wasn’t even a good one. But she needed to close this out as soon as possible and the longer she took, the more of a chance that the code would be sold or she’d be caught. Additionally, Kate couldn’t help considering the amount of bodies that could be piling up as Stražar’s red herring allowed Yelena and her team to pick out any threats and dispose of them.

    She’d learned a lot from the two teens’ jaunt around the top floor.

    Hechel was with Stražar from nine to one. The woman in the room was likely BG 7; probably whomever Yelena had mentioned when she told Hechel he was being asked for so many days ago. She dug into her brain and remembered after several frustrating minutes: Dominica. Yelena had called her Dominica.

    Apparently she was old but Kate wouldn’t put much stock into the descriptions of teenagers. It could be assumed that Old Lady was still very capable of taking Kate on without breaking a hip.

    The room they were staying in was on the top floor to the left of the elevator which meant it faced the same direction as Kate’s own room. It was before the stairwell which narrowed it down to just three possible targets.

    Each one of those targets also happened to be the largest suits in the hotel; two bedrooms with an entire living space in between them. She’d been able to take a look at the options on her first night even though they were booked out.

    Kate had also learned that Yelena and at least one other agent was staying at the hotel on the top floor even when they weren’t on shift. Filling in the remaining pieces of her chart, Kate grinned. Three agents were staying at the hotel, three at the resort. BG 7/Old Lady/Dominica was always with Stražar; likely the leader of Yelena’s team.

    She didn’t have all of the pieces but she had enough that she would be able to make her move.

    After planning as much as she possibly could, Kate had forced herself to rest. She knew she would need all of her energy for what she was going to go through in the next few hours. It was difficult, her chest pounding in anticipation and her body restless and ready to move. But eventually she was able to drift into semi-unconsciousness; her eyes fluttering open every few minutes before she slipped back into her oddly realistic dreams. When her alarm went off at two am, she blinked away the vague images of skyscrapers and blonde hair and rolled to her feet.

    The adrenaline spike up her spine and chest was almost instant as she realized it was time. Her skin broke out in goosebumps, her hair standing on end as she undressed and slid into the SWORD tactical suit. The cool leather calmed her slightly and she zipped it up her body until it was snug against her.

    Despite the close-fitting nature of the suit, she found that she could move quite well, her arms and legs barely hindered by the material. She fiddled with the hidden sensors in the colla, shifting the colors until she was blending into the darkness of her room. Kate’s chest felt tight, her hands clammy, and she wiped them on her bed before slipping on a pair of gloves to match. They had a sticky grip sewn into them that was giving Kate a bit more confidence about hanging around ten stories above solid concrete. The tips of the gloves were cut off for her pointer and middle fingers.

    She breathed in deeply though it did very little to calm her nerves or release the muscles that were squeezing her lungs.

    Kate clipped the Ronin sword to her suit, shoved her feet into a pair of black boots, and stared at herself in the hotel mirror.

    She looked like an agent, more dangerous than she’d ever felt before.

    She could do this. She would do this. She just needed to be fast about it because Yelena would be in her room only a couple doors down; hopefully asleep. It wasn’t the ideal situation, but Kate was pretty sure doing this any time during the day would be worse even if it meant getting more time while Yelena had to recall from the resort. Doing it tonight she’d have the element of surprise. Hopefully only the agent guarding Stražar, the one she had yet to get eyes on, would be awake at this hour. She planned on dismantling any security they had on the balcony before she even got near it. Kate slid her quiver around her shoulder. She had requested a few trick arrows and had been thrilled when SWORD had provided her with multiple; some she’d used and ones she’d never seen before. She’d even been given a visual, CGI demonstration on the tablet so she knew what the new ones did.

    Kate had carved each of the arrows near the nock with specific patterns to help her keep track of them if she didn’t have time to read the label printed on the shaft. Her method had never been pressure tested so she had no idea if she’d be able to differentiate the two squiggles on the taser arrow from the number sign on the net arrow but there was only one way to find out. Kate drew one of the grappling arrows and stepped onto her balcony to look up. The balconies weren’t staggered at all so she was going to need to lean out over the eight story drop and fire up.

    She slowed her breathing, drew, aimed, and fired. The bowstring snapped by her cheek as the arrow rose up and stuck into the thick stone of the tenth floor balcony. She winced at the slight ring of metal on rock and waited several moments, listening closely for any movement as her quiver stopped feeding out the cable line.

    When she felt it was safe, she gripped tightly to the bow and pressed the small switch on her quiver, heart lurching in her chest as the cable was reeled back in, launching her upwards with a little more force than she expected.

    Kate’s hair—even in her classic ponytail— whipped her face as she let out a quiet sound of surprise, her body now dangling just above the ninth floor balcony.

    She shifted her hands so that one was on the center of the bow, balancing it, while the other reached up and wrapped around the railing tightly. She set her bow on the balcony and climbed up as quietly as possible, glad that the entire floor had been bought out so she didn’t need to worry about being seen through the windows.

    Once Kate was fully inside the railing, she leaned under the lowest rail and unhooked the grapple from the stone. It would be unusable until she put it back together but she didn’t want to leave any evidence behind.

    She turned and kept low as she moved forward, looking out at the row of repeating balconies in front of her. One of them would be Stražar’s and another would be Yelena’s. There were only four to choose from, and from what the teens had said, Yelena’s was beyond where the hacker was staying.

    Kate stared closely at the balconies, eyes seeking out anything out of place. Her eyes, already adjusted to the moonlight, picked out a singular, slowly moving shape that was tucked into a corner above the glass doors two rooms down.

    Kate drew a regular arrow and aimed for the top of what she expected to be a camera, a shot she could make now with her eyes closed. She fired and watched the wires at the top of it slice in half as the arrow thudded into the wooden frame beyond.

    Kate grinned and wasted no climbing the railing and jumping the five feet between the balconies. She landed on the next one with a slight clang, her pulse jumping at the sound. The sliding glass leading to Stražar’s room began to open.

    Kate leveraged one leg through the railing in front of her and pulled an arrow, firing what she desperately hoped was a taser at the man that had just stepped through. She fought the urge to pump her bow in the air when it impacted his neck and lit up the space around them a sparking, bright red. He barely made a sound as he thumped onto the ground but Kate watched with wide eyes as the gun that had been in his hand clattered across the stone.

    Fuckity fuck fuck shit.

    She wasn’t dumb enough to hope that that wouldn’t wake up highly trained individuals and she clambered over the railing she had wedged into, ran to the other side of the balcony, and repeated the same jump.

    Cold sweat was pouring down her skin as she landed again, this time pushing up and over into a roll so that she could draw an arrow and aim it into the building. There was no movement inside, and Kate took a moment to lean down and check the pulse of the guy she’d downed. She wasn’t sure if taser arrows were meant to be used on such delicate areas.

    A light flutter pulsed under her two bared fingers and she patted his neck in apology where there was a bruise beginning to spread.

    Kate stayed crouched over his body for a few moments before slowly making her way through the sliding doors to the inside of the full living area. She saw the front door, a kitchen at the wall opposite from her, a bedroom door on her right and left, and in front of her was what she hoped to be Stražar’s set up.

    It was very high-tech, blue glow shedding bits of light in strips along the ceiling, walls, and floor even while most of it was shut down.

    Kate took in the space around the room rapidly, trying to find any sign of movement or attack coming her direction.

    She had a decision to make.

    She could bank on the code being out in the open with the rest of the equipment and block off anyone's access while she completed her mission or she could play it safe and invite the party, keeping her access to the bedrooms available.

    A realistic part of her was pretty sure she was just going to have to destroy the code no matter what she chose.

    The decision ended up being made for her.

    As she stepped further into the room she heard the slightest of movements to her right and she was nocking and firing an arrow as she dove out of the way of an incoming, very sharp looking blade.

    Kate hit the ground in a half roll, half tumble behind the couch and she heard a grunt of pain from the direction of her attacker.

    She popped her head up to peer over, hoping her arrow hit true, and was met with black as a boot drove into her face with all the force of a truck.

    Her head snapped backward and her vision blurred and she scrambled away as she made out the shadowy form of her attacker climbing over the couch with another blade in hand. It struck the tile between her legs and she kicked into the wrist holding it, sending the knife scraping across the ground and under the couch.

    Kate grinned, her mouth filling with blood from her likely broken nose as she cracked the upper limb of her bow across the attacker’s face.

    She used her momentum to roll to the side and onto her knees, bow out to block any incoming blows as she stood and created enough space to breathe.

    Her vision settled and she could make out the outline and faint details of a woman in her forties, her graying hair sleep-mussed and her temple bleeding profusely from where Kate’s bow had carved down it. She had a growing pool of blood on her shoulder from where Kate’s arrow had previously been impaled.

    The woman kicked out so quickly that Kate had to jump backward. She kept her footing and her balance and slid under the roundhouse that followed right after.

    Kate growled as the top of the woman’s boot clipped her ear and she darted forward, striking at the woman with a speed she hadn’t really gotten a chance to use since her training. Yelena was better than her. It was a simple fact that Kate couldn’t even begin to dismantle. But this? Kate was even, maybe better. And though her first jab was blocked, she feigned an attack with her bow and waited for the woman to react accordingly, leaning her head out of the way and right into Kate’s incoming left elbow.

    The woman lurched back but Kate didn’t let up, her body thrumming with energy and a sense of growing power. She watched the blood streaming down the side of the attacker’s face and went to take her next swing when she heard the buzzing of the front door’s lock. She acted without thought, punching her bow into the older woman’s chest to give herself space as she sprinted around the computers in the center of the room. She had line of sight to the door and she quickly fired an octo-grabber arrow as it began to open. It landed with a solid thunk and cables shot out in all directions latching into every single available surface and locking in place, halting the door's movement.

    Kate swung around, eyes locking onto the woman that was circling, the glint of metal clear in both of her hands.

    She heard bashing at the hotel room door as whomever was out there attempted to push through over two hundred high-tension cables.

    Kate’s eyes flicked back toward it and the distraction nearly got her killed. She almost didn’t see the first blade coming, the slight twitch from the woman ten feet from her and the splash of the moonlight on the blade as it flew its path was the only thing that kept her from getting a knife in the chest. She dodged to the side but it still carved a long wound into her arm. She ducked as the second dagger passed right through where her head had previously been.

    Kate nocked another arrow and released, watching the woman’s left shoulder jerk backwards as the point sunk through it. It drew out a pained groan and Kate’s eyes widened as the woman went to break and remove this one as she’d done the first.

    Kate launched herself over the coffee table in the center of the room and tackled the woman to the ground, landing on her and driving her bow down into her face. She hit her four times before the woman stopped moving, and Kate heaved in a strained gulp of air. She forced herself to shove off of the unconscious woman and sprint to the other bedroom. Stražar had yet to make a sound or leave her room, and Kate couldn’t waste any time catching her breath.

    As moved toward her target, a shadow cast across the room from outside and Kate knocked another octo-grabber, sinking it into the lip of the wall just above the doors. There was a flurry of pinging sounds as cables shot out and blocked the entrance to the balcony, effectively stopping whomever was attempting to get in.

    Kate’s chest rose and fell almost painfully as she tried to see around the mass of cables toward the partially open sliding door. It was just a silhouette, no details visible as they were backlit by the moon outside, but Kate caught a glimpse of blonde.

    She grinned, a feeling of triumph warring with the reality that she was still likely going to have to deal with Yelena sooner rather than later. She wondered if this was how Yelena felt every time she beat Kate.

    Probably not.

    Kate’s elation was bitter and black, born from humiliation. Yelena always expected to beat her.

    Kate wondered what she was feeling now as she lifted her fingers to grace Yelena with the same salute she had given Kate two days before.

    She turned her back and kicked in the hacker’s door.

    There, back to the corner of the room and eyes wide with fear, was a girl who couldn’t have been more than nineteen. She was pale in the moonlight, freckles dotting her skin and her red hair standing out starkly against the grays and shadows of the night.

    Kate paused, brows raised.

    “Um, Stražar?”

    Despite the chaos that had occurred the past three minutes, Kate was startled by the sound of her own voice in the quiet room.

    The girl glanced around in terror looking for an exit and Kate decided she wasn’t going to wait and see if this was an act.

    She pulled out another arrow, made sure to check its label for this, and fired. The girl yelped but the arrow thudded into the wall beside her, a net exploding out and circling around her. Stražar was knocked off of her feet.

    Kate moved forward, her eyes glancing at the window of the room warily to see if Yelena had somehow found there. There was no balcony underneath the window outside, but Kate wouldn’t put it past Yelena’s gadgetry and skill to somehow get to and through it. She pressed her boot to the girl’s chest.

    “Where’s the code?”

    The girl’s fearful expression slid from her features and formed into a cruel smirk. Kate gasped as she felt electricity shoot up her leg and spine. She jerked backward and groaned as she stomped her buzzing foot a few times into the carpet of the bedroom. When she looked back at the girl, she saw that she was knocked out cold. Kate rolled her eyes.

    “Aren’t you supposed to be some kind of fucking genius? How are you trying to taze me when you’re wrapped in metal wires?” Kate yanked the small weapon out of the girl’s hands and smacked her cheeks trying to wake her up. “Come on. Come on. We don’t have time for this.”

    The Stražar took a moment but stirred and groaned awake.

    Kate pulled her scariest looking arrow from her quiver, holding it against Stražar’s throat. Stražar tried to pull back but was held in place by the net and Kat’s knee pressed to her chest. “Where’s the code? I don’t want to kill you but I will.”

    She hoped her voice sounded like that of a murderer but wasn’t too hopeful. Stražar continued glaring at her and Kate feigned shrugging, lifting the arrow and driving it down toward her exposed throat.

    Stražar flinched.

    “Wait! It’s hidden in the equipment!”

    Kate studied her, searching for honesty, but Stražar had a look in her eyes that Kate did not like.

    She felt her time running out. It was confirmed when she heard the crash of glass distantly, likely from the other bedroom.

    “Where in the equipment? What equipment? Be more fucking specific!”

    Stražar bared her teeth.

    “Find it yourself. You thug types are always so dense. I could describe its exact location and you’d probably imagine a jump drive.”

    With a frustrated huff, Kate stood and jogged toward the living area. Her time was up and any opportunity to replace the code had disappeared with Yelena being alerted. Still, Stražar had revealed way more than she expected and Kate was not some common thug. She drew an electromagnetic arrow and fired it at the fridge, watching as wavelines of blue built and spread, yanking everything metal toward it within a 100-foot radius. Kate pressed back against the wall of Stražar’s bedroom as objects— large and small—started zipping toward and sticking to glowing cobalt glow amassing in the kitchen.

    Stražar in her cable netting skidded out of the room toward the arrow and Kate reached down to lock her hand into. She held on as every single metal object in the apartment, including all of the computer equipment, ripped past them and glued itself to the fridge. After a minute of this, the blue began to concave on itself and Kate felt pull on Stražar lessen immensely.

    She dropped the cables and nocked her most dangerous arrow yet, twice glad that nobody else was on this floor or the one below.

    As she aimed it at all of the electronic bits that were now starting to drop down to the kitchen floor, Kate saw Yelena step out of the opposite bedroom.

    No longer backlit, Kate was able to make out her expression.

    It was the same one she’d received when she’d been aiming at Yelena the first time she’d seen her face, when Yelena had shaken her head so intently; in a way that had overcome Kate and had her lowering her bow.

    This time, Kate felt thrilled by the power of Yelena’s gaze and her ability to disregard it. She also saw something else lingering within it.

    As Yelena stared at her, she seemed like she was seeing Kate for the very first time. Yelena took a step and Kate released the arrow.

    Kate was prepared for the explosion and yet the wave of heat and shrapnel that shot her back against the wall was disorienting. Something with an edge drove into the muscle beside her spine and she groaned. Her head slammed back and she heard cracking. Panic flooded her before she realized that, logically, her skull smashing open probably wouldn’t sound like plastic and electricity.

    The television, bolted to the wall, had survived the black hole of her electromagnetic arrow. Kate gasped as she hit the hardwoods and her hands scrabbled against the wall to pull herself up and take in the situation.

    The fire alarm revved up, blaring in her ears and setting off the sprinklers. Water splashed on her skin and her suit and Kate felt it like a salve. Her body was aching down to her bones and she felt sweaty and sticky everywhere as blood pooled into the cuts on her suit. She gritted her teeth as she looked for an exit. Somehow she’d expected that bulletproof meant stab proof as well. That would have to be a question for Eben after she got out of this.

    “Kate. Bishop.”

    Kate’s head jerked as she looked for Yelena’s furious, somewhat strained voice. She heard scraping and a deep grunt and found Yelena shifting a large marble slab that had been cracked off of the kitchen counter. It was currently covering most of Yelena’s prone form. Kate glanced at Stražar shaking in the cable netting and pressed against the wall. At least she was alive.

    Thud! Tink tink clack!

    Kate’s gaze shot to the front door as somebody bashed into it, two of the cables going slack as they were knocked from the wall.

    In front of her, Yelena had almost successfully dislodged the slab. Kate calculated if she could get to the open window in the room behind Yelena. As she was about to start running, another shadow moved from the room and Kate had to drop to the ground as gunfire sounded, bullets digging into the wall behind her.

    Fuck!

    Kate looked toward the kitchen where a bonfire was still blazing, metal warped and red hot. Just beneath a twisted piece of the fridge Kate saw a gap where the explosion had ripped into the floor. It was just large enough to possibly fit her body and she went for it, crawling as fast as possible and throwing herself behind the wreckage of the ruined counter as more bullets zinged past.

    Heat seared into Kate’s revealed skin, sizzling around her and singing her hair. She swung her legs in front of her as she slid the final few feet and she felt the sharp pieces of metal cutting across her suit. Her feet dipped into the hole in the ground and she was weightless as her body followed after, freefalling into the room below.

    She hit the ground and released the impact with a short roll.

    The stark difference between this room and the one she’d just left was jarring. It was a single, dark and cold, and besides the bits of the ceiling that were crumbled on the ground and the bed, it was perfectly clean.

    Kate ran toward the door as a voice that she didn’t recognize shouted out; somewhat muffled from above.

    “She’s on the floor below. Go! We’re following.”

    Kate’s heart was beating sharply as she slammed the handle down and almost collapsed into the hallway just as the fire alarms in the entire building blared out at once, piercing into her eardrums.

    She sprinted toward the stairwell and rammed into the door with force, almost stumbling down the steps as she took them two at a time.

    It hurt— the elevator would have been more pleasant but slow and dangerous— and Kate pushed through the pain to continue down one flight at a time. Her breath was wheezing out but she picked up the pace when she hit the seventh floor and heard the door on the flight above crash open.

    Footsteps pounded down the steps in pursuit.

    Halfway to the sixth floor landing, she heard a door far beneath her her slam open and footsteps climbing up Not good. So very, very, very bad.

    Kate kept moving, feeling the encroaching bodies from either side as Yelena and the two with her closed in.

    She eyed the sixth floor entrance before dismissing it and continuing by. If she tried to sprint through the floors of the hotel, she’d be caught quickly. Things were only going so well because Yelena was yet to catch up with her. It was better to get down as fast as possible and disappear.

    Kate pushed herself harder, leaping over the railing and skipping an entire half-flight as she swung herself to the fifth floor to buy herself time.

    Her feet hit the ground and her shoulder slammed violently into the wall but she ignored the burst of agony that spread from the impact and headed toward the fourth floor. Both pursuers were within a flight of her now and she grinned almost manically as she tugged out the web arrow and nocked it. She paused for a split second to aim toward the outer stringer of the first floor steps. Her arrow hit at an angle as expected and she glanced up just as boots hit the landing above her.

    Yelena’s face was covered in soot and there was a cut across her forehead. She had on sweatpants and a t-shirt that was torn at the shoulder where a bruise was already forming. Despite her disheveled appearance, her eyes were fiercely determined and they locked on Kate with the force of a blow.

    Kate grimaced and launched herself down the rectangular shaft between the flights of stairs before she was able to confirm that the webbing had settled in a way that would catch her. Yelena’s eyes widened and she lunged forward, hand outstretched as Kate began to hurtle toward the ground.

    She flew past the two pursuers sprinting up the steps and caught sight of Hechel and Tatt Man lurching to a halt and turning around.

    She clenched her teeth and her eyes slammed shut as she prepared for impact. She knew from the CGI video and breakdown that the Web arrow was filled with something similar to Spiderman’s technology.

    As Kate hit the fibers, feeling them conform to her shape, she realized what that actually meant.

    Her hand tugged the Ronin sword free and she cut desperately at the sticky strands that clung to her. The weapon carved through easily as she bounced up and down but it stole several precious seconds from her.

    Yelena’s team was only a flight away when she ripped herself forward and toward the ground.

    She hit with far more force than she’d hoped, stringy, sticky film clinging to her back, legs, and hair. She shouted in pain as her already injured shoulder made contact with the cement and knew as she tried to stand that she’d dislocated it.

    Scrambling to her feet, Kate pushed out into the hallway and she stumbled into swarms of guests, all eyes wide as they tried to exit the building.

    She bodied a few to the side, hearing curses and shouts as she beelined for the side exit. She wrapped her bow around her shoulders and passed her sword to the arm that was still working, retracting it in hopes that the crowd of people wouldn’t see it as they shoved sleepily into the night.

    Kate sprinted toward her car and ran her hand down the suit for her key she’d tucked into one of the hidden seams. She skidded to a stop and jammed the key in, her fingers shaking as she tugged her door open.

    As she started her car, she saw Yelena emerge from the growing crowd, followed by Hechel, Tatt Man, Blonde Dude, and Tall One.

    Yelena’s eyes found her almost instantly but Kate was already slamming her foot on the gas and peeling away from the gathering crowd toward the exit on the West end of the lot. She careened out just as she heard sirens blaring from a distance. Bullets shattered the back glass of the vehicle and Kate hunched down as screams sounded under the wail of the approaching emergency vehicles.

    The bullets stopped and she glanced in her rearview to see Yelena’s hand wrapped in Tatt Man’s collar, pulling him down and speaking to him sharply. Yelena shoved him away and shouted orders that Kate couldn’t hear.

    As she turned out of the lot and onto the road, she lost sight of them. Kate pressed the down on the pedal and turned away from the chaos and the police; which just had to be the opposite of the direction she needed to go. She knew that the main street wrapped around the peninsula and back West—the direction of Sandwich— and so she headed for it, passing hotels and then strips of large houses and shops. She just needed to make sure she lost Yelena and her team before she even thought of getting near the safehouse.

    She veered onto Main street and sped up. It was still early enough in the morning that nobody was on the road and Kate tried to gain her bearings as she tore along the coastline. It was hard to think through the adrenaline, the agony of her injuries, and the rattling her brain had taken in the fight and subsequent chase. It felt almost wrong to be sitting down after what she’d just gone through.

    Headlights appeared behind her after about five minutes and Kate growled out a string of curses.

    She felt practically useless, her arm limp and taking away most of her options for ranged attacks.

    Kate let go of the wheel and held it in place with her knees as she dug around for her phone. She began to veer off the road just as she found it and untucked it from the pocket in the thigh of her suit.

    She grabbed the wheel to right herself and heard the foreboding thump of her cellphone hitting the floormat.

    “Great. So awesome. You are so good at this. Unbelievable work, Kate.” Kate felt around with her left foot until she found her phone, hoping she could still reach it once she figured out what to do with her pursuers.

    The headlights behind her were closing in and Kate planned on asking Akilah why she didn’t have a faster car the moment she was able to get out of this.

    The engine was roaring as she pushed it to its limit, Kate’s eyes constantly flicking to take in what looked to be a black truck barreling toward her. Releasing the wheel again, she reached back and fisted her hand around all of her remaining arrows.

    Tossing them onto the seat, she started sorting through them. She had one each of the web, explosive, taser, and electro-magnetic arrows, two putty arrows, and seventeen regular arrows to work with.

    This was going to be incredibly difficult.

    Kate used her knees to hold her steady the wheel so she could roll down the window. She picked out the web arrow and grabbed the steering wheel again to wrench it as she approached a curve going far too fast. The car careened around the turn, the back end bumping into the guardrail and knocking it over. The tires burned rubber for several seconds before shooting her ahead at a short straightaway.

    As she sped up again, the truck veered around the corner and gained on her rapidly. Kate waited for the last moment, when bullets began piercing the truck, to toss the arrow out the window.

    A bullet licked across the top of her wrist and she yanked her hand back in as her own attack took form.

    She could no longer see out of her rearview mirror as it had shattered in the onslaught of bullets, but she was able to see in her sideview as a burst of sticky webbing wrapped itself around the frame and wheels of the truck.

    Kate pumped her fist as her pursuers lurched to the side and their vehicle looked to be slowing down. Before it could come to a stop, it crashed off of the road and barreled toward a row of houses. Kate winced as it rammed into a shed and flattened it but couldn’t spare any more time to make sure it didn’t hit a home.

    She glanced back every few minutes to make sure she was alone and didn’t settle at all until she’d put another two more miles of space between herself and where she’d left the truck. She glanced behind her again and breathed in a relieved sigh.

    Any air she’d taken in was ripped from her lungs as her eyes returned to the road ahead again and saw a motorcycle zip from a side road and into her view. She didn’t have time to do anything as the rider drifted the bike to a stop and pulled out a gun, firing four bullets that each hit their marks: two in the engine and one in each front tire.

    Kate’s entire body jolted as the rims hit the cement, metal grinding and sparks flying around her. Her face hit the steering wheel and her broken nose smashed further. White flared in her vision and Kate couldn’t even process the rest of the accident as she felt her body slamming into the seatbelt multiple times. She wasn’t sure if the car rolled or just flew off the road into hills, but every injury she had was making itself known as they bumped into the center console, wheel, and door over and over again.

    When it all came to a stop, the crunching of metal silencing and Kate’s body stilling in the seat, she realized that she wasn’t breathing.

    Kate’s vision spotted before she was able to get in a single lungful of air. She heaved in and out, tears in her eyes as she tried to gather herself.

    She heard her door tear open and hands grabbed her roughly, yanking her from the destroyed vehicle.

    Her body moved sluggishly when she tried to fight and all she could do was wrap her hands around the wrists of the person dragging her.

    Her back slammed into the side of the car and she blinked as Yelena’s furious face swam into her view.

    Kate wheezed.

    “Oh hey, funny seeing you here.”

    Yelena’s grip on her suit tightened and Kate saw her jaw tense and release. Her hair was wild around her head, blonde strands having escaped the braid they were in the chase. There was still a streak of soot on her face and Kate itched to rub it off even as her brain unhelpfully reminded her that she'd wanted to see Yelena like this.

    In the end, all she could focus on was how ethereal Yelena looked even when she was roughed up and radiating anger.

    Yelena’s voice was hard when she finally spoke.

    “You have put me in a very bad position.”

    Kate huffed and rolled her eyes, head dropping back on the car. She hated that Yelena could come across as smooth and calm even when looking like she wanted to put a bullet in Kate's brain.

    “Listen, threaten me, beat me up, hell, kill me. But I swear to god if you say something you don’t mean I’m going to pass out on purpose.”

    To really put weight behind her warning, Kate’s legs coincidentally buckled and Yelena had to step into her body to keep her from hitting the ground.

    Yelena’s eyes roved over her, took in her numerous injuries, and for the first time Kate saw conflict written quite plainly across her expression.

    The conflict dissipated as Yelena glanced in the direction Kate had come from. Kate could vaguely hear the sound of an approaching vehicle and she tried to think of a way to escape. Her body wasn’t in any shape to keep running and there was little to no chance of her breaking out of Yelena’s hold. She hissed.

    “They’ll kill me.”

    Yelena didn’t seem all that bothered by that fact.

    “I know.”

    Tires screeched to a halt and Kate rolled her head to the side to see the web-covered truck with its doors flinging open. Yelena was still staring at her as Hechel, Tall One, Blonde Dude, and Tatt Man approached.

    Hechel neared and ground to a halt.

    “Kate?”

    Kate felt Yelena tense slightly against her and she had a wild thought that maybe she just needed to be touching her constantly so she could read her better.

    She grinned at Hechel.

    “Hey, Hech. Did you confess your love?”

    Hechel’s face twisted into constipated confusion and embarrassment and Yelena spoke through her teeth.

    “That was you?”

    Kate laughed lightly and it turned into a groan.

    “Let’s just get on with this? I have places to be.”

    She wasn’t sure what was going to happen, truly, but she felt a distinct lack of fear. Maybe it was the way Yelena’s body was still in between her and the team, protective even if that wasn’t Yelena’s goal. She was literally only standing because Yelena hadn’t let her fall yet. She turned her head again and met Yelena’s eyes, for the first time not feeling intimidated or like she had to look away from the challenge she found there. Maybe it was due to exhaustion, but Kate felt a surge of reckless courage flood her veins. Kate’s eyes were lidded as she let her gaze flicker across Yelena’s face in her own rebuttal. “What are you going to do, Yelena?”

    Yelena’s team was speaking behind them but Yelena only had her eyes on Kate, gaze burning. In the next several seconds it felt like they were the only two for miles. Kate was startled from the haze as Yelena shifted sharply against her, four shots ringing out in quick succession.

    Kate squeezed her eyes shut in the following silence.

    Yelena spoke, voice decisive and daring Kate to argue.

    “We need to move. They will hunt us.”

    She felt Yelena adjust again and an arm wrapped under her only working one and around her back.

    Kate opened her eyes but didn’t look in the direction of Yelena’s team as she was guided toward the motorcycle. She didn’t want to process more death; especially when it had been an expense of her continued safety.

    Yelena was strong and steady under Kate’s weakening body but Kate tried to hold up as much of her weight as she could.

    Yelena helped her sling a leg over the motorcycle and waited until Kate was situated before sliding in front of her.

    “You need to hold on, Kate Bishop.”

    Kate slouched against Yelena’s back and wrapped her working arm around her waist, her fingers pressing into her abdomen and squeezing. Her voice came out weakly. “Thank you.”

    Yelena didn’t grant her a response, instead kicking the stand up, shifting to neutral, and starting the engine.

    As they drove forward, Kate hazarded a glance at her ruined vehicle and took in the four limp bodies sitting in pools of their own blood. Hechel’s eyes were wide and unseeing, his face pressed into the cement. Kate flinched and fought a wave of nausea as she looked away toward the road ahead.

    Chapter End Notes Woohoo! Almost every chapter from now on will be filled with tension and banter and I CAN'T wait.

    Chapter 6

    Chapter Notes To anyone that read the first version of this chapter: my apologies.

    Something was wrong and I realized that I felt disconnected from Yelena's character. She's a difficult one to nail down and I want to do her justice in the story. It helped to write from her perspective.

    That being said, I still don't love what I've done with this lol. It is three in the morning and I'm just sick of trying to get this right.

    Either way, I hope y'all enjoy.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Yelena had not slept well for several days, her mind churning out probabilities and assessing decisions in ways she never had to worry about before. She felt on edge, a taut wire ready to snap and lash against whomever happened to be nearby.

    Unfortunately for Hechel, that happened to be him more times than not. Yelena at least found some grim satisfaction in it.

    She pushed herself up from her bed, giving up on sleep entirely as she dropped into the desk chair in her room. She spun idly, her head lolling back against the headrest. The novelty of it incited paired feelings of mourning and curiosity, the two emotions that she had become most accustomed to after she’d gained autonomy. It was something she wished she could ask Natasha about. It was something she had spoken to Sonya about in one of their rare moments of emotional vulnerability that was born from finishing an entire bottle of Vodka.

    It was right after Yelena had found out the truth about Clint, about Natasha and her choice. She’d been blindly angry at so much and it was the first time she’d been scared of how powerful her emotions were, how unprepared she was to manage them. It was the first time she thought about how numb she’d been before the Red Dust was sprayed in her eyes, how that might be better than the ache that pried her apart.

    She’d gone to Sonya with the fraying strings of her sanity and a bottle of Beluga. Sonya had taken one look at her and pulled down her shot glasses.

    When they’d been nearing the end of it, Yelena’s body so heavy she felt she might sink into the couch, Sonya had finally started asking questions.

    ”Chto vy chuvstvuyete?”

    Yelena closed her eyes, words slurred and tired.

    “Так много боли. Это не уходит”

    Sonya was inebriated much the same but she had the awareness to find Yelena’s eyes, the sincerity there muddied by the alcohol.

    “It will. I promise you.”

    Sonya told her about the years that had followed her own freedom; the days she thought it had been easier to live hollow and at the whim of somebody else than to deal with the reality of what she had missed and what she had suffered.

    She’d talked about the countless one-off classes she’d taken that only served her own whims and wants. She’d tried pottery, gardening, and photography in an effort to find enjoyment outside of purpose.

    Sonya had recounted moments of failure that almost convinced her she would never be able to overcome her past. One story in particular had Yelena curling over herself in laughter as Sonya described how she’d smacked her photography instructor in the mouth with her own camera.

    Sonya was able to laugh as well, her eyes watering slightly, but she told Yelena that at the time she had never felt closer to giving up.

    She’d told Yelena about her wife– who turned out to be the photography instructor that Sonya had accidentally assaulted– and how she had helped Sonya feel okay even when she was at her worst.

    When the conversation grew lighter and more delirious, Yelena asked Sonya about the odd little things that had taken her off guard. Sonya had laughed and shared the time she’d first stopped in a park to look at birds. She’d diverted from her path and her goal and had stood there for over an hour just because she realized she now could.

    They’d both passed out sometime after three in the morning and Yelena had awoken to a brutal headache and the smell of greasy food wafting around the house. She’d met Wren between large forkfuls of hash browns and had been surprised at her demeanor. Where Sonya was sharp, Wren was soft. Harsh words were met with humor and quiet affection was rewarded with overt love.

    Yelena had felt intrusive and out of place but something about their home made her stay hours after she’d planned on leaving.

    When she’d finally stood to depart, heart closing off to prepare for the harsh reality that waited for her outside the door, Wren had handed her an envelope. She hadn’t pried or pushed or said too much and she had stepped away when Sonya walked Yelena to the door. They’d stepped outside and Yelena had felt a chill deep in her bones at the prospect of the emptiness that awaited her. She hadn’t met Sonya’s eyes and Sonya understood. ”Вам больше не нужно сражаться.”

    Yelena knew what she meant. She knew that Sonya was suggesting she leave the only life she knew she could excel in. But she wasn’t ready. She didn’t know if she’d ever be ready. “я очень хорошо умею драться.”

    Sonya laughed, her hand reaching out slowly to give Yelena the option to step back. When she didn’t, Sonya squeezed her shoulder. Yelena felt the burn of her touch and her body ached.

    “Find somebody that quiets your mind and hold onto them. Find somebody that sees where you are broken and holds you tighter.”

    Yelena rolled her eyes, fingers tightening around the envelope in her grasp as she felt the impossibility of the words.

    “You embarrass yourself, Sonya.”

    Sonya’s hand squeezed her shoulder one more time before she pulled it away, her voice low and sincere.

    “The truth is, Yelena, sometimes it is too hard to continue living for yourself. When that is the case, I live for Wren. I want that for you.”

    Yelena felt overwhelmed and the itch to close herself off and move on became too strong. She ignored Sonya’s gentle words and bobbed her head once in her direction. “I will see you, Sonya.”

    Yelena had waited to open the envelope Wren gave her until she’d found herself safely in her hotel room. She’d sat on the bed and pulled out three photos that had been taken at some point during her stay.

    Two she’d seen Wren snap. One was her and Sonya hungover at the kitchen table with their full mouths pulled into smiles. It was messy and unattractive but Yelena felt fondness bleed into her heart. The second was taken hours later, Yelena sitting with her back to the couch as Sonya showed her pictures of the cat they were trying to adopt.

    It looked almost foreign to her; the innocent joy so alive on her face that she wondered for a moment if the photo had been edited.

    The last photo was a jarring comparison and Yelena had been irritated at the time that she’d missed Wren taking it. She was standing by the house’s bay window, her hands in the pockets of her wrinkled slacks and her hair mussed from the lazy night and day she’d had. Yelena remembered the moment well. She’d been so deep in her thoughts of staying or leaving that she’d felt torn in two.

    In the photo she looked lost, directionless and unprepared. It was hard to see her inner turmoil reflected so plainly on her face. She went to tuck the photo into the envelope when writing on the back of it caught her attention. She flipped it over.

    ’Peace will find you. We’re here whenever you need us.’

    Yelena had stuffed the three photos in the envelope and folded it into her suitcase with care. Yelena pressed one leg out and stopped her circular motion in the desk chair. This was another time where the emotions she did not know how to process were beginning to overwhelm her. She’d learned to feed those emotions into anger because it was easier for her to understand and control. The Red Room had allowed her to feel anger. It was good for fighting.

    She leaned forward in her chair, tendrils of hair tickling her cheeks and jaw. The pain did not get better.

    Yelena didn’t know if Sonya had been wrong or if she was just too broken for repair.
