Yelena was about to stand, intent on going for a late run to push all of the pent up aggression from her body, when she heard a distant scraping sound. She listened in silence for several moments but heard nothing else. She would stop by the room before she left for her run. Not bothering to change, she tugged on tennis shoes and pocketed her key cards. Once in the hallway, she made her way toward Stražar’s room to pop in. As she neared the door, she heard the distinct sound of fighting and sprinted the remaining steps. She jammed the key card into the door, unsheathing one of her blades from the flat scabbard on her stomach, and waited for the green light.

    Just as she was opening the door a thwack followed by several high pitched metal tings rang out and her forward momentum stopped abruptly.

    She could hear the fight more clearly now and she stepped back to drive her foot into the door, attempting to kick through. She alerted her team with a sharp command to fall back on Stražar, slammed her foot into the door one more time, and returned to her room to go in from the balconies.

    She leapt from hers to Stražar’s with ease and her eyes took in the downed body of Tomlin; his gun useless on the ground and a strange-looking arrow next to it. A sinking feeling pooled in Yelena’s chest as she stepped off of the railing and looked inside the apartment. A figure was darting toward Stražar’s room but they turned and their bow, Kate’s bow fired toward the location Yelena stood. She prepared to catch it but it found purchase in the door frame and the same pinging sounds followed an array of extending cables as Yelena’s entrance was blocked again.

    Kate paused, her face barely visible in the moonlight, but Yelena could see the smile, she could see the salute, and she felt such a multitude of things in that moment that she couldn’t find a name for any of it.

    Kate disappeared into Stražar’s room and Yelena ground her teeth together. She could count on one hand the amount of times she’d felt indecision on a mission and every single one involved Kate Bishop.

    She stood on the balcony with all the pressure of her conflicted and overwhelming feelings roaring through her chest as her team chattered in her earpiece.

    ”Four minutes out.”

    Hechel and Mateo were making their way from the Resort and Yelena knew she needed to figure out what to do with Kate before they arrived.

    ”Something is barricading the door.”

    Ishita had made her way from the roof then.

    ”Yelena, do you have eyes on the inside?”

    Artem.

    Yelena could hear Kate speaking inaudibly to Stražar and she took a deep breath as Artem spoke again.

    ”Yelena?”

    Yelena forced herself to focus through the increasingly disturbing outcomes this night might have. Kate was not Yelena’s responsibility anymore. She had done everything she could to keep her safe and Kate had dug her own grave.

    “The balcony is barred. I am making another entrance. Ishita, come through my room. Artem, stay at the door in case they try to leave that way.”

    Yelena steeled herself looked at the two windows on either side of the balcony She didn’t want to risk Kate seeing her coming and blocking the window to Stražar’s room. She turned to the right and fired her grappling hook into the stone ten feet above the window of Dominica’s room. She leapt onto the railing and jumped out, her body swinging in an arc and her feet ready to break through the window.

    She shattered the glass and flew into the dark room, rolling to negate the impact. Yelena shot to her feet and took in the blue glow pulsing through the open doorway. She turned the corner and drew to a halt as she took in the scene before her. All of Stražar’s equipment had been sucked toward the kitchen and half of it was stuck to the fridge, the other half was gathered on the ground around it.

    Kate stood near Stražar’s open door, an arrow nocked and aimed at the equipment. Yelena saw determination carved into the lines of Kate’s face, blood drying under her nose and dripping down her arms. Power and confidence radiated from the archer and when she met Yelena’s eyes, there was a challenge there.

    The cacophony of emotions Yelena had suppressed on the balcony tore free and consumed her, blazing through her chest. She took a step forward and in the same moment, Kate released her arrow.

    The next several seconds were hot and painful as the explosion rocked the room and smashed through the counter in front of her. A massive slab of the marble flew toward her and Yelena was able to turn her shoulder into the projectile before it crashed into her and knocked her to the ground.

    The counterpiece landed heavily on top of her and Yelena grit her teeth as it crushed her ribs. She pushed at it, anger pouring from her mouth.

    “Kate. Bishop.”

    She grimaced and moved the counterpiece down her body until she was able to shift her arms free. Kate was looking around the room and beyond. It looked like she was about to run in Yelena’s direction but Ishita entered behind her.

    Her silhouette appeared, gun drawn, and a sharp panic filled Yelena’s chest as bullets tore toward Kate. She fully freed herself just as she saw Kate sprinting and sliding into a hole in the ground.

    Ishita pulled Yelena to her feet and spoke to the rest of the team.

    “She’s on the floor below. Go! We’re following.”

    The ensuing pursuit had Yelena’s blood pressure rocketing and her decision about what she would do with Kate flip-flopping every other moment.

    It was as impressive as it was irritating that the archer had been able to make it this far and Yelena grew tired of it as Kate peeled out of the hotel parking lot.

    Yelena shoved her team toward their vehicle and climbed onto her motorcycle. She stripped the paint-matched patch from the inside of her exhaust pipes and released the key there. Sunglasses on to protect her eyes, Yelena sped through the back roads of the Cape in the direction of the main street. Her bike drifted around corners at violent speed and her hair whipped around her and against her back. Wind buffeted her skin and billowed her shirt out behind her.

    Cutting Kate off was an easy task and she spun to a halt and dismounted. As she stared down the vehicle, Kate in her sights, Yelena did what she knew she was supposed to. She saw the car roll and watched Kate’s head loll forward when the vehicle settled to a stop.

    Even as she stalked toward the car and dragged Kate out, Yelena was trying to convince herself to do her job. She slammed Kate into the side door and saw that she was hardly conscious, her eyes lidded and cloudy. She still managed to meet Yelena’s eyes. “Oh hey, funny seeing you here.”

    Yelena wanted to shake Kate, wanted to make her understand how stupid she was being. How much danger she’d put herself in and how fucking angry Yelena was. She revealed none of that when she spoke.

    “You have put me in a very bad position.”

    Kate’s reaction did nothing to settle Yelena’s roiling feelings but she was saved from finding a response when Kate’s body collapsed against her.

    Yelena tried to figure out if the accident had damaged her more but couldn’t see anything overtly concerning. Kate looked delirious but she was staring Yelena down like she knew exactly how the next minutes would go. It was the worst time for Kate to suddenly be calm and unconcerned.

    As always with Kate Bishop and her irritating idealism and nievite, Yelena felt torn. There was really no choice here even as she tried to create options for herself. She could either save Kate and put a target on her back or she could let her die.

    Yelena heard her approaching teammates and knew that she could never choose the latter. She looked at the heavily damaged truck and was drawn back by Kate’s weak voice. “They’ll kill me.”

    “I know.”

    Yelena didn’t bother to look again as she heard her team exit the vehicle and approach them. Hechel’s moronic voice droned out and Yelena ignored him until Kate responded. “Hey, Hech. Did you confess your love?”

    At the question and Kate’s almost delirious laugh, Yelena was done wasting time. Kate was looking at her with so much trust and while Yelena thought it was foolish, she wasn’t about to let her down.

    It was incredibly irritating.

    “What are you going to do, Yelena?”

    Yelena glared at her but Kate didn’t seem aware enough to recognize it. She hated being predictable and she didn’t believe Kate had a right to make any kind of assumptions; especially not this one.

    Unfortunately, Kate was right.

    Pulling the trigger on her teammates brought two distinct things to the forefront of Yelena’s mind. One: she was making a decision that would change the direction of her life forever. Two: she was doing it for another person to whom she owed nothing.

    Kate was conscious.

    That’s about as much as one could say confidently about her current state. Houses formed and morphed away in her peripherals as Yelena carved through the winding roads and away from the wreckage and death that littered the pavement. Glimpses of the ocean peaked through and Kate kept her eyes on it. Wind sifted her mussed hair and brushed against her heated skin.

    It was peaceful and she would have struggled to remain lucid if it weren’t for the flares of pain making themselves known across her body. The adrenaline that was keeping her moving had dribbled away like wet sand and with it the numbness it had provided her. Her right arm was tucked uselessly in front of her and the other had a vice grip around Yelena’s waist.

    They’d been driving for enough time that Kate believed they weren’t being followed. She wasn’t sure where Yelena was taking her and she hadn’t really been able to walk through the repercussions of what had happened. She knew that Yelena had saved her– after causing her to wreck which seemed like a step they could have skipped– and that meant far more than Kate was ready to process.

    For now, a deep warmth had burrowed inside and curled up in her chest. There was something to be said about feeling safe when you were moments away from a bullet to the head and the only thing in between you and the gun was a pint-sized assassin. Kate giggled almost deliriously and was infinitely glad Yelena didn’t have the ability to read minds. She didn’t think she’d be able to survive being launched from a moving vehicle at the speed they were going.

    She pressed her cheek into Yelena’s shoulder harder but made sure her nose avoided contact. She’d made that mistake once and had jerked sharply enough that Yelena had shouted at her to hold still.

    They slowed at a red light and Yelena spoke for the first time since they’d driven off. “Where is your team?”

    Kate lifted her head and glanced at Yelena’s face. It was tilted slightly toward Kate, sunglasses blocking her view of Yelena’s eyes.

    “I’m not sure that’s–”

    Yelena interrupted sharply.

    “Kate Bishop, do you want to waste time or do you want to live?”

    Kate huffed, dropping her head back on Yelena’s shoulder as the light turned green. “Sandwich.”

    Kate had a strangely pressing hope that Yelena would find the name as funny as her but she didn’t receive a response as the motorcycle rolled forward and gently sped up. In Kate’s previous experiences on bikes she’d felt like she was holding on for her life, her body lurching and her arms constantly tensing around the driver’s midsection as they–mostly boys– tried to show off.

    Yelena handled the bike in such a way that it allowed Kate to maintain a steady, softer hold without the danger of losing her grip.

    It was likely due to Yelena’s expertise and not a desire to make Kate’s life easier, but she attributed the relief and gratitude she felt toward Yelena anyway.

    It was a growing list.

    They left the coastline behind as they ventured further into Massachussetes, repeating the same path Kate had taken twice before.

    By the time they reached the main street of the quaint town of Sandwich, Kate was doing all she could to hold back her pained sounds as every bump and jolt of the road aggravated her injuries.

    Yelena slowed at a stop sign and Kate didn’t wait for her to ask before biting out. “Take a right.”

    Yelena’s head turned slightly and she seemed to be considering Kate’s condition. “If you fall off I will have to leave your body.”

    Kate rolled her eyes, hand pressing into Yelena’s abdomen “Just drive. And maybe stop talking until my brain is functioning again.” Yelena offered her the hint of a grin and a shrug, her shoulder lifting and lowering Kate’s head with it. The motion was jarring enough that Kate felt nausea rise in her throat. “If you want me to barf keep doing that.”

    Yelena’s face twisted in distaste and she turned away.

    “You are very bossy for somebody that can’t hold up her head.”

    Kate grunted and gave clipped, somewhat breathless directions to Yelena until they were pulling up outside of the little cottage.

    Yelena killed the engine and carefully extracted herself from the motorcycle, her movements slow in order to keep from upending Kate.

    It didn’t help. Kate felt woozy, her body giving out when there was nothing to prop her up. Her vision blurred as she careened toward the fuel tank. She couldn’t even brace for impact, her limbs failing to respond with any element of urgency.

    Yelena caught her with an arm around her waist and held her tightly.

    “Kate Bishop, do you need to be carried?”

    Kate shook her head weakly, offering a pathetic thumbs up.

    “I’ve never been better.”

    Yelena nodded sagely.

    “Ok.”

    She released Kate and Kate immediately began to fall forward again.

    “Yelena!”

    Yelena caught her again, drawing out her first question What? You said you were okay? You are mad that I believe you?”

    Kate groaned in irritation, her left hand twisting into the fabric of Yelena’s shirt. “You are very annoying.”

    Yelena made a noise of offense.

    “You are so rude, Kate Bishop.” Yelena helped drag her from the motorcycle and get her feet under her. “It was asking a true question. Do you have the ability to walk or am I going to embarrass you in front of your cool secret agent friends?”

    “I can walk.”

    The front door of the cottage opened and Kate lifted her head to find Eben watching with concern. She had a gun at her side but it wasn’t aimed at Yelena and right now that’s all Kate cared about. She didn’t have the energy to deal with a standoff. She needed to get inside and collapse before she did it on the cement driveway.

    Eben watched as Yelena guided Kate up the driveway and holstered her weapon as they approached. Her eyes looked beyond them to see if anyone else would be showing up and finally stepped out of the way so they could move inside.

    Akilah looked far less inviting as Yelena moved into the room, her eyes following the assassin closely for any reason to intervene.

    Jack looked almost scared, their eyes wide as they tucked themselves by the swinging door to the kitchen.

    Yelena led Kate to an armchair beside the cluttered coffee table and the couch, lowering her slowly and pulling her arm away.

    Kate reached her tired hand out and gently held Yelena’s arm to keep her from pulling away. Yelena tensed, expression closing off as she waited so closely to Kate’s face. Kate was too tired to worry about that at the moment. She had something to say and she needed to do it before she passed out.

    “Thank you, Yelena.” Kate felt Yelena start to pull away and she risked tightening her grip. “I’m serious. I know you put yourself in a super shitty spot to get me out of there and I want you to know I’m going to help you fix it.”

    Yelena allowed herself to be held in place but her expression was empty. She gave the slightest of nods and removed herself from Kate’s space.

    Kate allowed her eyes to close and the black began to consume her before she felt a few taps on her face.

    “Kate, I need you to stay awake so I can help you.”

    She opened her eyes and saw a blurry vision of Eben kneeling in front of her. “Did you see? I did it? I destroyed the code.”

    Eben smiled.

    “I saw that. So did half of the country.”

    Kate chuckled, a dopey grin pulling at her lips.

    “Hell yeah.”

    Her grin disappeared into a grimace as Eben lifted her dislocated arm. Eben nodded and Kate closed her eyes against the pain.

    For the next thirty minutes Kate faded in and out as Eben put her back together. The shoulder relocation was nauseatingly unpleasant but the relief that came so quickly after made it bearable.

    Pills were dropped into her hand and Kate sloppily pushed them into her mouth to choke them down. An icepack was laid across her bruised and broken nose.

    Eventually, Eben was taking care of her last surface wound and Kate was beginning to feel the effects of whatever medicine she’d taken.

    Eben pulled the ice pack from her nose and Kate winced at the light that suddenly pressed against her eyelids.

    “Alright, Kate. Worst part for last. How are you feeling?”

    Kate mumbled out an incoherent string of words and Eben took that as her cue. She felt along the misalignment in Kate’s nose by dragging her fingers down the sides. “It’ll be a quick pinch and you can rest.”

    Kate tried to nod and Eben held her head in place. Before Kate could react she felt the bones in her nose shift but the pain was so minimal she wondered if she was dreaming. She was out before she could thank Eben for her work.

    Kate floated in and out of sleep, her body feeling almost weightless and her mind a heavy haze that wasn’t able to separate her dreams from reality.

    “–can protect her better than one person.”

    “The people I work for have infiltrated every single world security force: public, private, secret… it does not matter. They were in Hydra, they were in SHIELD when it still existed, and do not believe for a moment they have not found themselves within your SWORD ranks. Failure for Leviathan–”

    The conversation abruptly transitioned to Kate’s ability to turn her body into a fully inflatable tube man like the ones outside car dealerships and she was pretty sure it wasn’t a conversation that was actually happening.

    She closed her eyes to the lights and threw out her hollow arms to flap in the wind.

    When Kate found herself awake again, it was to the sound of a car on a highway and soft music. She jerked awake and yelped as every small and large wound on her body protested the movement.

    “Relax, Kate Bishop. You are safe.”

    Kate’s brain was still foggy but at least she didn’t feel as if she was going to throw up or pass out anymore. She dropped her head back on the headrest and her eyes drew toward Yelena. Her breath stuttered slightly in her chest.

    It was not a surprise to her that she found the assassin beautiful. It could be argued, objectively even, that anyone that laid eyes on Yelena would find her beautiful. So no, it wasn’t a surprise. But it was overwhelming how Yelena’s hair reflected the sunlight and wisped around her jaw and neck as pieces strayed from the braid. In profile, Yelena almost looked soft; the way her nose tilted up at the end, the bowed curve of her lips, and the slender lines of her neck could have made her seem a little less like a killer and a little more like somebody Kate might see in a magazine or a movie.

    But there was a deep knowledge of horror and pain that never seemed to fully vanish from Yelena’s eyes. It was a history held in flux to be wielded and to have it wielded against; and it was very similar to Clint’s.

    Kate struggled to drag her eyes away, and though Yelena’s didn’t shift, she spoke. “You should not be so obvious when you stare.”

    Kate turned a bit too quickly to be nonchalant and internally scolded herself for letting Yelena get the upper hand not even seconds after she’d awoken.

    By the small lift at the corner of Yelena’s mouth, she had recognized it too. Eager to change the subject, Kate took in their surroundings for the first time since waking up. Tall, fall-touched trees and rolling and rising hills went on infinitely on either side and an ice-blue river carved the earth beside the road. Few cars passed by them and Kate couldn’t tell if they were in the middle of nowhere or about to run straight through a town “Where are we?”

    “Vermont.”

    Kate tried to take in the scenery with the new knowledge before realizing she’d never actually been to Vermont before.

    “Why Vermont?”

    Yelena didn’t spare a glance as she responded.

    “There is a safehouse.”

    Kate questioned whether she should keep talking despite Yelena’s contentment with silence. She kept her mouth shut for two minutes before more words spilled out. “How long have we been driving?”

    At this, Yelena gave her a small glance before returning her focus to the road. “Five hours.”

    Kate’s brows rose as she looked around the car, peering into the back seat to see that there was nothing but a pair of backpacks. Kate hoped that one of them held her bow. “Whose car is this?”

    Yelena showed her first sign of exasperation and she took a slow breath before responding. “Jack. They were very upset but they got the better deal. A Triumph is much cooler than a BMW.”

    Kate had very little knowledge of any motorized vehicles but she figured, if compared to a BMW, Triumph was a brand name.

    “Um okay. So I know you probably want me to stop asking questions but I’d just love a quick rundown of how I got from passing out in the chair to six hours away in a car.” Yelena huffed out a small laugh, her lips turning up.

    “You fell asleep and I spoke to your team. You drool, by the way. It is pretty gross.” Kate’s cheeks flushed as Yelena continued. “I told Eben you would be safer with me and she agreed. The large one put you in the car and we left.”

    Kate nodded, trying to bite back the numerous questions still trying to climb out. She succeeded for all of thirty seconds.

    “Okay, last one–” Yelena’s head dropped back, a noise of discontent spilling out as Kate asked her question. “How long are we planning on being at this safehouse in Vermont?” Yelena was silent for so long that Kate was worried she’d decided not to respond. Finally, she turned her head fully and met Kate’s eyes.

    “I do not know. I am thinking of plan now but Leviathan has eyes everywhere.” “Leviathan?”

    Yelena’s gaze returned to the road, amusement written across her features. “That is another question.”

    Kate rolled her eyes and slouched slightly in her seat. She glanced at the sling wrapped around her right arm and pouted.

    “How long do I have to wear this?”

    “I do not think you know what ‘one more’ means.”

    Kate let it go and fiddled with the edge of her seatbelt, her fingers running over the stitching repeatedly. She didn’t think Yelena was actually annoyed with her but she did seem to be deep in thought as she drove them to their unknown destination.

    Yelena wasn’t one to waste energy; every movement and action she took had purpose even when it seemed like it didn’t. She didn’t fidget, at least not that Kate had seen. But now– almost too subtle to notice– Yelena was tightening and loosening her grip on the bottom of the steering wheel every thirty seconds or so.

    It was possible that Kate had just never seen her idle for an extended period. In reality, she had seen Yelena a whopping total of seven times and most of those had been in the past handful of days.

    Kate tried to see if the fidget was intentional but Yelena never showed any other sign that she was agitated.

    After a few minutes, the movement stopped. When Kate looked up she saw that Yelena was watching her pointedly. Kate’s cheeks heated even though she wasn’t sure she had done anything to warrant it.

    There was something about the weight of Yelena’s gaze on her and now that they weren’t in direct conflict, the sensation was far easier to discern from her anger. Kate looked out the window to keep from staring, a sinking feeling in her stomach as she realized what the sensation was. She wanted to smack her head into the glass repeatedly in order to knock some sense into herself but she refrained; barely.

    There was nothing wrong with a little attraction. Kate nodded firmly to herself. Attraction was a normal part of life and it didn’t have to mean a single thing. Who wouldn’t be attracted to Yelena? Even blind, her voice could still be heard and that in itself did something to Kate. “You are thinking so loudly, Kate Bishop.”

    Kate startled, her eyes shooting toward Yelena.

    “Wha– no. I’m not thinking. Like, at all. No thoughts.”

    Yelena gave her an unimpressed brow lift.

    “Ok. I believe you.”

    Kate nodded.

    “Good. Because it’s the truth.”

    Yelena’s eyes narrowed.

    “How do you plan to be a super spy Hawk if you cannot lie?”

    “First of all,” Kate lifted her finger. “I’m Hawkeye.” She lifted another finger. “Second, I’m a great liar.”

    Yelena’s raspy laugh sounded out and she shook her head.

    “No you are not, Kate Bishop. Your face talks even when you don’t. I told you this.” Kate pursed her lips, annoyance starting low in her chest.

    “I can lie if I want to.”

    “Ok, Kate Bioshop.”

    Kate huffed and glared over at Yelena.

    “Don’t patronize me.”

    “I don’t know what this means. How could I be doing it to you?”

    Kate opened her mouth to explain when the twitch of Yelena’s lips tipped her off. “You’re an asshole.”

    Yelena reached toward the radio and turned it up, a slow beat drifting from the speaker of the car.

    It was an ending to a conversation if Kate had ever seen one. She reached over to change the station in protest.

    Yelena’s hand snapped out and slapped hers away from the dial.

    “Hey!”

    “Do not change the music.”

    Kate went for the dial again and her hand was blocked. Yelena ignored her glare. Kate turned her head toward the window and waited an agitating five minutes before making another try, her fingers getting no closer than her previous attempts before it was knocked back.

    It was so reminiscent of their elevator fight that Kate felt soft amusement start seeping between her annoyance and irritation. She lunged out again, wishing she had her other arm to help her out while Yelena was distracted by keeping the car on the road. Yelena batted her out of the way with ease but Kate had decided that this was the hill she was dying on no matter how stupid it was. She shoved and smacked at Yelena’s hand until they were practically sparring across their center console. It continued until Kate’s wrist was snatched from the air and held tightly in place.

    Kate huffed and looked over at Yelena for the first time since their hand fighting had begun. Yelena tilted her head so that she could meet Kate’s eyes.

    Kate rolled hers at the smugness written across Yelena’s features and she pulled at her wrist to no avail. Yelena’s grip on her was vice-like but not painful.

    Kate attempted to get the upper hand.

    “If you wanted to hold my hand you could have asked.”

    Yelena gave her a curious look, her eyes studying Kate’s flushed cheeks and deepening frown. After a moment, the curiosity dripped away to something much worse. Kate had never seen anyone’s face shift from one expression to the next so smoothly and so quickly. One moment Yelena seemed amused and the next her eyes were smoldering and lidded and Kate’s breath grew thin. Warmth spread against her chest and cheeks and raced down her spine. She couldn't look away but she felt like Yelena could see everything. “You will know if I want something from you.”

    Kate could hardly do anything but nod slowly, not trusting her dry throat not to embarrass her.

    Yelena kept eye contact for another second and then laughed, releasing Kate’s wrist. “You are so easy, Kate Bishop.”

    Kate wasn’t sure she had blushed that hard in a very long time and embarrassment now mixed with the budding arousal that was pooling in her belly.

    “Yelena!”

    Yelena snickered and pointedly reached out to turn up the radio even further. “Relax. We are very close now.”

    Kate tried. She really, really tried. But her skin was warm and her body felt frenetic just under the surface of her skin. She looked for anything to distract her from the sensations, her eyes flitting around the vehicle until they landed on her own legs. Legs that were wearing navy sweatpants with an white stitching of Massachussetes that she guessed Eben had changed her into.

    She focused on them, tracing the outline of the state over and over until she felt her heart rate slow to a semi-normal level.

    Kate stayed silent the remainder of the ride.

    Within thirty minutes they were passing through a city and toward what signs called ‘Lake Champlain.’ It was gorgeous, the trees that had been surrounding them since she woke were now the front drop to a crystal blue lake. It looked like it went on for miles and Kate looked out at all of the colors moving across the water. Boats and jet skis created lines of white wake behind them and Kate felt a pang as she thought about her last vacation with her mom. It hadn’t even been all that wonderful, her mother tucked away for most of it in a study of the condo they were renting for the week. But they’d had a few good moments in between. Kate blinked the memory off and tried to figure out where Yelena was driving her. After a half-circle of the massive lake, Yelena turned off of the road onto a gravel pathway that led toward a secluded section of trees. There were signs with ‘private property’ and ‘trespassers will be persecuted’ that lined the raod ominously.

    Kate couldn’t see through the heavy foliage more than ten yards out but she thought that they may have been nearing the lake’s edge.

    After minutes of driving Kate saw a streak of light brown through the trees. She leaned forward in her seat as they rounded the corner and a modernized log cabin came into view.

    It was larger than she expected. The front of the home was beautiful and expertly designed; large, cedar beams of the body stood in contrast to the black shingles that protected the slanting roofs. The front entrance and garage doors were bordered and detailed with smokey, distressed stones. A paved driveway and walkway lead elegantly up to the house. Kate whistled.

    “I thought we were trying to be lowkey?”

    Yelena pulled up to the garage door on the right and put the car in park, her eyes searching the surrounding area.

    “To leave a home empty is for the rich only. This draws very little attention.” Kate struggled to believe that as she looked around but she followed Yelena’s lead when she exited the vehicle.

    Yelena motioned toward the house.

    “I will clear the area. Stay by the car.”

    Kate huffed.

    “I can help. Or I can check the perimeter.”

    Yelena was already walking toward the front door, a gun in her hand that Kate hadn’t seen on her person until that moment.

    “Stay. I need to calibrate the security system and you will be in the way.” Kate crossed her arms and waited impatiently with her back against the driver’s door, her eyes roving along the home and its intricate details. She wondered if Yelena had a hand in the design and tried to picture what she may have asked for if that had been the case. Kate rocked from foot to foot, the painkillers well and truly worn off and the aches of her body reminding her that she was, in fact, quite injured.

    The garage opening startled her from where she was all but draped on the car and she pushed herself to a standing position as Yelena’s legs appeared under the rising door. As soon as it lifted high enough, Kate peered around her and noted another vehicle inside and a motorcycle tucked in front of it.

    Yelena motioned at the house.

    “You can go in. There are five bedrooms. You may have any but the one on the main floor. That is mine.”

    Kate didn’t wait to ask questions, her body dragging her forward until she was stepping into a hallway that smelled like timber and leather.

    She passed several closed doorways and walked into an open concept living room with arching ceilings and a loft that stood above it on the second floor. Massive windows covered the back of the house and looked out onto a lawn of bright green grass and a dock that reached out into the lake. A boat was nestled into a covered portion of the dock. Kate stared out at the blue water rolling over itself. She could see specks of colorful movement as boats ripped across the water’s surface.

    At least this safe house had a view.

    She turned her attention back to the house. Beyond the living room was a dining room and a similarly stunning kitchen.

    As she stepped further into the living room, she drew to a halt.

    Off of the room to the left was a stairwell. The stairwell itself looked to be wrapped around a massive, preserved tree trunk that had been stripped of its bark. It looked to start somewhere in the basement and rise all the way to the second floor.

    Kate’s eyes flickered away from the tree to the stairs and an exhaustion weighed her body into her next choice. Without much thought, Kate turned toward the leather couches and dropped her body into the biggest one. A tired groan slipping past her lips. She sunk into the cushions and felt them conform around her, the cool leather chilling her before it began to pick up her heat.

    Kate’s eyes closed and she was asleep for the second time that day.

    By the time Kate woke, she felt as if her body had been flattened by a train. No longer were her injuries the only things screaming for attention. Now, after resting, her muscles were sore and protesting their intense use. Kate wasn’t sure she could lift her leg but she also knew that sleeping the rest of the night on the couch wouldn’t help her.

    She rolled off of the couch and gasped as her knees jolted against the hardwoods. “Mother fuck–”

    Kate ground the rest out in her teeth and sucked in a breath to stand. It was slow moving; embarrassing enough that she glanced around the space for Yelena to ensure she was alone. She’d just found her footing when the Black Widow’s voice rang out from above her. “I am wondering if I should have bought you a cane.”

    Kate’s shoulders dropped in defeat as she turned toward the walkway twenty feet above her. The same fluttering feeling from the car overtook Kate and she made sure to school her features as she took Yelena in.

    Yelena had clearly showered, but, instead of her usual braid, she had let the waves cascade around her shoulders. She had replaced her sweats and was currently wearing baggy, patched jeans, plain a white t-shirt, and the most absurd looking jade green cardigan with red and white mushrooms stitched into it.

    If Kate even attempted to pull that off she’d probably look like a garden gnome. Somehow Yelena made it look like top fashion.

    “No– um, I– uh–” Kate forced her brain to focus. “I’m fine.” She motioned to Yelena’s outfit. “You went shopping?”

    Kate hoped she responded within a proper amount of time but by the way Yelena was watching her she figured she’d missed the mark.

    To her relief, Yelena didn’t question it.

    “I did. There are small towns all over and they had some really cool things.” As she said it, Kate watched Yelena’s thumbs brush the bottom of her cardigan and a pleased smile appeared on her face.

    Kate melted a little.

    “That looks very cool.”

    Yelena dropped her hands and nodded, face switching back to its general resting place of non-expression. She pointed to her right– Kate’s left.

    “You didn’t choose a room so I picked one. It is a good one. I got you some things to wear.” Kate’s brow furrowed.

    “How do you know my sizes?”

    Yelena rolled her eyes and turned toward the steps, her feet silent as they descended. “It is like you listen to nothing I say.”

    Kate moved slowly to meet her.

    “I didn’t know you ‘knowing a lot about me’ meant the measurement of my waistline or the length of my leg.”

    Yelena moved far quicker than her and hit the landing before Kate had reached it. She looked down at her with a judging raise of her brow.

    “I think I will not bother to explain.”

    Kate was too tired to feel irritation at Yelena’s condescending tone and she shook her head, lifting her foot to start the ascent up the steps.

    Yelena slipped past her with lithe movements and Kate smelled a different perfume, this one like sandalwood and roses. It held the same underlying warmth as the one before and Kate was starting to wonder if that was just Yelena.

    She reached the top of the steps and continued in the direction Yelena had pointed her. There was a door, slightly ajar, that she pushed through into a spacious bedroom. The floors were the same wood as downstairs but there was a fluffy white rug that took up the space surrounding the queen bed. The duvet was cream colored and a brown, fuzzy blanket was folded at the foot of it. White pillows were crowded around the headboard and Kate felt the urge to launch herself into them with abandon.

    She resisted if only for the sake of her injuries and slowly sat on the edge. The room looked out onto the lake. It was dark outside but Kate could still see the moonlight rippling across the water. In front of the window was a rocking chair that looked handmade, the wood worn but polished. Beside it was a small desk that matched the dark brown bed frame and a lamp was shedding soft light from it.

    Kate glanced behind her shoulder to see an open door that led to a walk-in closet. She could vaguely make out clothing already hanging and she pondered the fact that Yelena had spent the time to do that. It was confusing. Yelena was so often cold in her engagement with Kate and yet she continued to do things to help her; things that couldn’t even be considered necessary ‘for her protection.’

    Kate glanced to the door beside the closet and knew it had to be a bathroom. She debated the pros and cons of trying to shower before crashing and decided that dealing with her many injuries and bandages could wait until she felt less like she’d played a football game without pads.

    With that solidified, Kate shoved her clothes off of her body and climbed half under the covers.

    Her head hit the pillows with a thump.

    Chapter 7

    Chapter Notes The struggle is real. Honestly I feel like I keep losing the thread of their characterization but maybe I'm being too hard on myself. I'm posting this tonight because I like most of it. Anything I hate in the morning I'll just replace without removing the chapter. This is unedited.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Kate wasn’t sure what was worse: being stabbed or having a multitude of injuries across the entirety of her body. Maybe it was just the freshness of the latter but she might have picked being stabbed over what she was feeling now.

    She groaned for the seventh time that morning as she tried to shift her leg out of the covers only to have it pathetically stuck in the sheets of her bed. The soreness of her muscles made it difficult to even attempt lifting her leg.

    She switched to a new maneuver, this one involving rolling her torso toward the edge of the bed. As she did so, her shoulder protested loudly, the ache from the dislocation still prominent. She slapped her hand into the comforter as the eighth groan of the morning left her.

    “You sound like a polar bear in labor.”

    Kate’s eyes shot open at Yelena’s voice and she found steady green eyes studying her in all her embarrassing ineptitude.

    “You try jumping four stories and getting into a car accident.”

    Yelena scoffed.

    “Ha. No, I would never be so stupid.”

    Kate glared and looked down Yelena’s frame to see a tight, white tee underneath a pair of blue overalls. There was a strip of skin visible where the shirt ended and Kate fought to drag her eyes away. She wasn’t sure how Yelena was able to take a look that would be so messy on somebody else and make it look fit for a killer, but she did it well. Kate turned her head away dramatically.

    “Leave me here to die.”

    The sound of clinking and loud chewing followed her statement and Kate peered over to take in the cereal bowl she’d somehow missed on first glance.

    “Did you bring me some?”

    Yelena gave her a disappointed look, her lips turning down.

    “Kate Bishop, you are so pathetic right now.” Yelena shoved another spoonful of cereal into her mouth. “It is downstairs if you want some. Fruity галька. You have to stop whining and stand up to get it so you might starve to death.”

    Kate waved her away, resigning herself to her fate.

    “I’m more of a Fruit Loops bitch.”

    Yelena’s voice came out as more of a question.

    “Fruit Loops? What is this? It is better than the Fruity… what is the word? It is галька?” Kate’s eye cracked open and she looked at Yelena from the corner of it. There was something so jarringly dichotomic about Yelena that never failed to keep Kate on edge. In moments like this, ones where Yelena was curious and mocking and arguably delightful, Kate could almost forget the rest of her. She could forget that just two days ago Yelena had been the bane of her very existence.

    Yelena had killed people. Multiple people. Yelena had kicked sand into her throat and shot out the tires of her vehicle.

    Kate wondered at her sanity as she let herself fall into the general appreciation she felt for the assassin.

    “Pebbles. They’re Fruity Pebbles. And yes, Froot Loops are better.”

    Yelena’s brow furrowed and she nodded her head a few times.

    “We will get it at the store today.” She met Kate’s one visible eye. “Get out of bed, Kate Bishop.”

    Yelena left her with that, her overall-clad body disappearing from the doorframe as Kate was left to figure out her body’s resistance to working.

    It was painful and unpleasant and irritatingly slow, but Kate finally got to a standing position and edged toward the bathroom with careful movements. As she stepped inside in just her bra and underwear, she was made aware of three things at once.

    One, she looked like shit. Her face was a mottle of bruises and scrapes that truly gave her the appearance of one that may have been run over by car instead of jostled inside of it. Her body looked much the same, though bandages covered the worst of it and only deep bruises stained her skin.

    Two, if she was going to shower, she was going to have a terrible time with it. Like maybe even ‘get Yelena to help her with her bandages afterward’ kind of terrible. With the mocking she’d already received that morning from Yelena, Kate wasn’t inclined to give her more ammo.

    Three, she had somehow, in the chaos of the past day and the wounds covering her body, forgotten about a very crucial detail to the mission.

    Kate stared at the healing line of stitches on her thigh with a mix of pride and embarrassment fighting for dominance within her. Her plan had worked; Yelena hadn’t found the drive. But it also had turned into a futile tactic and she was going to have to cut it out of her leg. The thought of it had her shaking her head, dismissing the idea for the time being, and reaching into the shower to fiddle with the knobs until the water was steaming. She hoped for the best with her bandages as she undressed fully– arm sling following– and stepped inside the scalding downpour.

    Kate stood under the spray for far longer than she intended with her eyes closed to the small space and the problems that lay beyond it. For now, she basked in the heat and let it reach every part of her until she thought it may as well have seeped to her bones. After twenty minutes she finally turned off the spray and stepped into the muggy bathroom to dry off. She carefully toweled herself down, checking the bandages and seeing that they were relatively intact on her arm. She knew she’d need to change them before the dampness fostered bacteria and infection but her first goal was to get into clothes that would be comfortable to move around in.

    She walked into her room, steam billowing out behind her and a towel wrapped around her waist.

    As she entered the closet, she drew to a pause.

    There was a collection of items hung along the rack that Kate could only assume were catered to her taste.

    There were few t-shirts that looked thrifted with two band names she didn’t recognize and one she did. They were varying colors but her favorite was the same purple of her suit. She tugged it from the hangar and tossed it to rest on her slinged shoulder. There were two pairs of sweats– gray and black– and she yanked the gray ones down to follow the shirt.

    It looked like everything was fit for a person recovering from injuries that required soft and breathable clothing.

    A few sweatshirts, sweaters, and a cardigan also draped over hangers down the line. As she reached the end of the options, a jean jacket caught her attention and her eyes widened. It was stunning. Dyed denim that looked distressed and worn in. The purple was distinct but not overtly jarring or tacky and she fought the urge to make it work with her sling and her bandages.

    Instead, she returned to the bathroom and picked up her bra. A grimace twisted her expression as she held up the sweat-stained and grungy material. She dropped it with a sigh and decided that it would be better to go commando than try to fold herself into dirty clothing.

    She sat on her bed and tugged her sweats up her legs slowly. Her t-shirt was a little more difficult but she was able to finagle her aching shoulder into the sleeve after a few minutes. She dipped back into the closet to get the thick, black cardigan and shrug it over her shoulders.

    Her sling was the last addition.

    By the time Kate made her way downstairs, hair wet and hanging down her back and body exhausted from the effort of dressing and moving about, Yelena was nowhere to be seen. Kate wandered into the kitchen, her stomach growling in expectation as she pulled open the fridge.

    Disappointment flooded her as she took in the single carton of whole milk, a carton of eggs, and yogurt. It was the oddest collection of items she’d seen and Kate wasn’t sure how these were the only things Yelena had bought. Surely if she could buy clothing she could buy acceptable food, right?

    It wasn’t like Kate was here to judge: her fridge contained leftovers from her favorite takeout places and a few essentials if she needed to scrap together a meal. But she wasn’t sure ordering delivery would be as easy here as it was in New York.

    Kate painstakingly worked a yogurt free with one hand and glumly and closed the fridge. Her heart shot through her throat when she saw Yelena’s face in her peripheral vision. She startled and about dropped the yogurt she’d managed to pry off the pack. “Yelena.”

    Yelena made a face.

    “Your observational skills are in need of work. I was not even trying to be quiet.” Kate rolled her eyes and set the yogurt on the black island countertop. She began the steep task of trying to peel the foil lid from the cup. She was pointedly ignoring how strange it felt to be in such a domestic situation with Yelena. The tension now was nowhere near the heights it had reached when they were going toe to toe at the resort but it was still there. “Your shopping skills are in need of work.”

    Kate used her body to try to gain leverage on the foil top, pressing the plastic cup against her stomach as she struggled.

    Yelena did nothing to help her predicament.

    “What is wrong with my shopping? You have cereal and eggs and yogurt. That is many options.”

    Kate accidentally knocked the yogurt to the floor and growled out a string of curses. “Yelena, any help here?”

    Yelena watched blankly.

    Kate gave up, her head dropping back to release a breath before she turned and walked from the kitchen. She pettily left the unopened yogurt on the floor for Yelena to deal with and made her way to the back door.

    If she couldn’t eat she was going to go take in the view outside. She wanted nothing more than to collapse on the couch but doing so would mean she wasn’t moving for the foreseeable future.

    Kate unlocked the three locks to the patio and stepped out onto the stained wood in her bare feet. She felt the cool breeze that came with Fall warring with the heat from the sun against her skin. It was a perfect mixture and Kate walked further out toward the railing. She rested her hips against the top rail and looked out over the freshly cut grass– which, how?– and the beach just beyond it. The water looked even more inviting in the morning sun and Kate felt a desire to launch herself into its depths despite the less than ideal temperature. She lost herself in the beauty of it until she heard the door slide open behind her. She didn’t turn around even though she heard no approaching footsteps.

    An opened yogurt was placed on the railing beside her and a spoon was stuck in the pink mixture.

    Kate felt her chest warm in gratitude and she turned to find Yelena staring out as she had just been.

    “Thank you.”

    Yelena nodded once.

    “We do not have time for you to throw yourself a pity party.”

    Her gratitude stripped away like wallpaper. Kate clenched her jaw and tried to keep the bite from her tone.

    “I’m not throwing myself a pity party. I am tired and in pain and I deserve a little break before jumping back into whatever our next steps are.”

    Yelena shook her head.

    “You will learn quickly that you have chosen a life where you do not get those things. You need to learn to overcome the pain and the exhaustion and harness it for your benefit.” Kate knew she was being defensive, knew that her words were a little too harsh for the context, but Yelena’s words hit a nerve.

    “You’re not a Black Widow anymore, Yelena. You’re allowed to take a day off.” Yelena was so still and silent beside her that Kate wouldn’t believe she was there if she couldn’t see her.

    Yelena eventually turned her body toward Kate.

    The air in Kate’s lungs grew thick and hard to expel as Yelena’s eyes bored into hers. There was something about their intensity that never failed to send Kate’s heart into her throat. Yelena’s eyes when she fought were animalistic, vacant of emotion and vicious at once; sure of her impending victory. It was terrifying and Kate felt a certain kind of adrenaline crash through her in those moments.

    When Yelena was angry her expression was just as overwhelming. The hazel was alight and scorching, carrying the weight of so many things that Kate would never understand. It triggered Kate’s fight or flight response every single time.

    Yelena’s voice was hard when she spoke.

    “This is the last time you will speak of things you know nothing about, Kate Bishop.” Kate barely refrained from taking a step back.

    “Listen, I’m–”

    Yelena cut her off, voice precise and empty.

    “No, I do not have to listen. You are alive because I allowed it. You are here because I allow it. Your continued safety will be because I provide it. You can wield your arrogance at your SWORD puppets but do so at me and I will make this very unpleasant for you.” Kate bristled, her own eyes narrowing against Yelena’s burning expression. “I thought I told you to stop threatening things you didn’t mean.”

    Yelena was quiet for several seconds. After a moment, she gave a slight nod. “We will see.”

    Yelana turned and walked back inside the house and Kate felt a shred of disappointment in her chest at Yelena’s withdrawal. She also felt the inklings of guilt and concern but she pushed them aside for a moment.

    Kate tried to settle her nerves as she scooped out too-big-bites of her yogurt and shoveled them in her mouth. She licked the dredges of the snack and turned back toward the house. When she stepped inside, Yelena was slinging a backpack around her shoulders. Kate frowned, irritation still alive under her skin.

    “Where are you going?”

    Yelena gave her a look like she might be stupid.

    “I have many things to do, Kate Bishop. Not all of us like to waste time.” Kate huffed and looked around for any kind of shoe she could slide on to follow Yelena out. She wasn’t about to be left behind while Yelena worked on their current problem. She saw no way that that ended in anything other than more things to be held over her head. “Fine. I’m coming with you.”

    Yelena shook her head as she moved toward the garage.

    “No, you will stay and take your break.”

    Yelena made to pass by her and Kate’s hand moved before she could think better of it. She had barely brushed the skin of Yelena’s arm when she felt her world twist on its axis. Kate felt her arm lifted as Yelena moved under, spinning her and pulling her arm at an acutely painful angle. Yelena stopped just before any damage could be done, one hand on Kate’s shoulder and the other locked around the wrist, pulling it backward.

    Kate ground her teeth in pain and waited for Yelena to let go or finish the move, likely leading to another dislocated shoulder.

    Yelena released her and Kate stumbled forward.

    By the time Kate was able to gain her bearings and turn, she saw Yelena’s rigid form retreating into the garage.

    It felt like all of their progress had ground to a halt and started backpedaling and Kate didn’t know how to stop it.

    Yelena’s easy demeanor could turn on a dime and Kate was beginning to wonder if one side was an act. It didn’t feel like it. Yelena’s laughter felt just as real as her harshest words and Kate truly had never met such a difficult person to understand.

    By the time Kate heard the garage door open, hopefully signaling Yelena’s return, she had spent a significant amount of time exploring the large home. After changing her bandages, she’d found the three bedrooms that didn’t belong to Yelena; one more upstairs and two in the basement.

    She’d found an office right across from Yelena’s bedroom but the door was locked. In the basement there was a pool table and fully stocked bar that had Kate getting lost in the luxury of it. Then she’d opened a second door and found an entire gym and training studio with a significant amount of equipment that Kate didn’t recognize.

    Last on her journey, and where she’d settled in, was the tower, the highest level and where the tips of the tree decoration ended. There was a pair of leather chairs on either side of a medium table and windows covered every wall as it looked out over the stunning landscape. Kate watched Yelena’s other vehicle from the garage curve around the corner and she debated whether she should try and bridge the gap that had been created from a single conversation. She decided to stay put.

    Kate had thought her own emotions had simmered off but even the thought of trying to deal with Yelena’s hot and cold mood swings in that moment had her irritation returning. She wasn’t sure why it bugged her so much but she knew that starting another fight when she couldn’t use one of her arms was nowhere near a great idea.

    She looked back out toward the slowly setting sun and tried to guess how much time she had left before she’d need to get up and turn on the lights.

    The light had just dimmed to that point when she heard footsteps climbing up. It seemed like Yelena was trying to alert her to her presence and Kate closed her eyes to the spark of nerves that fired in her chest.

    By the time Yelena reached the top floor, Kate had forced a neutral expression. Yelena came to a stop and looked over at her from the top of the steps. Her expression was far less dangerous now and she revealed what looked to be a new burner phone.

    “You can make a call if you need to. I am sure Clint Barton is very worried for your safety.” Kate pushed herself into a standing position, ignoring the spikes of pain across her body, and walked toward Yelena to retrieve the phone.

    “Thank you.”

    Yelena nodded once, and for the barest moment Kate thought she might say something else. Instead, Yelena turned and walked back down the steps.

    Kate watched after her until she was out of sight. She shook her head, took a deep breath, and refocused on the burner phone.

    Clint answered on the first ring.

    “Kate, where are you?”

    A smile pulled at her lips and she returned to her spot on the couch. “Just wasting away in another safe house. People really seem to want me dead.” Clint grunted.

    “Not funny. What are you doing, kid?”

    It almost sounded disappointed and Kate felt it like a blow. She waited several moments before responding, the emotions of the past week cresting and ready to crash on her shoulders.

    “I did the job.”

    It was Clint’s turn to pause.

    “I heard. You also almost died.”

    Kate squeezed her eyes shut.

    “No. I was fine. I am fine.”

    Another beat. Clint’s voice came through a little lighter when he spoke. “That so? I seem to recall receiving footage of you jumping down four flights of stairs.” Kate laughed. If it sounded a little watery, neither of them mentioned it. “It was a do or die kinda’ moment, ya know?” She grimaced. “Bad phrasing. It was a do or get caught kinda moment. And I bet it looked so sick.”

    Clint scoffed into the phone.

    “Hardly. You look terrified and your nose is broken. Lila wants to print it and frame it.” Kate’s eyes widened and she sat up straighter.

    “I think that’s a great idea. You can hang me on your wall so that any visitors know you have a pretty sick replacement.”

    Their combined laughter morphed and then petered out and Kate could feel the weight returning to the conversation.

    “Kid, I need you to stay alive.”

    Kate was able to place the tone this time: it wasn’t disappointment. Clint was scared. “I will. I’m only getting better. I know this isn’t ideal but Yelena and I will take care of everything.”

    There was a pause.

    “Eben didn’t give me much but she said you were with her.”

    Kate lost his tone again, this one feeling vaguely concerned and something else. “Yeah, she said I was safer with her.”

    Clint’s response was so quick and sincere that Kate felt a hint of relief that she was at least doing the right thing.

    “You are. Just be careful. Black Widows. . . their wounds run very deep.” Kate scoffed, the bitterness from her conversation with Yelena returning. “I know. It’s like there’s a switch and I keep hitting it even when I don’t mean to.” Kate heard Clint shifting on the other side, likely sitting on his front porch swing. She could hear slight creaking as it swung.

    His voice was low and reverent and Kate knew what was coming.

    “Natasha was like that. For so long I thought there would never be a moment where I could relax around her. Be patient and be careful, Kate. You’re not going to be able to bulldoze your way through this.”

    Kate was offended at the assumption even though she knew that was her usual method of breaking people down.

    “I know. I will.” She felt a pressing desire to change the subject, a sense of discomfort settling in the longer she had to talk about Yelena. She forced a grin. “Soon enough I’ll be coming to visit your weird farm.”

    She could practically feel Clint’s eye roll through the phone.

    “It’s not weird. Stop calling it weird.”

    “You own acres of land and you don’t even grow your own food.”

    “I’m not having this conversation with you again.”

    Kate laughed and rested her head back on the chair.

    “Fine. Fine. I desist. Your farm is very normal and not weird at all. How is Laura?” “Worried about you.”

    Kate ran her thumb along the soft leather of the chair, her chest tight. “Tell her I miss her too.”

    “I will.”

    They spoke for a bit longer before Clint called it a night, his voice becoming gravelly in her ear as he updated her on the kids. Nate had apparently been losing his baby teeth and his newfound obsession with the tooth fairy had Kate in stitches as Clint described Nate’s attempt to pull out more of his teeth.

    Lila was in her first year of high school and had already joined at least four after school activities. She’d tried for a fifth but Model UN and the Debate Club had schedules that were too similar.

    Cooper had asked his friend to Homecoming in such an endearing and embarrassing way that he’d ended up with a girlfriend. Clint told her she’d have to hear the story directly from him. Kate felt so much lighter as the call came to a close, the ability to speak to somebody she loved and that loved her– even if he tried to pretend he didn’t– had truly returned some of her sanity.

    She ended the call with a promise to reach out if she needed help and looked at the time. 10:15pm And she still hadn’t eaten anything but yogurt. Her stomach’s reminders for her to eat had been easily ignored when she was so focused on her rampant emotions. Now that those had settled, it was all she could do not to fall down the stairs to get to the eggs. Not ideal, but they would do for filling her up. She could get to the store tomorrow for a better haul.

    Kate’s feet padded to the main floor and she walked through the dining room into the kitchen with single-minded purpose.

    Opening the fridge, she took in an entirely different scene than the one from that morning. The shelves were filled almost to bursting with products that Kate was pretty sure she and Yelena couldn’t consume on their own.

    It looked like Yelena might have bought everything that would fit in her cart and under it. Some of it made complete sense; veggies and fruits, ground beef, chicken, what looked to be an entire rack of ribs. Others made little to no sense; a pack of vacuum packed baked beans, a full chicken, chocolate hummus, and a cauliflower pizza crust but seemingly no toppings to go with it.

    Kate stared at the overflowing fridge long enough that she was wasting energy from the escaping cold. Fondness was probably the word to describe what she was feeling in that moment but it seemed inadequate.

    Kate peaked in the freezer and found it jammed with frozen meals. Curious, she walked to the pantry and the fondness doubled as she took in the stocked shelves with equally useful and non-useful items.

    It was likely a quarter of the items would go bad before they could use them but Kate couldn’t bring herself to care.

    Yelena had probably had to make multiple trips to get all of this food inside from the car and she hadn’t said a word to Kate about it.

    The options gave her the same issue as the previous lack of one and she didn’t walk to her room with a stacked plate of pizza from one of the freezer boxes until almost eleven-thirty. She burned her tongue four times in her impatience to eat but was able to satiate her hunger for the time being.

    She managed to brush her teeth and collapse into bed with a piece of pizza still remaining on her plate.

    Kate woke to a slightly worse pain than the day before, her body reaching its peak soreness, and she made herself stand on her first time without groaning. She wasn’t ready to deal with Yelena’s mockery when she was so grumpy.

    Clint had told her to be patient and grumpy Kate was the literal opposite of patient. Once she’d almost incited a game of bumper carts at the grocery store when an older woman was taking up the entire aisle with her cart.

    Kate had waited for approximately forty seconds before walking forward and listening to the drag of metal as she pushed the other cart out of the way with her own. That hadn’t been one of her best moments and though she liked to think she’d grown since the incident, she wasn’t going to risk it.

    She ignored the closet, knowing she was completely fine wearing her outfit two days in a row, and ran a brush through her hair so that it wouldn’t look crazy when she pulled it into a messy bun.

    As she descended the stairs, she heard voices conversing in the kitchen. Her heart rate rocketed up and settled moments later when she recognized Yelena’s voice. She debated trying to eavesdrop but she was ninety-nine percent sure Yelena already knew she was there.

    Kate finished her journey down the steps and wrapped around the wall to the Kitchen. Standing across the island from Yelena was a woman almost as stunning as her. She had short, black hair that curled on her forehead and full lips that did nothing to detract from the severe expression on her face. She exuded the same kind of power as Yelena, her body just as still and controlled, but it felt subdued, a little softer. Kate lifted a hand and gave an awkward wave.

    “Uhm. Hi. Hello. I’m Kate.”

    Awesome. Excellent communication skills. High five.

    The newcomer looked to Yelena out of the corner of her eye before giving Kate a nod. “Sonya.”

    That was it. Yelena watched passively and Soyna continued to stare at her and Kate felt a rising embarrassment even though she was pretty sure she hadn’t done anything wrong. After several seconds she realized her hand was still up and she dropped it to her side a bit too quickly.

    “Right. Sonya. Nice to meet you.”

    Kate really wanted Yelena to step in but she seemed perfectly content watching Kate struggle. She took in the tablet sitting on the counter between them and moved forward to look at it.

    “So what’s up?”

    Finally, Yelena broke her silence.

    “What is up, Kate Bishop, is a cloaking measure that will give us time to retaliate.” Kate approached the counter and looked down at a map of the United States. There were a multitude of red dots marked across it with one selected and enlarged. A box with rows of details including a timestamp and what looked to be a codename stood out from a point in Kansas.

    Kate’s brow furrowed and she looked at the locations of the other dots. There was a larger white point located above Cape Cod and a smaller one where she expected they were now within Vermont. Kate reached out and clicked a red dot on the coast of California. This one had a countdown on it that read 27:54:32.

    She clicked on another, totally disregarding the fact that she’d interrupted a conversation. Titan Forest Grove, Montana 06:21:58 HR: 58 She tapped another dot.

    Castle New York, New York -03:38:12 HR: 61 Kate glanced up to find both women watching her but she settled on Yelena. “Decoys?”

    Yelena nodded, sliding the tablet back toward herself.

    “Yes. Many Widows are protecting us.”

    Kate’s eyes widened and she looked back at the map. There had to be at least two hundred points on it that were either counting down or completed.

    “I didn’t know there were that many.”

    Yelena tilted her head, a small smirk appearing, and zoomed out on the map so that it revealed the entirety of the globe.

    Kate barely kept her mouth from dropping open.

    Hundreds of other dots appeared across the screen and Kate edged forward to get a better look.

    “Holy shit. How many are there?”

    Yelena looked down with her, eyes taking in something that Kate couldn’t see. “Seven-hundred and forty-three Black Widows are helping us. There are many more than have moved on.”

    Kate’s eyes shot up.

    “Like they died?”

    She wanted to punch herself in the face but the words were already out. Luckily this didn’t seem to set Yelena off.

    Yelena’s brow raised slightly.

    “No, they have not died. They no longer wanted to pursue this life. With their freedom, they chose to stop fighting.”

    Kate had dozens of questions that were actively trying to pour from her mouth but she manhandled them to the back of her mind. Now was definitely not the time. She glanced toward Sonya and didn’t need to ask whether or not she was a Black Widow. “Do you think the diversions will get Leviathan off our back?”

    Yelena pursed her lips and tapped the screen. Almost thirty percent of the red dots within the US had blue ones next to them.

    “No. As many as we are, Leviathan is bigger. They’ve had teams at these locations within the hour of the Widows’ movements. We are simply buying time and gathering data.” Kate tried not to feel overwhelmed by the magnitude of what they faced. It was different when she thought they were up against a localized organization. This was international and Kate’s breath felt useless in her lungs.

    What had she started? She had ignored Yelena’s attempts to remove her from the situation and had created a world-wide chain reaction.

    “How do we get out of this?”

    Yelena studied her carefully, noting the slightest waver that sounded in her voice. “We will find leverage. The Widows will interrupt Leviathan’s business and we will locate Valentina. If we make ourselves enough of a problem, by the time we confront her she will be ready to listen.”

    Kate felt a quiet rushing in her ears. Her voice came out far calmer than she felt. “What if the Widows get hurt? What if they die and I’m the one that caused it because I didn’t listen when you told me to leave?”

    Yelena’s brows pulled together but she didn’t seem to have words for her. Sonya spoke before Kate could well and truly spiral.

    “Leviathan is a plague. Crippling them will help a lot of people even if it’s not permanent.” Kate looked over and saw understanding there; reassurance. “Do not underestimate the abilities of a Black Widow. That is how they kill you.”

    Her lips turned up as she finished and the words were light, teasing even. Kate took a deep breath and nodded her head.

    “Okay. Yeah I– okay.” Kate pressed her hands into the marble countertop. “How can I help?” Yelena returned to the conversation.

    “For now, nothing. I have contacts tracking leads on Valentina and we need to lay low.” That was the second time Kate had heard that name now.

    “Is Valentina the leader?”

    Yelena scoffed.

    “Hah, no. She is a pawn. But she is also a mouthpiece to Leviathan leadership. We can work with that.”

    Kate took everything in with a breath.

    “Okay. So basically we stay here and do nothing while other people put themselves in danger for us.”

    Yelena frowned but Sonya beat her to a response.

    “We owe Yelena this.”

    Kate saw Yelena’s expression shutter closed, her body tensing at Sonya’s words. “You owe me nothing, Sonya.”

    Sonya gave Yelena a genuine smile, one that carried with it so much history and pain. Kate felt like she was intruding.

    “Вы причина, по которой мы здесь и можем помочь.”

    Yelena’s jaw clenched.

    “I need to check in with my contact.”

    She said nothing else before exiting the kitchen, body graceful despite the rigidity of her spine.

    That left Kate alone with Sonya and she ignored the possible awkwardness to push for answers even when she knew she probably shouldn’t. Before she could even open her mouth, Sonya let out a soft laugh.

    “I will not answer your questions.”

    Kate pouted and Sonya’s grin spread further.

    “You are quite the character. More money than the entire state of New York combined and you choose to be a Hawkeye imposter?”

    Kate’s mouth dropped open.

    “I am not an imposter!”

    Sonya tilted her head, mirth swirling in her eyes as she watched Kate. “Relax. I know you are his new protegé. I hear you were a nuisance when Yelena was trying to kill him.”

    Kate felt a hint of irritation that Yelena had relayed the information like that. She knew she wasn’t a match for her, but she’d hoped Yelena had enjoyed their sparring as much as Kate had.

    “She had it wrong.”

    Sonya’s eyes narrowed for a moment and Kate felt the hairs on her arms stand on end. It was her goal to eventually be able to drop the temperature in a room like Yelena and Sonya could. It was like a non-superpower superpower.

    After a moment, Sonya nodded.

    “I suppose she did.”

    That seemed to be the end of that discussion and Kate sighed.

    “How is it fair that she gets to tell everybody about me but I don’t get to ask questions about her?”

    “Saving a dog from traffic is different than a life spent without autonomy.” Sonya’s voice held a gentle reprimand and Kate winced. She wasn’t sure how the Black Widows had been forced to cooperate but it couldn’t have been pretty. Maybe one day Yelena would tell her.

    “You’re right.” She shook her head at herself. “I’m sorry. I don’t know why I said that.” Sonya moved around the island and came to a stop in front of Kate, her dark eyes searching. Kate fought the urge to take a step back.

    “You have dealt with tragedy, Kate. I know this.” Sonya’s eyes clouded slightly though she didn’t look away. “But unless you are a Black Widow, you cannot understand the pain of what we carry. You will never understand it.”

    It wasn’t said harshly or with mal intent. In fact, Sonya almost seemed sorry to be saying it. But Kate still felt it like she’d been slapped. She swallowed roughly and nodded, not having the adequate words to speak aloud.

    Sonya seemed to appreciate it. She looked in the direction Yelena had disappeared to. “I will go see about this contact. You should eat. You look like you were in a car crash.” Kate rolled her eyes as Sonya smirked and walked off, leaving her in the kitchen by herself. She stood there trying to break down her conversation with Sonya and what had been said before Yelena left the room. It was disorienting to be so often out of the loop. Yelena was this enigma of a human; one that could seem so effortlessly normal one moment and inhuman the next. Her mind was so foriegn to Kate and she’d thought if she just paid enough attention and asked the right questions that she’d be able to figure her out.

    But Sonya’s words made things far clearer. Kate had no chance of figuring Yelena out. If she wanted to know more, it could only come from Yelena.

    The next two weeks went by slower than Kate thought possible. Her first stint in a safehouse had come in a similar context: severely wounded, people trying to kill her, etc. It had been slow, boring, and she’d had entirely too little sunlight.

    But this? Kate was itching to move and be healed so that she could actually help. She was the reason– whether good intentions and outcomes were involved– that over seven-hundred Black Widows were causing havoc for a terrorist organization across the world. The magnitude of what was happening was far larger than Kate had ever encountered before and she was useless.

    Yelena had been communicating with the other Widows from the moment that Sonya had arrived. The two had turned the office outside of Yelena’s bedroom into a sort of war room. There was a massive screen that had been carried in one day and set in front of the bookshelf and the small map that Kate had seen on the tablet was now portrayed on it. Even more Widows had taken action, drawing members of Leviathan off-course and throwing them off Yelena and Kates’ scent.

    It was low risk according to Yelena and Sonya, but Kate watched the decoys’ heart rates with a rising sense of fear, hoping she never clicked on one to read it as a flatline. She spent as much time as possible offering herself as a sounding board but there was only so much she could do. Even Yelena could strategize very little without information and most of her time was spent receiving information from those outside of the safehouse and piecing it together.

    She had people looking for Valentina and others keeping track of key players within Leviathan to see if they could catch a break. It wasn’t really a two-person task considering Kate had zero knowledge of the organization or its methods.

    After a few days of staring at a screen filled with people doing far more than she was able to, Kate had stopped dropping in all together.

    Though her body was healing quickly, her arm had to remain immobilized. Kate killed time by working the muscles that weren’t currently slinged up.

    It was days of leg exercises that had her muscles spasming and sore. She worked on her left arm, arguably weaker than her right, and practiced what few drills she’d been taught in wielding her sword.

    It wasn’t enough; not even close to it. But it kept her busy for a few hours during the sunlight hours.

    Sometimes in those fourteen days, Sonya and Yelena would step outside to spar with one another.

    During those training sessions, Kate couldn’t tear her eyes away. She would settle in the viewing tower or the living room and watch as the assassin’s flew around the grassy lawn, bodies a blur as they matched blow for blow. Neither held back, every strike violent and intending to cause harm. The speed at which they moved almost seemed inhuman. Kate began to truly understand how much Yelena had held back when they fought. She may have been a bit more brutal at the resort than back in New York, but if she had fought her like she was fighting Sonya, Kate would have been out in seconds.

    It was irritating to accept and brought other questions to mind about Yelena’s behavior that she couldn’t let go of.

    Why had she gone so easy on Kate even when she seemed as mad as she was? Obviously Yelena had taken the route of humiliation which Kate almost hated more than the idea of being physically brutalized. But why work so hard to get her to leave and then save her when she didn’t listen? Why not finish her off?

    Yelena was stone cold, so hard to read and understand, and yet every time Kate thought about it, she came to the same conclusion.

    Yelena cared about her. Or at least didn’t want her to be dead. It was almost obvious looking back and taking everything in. Despite knowing Yelena wasn’t going to hurt her when she was being held up against the battered car, she hadn’t understood why. She wanted to ask Yelena but she was certain nothing would come from it. Yelena seemed to be making a point to avoid her. It wasn’t blatant. Yelena engaged with Kate when they crossed paths, but she didn’t stick around long in any room Kate was in. Kate had tried to drag Yelena into conversation but the Widow was far better at avoiding and escaping subtly than she’d wanted to give her credit for.

    The long days, the inability to train, Yelena’s avoidance, and her uselessness in their current predicament was enough to have Kate on edge and bored out of her fucking mind.

    At two weeks Kate was finally cleared to remove the sling and begin physical therapy to regain any lost strength in her shoulder. It wasn’t training at the level she’d gotten used to, but she didn’t feel so trapped now that she could start using her arm. It was stiff and achy but Kate started her exercises early in the day. She pushed through the growing discomfort even as it turned into pain.

    Sweat was beading on her skin and her shoulder was on fire. Almost an hour in her frustration was mounting as she realized how much mobility she’d lost. “You will make it worse if you do too much.”

    Kate glanced up from the exercise and grimaced. Yelena was leaning against the doorframe of the gym, straight-leg jeans loose on her and a tank that ended in the middle of her rib cage. It seemed almost unfair that the assassin had such a love for cropped shirts.
