They had gone to a town an hour away from their safehouse the day after the yogurt incident– Kate’s name for their little spat– and while Kate had gotten the essentials, Yelena had purchased even more clothing.

    Kate once more kept her eyes level when she responded.

    “Are you a physical therapist now?”

    Yelena’s brows raised at her tone and she pushed from the wall, stepping further into the room.

    “Widows were taught how to take care of themselves in any situation, Kate Bishop. It would be impractical to have agents that could not recover from injuries without aid.” Yelena was definitely correct, but Kate felt an irrational certainty that she would be the exception. She restarted the exercise and felt a spark of petty pride when Yelena’s lips turned down in irritation.

    “I’ll know when to stop.”

    Yelena’s jaw clenched.

    “Вы никогда не знаете, когда остановиться.”

    Kate wasn’t sure what was said but the tone wasn’t positive. She went to say something else snarky and was cut off.

    “We found something. That is why I am here. Do you want to keep stunting the healing of your shoulder or would you like to come see?”

    Kate dropped her arm from where she’d been moving it, the burning settling once she stopped putting the joint under stress.

    “What did you find?”

    Yelena turned and left the room.

    “Come upstairs.”

    It was stated as a command and Kate felt her agitation with Yelena and their situation growing. She waited several minutes before complying and trudging up the steps. She found Yelena and Sonya in the office.

    On the screen, instead of the normal map, was a zoomed in photo of a woman walking into a building that Kate recognized.

    “The Saint Isidore Museum.”

    Sonya raised a brow.

    “You’ve been there?”

    Kate squinted to try and make out more detail.

    “My mom really loved– loves– Spain. I’ve been there a few times.”

    Sonya hummed thoughtfully.

    “Well, good. You can tell us fun facts when we are there looking for Valentina.” Kate turned toward the exit.

    “I’ll pack a bag.”

    Yelena’s voice and following response had her wavering patience snapping violently. “No, Kate Bishop. You will stay here and rest.”

    She whirled around, chest tight and body already thrumming with too much pent up energy. “Like fucking hell, Yelena.”

    Yelena was watching her expectantly, her eyes challenging.

    “Was it not you that said you wanted a break? So I am to be surprised when you want to help? You have been sad all over the safehouse for our whole stay.”

    Kate moved into her space with furious steps.

    “There’s nothing I can do here! And you want me to stay when I can finally be helpful?” Yelena didn’t react beyond a careless shrug, her lips turning down in dismissal. “How helpful can you be with one good arm?”

    Kate reached out, left hand driving into Yelena’s shoulder with enough force that the assassin should have stumbled. She didn’t.

    Kate watched Yelena’s eyes narrow and the same fire she was feeling was reflected back at her with that frightening intensity.

    Yelena’s words came through clenched teeth.

    “Put your hands on me, again. See if you won’t need another two weeks for your second arm.”

    Kate was going to do it consequences be damned when Sonya’s too-calm tone filtered between their anger.

    “Okay, not that I don’t love the tension but we really need to get moving. Our flight leaves in an hour.”

    Yelena didn’t step away so neither did Kate.

    “Yelena, I’m coming with you. If I’m not on that plane I’ll find my own way there.” Yelena seemed to be reigning herself in but she bit back one more time. “You will reveal yourself to Leviathan within seconds of leaving this safehouse alone.” Kate felt a fluttering of satisfaction as she spoke her next words.

    “It’s just too bad that you care too much to let me die.”

    For a moment a flicker of bare vulnerability appeared in Yelena’s eyes. It was angry and confused and terrified and Kate felt it bury into her chest with enough force to knock the breath from her lungs. The look was gone before she could process anything. Yelena was gone soon after.

    As Kate stood there, heart pounding rapidly in her chest, she tried to match Yelena’s words with the look in her eyes and she couldn’t do it.

    Sonya saved her the headache of working through it.

    “Get a bag packed quickly, Kate. She will leave without you if she has to wait.” Kate let out a frustrated sigh and turned toward the door.

    “She’s the most stubborn asshole I’ve ever met.”

    Sonya laughed, her tone continuingly light.

    “It seems she has met her match then, hasn’t she?”

    Chapter End Notes A friend of mine found this story and said she was going to read it. Yikes. Mere, if you get to this point I hate you.

    Chapter 8

    Chapter Notes Whenever I finish a chapter and I don't like it I end up having to step away for awhile. This time it happened to be about three weeks so my apologies for the wait. It's not the best thing I've ever done but I have accepted the chapter as is lmao. This is not edited at all. Like, zero, nada, zilch-a-rooni, etc.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Kate ignored the backpack strap digging into her shoulder as she tried not to stumble through the foliage Yelena was dragging them through. Her boots kept getting caught in the branches hidden in the long grass and she tried not to imagine the sensations on her legs as ticks finding their way to her blood.

    It wasn’t like Kate hated nature. Far from it. Sort of. She just preferred nature in pictures; at a distance. She’d grown up in New York and had rarely strayed from similar settings even in her travels.

    It didn’t help that her mother also hadn’t appreciated the rugged nature of outdoor activities. The closest they’d ever come to camping was a cabin stay in the Swiss Alps. Even that had her mother taking a hit on early check out so that they could find somewhere a little more appealing to spend the remainder of their trip.

    Ahead of her, Sonya and Yelena seemed to be managing just fine. Their legs moved gracefully like the very earth itself wasn’t trying to yank them to a halt. Yelena hadn’t spoken to Kate since the office incident hours before; not directly at least. Her voice had been clipped and commanding, aimed at the collective group as they took her vehicle from the garage and drove two hours toward and into a mountain range that Kate couldn’t name.

    Kate had stewed silently in the back seat while the two Widows spoke Russian in low tones across the console in the front seat.

    They drove winding roads, passing public campgrounds and park stations, until they had moved deeper into the mountains.

    Upon reaching a distant viewing point, Yelena parked the car and spoke so that Kate could understand.

    “We walk from here.” She glanced back the slightest bit. “No breaks.”

    Kate rolled her eyes but didn’t let loose the rebuke that rose to her mind. She shoved her door open and pointedly slammed it as a response.

    Yelena’s eyes narrowed but she locked her car and started walking.

    Sonya rounded the car and caught Kate’s eyes, her own amused and exasperated in equal measure.

    “Very mature.”

    Kate had a backpack with her bow and quiver tucked inside slung over her good shoulder and she shifted it higher.

    “She started it.”

    Also mature. Kate ground her teeth and ignored the growing amusement on Sonya’s face. Yelena was already yards away and they followed after her.

    They hiked for an hour and those were the only words that were spoken. Kate wanted to ask where they were headed but she knew it would somehow be used against her. She tripped over another branch and kicked at it roughly.

    Yelena glanced back at her.

    “If you move with the ground instead of fighting it you will not have these problems.” Kate opened her mouth to snap back before veering into a different route. Yelena didn’t look like she was trying to poke fun. Her brow was relaxed and her eyes were less guarded than they’d been in days.

    She seemed looser out in the middle of the wilderness and Kate decided to take advantage of the better mood.

    “I’ll work on it.”

    Yelena nodded once and turned back around.

    Sonya gave her a thumbs up and Kate huffed in irritated amusement.

    After another hour of trekking, they moved through a thick crowding of trees into a large clearing. They were at least six miles from the lookout point and Kate wasn’t sure what the plan was, but Yelena slowed to a stop in front of her.

    Yelena looked around casually, her eyes tracking along the trees and their branches. After a moment she paused.

    “You should be better at hiding, Mason.”

    Kate’s eyes widened and she looked into the direction Yelena had focused. She saw nothing for several seconds. Then a shape moved from the branches of a tree and a man dropped to the ground with mediocre grace.

    He stumbled through the landing and a charming smile pulled at his lips. “Yelena. I didn’t think I’d ever see you again. Color me surprised when I get your message asking for my help. How could I pass up such an opportunity?” He turned toward Sonya and Kate, his hand pressing to his chest. “Jack Mason.”

    Sonya gave him a nod which left Kate the responsibility of taking his proffered hand. “Kate Bishop.” Sonya didn’t look inclined to speak further and Kate nodded toward her. “Sonya.”

    Jack grinned at her and shook her hand vigorously, his accent light but likely a British dialect. “Great to meet you.” He turned back toward Yelena. “With the warning and the money I was able to deliver just like I like. This way.”

    Kate felt a sense of ease from their new friend and wasn’t sure where it came from. It could have been Yelena’s relaxed posture or the way he seemed to know her. She wasn’t exactly chummy with him but she seemed less tense around him than she had around Kate the past few weeks. Either way, Kate felt herself settle.

    They were led back into the trees and out into a new clearing a few minutes later. Rick smiled out proudly and for a moment Kate wondered if she’d pegged him wrong; maybe he was actually crazy and they were out wasting their time.

    Yelena rolled her eyes.

    “Enough with the show, Mason.”

    Rick grinned and nodded, fiddling with something in his pocket.

    Kate’s breath wheezed out of her lungs in shock as the air in front of them rippled and warped, revealing a large, sleek aircraft.

    Even Yelena’s lips twitched up in pleasure as she took in their ride.

    Rick started walking forward, his expression unchanging.

    “The Quinjet: hybrid wing, VOTL capable, unprecedented maneuverability, fully weaponized, space travel optional, and, best of all, advanced cloaking technology that I ensured was up to snuff against current international tracking measures.” Kate stared in awe at the massive machinery, her eyes flitting from one end to the other and struggling to land on anything in particular.

    Yelena walked with Mason and Kate heard her speak quietly, just barely making out the words.

    “You’ve outdone yourself, Mason. Thank you.”

    The tone was sincere and laced with a deep melancholy that Kate tried to connect to their new companion. It didn’t fit until she heard his response.

    “Family to Nat is family to me. You have me in your corner anytime you need me.” His tone matched Yelena’s and Kate suddenly felt as if she was intruding. Yelena’s expression was closed off but Kate couldn’t imagine what she was feeling beneath it. Yelena nodded once and held out her hand.

    “Need us to drop you somewhere?”

    Rick pulled out what must have been a sort of key. It didn’t look like anything Kate had ever seen before but her only run-in with such advanced tech was an alien invasion when she was a child.

    “Nah. I have business in the area. This was really quite coincidental.” Yelena took the key and glanced back at Sonya and Kate.

    “We go.”

    Sonya was quick to follow, offering Rick another nod and a small smile. Kate stumbled past him, still trying to process that she was about to travel in Avenger’s-level tech that could go into space. A small part of her brain wondered why Yelena knew how to fly a Quinjet but she silenced it with the reality that Yelena could likely do millions of things that would surprise her.

    She waved goodbye at Rick.

    “Nice to meet you! Thanks for– this– this is so cool.”

    Her foot hit a root and she managed to only stumble a little.

    Rick laughed and he waved back.

    “Nice to meet you, Kate Bishop. Safe travels.”

    Kate refrained from jogging to catch up to the two Widows that were already nearing the lowering ramp at the base of the aircraft. She would never live it down if she fell and it would likely aggravate her shoulder back into a sling.

    She came to a stop as she reached the bottom, the smell of fuel and metal assaulting her senses as she stared into the hold.

    Lights lit the aircraft in strips along the sides and Kate could vaguely make out rows of seats with loose harnesses. The interior was a little worn, scratches and burns marking the otherwise smooth metal, and Kate wondered where exactly Rick had procured it. Kate walked up the ramp and looked beyond the empty chairs toward the cockpit. Yelena and Sonya were already strapping into the chairs there and Kate felt disappointment settle in her chest when she realized she wouldn’t get a front row seat.

    She turned her attention to the crates stacked inside as the ramp started the raise. A rumbling sound followed by the deep hum of massive engines only distracted her for a moment before she was flipping the lid of the first crate.

    “This is a lot of guns.”

    Sonya looked back and chuckled.

    “We are going into an unknown situation; it is always better to be prepared.” Kate flipped the lip of the second crate and her heart swelled.

    Arrows. So many arrows. Kate knelt down in front with a wide grin on her face, all tumultuous feelings and thoughts forgotten for a moment as she ran her fingers across the wide array of standard and trick arrows. Kate tugged at one and heard a hiss and click as the arrow was released. A disembodied, robotic voice sounded from the crate. “Burst arrow: a ring of secondary projectiles stored in the arrowhead fire in a circular pattern upon contact with the original target.”

    Kate’s eyes widened and she froze in place as it finished its description. She didn’t bother looking back toward the two Black Widows.

    She returned the arrow to its slot and looked for a way to lower the voice of the crate. She rolled her eyes at herself as she trailed her fingertips around the edges. Voice of the crate. Wild.

    Kate’s fingers found a small keypad and she pressed the first button her finger landed on. She felt instant regret.

    “Full content relay. There are one hundred miscellaneous arrows in containment. Sixty standard. Seven explosive arrows. Explosive arrow: a solid-fueled missile head attached to a tempered shaft. Capable of attaching themselves to their target with–” Kate scrambled, cheeks flushed painfully, to turn off the voice. She managed to increase the volume, change the tone, and skip to four different arrow descriptions before the voice was cut off.

    Despite the sound of the engines, the silence was starkly imposing.

    Yelena’s voice broke the silence.

    “That was embarrassing, Kate Bishop.”

    Kate’s skin itched with irritation and she closed the lid of the crate before it could start reciting the remaining arrow capabilities. Words left her lips before she’d consciously put them together.

    “So was you pretending you weren’t going to bring me on this mission. It takes a lot of time to get ahold of this many tricked out arrows.”

    Yelena’s following silence was enough to return some of Kate’s dignity and she shuffled to one of the empty seats as she realized that her statement was true and revealing of. . . something.

    As she went to sit, the aircraft jerked and Kate was launched forward toward the ground. She landed on her knees, good arm taking the brunt of the fall. Her heart lurched in her chest and she looked toward the cockpit for the first time since the crate started yelling at her. They were in the air.

    She hadn’t even realized they’d taken off. Which meant that the aircraft’s movements were impossibly smooth. Which meant that Yelena had done that on purpose.

    Kate grit her teeth and pushed herself to her feet, making sure to grab onto the thick straps of her seat in case Yelena did it again.

    She managed to collapse into her seat.

    She shot a glare toward the front of the ship and found only Sonya’s annoyed expression aimed at the blonde assassin next to her.

    Kate was glad she wasn’t the only one that found Yelena behavior irritating. She buckled herself in for good measure and leaned her head back against the worn leather. Yelena had gone out of her way to provide Kate with the gear she was most comfortable with. Considering the sparsity of Clint’s collection and even SWORD’s, Kate wondered how much trouble Yelena had had getting so many together.

    Kate would have thanked her if she hadn’t been such a dick. Instead of being a bridge to more amicable interactions, Kate was feeling further agitated.

    She closed her eyes and tried not to make assumptions about whatever was going on in Yelena’s brain.

    Kate woke to a hand on her shoulder and she blinked in the dark cabin. It was clearly night, meaning she’d been asleep for at least a couple hours. Sonya tilted her head toward the cockpit.

    “Tagging you in. She’s grumpy and I’m tired of listening to her.”

    Kate was unbuckling her seatbelt and standing before she could question if it was even a good idea. She could stay put and avoid any more unfortunate encounters with Yelena. It would have been the logical thing to do considering the previous twelve hours of tension. The thought of staying put struck Kate as unpleasant and she didn’t want to figure out why that was. Besides, she was already moving and Sonya obviously wanted her to go sit with Yelena. She was just doing what she was supposed to.

    She missed the smug smile on Sonya’s face as she departed.

    Kate had just breached the cockpit when Yelena spoke.

    “You can go back to sleep. I cannot imagine you will be a good copilot.” Kate sighed and slid into the seat beside Yelena.

    “Can we just not do this? I’m tired and I don’t want to be mad at you anymore.” It was a very mature move from Kate if she did say so herself and she hoped Yelena would take the proffered olive branch.

    “You are mad at me?”

    Kate didn’t bite but her voice held a sharp note of warning.

    “Yelena.”

    Kate didn’t have to look at her to know that Yelena was trying to decide what path to take. She couldn’t imagine the assassin felt too kindly about being scolded; especially not by Kate. She braced for harsher words but Yelena was silent. Finally: “You are very irritating to me, Kate Bishop.”

    Kate might have been offended had it been said in any other tone. But Yelena seemed almost reflective, like she didn’t mean it the way that it may have seemed on the surface. She looked at Yelena and saw the furrow in her brow and the downturn of her lips. “Tell me why.”

    Yelena’s jaw flexed and she tracked Kate from the corner of her eye.

    “I don’t know. A lot of people irritate me.”

    Kate grinned and she settled further into the seat.

    “People suck.”

    Yelena’s lips twitched.

    “Yes.”

    Kate leaned her head to the side, watching Yelena fully.

    “You don’t think I suck.”

    She said it as a statement even though she felt it as a question.

    Yelena met her eyes and Kate’s heart did so many unhelpful things that it was all she could do to keep it from her expression.

    Yelena studied her, eyes roaming Kate’s face openly as if she might be able to understand what Kate had meant by her comment if she stared long enough. Kate let her look. After an indiscernible amount of time, Kate really couldn’t track seconds when Yelena eyes were burning into her, Yelena turned away.

    “No. I do not think you suck.”

    Kate felt the tension that had been building since their argument two weeks ago slip off her shoulders and she fought a smile.

    It wasn’t long before Europe was visible and Kate wondered at the speed of the Quinjet. A normal flight to Spain was around seven hours give or take. She couldn’t imagine they’d been flying for more than four.

    Yelena flew over the Sistema Central Mountains and landed them in a tight grove of Autumn- touched trees.

    Sonya appeared at Kate’s shoulder and leaned down to look out.

    “I’m assuming we have another hike ahead of us?”

    Yelena began the process of shutting down the aircraft and Kate watched with rapt attention, trying to remember the steps.

    “There is transportation. We have somebody waiting for us.”

    The ramp lowered and Kate saw a figure on the other side backlit only by the moon. She tensed, her fingers itching for her bow, but Yelena moved to meet the stranger with ease. Even more surprising, Yelena’s arms opened and she embraced the figure tightly and spoke quick words in Russian. Sonya was just behind her and she wasted no time following suit. Curiosity tore at Kate but she remained still and waited for her introduction. After several minutes of murmurs and more touching than Kate had ever seen Yelena engage in without it being combat, the three turned toward her.

    The newcomer stepped into the cabin and Kate was able to see her face for the first time. She tried not to react.

    Mismatched eyes met hers, one distinctly pale and the other a similar though less golden hazel than Yelena’s. Most of her face was covered in scarring from a severe burn, only a patch on her left cheek and chin remained unblemished. She had an intensity to her that was less hidden than Yelena and Sonyas’ and Kate tried not to be intimidated. Sonya made the introductions.

    “Antonia, Kate. Kate, Antonia.”

    Kate didn’t offer her hand because for once in her life she knew without embarrassing herself that it wasn’t the right move. She swallowed once and bobbed her head. “Hey.”

    Antonia returned the gesture and then looked past Kate.

    “Let’s get the gear. Vehicle’s just outside.”

    Antonia’s voice was harsh, like her throat had been scarred as well, and Kate turned immediately to help offload the crates.

    They made quick work and loaded the gear into the storage compartment of a tan military vehicle with sturdy tires and a roll cage. The rest was open to the elements and Kate wondered at the street legality of it as she climbed into the back seat. Sonya sat on the other side of a short divider in the middle. Yelena took the passenger seat and Antonia climbed into the driver’s side.

    Antonia started the engine and glanced at Yelena.

    “We’re an hour outside of Madrid. I will get you close to the city before dropping you at a checkpoint. You will be able to take a vehicle into the city proper.”

    Yelena nodded her understanding.

    The following journey was as jarring as any offroading trip Kate had been on before. She’d strapped in after the first bump had sent her skyward and hadn’t let go of the straps since. They veered around tight groupings of trees, climbing large sections of rocks, and splashed through creeks and shallow rivers that sent mud and water flying around and behind them. By the time Antonia brought them to a stop near a dilapidated shed, Kate’s teeth ached from clenching and her clothes were damp.

    She released her death grip on the seatbelts and noted irritably that none of the Widows seemed phased at all.

    Antonia didn’t turn off the engine but she pointed toward the shed.

    “Your ride. I’ll catch up with you in Madrid.”

    There was very little conversation as they switched the crates to the new vehicle that was tucked into the shed: a compact SUV that Kate didn’t recognize. It read Ateca across the back and the gray coloring made it look like any other SUV Kate had come across. Yelena and Sonya returned to Antonia to speak after they’d loaded the car and Kate grumpily climbed into the back of the SUV. It felt wrong to claim the front seat but she wasn’t exactly sure why. She kind of felt like she being left out of an exclusive club and Kate did not have a lot of experience handling that. Her mother’s money had gotten her everything she ever wanted; at least in shallow terms.

    Yelena and Sonya joined her soon, the former taking the wheel.

    “We have an apartment fifteen minutes from the Museum. Antonia tracked Valentina to a hotel on the same block. Once we get stock of the situation we will create a plan of attack. For now, the goal is to remain out of sight and gather as much information as possible.” She looked at Kate alone. “Out of sight…”

    Yelena trailed off of what Kate expected was going to be a scathing comment on Kate’s skills of subtlety and changed directions.

    “I will teach you to navigate without being spotted.”

    It wasn’t necessarily a validation of Kate’s skills but she counted it as a win.

    The drive from the shed toward the city was majorly uneventful if a little bumpy. Kate only relaxed once they reached a paved road.

    The traffic picked up the closer they got and Kate watched passing vehicles. After being isolated for so long, she felt paranoid about being around so many other people. Yelena drove them expertly through the city until they were forced to park and move on foot. She produced a hat and passed it to Kate.

    “Keep your head down. Whatever you do, do not look up. The Widows do not have a footprint. Our faces are wiped from databases should they be flagged. You do not have the same luxury.”

    Kate donned the cap and tugged it low over her face. She kept her eyes on Yelena’s graceful stride and followed behind them, only bumping into one or two pedestrians as they moved. She tossed out apologies in the minimal Spanish that she knew but otherwise did not shift her head. By the time Yelena came to a stop, Kate was sweating at the concerted effort to not slip up. It was surprisingly hard not to look around at the rising apartments and quaint shops that lined the streets.

    “In here.”

    Yelena led them to a building made of bright, brown bricks and stepped inside. She pushed into a stairwell and started the climb upward.

    “There are no cameras here.”

    Kate allowed herself to look up for the first time since stepping out of the car. They were in a well-cleaned but worn stairway, the walls a soft cream color and the steps made of uneven but smooth concrete.

    At each floor they passed, a different plant was resting in the window on the landing. It was homey and the sunlight that flooded through warmed the space.

    Yelena pushed out onto the third floor and led them to a room just beside the stairs. She pulled out a key and let them inside a well-kept space.

    It wasn’t anything overtly fancy, the furniture well-used and the scarred wooden floors creaking under their feet. But it held a quaint atmosphere with bright walls and plenty of windows that led to the outside.

    Kate could see a fire escape off the living room and a building’s roof just beside it. The living room couch was patterned and had way too many pillows covering its surface. It faced an old television that looked like it might be there for decoration. There was a single, wooden chair with different upholstery than the couch but somehow it all blended together into something visually pleasing.

    A worn rug lay beneath a chipping coffee table at the center of it all and Kate thought she could probably stay in this apartment forever.

    She looked toward the kitchen that was truly just a walkway with smaller-than-average appliances tightly installed to make space.

    A narrow hallway led out of the living room toward what Kate guessed were the bedrooms. Yelena motioned toward them as she set her backpack down on the kitchen table that was pressed against the wall. It was free of any paint and it seemed like the stain of the wood was all but worn off.

    “There are three rooms but a single bathroom. Take your pick.”

    Yelena sat at the table and was on the phone before Kate could ask her if she had a room preference.

    She looked back to find Sonya already stepping through one of the doors and she followed suit.

    The first door she opened led to a small bathroom with salmon-colored tiles, a porcelain toilet and tub, and several folded towels on a white cabinet.

    It was tiny, barely able to fit all that was inside plus a human being, and she stepped out before she could get claustrophobic.

    The next door she opened was on the opposite wall of the bathroom and Sonya’s room and she found a modest space with a full bed and two windows framing either side. Embroidered curtains hung from the windows and plants stood in each of them, their colorful flowers open toward the sun. The comforter on the bed was more of a thick quilt with a depiction of the sunset abstractly crafted into it. In the corner of the room was a small table and chair, a bookshelf right beside it.

    Two rugs padded the floor and Kate dropped her bag down by the bed. She walked toward the window and looked out, finding another building almost a step away. She hoped nobody lived beyond the window facing her but it looked like a light was on further into the apartment. She closed her curtain just to be safe.

    Kate returned to the living room to find Yelena speaking fluently in Spanish, her eyes tracking something on the tablet in front of her. She hung up the phone after a moment and turned to Kate.

    “Valentina has been welcoming members of Leviathan to the hotel the past several nights. I believe there may be a large gathering of leadership in the coming week to discuss the pressure they are feeling on all ends. We are going to wait for that meeting and then we will show them what it means to cross a Black Widow.”

    Kate’s adrenaline spiked at the thought that they’d be able to do something soon. “What do we do in the meantime?”

    Yelena frowned.

    “I just said, Kate Bishop. We are to wait.”

    Kate tried to catch her grimace but with the way Yelena’s lips turned up she knew she failed. “Gotcha. Cool cool cool.”

    Yelena rolled her eyes.

    “You will find something to occupy your time. Look, there is even a nice tv for you to use.” Kate ignored the smug look on Yelena’s face and turned toward the living room. Yelena was right in that regard. She would definitely be finding something to do to kill time while they waited for the fun to start.

    Kate laid out another set of cards on the table.

    “I’m out.”

    Sonya tossed her cards down but Yelena looked like she was stuck somewhere between agitation and determination.

    “I am about to go out and I will have more points than you.”

    Kate bit back her amusement.

    “But the game is over. I’m out of cards.”

    Yelena dropped her cards all over her sets and Kate’s eyes landed on two fours. “Yelena! I asked if you had fours and you said ‘Go Fish.’”

    Yelena looked at her like she'd said something mornoic.

    “Of course, Kate Bishop. It is dumb to tell everything you have.”

    Kate pressed her palm to her forehead. She’d explained the rules so many times. “It’s a rule, Yelena. You have to say if you have a card.”

    Yelena threw up her hands.

    “Bah. This is stupid game. It is not even allowed strategy. Only luck.” Sonya was unabashedly grinning beside them as she watched Yelena and Kate go back and forth. She hadn’t had any problem following the rules and Kate wanted to thank her for being so agreeable.

    “Would you like to play something else?”

    Yelena huffed and glanced down at the cards with this look that Kate would peg as excitement if she had any confidence in her ability to read the assassin. “Yes. A better game.”

    Kate gathered all of the cards and pulled out the cribbage board she’d found tucked away on a shelf in her room. It was handcrafted and made of a pale wood; the colors worn almost entirely off. All of the pegs were stored in the slot at the bottom of the board and she placed a gold, a silver, and a black set in the starting positions: two each.

    The next twenty minutes was a test in her already pathetic patience, Yelena questioning every rule and asking if Kate was sure as if Kate didn’t grow up crushing her mother in Cribbage from the age of ten. Sure, it was still mostly a game of luck, but there was an element of skill that gave you an edge over opponents.

    “Yes, I’m sure that you get points during the play phase.”

    Yelena frowned at the board.

    “You are not even telling us the rules before they happen.”

    Kate laid her cards down face up, her tone exasperated as she explained for the fourth time: “This isn’t a real game! I’m just teaching you how to play.”

    They managed to get through the test rounds in another extended bout of arguments where Sonya watched with a thrilled expression and Yelena and Kate argued in increasingly terse tones.

    ”You can’t make up words.” “I’m not making it up! This game was made in the sixteen- hundreds. His Nobs is just what it’s called.” “Nineteen.” “What? How do you say nineteen? You don’t have any points!” “Nineteen is an impossible score to get, it means you have zero points.” “That is so stupid. If you have nothing, say ‘nothing.’ Nineteen. So dumb.” By the end of the practice game Kate was about to challenge Yelena to a duel, mismatched skill and fucked shoulder be damned.

    She stood from where she was sitting on the couch and Yelena’s eyes shot to her. “Where are you going? We play for real now.”

    Kate looked at Sonya for help but found that the woman was clearly in Yelena’s corner, eager to play though her learning process was far more subdued and respectful. She sighed, dropping back down and gathering the cards.

    “Fine. But only for Sonya because she is far less irritating than you.” Yelena looked offended and Sonya gave Kate a broad smile.

    “Thank you, Yelena has always been a very bad loser.”

    Yelena focused on the cards, ignoring the both of them as they each received their hands. To her surprise, despite Yelena’s many vocal complaints, she had practically mastered the game. Sonya as well. They played with sound strategy and speed and it was vaguely annoying but mostly impressive as the three of them raced toward the finish line. Kate felt delicious victory as she crossed first, Sonya four pegs behind her and Yelena eight back.

    She threw up her hands, a grin overtaking her face.

    “YES!”

    Yelena glared at her and Sonya relented with grace. Before another fight could break out, Kate stood.

    “Let’s take a break. I’m fucking starving.”

    Yelena seemed disinclined to stop playing but she pulled the cards together and left them stacked neatly on the table. She looked at the cribbage board and for the first time since they began the game, Kate really looked at her.

    Her heart squeezed almost violently in her chest as she took in the shockingly childlike look of joy that was slowly retreating behind the familiar vacancy.

    It made Kate desperate to know what Yelena’s life had been like before leaving the Red Room. She smiled at Yelena.

    “We can play again after?”

    Yelena seemed to pick up on her soft tone and her eyes narrowed as she nodded and walked past Kate to the kitchen.

    Kate’s eyes drifted to Sonya and she found the Widow already watching her. There was something a lot like respect and a little like hope drifting from her and Kate wasn’t sure where it had come from or why it was there. But she smiled at her too before following Yelena into the living room.

    Despite the card games and the shitty television that got around four channels, Kate still found herself bored through the days of doing nothing.

    She was starting to wonder if she’d picked the wrong line of work; she’d never imagined being a badass would require so much sitting around.

    Sonya and Yelena didn’t seem phased, but Kate willfully believed that was due to their deceptive abilities and not because they weren’t bored.

    Her favorite moments came in the times where the three of them would gather around the table, eyes on Yelena’s tablet as they walked through possible entry routes and plans of attack.

    “There are several conference rooms in this hotel that would be a great target for a full-scale attack. There is one in the lobby that would put too many civilians in danger. So far Kate had them separated in her brain with a very sophisticated naming system. Plan A: “If they choose one of the upper-level conference rooms we will enter through the roof by way of these buildings surrounding the hotel and make our way to them. It’s likely they have somebody on the cameras so any interference will alert them. We’ll need to be smart about our movements and block their exits. That includes the roof.” Yelena had walked them through their plan of attack several times before talking about Plan B: “If they choose the lobby conference room our job becomes far more difficult. I believe we split our attacks, the Widows targeting Leviathan leadership after they’ve left the hotel while we trap Valentina in her room.”

    Kate had asked about the possibility of all of this being a trap and Sonya had grinned. ”It is likely they expect us in some capacity due to our movement across the globe, but if the only purpose was a trap they would not gather so many important members in the same space.”

    The way Yelena and Sonya spoke about it instilled Kate with confidence she wasn’t sure she should be feeling. Sometimes it was difficult to decipher whether the Widows expected everything to go according to plan due to their skills or if they just knew they’d adapt on the fly.

    At least Kate fell into that second category pretty well.

    On the fifth day, there was a knock on Kate’s door.

    “Come in.”

    Yelena appeared in the doorframe and paused when she found Kate hanging off the side of the bed, the top of her head inches from the carpet beneath her. Strands of her hair had fallen loose from their pony tail and were dragging airily beside the rest.

    “What are you doing?”

    Kate puffed out a breath to displace the hairs in her face.

    “Waiting for the good part.”

    Yelena looked unimpressed.

    “It has been five days.”

    Kate groaned and rolled her legs over her head; quick enough that it didn’t hit the floor as she landed on her feet. She felt the blood rush from her brain and blinked the black spots from her eyes.

    “It’s been longer doing nothing. What’s up?”

    Yelena watched her a few seconds longer and motioned toward the hall.

    “Okay. We go now.”

    She disappeared out the door and Kate scrambled after her.

    “Go? Go where?”

    Kate followed Yelena into the living room and found the two crates from the car had been brought inside and opened on the coffee table.

    She blinked as she took in Sonya in a black tactical suit much the same as Yelena often wore. There were few visible weapons on her but the crate was half-empty.

    Kate’s heart thudded with glee and she sprinted back to her room to don her own gear, adjusting the SWORD suit to black. She slung her bow around her and carried her quiver to the living room to load it.

    Yelena stood next to Sonya in her own suit and Kate tried not to stare. There was just something upsettingly attractive about Yelena decked out and severe. Her hair was kept from her face in an intricate braid on either side of her head. Her eyes seemed to glow in the setting sunlight and she watched Kate kneel in front of the arrows. “Can you wield your bow?”

    Kate froze. Yelena’s comment wasn’t harsh but analytical.

    It was a fair question too; one Kate knew she’d have to answer in the negative. She didn’t know why she hadn’t thought of it before this moment. It also revealed that Yelena had expected them to have more time before this mission. She wouldn’t have procured the arrows if she hadn’t expected Kate to be able to use them.

    Kate glanced up at Yelena.

    “I’m not staying behind.”

    “I did not say you should. But you should have a weapon you can actually use or you will be in the way.”

    Kate’s stomach twisted as a sleek handgun was held out to her.

    “I don’t know how to use it.”

    That wasn’t necessarily true. She’d spent a year in high school learning from a girl she’d dated that drove motorcycles and thought guns were cool. Her and her mom had been in a rough spot and Kate had rebelled in a strikingly predictable way.

    Yelena stepped toward her and looked like she somehow knew.

    “You point and shoot, Kate Bishop. At anyone that gets too close.”

    Kate swallowed and took the gun. The metal burned in her grip as she looked at it. Yelena pointed to a small switch on the side.

    “That is the safety. To release you flick this up.” She handed Kate a body holster. “Do not point it at anyone you are not willing to kill.”

    The words felt like acid in Kate’s ears and she looked back at the weapon. As if sensing her trepidation, Yelena shook her head.

    “Look at me.” Kate’s head snapped up at the sharp command. She met Yelena’s eyes and felt vulnerable at the intensity waiting for her. “You choose to become all of what being a Hawkeye entails or you fail. This world will not bend to your moral code. You kill or you die.”

    Kate was too busy trying not to succumb to the overwhelming feelings she was having to be irritated at the tone in which Yelena said her title.

    Kate took a deep breath and swallowed, bobbing her head absently though she knew Yelena would see through it.

    “Yeah, no, I understand. I know what I need to do.”

    Kate donned the holster and slid the gun into it, securing it tightly. Only then did Yelena step back.

    Sonya was focused, eyes sharp and ready to move, but she graced Kate with an understanding smile and turned toward Yelena.

    “What changed the timeline?”

    Yelena tapped the tablet in her hands.

    “Valentina moved the meeting forward. A safety precaution I’m sure, but we will need to improvise.”

    Kate tilted her head to look at the image on the screen. It was from a security camera: a hotel lobby with people flooding through it. In the back, almost invisible, Kate could make out a closing door. In the space between the door and the frame she saw multiple heads gathered around a table.

    “Plan B?”

    Yelena flicked to a new screen, this one a blueprint of the hotel and surrounding areas. “Yes. We get to Valentina’s room without being seen and confront her alone.” Sonya leaned over.

    “We have Widows in place. We focus on Valentina and they can cause a little mayhem for the other members.”

    Yelena considered it for a moment and nodded slowly as the plan was rehashed. “We want to pressure them, not incite a war. We have to be smart about this.” Kate tried to follow the thread of the conversation, but just as before, it seemed she didn’t have enough information to provide helpful suggestions. She tucked her sword into a hidden seam of her suit and set her bow on the ground beside the crate.

    Kate flexed the hand of her injured arm and tried not to dig further into self-pity. She doubled down on the adrenaline and let it work its way into her fingertips and numb her anxieties. Yelena received a message on her burner phone and nodded.

    “Security cameras on our route will reset as we pass by. They won’t capture our faces.” Yelena reached for a long coat hanging in the closet and she slipped it on over her suit in a similar fashion to her attire at the Rockafeller center. It was a plaid long coat with the sleeves folded at the wrist to reveal a mustard inner lining. Only the top of the suit was visible and the black accented the coat and her face so well that Kate wanted to have a word with whomever gave Yelena her genes.

    Kate’s cheeks flushed as she watched Sonya don a similar coat, though hers was a thicker wool and was completely black. Was this a thing? Was she supposed to know to bring a fancy coat on a mission where she only had a backpack? How did Yelena and Sonya even get their coats there?

    Yelena looked at her expectantly and Kate held up her hands helplessly. “I have a sweatshirt.”

    Yelena pursed her lips and turned toward the closet, pulling out a forest green coat and handing it over to Kate.

    “Don’t get blood on it. It is my favorite.”

    Kate’s eyes widened.

    “These did not fit in your backpack.”

    Yelena scoffed.

    “Of course not, you should not fold them. Antonia delivered them.”

    Kate rolled her eyes.

    “How was I supposed to know that was an option? Was there a memo sent out?” Yelena shrugged but said nothing more as Kate slipped her arms through the sleeves of the soft fabric. It was somewhat small on her, pulling tight across her shoulders, but it would do. She buttoned the front and tested out her movement in it while both Widows studied the route they’d be taking.

    Kate stepped toward them.

    “So our path is going to be clear. What about once we enter the hotel?” Yelena turned the tablet toward Kate and zoomed in on blueprints that were for a different building.

    “This apartment is a similar height to the hotel and just next door. We will jump across the roofs. Kate, you and Antonia will cover the hallway and exits. Sonya and I will enter through the window.”

    Kate’s brow furrowed.

    “What about all the Leviathan members at the hotel? They’re not just going to sit by while we trap Valentina.”

    Yelena flicked back to the map where Kate saw seven dots representing the other Widows in the area. Whistler, Memphis, Taskmaster, Baron, Apollo, Temple, and Zion. Each one was in a four minute radius and Kate wondered, not for the first time, how Yelena held so much sway over so many.

    “We will hold for their departure. It is unlikely most will want to remain in the area. The Widows will choose their targets carefully and the attack on those members and Valentina will be synchronous.”

    Kate nodded along with the plan although the amount of moving parts had her rattled. She’d been scrambling all over the place for information when she’d been preparing to complete her mission in Cape Cod.

    Yelena had crafted her plan and all of its pieces in the span of five days. She had access to a deeply intelligent network and had the aid of individuals whose skills matched a woman that had been an Avenger. It was a level of depth and preparation that Kate had trouble fathoming. She followed the two women from the apartment building, attempting not to look like a fish out of water behind them.

    She knew she was attractive; it had never really been a question she had about herself and she was lucky for it. But these two? They existed on a different level of lethal allure and Kate wondered if that could be taught or if somehow they’d been selected for the Black Widow program due to their intangible qualities.

    Down the steps and quickly onto side streets, the three women walked amongst the crowds. Yelena and Sonya blended seamlessly, their Spanish sounding almost as fluent as their Russian to Kate’s ears. She caught enough words to realize that they were talking about things unrelated to their current goal. It sounded as if Sonya was sharing a story about somebody named Wren. If there was anything to be said by her tone, this person was important to Sonya.

    It only took them a few minutes to arrive at the apartment beside their target and Yelena caught the door as a resident left, her fingers slipping in at the last moment. She held the door for Sonya and Kate to pass, her eyes set out behind them to ensure they weren’t being followed.

    Sonya led the charge up the stairs, her footsteps silent and her eyes peeled. As they neared the fifth floor they heard the low conversations of two men speaking Russian. Sonya slipped effortlessly out the door on the fourth floor and Kate followed, Yelena behind her. Kate could only guess why they’d diverted.

    “Leviathan?”

    Sonya nodded and led them toward the elevator.

    “It’s not a sure sign that we’re expected; just smart play keeping an eye on the surrounding area. We’ll take the elevator to seven and see if we run into any more. If we need to, we have another method to the top.”

    They took the elevator to the seventh floor and Yelena moved toward the stairwell to listen at the door. She frowned and shook her head.

    “They’re here as well. Apartment 713.”

    Kate frowned.

    “What about it?”

    Sonya was already moving, her fist rapping on the door seconds later.

    “A sense of smell is greatly overrated.”

    A voice called through the door.

    “Break your nose then. We’ll see how you fare.”

    Sonya chuckled and the door swung open to reveal Antonia decked out much the same as the other Widows.

    “Everything ready?”

    Antonia nodded, her head tilting into the room.

    “It’s not the ideal situation but with a distraction nobody should see us.” Kate was still trying to process what was happening. She’d heard no word about where they’d be meeting Antonia, just that they would. She was starting to recognize that there was a third option beyond things going according to plan or adjusting on the fly: Yelena had prepared solutions to any and all possible contingencies.

    They fed into the room and the door was closed behind them. Kate looked around at the empty space; the walls a peeling, salmon wallpaper and the only furniture a pair of wooden chairs. Across from where they stood were floor to ceiling windows, one with a large panel cut out and set on the carpeted floor.

    Antonia moved without preamble and before Kate could ask questions she’d leaned out over a seven story drop and looked toward the street below. A few moments later the sound of honking and metal crunching filled the air. People yelled in the street and Antonia took that as her cue.

    She aimed upward, and let loose her grappling chord from the Widow Bite at her wrist. Sonya followed suit seconds after, leaving a stunned Kate with Yelena who was already preparing to tail them.

    She glanced at Kate, arm motioning impatiently.

    Kate’s eyes widened.

    “I don’t have my bow.”

    Yelena sighed.

    “I know, Kate Bishop. Come here and hold on.”

    Kate narrowed her eyes at Yelena’s tone but walked toward her, incredibly unsure if she was supposed to hug Yelena or cling to her like a koala. Grappling was already terrifying on its own; she wasn’t thrilled at the idea of doing it while relying on somebody besides herself to keep her from falling.

    Yelena did the work for her, clipping a latch on her suit to a loop on Kate’s. “Hold onto me. Do not panic.”

    Kate gritted her teeth and she was practically tugged into Yelena’s chest, her chin knocking into Yelena’s temple.

    “You know you could have told me the plan and maybe I would have been able to prepare better. I thought we were doing this together.”

    Yelena leaned back and met her eyes.

    “You are here.”

    Yelena reached out of the window and aimed upward, her own grappling line firing out and giving Kate very little time to prepare herself before she was jerked from her feet and into open air.

    She tightened her grip around Yelena’s shoulders, adrenaline crashing through her so hard that she felt like she might do what Yelena explicitly asked her not to do and panic. Her fingers dug into the fabric of Yelena’s jacket where she held on, fighting the urge to close her eyes. They slowed to a stop at the top and Kate felt strong hands on her yanking her over the ledge.

    She sat, staring out from fifteen stories up as Yelena unlatched them and retracted the grappling cable.

    Kate took a deep, calming breath.

    “We couldn’t have taken the elevator?”

    Antonia frowned.

    “If they have agents on the stairs it means that on the fifteenth floor we would have walked into multiple guards. We wouldn’t have reached the steps to the roof without alerting Leviathan.”

    Kate nodded.

    “Of course. Right. Totally makes sense.”

    Antonia looked at Yelena and Sonya to see if Kate was being serious but Yelena simply rolled her eyes and Sonya offered a shrug.

    Kate felt a prickling of frustration settling under her skin. She’d expected that once the action started she wouldn’t feel so far out of the loop. It seemed she was wrong. Yelena had shared the bare bones of their plan and left the rest for Kate to discover as she was dangling over half a football field above asphalt.

    She pushed to her feet as she took in the distance from their apartment building to the hotel that looked to be a floor shorter and way further than Kate was able to jump. Before she could ask questions to clear up the actual plan, Antonia had managed to retrieve a collapsible bow. Kate felt irrationally jealous that the woman could use the weapon and Kate could not.

    Antonia aimed at the roof they stood on and fired an arrow connected to a cable, the arrowhead expanding and creating an anchor point. Antonia’s second shot was aimed toward the hotel, the arrow pulling the cable with it, giving itself slack until it sunk into the hotel roof and tightened.

    Yelena gripped Kate’s shoulder and held out an earpiece.

    “Take this. You are with Antonia now. Listen to her and do as she says. Sonya and I will be coming in from the windows while you block the exits.”

    Kate nodded and glanced back toward the twenty foot gap between the buildings. “Roger that.”

    Yelena’s nose wrinkled up in a way that shouldn’t have made Kate want to swoon. “What is Roger?”

    Kate frowned slightly.

    “Well, it’s like–”

    She paused, trying to decide if she knew where it came from. Before she could, Antonia pressed a hunk of metal into her chest.

    “Time to go.”

    Kate fumbled the piece and only realized it was meant to help her zipline across. Kate tightened her hands on the grips and tried to figure out if her shoulder was in good enough shape to carry her weight.

    Yelena seemed to be of the same mind, her eyes trailing Kate with concern. “Can you make it? With your arm?”

    Kate nodded despite her lack of confidence.

    “I’m sure it will be fine.”

    Antonia brought out another cable and held it out.

    “For safety. If you let go, you won’t plummet to your death.”

    Kate grimaced.

    “Thanks so much.”

    As Antonia turned to attach herself to the zipline, Sonya watching the growing chaos down the street from whatever distraction had been triggered, Yelena stepped into Kate’s space. She took the cable from Kate’s hands and began attaching it for her, locking it to the same hoop that she’d used earlier and connecting it to the metal mechanism that would attach to the zipline. She did so with precise motions, tugging harshly after each connection to ensure it was ready to go.

    Kate wasn’t sure whether the action was for her sake or not but she appreciated the surety it brought her as she walked toward the zipline.

    Antonia nodded to her from her crouched position by the cable.

    “Follow just after me. The worst part will be the drop here. Try to give yourself cushion by bending your elbows so that you’re not instantly at the full tension in your arms. That is where it can be jerked away from you.”

    Kate nodded and watched while Antonia swung herself gracefully from the roof, her body swaying to the left with the momentum as she began sliding downward.

    Kate crouched down, her heartbeat in her throat as she stared out over the side of the building. She was getting better at heights, sure, but they never made her feel awesome. Yelena knelt beside her and took the zipline rig from her, securely connecting it to the cable. She said nothing but she gave Kate an encouraging nod.

    Not willing to wait for more apprehension to build, Kate mimicked Antonia’s movements and swung down, attempting to follow the instructions she was giving.

    As her body fell, she tightened her grip and locked her elbows for a slower release. It worked better than she’d hoped, but her shoulder was still weak and achy and she felt it as her arms took her full weight. She tightened her grip even further, holding on for dear life as the mechanism picked up speed and launched her toward the other building.

    After about ten feet her wounded arm was truly struggling, bouts of weakness sapping her arm until she felt like it would slip off. She grit her teeth and held on with all her strength. She was five feet from the other building when her arm gave out, forcing her to finish with just her left hand desperately latched to the mechanism. Kate held on just long enough for her to cross the threshold of the hotel’s roof before she dropped off, feet hitting the pavement at an odd angle and flinging her forward.

    She instinctively aimed for a roll, realizing too late that she’d chosen the wrong shoulder. A grunt of pain left her as it hit the stone surface of the building but she completed the maneuver and landed back on her feet, pulling the safety chord taut.

    Kate took several sharp breaths, her lungs desperate for air, and she slowly released the safety cable connected to her belt.

    ”Kate Bishop, you are always very close to catastrophe.”

    Kate laughed at the agitated tone of Yelena’s voice and wiped the sweat that was beading on her brow.

    “Part of my charm.”

    She heard a scoff through the coms.

    ”Death is not charming.”

    Kate rolled her eyes.

    “Stop being such a buzzkill, Yelena.”

    ”I am not whatever you are saying.”

    Kate chuckled and followed Antonia toward the hotel’s door, a grin pulling her lips. She liked Yelena like this; sharp but only because Kate knew she cared somewhere deep, deep down in her subconscious. Probably hidden behind the ten different ways to kill a person should she only have a paper clip.

    “We need to get you a book on American slang.”

    ”That would be waste of my time.”

    Kate was cut off by Antonia.

    “Are you two going to do this the entire time?”

    A laugh came through belonging to Sonya.

    “Yes. They have a lot to say to one another.”

    Kate’s cheeks flushed and she was glad for the fact that nobody was looking at her face. She heard Yelena grunt and knew their conversation was over.

    Still, it had done a good job of relaxing her after the harrowing zipline debacle. Kate posted up behind Antonia as they reached the door.

    The Widow listened through the doors for several seconds.

    “I don’t hear anything. Once we have the word from the others we will move into position.” Sonya responded, amusement coloring her tone.

    ”Roger that.”

    Three things happened at that moment.

    Kate heard Antonia sigh in exasperation. Kate blushed even harder than she’d been for no reason at all. And Yelena bit out a sharp line of Russian that sounded a lot like a reprimand. Sonya did not seem all that remorseful as she answered.

    “Whatever you say, Yelena.”

    Chapter End Notes I'm bummed that so many Bishova stories have fallen off but I'm also like, "I've done that so many times." I have two other accounts on AO3 that I just abandoned; not finishing stories that were going strong and had pretty large followings. My intention is to complete this entire story because I want to see it happen and y'all deserve to get an ending haha. I've also gotten very good at followthrough due to my D&D campaigns lol.

    Chapter 9

    Chapter Notes Quick update for y'all! It was fun to write this one :)

    See the end of the chapter for more notes In all, Kate stayed in place behind Antonia for fifty-six minutes before they were alerted to movement from the Leviathan assembly.

    ”They’re dispersing. Valentina is on her way up.

    Yelena’s voice came through, startling Kate after their extended period of silence. She’d wanted to break it multiple times as she stood on the rooftop, her jitters from the anticipation waning away as she grew bored.

    Her nerves, all but settled, sparked to life two-fold.

    Antonia shifted forward, the movement her first. Kate envied her ability to remain so inhumanely still.

    “Once she is on her floor, we move. I expect there will be many guards but we will handle them.” Antonia looked back then, her eyes sharp on Kate’s. “If you are not going to use that gun I need you to tell me now. I don’t want to expect your support if I will not receive it.” It felt like a reprimand and Kate was rightfully affronted.

    “I’m not going to put you in danger.”

    Antonia stared at her long enough that it became uncomfortable. She looked at Kate like she didn’t know why she was there now instead of lounging around in the temporary apartment blocks away. It triggered the same feelings as Yelena’s constant dismissal at the resort and Kate felt the determination lock into her chest.

    She was tired of the doubt and she wasn’t about to crumble in front of three Widows. She was good at this and she would prove it.

    Kate lifted her chin, jaw clenching and eyes narrowing at Antonia.

    Antonia seemed unfazed, her eyes holding the contact for a few more beats and returning to the door in front of her.

    Kate rolled her shoulders and took steadying breaths.

    Valentina is on the twelfth floor.”

    Antonia moved then, her body graceful just like Sonya and Yelenas’. But Kate noted as she followed that there was a level of brute power in Antonia that was unique to her. Broad shoulders and dangerous strides that seemed to carry so much strength in them. Kate trailed after Antonia, their feet quiet on the steps as they neared the bottom of the service stairwell on the fifteenth floor.

    Antonia stopped and listened to movements on the other side, ear pressed to the metal of it. She held up a ‘two’ with the pinky and ring finger of her right hand and Kate figured that meant she heard two people outside the door.

    Antonia glanced back once at Kate before casually pulling the door open, her shoulders relaxed.

    Kate barely got through the door before she saw two Leviathan guards writhing on the ground, the red of the widow bites’ shock keeping them in place. Antonia swiftly knocked them unconscious. With minimal effort, Antonia gripped the collars of their black uniforms, the red detailing of their collars twisting under her hand as she lifted them. Kate fought to keep her mouth from dropping open. That was not human strength, nor were the two guards small or lightweight.

    Antonia turned toward the service stairwell they’d just left and Kate had the awareness to press the door open for her to throw the guards inside with a careless toss. Their bodies crunched to the ground, one’s head hitting the stair with a nasty crack and the other folding in an uncomfortable way. They probably weren’t dead, but Kate grimaced and let the door close on them.

    Antonia motioned down the hall.

    “The building is a single L shape with an elevator on the other end. We don’t have access to the cameras but Leviathan guards are likely at each floor; posted at the elevator and stairs. They didn’t buy out these floors so our presence is likely only a problem when we start punching them. By then it’ll be too late.”

    Kate nodded, grateful that Antonia seemed to have taken her at her word. At least one Widow knew how to work with a partner.

    “I can take the next group.”

    Antonia raised a brow, eyes glancing at Kate’s injured shoulder.

    “Will you choke them out with one arm or do you plan to punch them very hard in the head?” Kate reached into the strategically placed hidden pockets of her suit and pulled out a blue disc.

    “SWORD has tricks too.”

    Antonia didn’t seem impressed as she moved toward the turn of the hall, offering Kate a small nod.

    Yelena’s mocking voice sounded in her ear.

    ”Yes, so tricky to send new agent against a whole team of Leviathan mercenaries.” Kate rolled her eyes, a sigh leaving her lips unbidden as Yelena teetered toward being an asshole again.

    “I beat you, didn’t I? Doesn’t seem like such a bad move.”

    A sharp laugh followed by a slow chuckle and Yelena answered in a light tone. “Kate Bishop, you are so funny. You almost died. Multiple times. I had to save you.” Kate knew her answer would likely set Yelena on edge but she was curious and just irritated enough that she wanted to see it.

    “Even if I had died, I still would have accomplished the mission.”

    There was silence on the other end for several beats but Kate could almost hear the frown turning down Yelena’s features.

    When Yelena responded, there was a bite to her tone.

    ”Dying for a code that can be created again in less than a year. Very impressive work, Kate Bishop. I’m sure Hawkeye would have been proud of your legacy.”

    It was a low blow and Kate clenched her jaw, her next words just on the side of too sharp. “Better than being complacent to the actions of monsters.”

    ”You sit on a tall horse.”

    Kate almost slowed as her and Antonia rounded the corner, trying to process what Yelena meant. Antonia interrupted before she could.

    “Focus, Bishop.”

    Kate let Yelena have the last word even though it pained her and she adopted a pleasant expression, her hands slipping into the pockets of Yelena’s coat. She had a plan to get them close to the guards without seeming a threat.

    She bumped Antonia’s arm as the three guards at the end of the hallway turned to look at them.

    “Do you think there are people in the world with a permanent stick up their ass?” It may have been a targeted comment, one toward the jackass assassin listening on the other end of the comms, but Antonia played along despite the absurdity of the question. “Is this literal or metaphorical?”

    Kate laughed, pretending not to notice the group waiting for them ahead. “Kinky. Metaphorical unless you want to take it another route.”

    Antonia shook her head.

    “I do not.”

    Kate shrugged, twenty feet from the guards.

    “Honestly, either way, the answer is probably just good sex.”

    Antonia frowned.

    “For the stick?”

    Kate slowed to a stop, now only ten feet away and Antonia followed suit. “If the stick is literally there then it’s for sex. What else would you have a stick up the ass for? Metaphorically, removing a stick from the ass can be done with a good lay.” Kate palmed the disc and subtly maneuvered her hand into position.

    Antonia sighed.

    “I do not like this conversation.”

    Kate grinned and simultaneously flicked the disc at the closest guard, letting out a relieved breath as arcing electricity climbed out and brought the three to the ground. Antonia was quick about the dispatch, this time dragging the unconscious bodies to sit against the wall.

    Antonia pressed the button for the elevator and glanced at Kate.

    “How many of those do you have?”

    “Six, I believe. And a few others that’ll come in handy.”

    A ding signaled the elevator’s arrival and the doors slid open for them to step inside. Antonia pressed the button for twelve.

    “There are likely more at Valentina’s floor and we will need to be quick to cut off her exits.” Kate nodded, feeling like she was a part of this mission now rather than a tag-a-long here to irritate the big, bad Black Widows. Antonia had stepped back and given her a chance and Kate had capitalized and executed her plan.

    She wanted to thank her but she wasn’t sure Antonia would understand what Kate was getting at.

    Kate pressed her hand against the front of the long coat, close to her weapon. “If there’s more than we can knock out in a few seconds, you can hit a couple and make for Valentina’s location. I can finish them off.

    Antonia pursed her lips.

    “And if there are too many for you?”

    The question didn’t cause her to bristle like it may have if Yelena was the one asking. Something to unpack later.

    “I don’t expect that will be the case.”

    The elevator slowed, the adrenaline that had been steady in Kate’s veins for the better part of an hour kicking up violently as her hair stood on end and her fingers tingled; her body realizing before her brain that things were about to get a little crazy. She walked out of the elevator with Antonia, shoulder to shoulder, and they found themselves with six members of Leviathan standing none-too-casually around the elevator with tense expressions and hands on their weapons.

    Kate gave the closest guard a smile. The woman was compact but strong, her hair cropped short to her head and a few scars stopping the hair growth in patches. “Hallway party? Where’s the music?”

    The woman’s thick, Russian accent curled around her response and it was a lot less pretty than Yelena’s.

    “No party.”

    Kate pouted, scratching at her face absently.

    “That doesn’t seem right? Toni, did you bring a speaker?”

    Antonia looked at her then and Kate saw something very close to amusement in her expression.

    “No, but I’m sure they can sing.”

    It was like an unspoken signal, one Kate planned to use in the future because it led so well into the pained shout that came from behind her, Antonia already breaking her fists across the guards’ faces.

    Kate sprung the Ronin sword and swung around, creating space and sweeping the legs of the scarred woman with the flat of the blade. She hit the ground and Kate kicked her head, watching it bounce into the wall and loll back onto the carpet.

    She focused on the three in front of her as two bodies thudded on the ground at her back. “I’m moving.”

    Kate didn’t bother responding as she heard Antonia’s departure.

    One of the guards moved to sprint after the Widow but Kate cut down across his shoulder with the blade and jerked her hand back up, twisting her wrist so that the pommel crashed into his chin and sent him staggering back.

    Kate grinned as she flourished the blade, spinning it once in her hand, meeting the determined and angry expressions of the two guards. Both were taller than her; the woman bulky and blonde haired and the man clearly blading and upset about it if his hairstyle was anything to go by. They looked like any other mid-level douchebags Kate had fought and she tilted her head, confidence continuing to build.

    This, she could do.

    Both guards still standing had knives in their hands, and Kate had never been in an actual sword fight, but she assumed the bigger the better.

    She parried the first lunge at her, knocking the blade to the side and turning her body away from the second knife when the woman tried to take advantage. She grabbed the woman’s wrist with the knife, twisting it unnaturally until the blade in it clattered to the carpet. Kate yanked the woman to her right as she ducked under a stabbing attack from the man. Kate slid behind the man and cut up his back, blood spraying the ceiling as he shouted out and grabbed at the area fruitlessly. Kate kicked the back of his knee to send him to the floor and caught the incoming slash from the woman that had retrieved her weapon, bouncing the knife off the guard of her sword. It created an opening to cut across the woman’s chest. As she jerked away, Kate brought the sword’s pommel backward with force, slamming it into the temple of the man she’d knocked to his knees.

    He collapsed in a heap and Kate moved quickly to dispatch the last guard. She felt a feral grin splitting her features as she ducked and dodged with ease. She crossed blades with the remaining guard, noting the heavy panting and blood seeping from the woman’s chest. It didn’t take much more than a powerful strike to send the knife out of her way and cut once more, this time along the woman’s arm. The weapon dropped and Kate jumped forward, the flat of her blade striking the guard’s cheek followed by Kate’s knee landing solidly in her gut. When she doubled over, Kate sent her to the ground with a temple strike.

    She looked toward Antonia to find the woman down the hall in the midst of oncoming Leviathan members, five of them still up with four already on the carpeted floor. Kate sprinted toward her, watching in awe as attacks from the Widow lifted the guards off their feet and sent them into walls or down the hall. There were craters in the surfaces around the Widow where bodies had struck them. She moved just as quickly as Kate expected, providing a spectacle of might that Kate had never gotten to see in person before. She didn’t count the time Kingpen had flung her around and broken half her ribs. Kate would easily fall to the side of watching such a display over being part of one. As she approached, two of the guards– one smaller and one towering over her– split off and came at her, ready for an easier target that Kate had no intention of giving them. She feigned a left slash and when the smaller guard jerked right, Kate swung around and kicked him viciously in his ribs. He doubled over and moved backward and Kate barely saw beyond her second attacker as Antonia grabbed the smaller guard by his hair and used him as a weapon to sling at the two in front of her.

    Kate refocused on the remaining guard. This one large enough that his bulk took up a good portion of the hallway. Kate lurched back as his meaty fist came toward her face. He was faster than she expected, and she found herself off-balance for the first time since the fighting started. He crowded her space, taking away the range of her weapon and slamming his head into her own.

    She turned slightly and a sharp burst of pain echoed in her cheekbone instead of her nose as she drew her blade up with as much power as she could, digging the weapon into his outer thigh. He snarled and continued to encroach, a right hook coming with a promise of serious pain.

    Kate held her left hand, blade and all, by her head and blocked the blow as best she could. It was still jarring, the force jolting her to the side and crossing her feet. She tasted blood in her mouth and collected it, slipping away from another round of brutal attacks, these ones forcing her back toward the wall.

    As her back hit, his eyes lit up with victory and she smiled back, all bloody teeth, before spitting a huge coagulation of crimson into his face.

    He reeled back, his hands coming up to rub at the blood in his eyes, and Kate took advantage, cutting across both of his forearms. He swatted at her and she kicked with all her might into his crotch.

    As the guard doubled over, eyes squinted shut and a groan pouring from his mouth, the sound of shattering glass echoed from inside Valentina’s room. The part of Kate’s nerd brain that wasn’t focused on the fight wished she could have seen how cool Sonya and Yelenas’ entrance had been.

    ”She’s making for the exit!”

    Sonya’s words came out fast and she turned just as the door to the hotel room opened. A woman stepped out, a likeness to the photos Kate had been staring at for the past two weeks. She looked irritated, eyes narrowed as she lurched from the room and looked toward where Antonia was just finishing off her last attacker. Her gaze turned to Kate and she seemed to found that a more appealing route.

    Shit.

    Valentina began to run and Kate took in her situation. She had a mammoth man folded over in pain, blood in his eyes but in between her and where Valentina was trying to slip by. Kate quickly made a decision.

    She lunged and jumped off of the wall to give herself momentum as she kicked the wounded guard’s head, snapping it backward and sending him colliding spectacularly into Valentina. Valentina hit the wall and the guard fell on top of her with all of his weight. He apologized profusely– voice high and pained– as he tried to get off of her. Kate kicked out again, this time right in his ass, and sent him slamming into a rising Valentina.

    Valentina seemed far more annoyed than scared as she met Kate’s eyes. “That’s annoying.”

    Kate let a laugh slip past her lips just as Antonia came up beside them. “People say that about me a lot.”

    Yelena appeared moments later.

    Sounds from the room meant Sonya was still in the middle of a fight but neither Widow seemed concerned.

    Antonia reached down and grabbed Valentina by the collar, hauling her to her feet. Valentina seemed far less concerned than she should have as she held out her hands. “Look, ladies, I’m sure we can come to an agreement of some kind. This doesn’t have to get uncivilized.”

    The guard that Kate had used as a battering ram tried to stand and Kate reacted swiftly, a front kick to his face sending him to the ground a third and final time. Antonia dragged Valentina back into the room and Yelena stared at Kate for an elongated moment before offering a nod.

    “Watch the door and keep your eyes peeled. Alert us of any more guards. Sonya is going to prepare our exit.”

    Kate nodded and tried not to let pride settle too comfortably in her chest. She was holding her own, side by side with the Widows. She was proving that she was an asset even without full access to the plan.

    As she stripped off the longcoat Yelena had loaned her, tossing it into the room, Sonya appeared at her shoulder. The Widow slipped into the hallway, sprinting to the left. Kate moved into the doorway once Yelena retreated back into the room and kept her eyes roving left to right, mostly out of sight should anyone come from the elevator or stairwell. Inside the room, Yelena was speaking in low tones, the only sounds coming through the earpiece currently being Sonya’s slightly quicker breathing as she ran and Antonia’s subtle movements.

    An ember of frustration bloomed in Kate’s chest when she realized that she wasn’t going to be privy to the conversation with Valentina; something Yelena seemed to have done on purpose. Her placement out of the loop felt intentional and Kate swore to herself that she’d get a full breakdown of the conversation if she had to fight one of them for it. Maybe a game of Cribbage to make it fair.

    In the following ten minutes Kate was on high alert, her senses prepared for anything and everything to start beelining for her down the hall. She was sweating in her tactical suit but only barely, the fabric made for such situations.

    At some point, Kate thought she heard her name spoken by Valentina but when she tried to focus on the conversation she could only hear the tone of Yelena’s growled out response. She was weighing the risk of stepping further into the hotel room when the elevator dinged. Kate ignored the voice in her head that told her that now would be a great time to unholster her gun, knowing this was likely not going to be a vacationing couple heading for their room. She heard the door to the stairs open moments after and readied the Ronin sword. Boots pounded the carpeted hallway to her left and her right and Kate peered toward the right, those sounding closer. Four Leviathan guards with guns drawn were bearing down on her and she barely jerked back in time as gunfire echoed down the hallway, bullets embedding into the wooden door frame as she found cover.

    Yelena’s voice returned to her earpiece, voice level and commanding.

    ”Status?”
