    “Peachy.”

    She grit her teeth and dug into a seam of her suit with her right hand, pulling free a small, white disc. She wasted no time flicking one to her left where she glanced to find six more guards sprinting toward her.

    The disc pinged against the front runner with a plink and the following, deafening crack of noise and explosion of light that poured out of it was enough to stun Kate a bit even at this distance. She watched the six bodies stumble and fall, hands to their heads and groans on their lips.

    Kate heard a pause in the other guards coming from the right, the gunfire stopping, and she turned their direction with the second disc. They were too close for a flash bang, the one in front just fifteen feet from her.

    Kate leaned back inside as the frontrunner fired several more shots toward the door. Kate tugged free another disc from her suit and flicked this one without looking out, the sound of high voltage electricity coming seconds later. Guards groaned in pain and thuds followed as they hit walls and the floor, their bodies out of their control. Antonia appeared at her side and Kate didn’t bother attempting to act tough. She preferred the superpowered Widow take on the extra enemies.

    “I’ll go right.”

    Kate moved into action, sprinting from cover toward the writhing guards, blue, staggered volts pulsing through their bodies but beginning to slow as she made her way toward them. One jumped away from the radius of the disc and tackled her legs, knocking her to the ground and sending the sword against the wall and out of her reach.

    Not good.

    She drove her knee into his face, then used his shoulder to push away and roll to her feet. A swift kick to his head had him stilling and Kate found herself deeply grateful for her recent legs-centered exercise she’d been forced to do without the use of her arm. The electric field fell off and the last three guards moved to stand, albeit slow and disoriented. Kate wasted no time, shouldering the closest woman and sending her to the ground while the other two dropped into combative stances.

    Kate slipped to the left as a fist careened towards her face and she countered with her own jab, the blow catching the attacker in his chin. As she blocked the second woman’s flurry of attacks, multiple towards her face and neck, the man came under her guard and clipped her with a wild, flailing strike that knocked her head to the side. It lacked power but it was enough to draw her focus as the woman tackled her.

    She huffed out a breath and tensed to avoid having her lungs pressed of air as her back slammed into the ground, the woman’s shoulder digging into her ribs. Kate drove her elbow brutally into the woman’s temple twice and jolted her hips up, knocking the guard to the side so that she could reverse their positions.

    Kate landed a single blow before an arm came around her neck, yanking her back and cutting off her airways. She reacted before her feet could leave the ground, stomping on the downed guard and using the momentum from it to shove the one choking her off-balance. They fell backward and Kate heard the breath wheeze from his lungs. The grip on her loosened slightly and she rammed her hand between the arm and her neck. She pushed up and slid down, releasing herself just in time to receive a violent kick to her right hip. Kate ground out a pained shout and swung her feet to knock the legs from under the guard that had rejoined the fray.

    Kate distantly considered that most of the fight taking place on the ground couldn’t possibly be a great strategic play as she rolled to her feet.

    ”Status?”

    Kate heaved a breath and backed away as the three guards spread as far as they could in the narrow hall, their bodies battered just like hers.

    She could hear fighting behind her from Antonia further in the hall.

    “Alive.”

    ”Do you need assistance? Sonya is almost done.

    Kate took a deep breath, her lungs burning and body aching, but she felt the benefits of her training like never before.

    “I’m fine.”

    Two of the guards moved at once, their bodies thrown toward her to catch her off guard. Kate crouched under and between them, recklessly placing herself in the midst of the three and surrounding herself. She ran at the last guard, feinting left and spinning back around to drive her elbow into her face.

    The momentum provided a knockout blow and Kate felt relief as she turned toward the two remaining guards. They were fast, running at her before she could get her bearings. She took a blow from the woman in order to set herself in position, her brow splitting and seeping blood down her cheek.

    As the woman’s momentum carried her out of a protective stance, Kate grabbed her around the back of the neck and yanked her head down, driving her knee up and hearing the crunch of bones. She tossed the woman’s unconscious body to the floor and received an unfortunately powerful kick to the side of the head, disorienting her as it snapped to the left. She jumped and rolled despite the way her vision blurred, ensuring she made space for herself. As she came up, stumbling slightly, she was able to just lurch away from another kick.

    Kate bit her lip to sharpen her focus, the pain bringing clarity and blood to the forefront as she brazenly tackled the last guard. He was smarter this time, protecting his body from the air-stealing impact, and Kate used both hands to find his face. Her stomach twisted as she pressed her fingers into his eyes, waiting for his panic, his hands scrabbling for her wrists, to draw back and drive her fist across his face over and over.

    She felt him slump under and and pushed to her feet despite the exhaustion now pooling in her body. Kate turned toward Antonia where she was easily dispatching the last of the six guards. She looked far less damaged than Kate, sweat beading on her brow and a single gash in her arm, the only distinct mark that she’d even been in a fight.

    She walked toward her as Antonia slammed the head of her remaining attacker into the wall, stilling him.

    Antonia turned toward Kate, eyes intense and burning from the battle. She studied Kate for several seconds, then, to Kate’s surprise, offered a sharp nod.

    “You have good instincts.”

    Kate felt pride bloom in her chest as Antonia walked toward her and back into the hotel room. She took two more steadying breaths and followed, posting herself at the door again. Her nerves were alive, frenzied, and she clenched and released her jaw in an effort to settle down.

    The hairs on her neck stood on end and Kate knew Yelena was behind her. She glanced back to find she was correct.

    Yelena was at her shoulder, eyes roving over Kate but void of any emotion. “Are you injured?”

    Kate glanced down at herself, taking in the deep, red, splotchy bruises on her body that would turn purple in the next few days. When she really focused she could feel the sticky blood drying on her face and she knew that there were multiple injuries elsewhere. Her shoulder was on fire but Kate had been pretty good about keeping it out of combat. She nodded to herself and then returned her gaze to Yelena’s, catching a flicker of something strange though it was quickly masked as their eyes met.

    She offered a small smile.

    “I’m good. Just a few bumps.”

    Yelena’s expression didn’t change as she nodded and tilted her head to the door. “Time to go. Our exit is ready.”

    Kate picked up her coat, waiting for Yelena and Antonia to leave the room. She could vaguely make out Valentina unconscious but otherwise unharmed with her hands and feet tied to a chair that had been dragged from the desk in the corner. There were also unconscious guards spread about the room, faces pressed into the carpet. Curiosity ate at Kate as she closed the door to the hotel room and followed behind the Widows, keeping an eye at her back in case further guards filed out.

    They sprinted toward the direction Sonya had vanished fifteen minutes prior. Kate expected to hit the steps when they turned the corner but Antonia pulled them up short and knocked sharply on another hotel room door.

    It opened to reveal Sonya, brows raised.

    “Took you long enough.”

    Antonia huffed.

    “You didn’t have to do any of the hard work.”

    Sonya’s feigned offense.

    “I punched at least four guards.

    She stepped aside so that they could pass.

    Antonia was first and as she entered, Kate heard the door to the stairwell slam open twenty yards away. Her eyes shot to the sound in time to see a shotgun barrel light up and flare out, a crack sounding as Kate shoved desperately at Yelena to send her into the room. The momentum of the push sent Kate backward and ultimately might have been what saved her life.

    The attacker was barely out of the stairwell, unable to aim, and the pellets from the shotgun pelted the wall and door in front of Kate, two burying themselves painfully in her arm. She launched herself away as she heard the pump of the shotgun, her legs carrying her at a dead sprint, snaking left and right. Another shot fired out and Kate cried out as a full blast caught her in the back, too far to do real damage but enough to launch her to the ground. ”Kate!”

    Kate could barely hear over her pounding heart, blood pumping through her body and her lungs tight as she narrowly avoided having her head blown to bits. She couldn’t breathe. She couldn’t breathe and her mouth opened and closed multiple times as she tried to crawl away. Panic crawled up her spine, fear sharp enough to taste.

    Return fire sounded before the third shot could come, and Kate looked back to find the guard wielding the shotgun collapsing in similar fashion to her gun that had already hit the floor. There was a bullet hole in her forehead and two in her chest and she fell to the ground unseeing.

    Yelena sprinted down the hall toward Kate as Sonya and Antonia kept watch, their faces severe.

    Kate pushed to her feet and started toward Yelena, her movement slow, and she felt her body protest each step. She was wheezing, air entering her lungs now but at a slow enough pace that black was filling her vision. She stopped, shoulder hitting the wall as a bout of dizziness knocked her off-balance.

    Yelena reached her, hands instantly on Kate’s body before sliding up to Kate’s face and trying to get Kate to meet her eyes.

    “We have to move. More are on their way.”

    Kate nodded but slumped further onto the wall as her lungs finally opened and allowed her to breathe.

    When she was finally able to meet Yelena’s gaze, Kate felt off-balance for an entirely different reason.

    Yelena’s eyes burned. The emotions there were clearer than Kate had ever seen them; so much anger alive and raging that it presented itself in the lines of Yelena’s face and the tremor of her hands on Kate’s cheeks.

    “Kate, can you move?”

    Her voice was colored with iron and Kate latched onto it, nodding her head and letting her hands find Yelena for support.

Kate, breathing normally now around the newfound tightness, pulled her arm back. She was sure by the stinging pain at her shoulder blade and lower back that a few of the pellets had penetrated the fabric of her suit.

    She dropped her head back, opened her lungs to drag in more oxygen. She barely had time to turn around before Yelena was crowding her space, hands twisting into the collar of the long coat, the unfurling rage still pouring from her eyes streaming from her mouth. “о чем ты думал!”

    Yelena’s tone was harsh and Kate could only blink at her, her hands coming up to wrap around Yelena’s wrists out of instinct.

    She opened her mouth to respond but Yelena pulled her closer.

    “Yel–”

    “Нельзя подвергать себя такому риску!”

    Sonya appeared at her shoulder and gripped Yelena’s arm.

    “Yelena! Она в порядке, that’s enough. Let her go.”

    Yelena didn’t comply, her eyes boring into Kate’s without relenting. Kate, for her part, was trying to shake out of her state of shock; both from the shotgun blast to the back and Yelena’s unfair fury.

    She blinked. And she looked. Really looked. Past the fury and the biting words and the hands tight on her coat. And she saw something she’d never really seen in Yelena before. It looked a lot like fear.

    Kate pulled in a shaky breath.

    “I’m okay.”

    Yelena’s gaze was penetrating.

    Sonya squeezed Yelena’s arm.

    “Now, Yelena.”

    Finally, Yelena stepped back and breathed rough, ragged breaths. She wouldn’t look at them as she motioned toward the balcony, tone hard.

    “Go. Let’s go.”

    To her relief, Yelena only pressed her fingers into the point of her neck. It was unpleasant but bearable.

    “Our bodies are filled with nerves, Kate Bishop. Some of them make for very good targets.” Yelena withdrew and settled into a loose stance, a smile lighting up her face. Kate was distracted enough for a moment that she didn’t realize what it meant.

    “I go half-power. We’ll see what you can do.”

    Kate blinked owlishly then fumbled to respond.

    “Oh. Yes. Okay.”

    She dropped back into her own stance and felt the slow thrum of adrenaline seeping into her limbs. This wasn’t necessarily what she’d hoped for when she confronted Yelena but it was better than the worst case scenario she’d thought up involving Yelena handing her ass to her and sending her home.

    Yelena struck first, her speed blinding as she jabbed toward Kate’s face. Kate barely managed to parry it to the side, Yelena’s knuckles clipping her bruised cheek. She grimaced and let her muscle memory take over, knocking the incoming cross up and lunging into Yelena’s body with her shoulder.

    Yelena kicked her knee outward, opening Kate’s body and providing Yelena the momentum she needed to turn Kate in her grasp, pulling her into a chokehold.

    “Do not get close to an enemy without a plan. You open yourself for attack and deal little damage in trade.”

    Kate grunted as she futitley tried to disengage from Yelena’s hold, her back tight against Yelena’s chest.

    “My plan was to hit you.”

    Yelena clucked disapprovingly in her ear.

    “That is not a plan. That is a piping dream.”

    Kate huffed.

    “It’s pipe.”

    Yelena increased the pressure on her neck and Kate coughed.

    “Listen to me, Kate.” Yelena released her and twisted Kate around by her shoulders. “A plan involves much more than a hope. If I am going to hit you, I am going to know exactly where and why and what follows. I will know what I will do no matter how you respond.” Kate sighed.

    “Seems like a lot of thinking in the middle of a fight.”

    Yelena dropped and swung her leg out, catching Kate’s ankle and sending her to the ground with no effort. She waited for Kate to stand back up and wasted no time in repeating the exact same move. Kate yelped and tried to jump, and Yelena increased the height of her own swing to catch her. Kate’s body hit the ground again with a thud.

    She glanced up at Yelena’s indifferent expression, blowing her hair out of her face and trying to think about what she’d learned that might stop Yelena from succeeding with the same attack.

    Slowly pushing herself to her hands and knees, she was able to stand in a more stable position, her feet spread and her body prepared for the strike. As expected, Yelena repeated the leg sweep and Kate tried to step back and out of the way this time. Her shuffle ended, her foot landing solidly on the ground and Yelena’s foot bypassing her.

    With unfathomable precision, Yelena redirected the attack and slid, managing to hook Kate’s leg with the back of her heel and yank her forward.

    Kate stumbled to a knee and Yelena was fast to twist her hips and strike out with her other leg, sending it into Kate’s chest and putting her on the ground.

    Kate groaned and laid there a moment as she tried to catch her breath. Yelena was already on her feet.

    “If we did this one hundred more times, each time you would end up in the same position.” Kate pushed herself to her feet and prepared again, brow furrowed in concentration. “We’ll see about that.”

    In hindsight, Kate could recognize her own stupidity.

    For the next thirty minutes she hit the hardwood floor upwards of fifty times, gathering a new slew of bruises and aches that she would vehemently regret over the next few days. Whenever she thought that she’d figured it out, Yelena would adapt her attack. She started with the exact same leg sweep every time and yet every time Kate failed to predict how it would end.

    It was maddening, disheartening, and stupidly attactive and Kate wasn’t sure whether the disorientation she was feeling near the end was from a concussion or Yelena’s level of skill. Kate stepped out of the next sweep, and shuffled back, keeping Yelena at a distance. The Widow used her own momentum to launch into a back handspring that ended with her legs wrapped around Kate’s torso. Kate’s body was squeezed and then tossed with ease toward the front door. She slammed into it with her injured shoulder and had no time to recover before a hand at her back wrapped into her t-shirt and sent her to the floor.

    The shot of pain from her shoulder was enough to keep her down for longer than normal, her face pinched in pain and her hand gripped around the joint to ease the throbbing. She let out a strained breath as her head spun, “Fuck. Okay, I get it.”

    Kate kept her eyes closed and breathed through the nausea for several beats, her skin clammy with exertion and the lack of moving air in the apartment. The soft fan that she could hear spinning overhead was doing very little in that moment to cool her down. Kate heard movement and the sound of the fridge opening but didn’t bother opening her eyes. She couldn’t find it in herself to be upset that Yelena had chosen that moment to make herself some food. Maybe Kate had interrupted her earlier when she’d so eloquently challenged her to a fight.

    Kate heard the boards by her head creak and she opened her eyes to find Yelena standing over her with searching eyes. She held out a hand and Kate took it despite wishing she could just lay on the ground for the foreseeable future.

    Yelena pulled her to her feet and Kate felt something cold pressed into her hand. “Put ice on it. I will get Sonya and we will talk about Valentina.”

    Kate nodded and fumbled the ice pack slightly as Yelena stepped out of her space, startled at the gesture and the words that followed it.

    She’d been asking Yelena about Valentina for days. Is this how she was supposed to drag information out of the assassin? Start a fight and hope it put Yelena in a better mood? Kate gingerly made her way to the living room couch and collapsed down, her body protesting. She leaned her head back and closed her eyes until she heard the two Widows approaching.

    Sonya sat next to Kate on the couch, shooting her a sympathetic grimace before focusing her attention on Yelena.

    For her part, Yelena’s skin was barely gleaming from the exertion. Her hair, pulled into a loose bun, was falling out in wisps. Kate almost rolled her eyes at the fact that the assassin could make messy look so fucking good.

    Yelena dialed a number on her phone but didn’t call it.

    “I was contacted earlier today by Widows in Tahiti. I was coming to get you both when I was momentarily interrupted.”

    Kate ignored the jab and waited for more information.

    Yelena pressed call, put the phone on speaker, and set it on the table. Kate frowned, trying to learn any hints about what was happening from Yelena’s stoic face. The phone was answered before she managed to glean anything.

    ”I can’t say I enjoyed being caught in your web of Widows.”

    Kate didn’t recognize the voice, but it was deep and clearly put out, commanding respect she wasn’t sure they’d receive from Yelena.

    Yelena’s lips twitched up in amusement.

    “If it makes you feel any better, it took them a full three days to find you. I guess being the previous director of a failed international protection agency still carries weight.” Kate’s eyes widened as her brain scrambled to put together the pieces of their conversation. ”I guess I’m lucky.”

    Kate’s jaw dropped as it all clicked.

    “Director Fury?! Holy shit. Clint’s told me about you. Woah.” Kate leaned forward toward the phone like he could see here. “Everyone thought you died.” She laughed. “Clint says that happens a lot.”

    The entire time she spoke she watched Yelena’s brows raise in disbelief. Yelena shook her head.

    “Kate Bishop, if you want to have a private chat to embarrass yourself you will need to use your own network of highly trained assassins to painstakingly track him down and trap him in a dingy hotel.”

    Kate frowned at her but her attention was dragged back to the phone as Fury’s grunt of a laugh sounded through it.

    ”Hello, Agent Bishop. I hear you’ve left quite the mark with your first mission.” Kate felt a thrill of pride at the title and she ignored the way Yelena’s eyes darkened. She made to respond when Yelena interrupted.

    “It is surprising to me that such an organization would send a new agent to take on Leviathan. She should be dead.”

    Kate bristled.

    ”Sounds like she handled herself. It is unfortunate she lacked the proper intel. SWORD is still collecting the pieces of SHIELD’s destruction and it’s a messy business.” Yelena ground her teeth.

    “She handled herself into a car crash. This has put us both in very bad positions. I do the work that your infant organization can’t, I get to Valentina, and what does she say? So surprising to hear your name from the mouth of a terrorist.”

    Kate’s annoyance slipped off into shock.

    Fury was silent for several moments.

    ”Is it so surprising? My name was leaked like all the rest.

    Yelena’s lips curled up without amusement.

    “Here is how this goes. You tell me the information I am looking for or my Widows kill you for good. No coming back from a rifle caliber bullet to the brain.”

    Kate looked up at Yelena to see if she was bluffing, seeking any hint of a lie. She found none and her stomach curdled, her chest squeezing at the unpleasant reminder of what Yelena was willing to do.

    Fury sighed, unperturbed.

    ”You didn’t tell them to be stealthy. I can see them.”

    Yelena hummed.

    “You can see some of them.”

    ”Valentina is a double agent. She’s been undercover with Leviathan for a complicated amount of time. She was in before the fall of SHIELD and we lost contact with her for five years. Then the blip took her. When she came back she made contact once but that was it.” Kate felt like she’d been sucker-punched but she wasn’t sure if it had to do with the revelation or what it meant.

    Yelena was unphased.

    “Is she still a double agent or has she been lost to the ideals of an organization that promises peace through rigid control?”

    ”I don’t know. Did she say anything else? Is she still alive?”

    Kate glanced at Sonya to see if she held any of the same shock but the Widow was watching with little expression, her eyes on Yelena instead of the phone.

    Yelena tilted her head.

    “She is. And she did. She said she would see what she could do about removing the hit on Kate Bishop and myself.”

    ”Is that not what you wanted? Seems like your enthusiastic measures of hunting me were for show.”

    Yelena let out a dry laugh.

    “Ha. No. You are not affiliated with SWORD. Not with measures that can be found. Just because she is with you does not mean she is with us. I am not banking Kate Bishop’s safety on the words of a psychopath.”

    ”But you trust me?”

    Yelena’s jaw clenched.

    “No. But Natasha did.”

    Fury let out a tired exhale, moments passing by with the impact of Yelena’s statement settling.

    ”She was the heart of us. Nobody was the same when Clint came back alone.” Kate’s throat tightened at the words and she remembered Clint’s eyes, the way they always glazed as he relived the worst moment of his life.

    She looked up and her heart squeezed sharply, noting the harsh agony raging starkly in Yelena’s eyes. Like always, there for a moment and gone the next, it left a brand on Kate. She wanted to reach out toward her but knew it wouldn’t help.

    Yelena’s voice was like bitter iron, carrying the weight of so much suffering on her shoulders. “It seems that SHIELD was no better at protecting its people than SWORD is now.” The silence that followed was tense and Kate didn’t consider breaking it. Finally, ”I’ll see what I can find out.”

    Yelena reached down and picked up her phone.

    “Good. Do that.”

    She hung up without a word and took a slow breath.

    For just a moment Yelena looked lost. It was barely there, a sense of helplessness that Kate had never seen in her. She looked like she might float off into nothing if not for the pain of everything dragging her down.

    Kate never wanted to see it again.

    All anger at Yelena’s words dripped away and Kate wanted desperately to root Yelena in something other than hardship.

    When Yelena came back to herself she looked like she couldn’t manage to settle and Kate knew that staring at her wouldn’t help. She stood and made her way to the kitchen, hoping to give Yelena an escape.

    “It looks like we’re staying here until he calls back. Do you guys want something to eat?” Sonya trailed after her with ease.

    “Do you know how to cook, Kate?”

    Kate sidled up to the cabinets that were sparsely filled with ingredients that had been picked up by couriers and delivered. She grabbed a spoon and pointed it toward Sonya. “I do not. Do you want to help?”

    Sonya leaned against the counter and crossed her arms.

    “No. Whatever you come up with will be funnier. I will go pick up something to drink. Both for celebrating some incoming answer and to wash down whatever concoction you throw together.”

    Kate rolled her eyes and glanced past Sonya to find that the living room was empty. She couldn’t help the way her thoughts reflected in her expression and Sonya wasn’t one to miss things.

    Sonya stepped away from the counter, her voice quiet.

    “She’ll be okay.”

    Kate almost felt embarrassed to show so much concern for somebody that acted like she could barely tolerate her most of the time. It was a gut reaction, one that quickly dissipated as she recounted the past week.

    Yelena had made quite the scene without regard for who saw.

    Kate blinked away the thoughts before Sonya could take notice and shook her head. She was allowed to care about Yelena because Yelena cared about her… in her own fucked up way.

    “I–” Kate chewed the inside of her cheek for a moment. “Yeah. I know, I know.” She turned to the cupboard and sifted through ingredients. “I’ll try to have something edible for you when you get back.”

    Sonya chuckled.

    “We’ll see about that.”

    Kate didn’t look back until she heard the door close after the Widow. She glanced to the hallway and thought better of any reckless ideas that popped into her head. She needed to leave Yelena to her privacy unless otherwise requested. Kate let out an audible snort at the idea of Yelena ever asking for Kate’s help.

    Kate stood on her tip-toes, trying to get a glimpse of the tallest shelf. A black package near the back caught her attention and she half-climbed up the counter to grab it, an idea already forming in her head.

    She dropped the plastic-wrapped pack of ramen on the counter with a grin and pulled out her phone.

    Ten minutes later Kate was frantically running to the windows in the living room to let billowing smoke pour into the darkening sky. She was sweating and somehow she’d gotten at least a tablespoon of soy sauce on her shirt in the process of stirring which was incredibly embarrassing.

    She was infinitely glad Yelena had dismantled the fire alarms in the apartment but that didn’t stop the smoke from filling the space with the smell of burnt sugar and cloying sour notes that curled in her nose.

    Kate ran back to the kitchen and stared forlornly at the pan, blackened brown sugar and runny puddles of soy sauce the only elements visible. She started scraping the pan, determined to try again and pay closer attention.

    “Kate Bishop, what are you doing?”

    Yelena’s voice startled her and she fumbled the pan in her hands.

    “Jesus, Yelena.”

    She looked over and noted that Yelena’s expression was once again inscrutable. Yelena glanced at the mess Kate was cleaning, dirty-looking rivulets draining into the sink. “You think you can burn down the apartment and nobody will come looking?” Kate huffed and wiped the sticky remainder of the sauce into the drain. “That’s so dramatic. It’s just a little smoke.”

    Yelena looked pointedly at the still-foggy kitchen and then down to Kate’s collection of ingredients. There were ramen packets unopened on the counter and partially minced garlic cloves sitting on the cutting board. A carton of eggs sat open and untouched on the counter beside a bag of brown sugar and a stick of butter with a chunk missing from it. The soy sauce container was shoved near the edge of the stovetop for easy grabbing and Yelena noted Kate’s opened phone in the midst of it all.

    “What are you making?”

    Kate flushed and reached for her phone.

    “It’s just a recipe online.”

    Yelena snatched it from the counter before she could, looking at the screen and frowning. She pressed play.

    ”...add your egg and let that cook and combine everything once more to be topped with sesame seeds…”

    Yelena let the video loop and watched it in its entirety before looking at Kate. “This looks very easy.”

    Kate dried the pan out and set it on the counter with a clank.

    “I know that! It is easy. I just got distracted for a second.”

    Yelena looked around feigning cluelessness.

    “With what? There is nothing to do here? You have said this many times.” Kate crossed her arms, eyes narrowing at Yelena.

    “Then you do it. Sugar is really tricky sometimes and I just looked away for a second.” Kate watched the challenge register and bit back a smile at how predictable Yelena was. It was aggravating and adorable the way the assassin latched onto competition; especially when that competition was with her.

    That was the theory she was working with at least. She’d never seen Yelena so eager to beat out Sonya at anything.

    Yelena took the pan from in front of Kate and set it back on the stove. She looked around and then gave Kate a look.

    “You don’t even have a pot for the ramen? Were you going to put it in dry?” Kate rolled her eyes.

    “No. I just. . . went out of order.”

    Yelena located a pot and started filling it with water, head shaking like she was sad Kate was so stupid.

    “That makes no sense, Kate Bishop.”

    Kate huffed and pushed herself to sit on the counter just beside the cutting board, intent on being as in the way as possible. She reached for her phone but Yelena pulled it away. “Just let me see it for a second. I’ll give it back.” Yelena pressed it into her hand and Kate huffed, mumbling. “Not like it’s my phone anyway.”

    Yelena shot her a look and Kate buried her grin as she surfed through her Spotify account, intent on filling the space with music in case Yelena didn’t want to talk. Or their talking turned into bickering.

    The possibility of making Kate look stupid seemed to be distracting Yelena from the previous conversation with Fury and she didn’t want anything to drag them out of this space. Bass, drums, and an acoustic 12 string filtered into the hazy kitchen and Kate leaned her head back against the cabinets behind her, handing Yelena the phone back.

    Yelena glanced down as the growling, imperfect voice of Jimi Hendrix fed into the intro. “I’ve heard this before.”

    Kate pressed her hands into the counter.

    “It’s a good one.”

    Yelena didn’t respond and Kate didn’t press, hearing the water boil in the pot. Over the next few minutes, the music pausing anytime Yelena wanted to reference the video recipe, Kate did her best to keep her eyes from tracking the Widow’s every move.

    It was difficult. Yelena was such an enigma to her, so lethal and cold and at the same time so eager to learn and determined to protect the people she cared about. It was strange to know that she was one of those people.

    Kate thought about the aftermath of the shotgun debacle again, the intensity in Yelena’s eyes as she yanked her in and berated her. She wanted to chuckle but refrained to avoid disturbing the chef. The way Yelena cared was confusing and a little mean but it warmed Kate. It was so rare that Yelena lost control like that, such an outlier to her behavior, that Kate relished it. It did something to her. That something was given a name when she’d been so close to Yelena in the tiny bathroom.

    Kate blinked before the feelings from the fresh memory could wind their way through her again and refocused on Yelena.

    Kate watched the assassin melt butter into the pan, measuring out a specific amount and proceeding to mince Kate’s already perfectly minced garlic. It sizzled in the pan and Yelena reached out for the soy sauce.

    Kate noted the dexterity of her fingers, the way the cap spun off from a flick of her thumb and landed on the counter. Her eyes trailed up Yelena’s arm, her shoulder, her neck, jaw, and Kate’s stomach dropped as she realized Yelena was watching her.

    Kate felt the flush of her cheeks and despite the desperate urge to look away, she was starting to realize that she could push Yelena if she was careful about it; if her methods were not invasive.

    Surely looking couldn’t be considered invasive.

    If Yelena was surprised by Kate’s continued gaze she didn’t show it. She looked away as a new song played out from the phone and the cha-ching and clatter of coins on repeat caught her attention. An upbeat bass guitar led David Gilmour into raspy, blues-y lyrics. Kate watched Yelena listen.

    “Have you heard this one too?”

    Yelena glanced back up, shaking her head and turning back to the pan. She measured out a very perfect scoop of brown sugar and plopped it into the pan.

    “No. It is new.”

    Kate reached over and stuck her hand in the brown sugar bag, pinching the granules in between her pointer and thumb and dropping the small bits into her mouth. She meant to ask Yelena another question but found the assassin watching her with a grimace. “What?”

    Yelena stirred the pan idly.

    “That was very gross. Your hands are not even clean.”

    Kate grinned at the genuine distaste Yelena was showing and went to reach back toward the bag.

    Yelena’s eyes widened and she whacked Kate’s hand away with the spoon. Kate huffed.

    “Ow.”

    Yelena pulled the brown sugar away and closed the bag so that she could throw it back into the cabinet.

    She grabbed the bottle of red pepper flakes and dumped in far more than Kate expected. “Woah, are you trying to melt my mouth?”

    Yelena looked unimpressed.

    “Spicy things are good, Kate Bishop. Do not be a baby.”

    A few more shakes of red pepper and Kate wondered if she’d be able to stomach the meal at all.

    Yelena stirred a few more times and set the spoon aside in order to drain the ramen noodles. She dumped the noodles into the pan and Kate’s heart swelled at the satisfied grin that spread across Yelena’s face.

    Yelena cracked three eggs, cooked them and chopped them into the noodles, before pulling the pan from the heat. Kate reached behind herself awkwardly and grabbed three plates to set on the counter. She turned back to find Yelena grinning smugly at her. “I told you it was easy. You are a bad cook.”

    Kate played along for Yelena’s sake but she couldn’t get over the warm tinge to Yelena’s cheeks or the way her eyes continued to smile even after her mouth stopped. She was having fun and Yelena having fun was Kate’s new favorite thing.

    “I could do it easy if I focused as hard as you did.”

    Yelena gave her a disbelieving look.

    “You got distracted by nothing.”

    Kate laughed, reaching her foot out to push at Yelena’s hip.

    “Shut up. You’re so rude.”

    Yelena made a sound of offense.

    “It is not rude if it is true.”

    Kate ignored her and leaned over the cutting board, intent on snaking a noodle from the pan. Yelena gripped her wrist before she even made it close.

    “Do not dare. ты отвратителен.”

    Kate grinned and tugged fruitlessly at her wrist.

    “I can’t feel insulted if I never know what you’re saying.”

    Yelena shrugged.

    “But I know it bothers you more when you do not understand.”

    Kate stopped fighting Yelena’s hold and tilted her head.

    “You like to bother me, I think.”

    Yelena’s grip on her slackened but Kate didn’t withdraw. Her wrist was released fully and Yelena met Kate’s gaze.

    “You are easily bothered.”

    Kate huffed out her disagreement.

    “But I’m not. You do it on purpose.”

    Yelena opened her mouth to answer when the front door opened and Sonya walked back through, two bags in hand. Kate cursed her timing but tried not to show Yelena her disappointment.

    Sonya set the bags on the kitchen table and walked over.

    “It smells burnt.” She leaned over the counter to glance in the pan. “It doesn’t look burnt.” Kate grinned cheesily at her.

    “I made us ramen.”

    Yelena’s jaw dropped.

    “You burned butter and brown sugar. I made us ramen and you stuck your dirty hands in the bag of sugar like a mannerless child.”

    Kate laughed and pushed herself onto the kitchen floor, grabbing her phone and stopping the music in the midst of R.E.M.’s take on losing civility.

    “That’s not how I remember it.”

    Yelena mumbled out a slew of words in Russian that Kate couldn’t make out but the way Sonya’s eyes widened made Kate think it wasn’t great.

    Kate found three glasses, none of them matching, and set them on the counter in front of Sonya.

    “What did you get for us?”

    Sonya smirked and turned away to unpack her bags. She presented three bottles: two Vodkas that Kate didn’t recognize and a third bottle that read ‘Samogon.’

    Yelena’s brows shot up.

    “You intend for us to sleep on the kitchen floor tonight?”

    Sonya’s smirk widened.

    “You cannot hold your liquor anymore?”

    Yelena rolled her eyes.

    “That is not liquor. It is petrol.”

    Sonya broke the seal and poured a shot in each glass much to Yelena’s distaste and Kate’s dismay. She wasn’t a fan of shots in the best conditions and this definitely wasn’t that. Usually her downing shots came after she was already hammered.

    Sonya picked up her glass and held it out expectantly.

    Yelena was quick to follow and Kate selected the last glass, her stomach already churning in regret.

    Sonya smiled at Yelena.

    “однажды пусть память о ней будет светлой.”

    Their glasses clinked and Yelena and Sonya threw back the shots. Their faces revealed nothing as Kate expected. She steeled herself and followed suit.

    The liquor had barely touched Kate’s tongue when she regretted every choice in her life that had led her to this moment. Kate would give up her mantle as Hawkeye if she could have avoided this moment. It was possibly the most horrendous tasting drink she’d ever consumed and Greer had once attempted to make her own moonshine.

    Kate’s face twisted in pain as she forced the shot down her throat, her gag reflex attempting to force it back up. She coughed and felt her eyes watering as the liquid burned down her esophagus and the hair in her nostrils.

    She blinked through the tears gathering in her eyes until she felt like she wasn’t on the verge of vomiting. The amused stares of the Widows met her pained expression. Kate didn’t feel a shred of embarrassment.

    “That was disgusting. No, horrendous. I can’t believe you drank it with a straight face.” Kate almost gagged at the thought and she rubbed a hand over her eyes. “Please tell me you got mixers.”

    She heard the clatter of something that was definitely not a mixer. She dropped her hand and found a bag of ice resting lopsided on the counter.

    Sonya grinned.

    “For the little hawk.”

    Kate made a face, her shoulders dropping as she grabbed the closest bottle of vodka and refilled their glasses.

    “Ha. So funny.”

    She glanced to her right and found Yelena’s face minutely softer than normal. She decided in that moment that she would happily drink ten more glasses of Samogon if it brought Yelena any kind of joy.

    Kate lifted her glass this time, intent on participating in the toasts. “To my ramen. The ramen that I made. Very good ramen crafted by my hands alone.” Yelena withheld her drink as Sonya tapped Kate’s glass.

    “Kate Bishop.”

    Kate finished her shot and lowered it, lips curling into a smug smile. The warmth that always followed ingestion of any alcohol started in her feet and traveled up her legs to her face. “Yelena.”

    Kate probably should have felt bad for Sonya with the way her and Yelena stared at each other, neither willing to give.

    They were only pulled from their glaring contest at the scrape of a pan and turned to find Sonya scooping herself a plate of ramen.

    She glanced back and waved dismissively at them.

    “Don’t mind me. I’m going to eat while you finish whatever that is.”

    Yelena shot Kate one last glare before grabbing her own plate and taking the remainder of the ramen.

    Kate’s eyes widened in time with a loud growl of her stomach and she reached out with a strangled shout. Yelena paused with the last scoop in the spoon, her eyes cutting to Kate with a sly edge.

    “Yes, Kate Bishop? Do you want some of my ramen?”

    Kate rolled her eyes.

    “Yes, Yelena. I want some of your ramen.”

    Yelena frowned, tilting her head toward Kate.

    “I do not think I heard you.”

    Kate stepped into her space with the plate, trying to maneuver it under the spoon. “I think you heard me just fine.”

    Yelena’s lips curled up and Kate’s heart beat a heavy one-two in her chest. “I do not think Sonya heard you.”

    Kate rolled her eyes and glanced back to find Sonya watching them with amusement from the couch. She had the open bottle of vodka in front of her and seemed content to eat without them. Kate pouted at Yelena and watched curiously as Yelena’s eyes flickered downward and back up.

    With a resentful sigh, Yelena dumped the scoop on Kate’s plate and stepped around her, their shoulders brushing lightly.

    Kate grinned and trailed her into the living room, taking the chair as Yelena sat beside Sonya on the couch. She wasn’t sure what to expect from two Widows that seemed intent on getting plastered but she knew she didn’t want to miss it.

    Kate had tears in her eyes as Sonya regaled her with the story of meeting her wife. “She fell into a lake?”

    Sonya’s cheeks were flushed with the alcohol she’d consumed and she nodded her head. Beside her, Yelena was grinning although it seemed like she may have heard the story before with the way she’d start smiling before anything funny was said.

    Sonya slumped further into the couch.

    “She came to ask me how I was doing and I slapped her camera into her face. She lost her balance and stumbled into the lake.”

    Kate let out another laugh and wiped at her eyes.

    “I can’t believe that worked out for you. I’ll have to try that sometime.” Sonya’s smile went softer and she sipped from her glass.

    “I was very lucky.”

    Kate sank deeper into her chair and looked at the two Widows beside one another. Sonya was taken by the alcohol, her shoulders loose and her eyes unfocused as she thought about her wife. She seemed content to be calm and careless. There was still an alertness to her that Kate envied, but Sonya had given into the nature of the night.

    Yelena was a stark comparison, her cheeks flushed just the same but her body held at attention like she expected at any moment she’d need to run or fight. Her eyes tracked across Kate and Sonya but often wandered toward the windows and doors like she felt she needed to be on watch.

    Maybe it was smart. They were, after all, only a couple days past a rather daring operation.     Yelena looked on the verge of protest before the meaning of Kate’s words settled. It wasn’t a provocation as much as an understanding and Yelena seemed more willing to accept that in her current state.

    “Ask your question.”

    Kate’s head lolled to the side, resting against the back of the chair as she studied Yelena. She allowed a tease to underlay her words as she tipped the scales.

    “What makes me so difficult to handle?”

    Kate wanted to laugh at the normalcy of the action as she watched Tamar tuck into the pages, her brow already beginning to furrow. It looked like she was flying through whatever puzzle was in front of her while her pen– previously tucked into the tight circle of the book– filled in the page.

    The first hour was almost entirely silent save for the scratching of pen on paper. Kate kept her eyes closed and had almost fallen asleep when she heard the pen stop. Curious, she opened her eyes and found Tamar watching her, this time with a thoughtful look on her face. Kate decided to bite the bullet.

    “You have something to say, don’t you.”

    Tamar chuckled and placed her pen in the center of the booklet before beginning to twist. She stuffed it into her pocket and crossed her arms, slouching slightly in her seat. “More a question to ask.”

    Kate could appreciate that. She never stopped asking questions; even when she definitely should.

    “Shoot.”

    Tamar kicked one foot over the other and watched Kate closely.

    “You and Yelena–”

    She cut off but Kate wasn’t able to control the way her body tensed; something Tamar definitely picked up on but bypassed as she continued.

    “How did you meet?”

    Kate scoffed.

    “She was trying to kill my childhood hero.”

    Tamar frowned slightly in thought and nodded her head.

    “I remember hearing about that. And after? You must have gotten close for her to have put a blanket protection order on you.”

    Kate’s brows furrowed, her mind trying to pinpoint what she meant.

    Tamar seemed almost delighted with this response and she sat up straighter. “You didn’t know.”

    Kate leaned forward instinctively as she felt the proffered information carrying a weight to it. “Know what?”

    Tamar waved a hand at herself and the two Widows in the front.

    “When there was a hit on your head for months, Yelena sent word that no Widow was to take the contract. Shame too considering the price point.”

    Kate’s heart squeezed like it was being cradled a little too tightly by hands that couldn’t understand gentleness. She placed the overwhelming emotions it caused aside for later rumination.

    “That’s. . . wow. I guess I’m deeply grateful. I wouldn’t have survived a Widow.” Tamar nodded solemnly.

    “No you would for sure be dead now.”

    Kate let out a startled laugh and shrugged one shoulder.

    “Can’t argue with that.” She studied Tamar like the other woman had been studying her. “Would you have taken the contract?”

    Tamar seemed a bit startled by her words and Kate wondered at the difference between her and Yelena. Tamar didn’t seem as locked in; like she wasn’t interested in concealing her reactions to things even if she was capable of it. She exuded a certain freedom. “I do not think so. Abayomi says that we shouldn’t kill good people if we’re going to continue to kill.”

    It was Kate’s turn to be surprised.

    “How do you know I am good?”

    Tamar grinned.

    “You like, radiate golden retriever energy. And I heard you saved a dog once.” Kate let out a startled laugh.

    “One, I like that you even know that comparison. Two, why is that every Widow’s qualifier for goodness.”

    Tamar tapped her fingers against the side of her thigh as she answered, eyes still on Kate. “Because animals were always good to us even when people were not.”

    Kate saw the Widow’s desire to move past the comment and she quickly changed subjects. “So where are you off to after this?”

    Tamar shrugged.

    “Not sure. I’m still trying to find out what suits me. I planned on going to New York to see if the city is for me. I like to be around a lot of people.”

    Kate wasn’t sure what drove her to offer but the words were out before she could rethink them.

    “You’re welcome to stay in my place while I’m stuck at the safehouse.” Tamar seemed pleasantly surprised by the offer but she quickly recovered, her head tilting as she toolkit in and what it added to her internal dossier of information about Kate. She folded her hands in front of her.

    “See? You are a good person. If you’re sure, I’ll take you up on that. At least until I decide where I want to stay. I like to give myself six months somewhere before deciding if it’s going to fit.”

    Kate’s curiosity got the better of her.

    “Of course. What other places have you tried?”

    Tamar lit up at that and for a moment she truly looked like she was Kate’s age. For the remainder of the trip, Tamar talked. She shared about her stay in California with its beach cities and towns and the people that couldn’t imagine living anywhere else. She mentioned Colorado and how informal its inhabitants were; stating that one time she’d gone to a business casual charity event– for a job– and almost everyone had worn shorts. Kate listened in fascination as Tamar walked her through every recalled experience with strong opinions and humor. She found herself a little enamored by the way Tamar seemed to fall into story-telling with so much skill. Oregon, Washington, Philadelphia, Florida, and the Carolinas were all touched on at some point.

    Kate realized all of the destinations were in the US and questioned Tamar on it. The only response she got was a cheeky, “I have my whole life for this.” There was so much meaning behind the words and Kate found her mind immediately back on Yelena. She wanted her to feel like Tamar; like she had her whole life to heal and experience the world freely. The knowing smiles Tamar had been offering Kate early in the trip had vanished into something more genuine and excited. Kate grew comfortable enough to ask questions without a debate on the likelihood for her questions to cause irritation. It was nice to not have to think so carefully about her words. Yet, any time she thought about why it felt so relieving she thought about Yelena and she somehow found herself missing her.

    By the time they landed, Kate and Tamar had exchanged the numbers of their current burners and had ended up tossing a small sandbag from the storage back and forth with increasing strength until it eventually split open against Tamar’s hand.

    Kate unbuckled and grabbed her gear feeling lighter and more at ease around a Widow than she ever had before. It didn’t hold a candle to the things Kate felt with Yelena but it was sincerely nice to find connection without the barriers of distrust and fear. Her mind was swirling with suggestions to offer Tamar regarding her upcoming exploration of New York as she descended the ramp onto the lawn of Yelena’s safehouse; the ship around her still invisible. All thoughts left her mind entirely as she took in the figure standing near the bottom.

    Kate slowed to a stop for a moment, her heart thudding in her chest the way it always tended to do when Yelena was near.

    Yelena was several yards from the ship, her body language stiff and her expression blank. She had on a pale green and yellow colorblock pullover that made her look softer than she had any right to look. Kate melted a bit as she took in the too-long sleeves that hung over the backs of Yelena’s hands and she tried not to look as relieved that Yelena was there to greet them. A part of her had expected Yelena to avoid her for a full week before reappearing when Kate annoyed her hard enough.

    A hand tapped at her shoulder blade.

    “You’re holding up the line, Kate.”

    Tamar’s amused voice cut through the haze Kate was feeling and she flushed, hurrying down the remainder of the ramp and desperately hopeful that her error hadn’t been noticed. A glance behind her at Tamar crushed those hopes as gleeful eyes met her own. Kate turned away quickly and approached Yelena despite being at a loss for what to say. All her brain could supply her with was the repeated memory of Yelena’s hand on her face, her thumb parting her lips. Kate’s body flushed and she felt the drag of heat toward her belly with enough force that she wasn’t sure she was masking it well.

    Yelena managed to look entirely unbothered as she offered a genuine smile to the two Widows. She embraced Abayomi first, the two sharing words before Yelena grinned at Tamar and tugged her into a hug.

    Kate always enjoyed watching Yelena with the other Widows, the connection that ran amongst them powerful enough to draw water from stone.

    Yelena didn’t meet Kate’s eyes again as she turned toward the large house. “The jet can stay for a day without trouble if you would like to rest. I appreciate the help you offered us in Madrid.”

    Kate fell back a bit as the Widows entered the house and Sonya was the only one to react. She drew to a halt as Kate did, brows raised in question.

    “Kate?”

    Kate offered a smile.

    “I think I’m going to stretch my legs; go on a walk. I’ll be back in a bit.” Sonya didn’t question her but she watched Kate depart before moving inside. Kate wasn’t sure of the exact reason she wasn’t ready to enter the safehouse but she listened to her gut and turned toward the side of the house. She felt the need for fresh air; hopefully enough to drive out the completely inappropriate thoughts that had floated through her mind at the first sight of Yelena.

    Kate almost laughed to herself at the fact that she’d be so certain Yelena would avoid her and yet here she was skipping out. Kate’s stomach growled in hunger and she groaned as she continued forward, leaving the driveway and finding the walking path that circled the entirety of the lake.

    Sonya watched Kate descend and pause in the middle of the landing gear and it didn’t take seeing Yelena to know she was outside. She shook her head and trailed her out, amusement growing when she found Yelena staring right back at Kate.

    Yelena was always so good at masking her emotions and holding herself as if empty but the truth was, if she wasn’t affected, she wouldn’t be so accepting of Kate gawking like a fool. The following moments passed with aching yet endearing awkwardness and Sonya could tell that Tamar was piecing things together. She seemed to have been figuring this pair out even before seeing Kate and Yelena in the same room. A feat to be applauded. Abayomi was kind enough to leave others to their business but Sonya veered to Tamar’s nosiness; especially considering how much Wren was invested in this budding spark. It was a constant topic of conversation, one they laughed over in their sparse calls and stolen minutes. Sonya missed her wife enough that she was likely as eager as Kate to return to her normal life. This was the longest they’d been separated since their marriage and there was a part of Sonya that worried the life she’d built for herself would fade away before she could return. It was an irrational fear, called out as such by Wren any time she mentioned it, but the reassurance was necessary and appreciated.

    Sonya followed Abayomi into the home and felt as much as heard Kate’s anxious departure. She saw Yelena’s head turn toward the door ahead, noted Tamar almost fully turning around, and quickly chose to be the one to intercept.

    “Kate.”

    Kate stilled and the smile she gave to Sonya was sincere if laced with tinges of discomfort and –Sonya would call it arousal if she was a betting woman (she was)– she was excited to share the news with Wren. It meant that something had definitely happened the night before to cause Yelena to hightail it out of the country early and Kate to ignore her bodily need to eat just to get a few more minutes away. She hadn’t consumed anything after waking up and throwing up; quite loudly.

    Sonya watched Kate leave before entering the safehouse.

    She found Yelena frowning at the door even as she spoke to the other two, her gaze meeting Sonya’s in silent question.

    Sonya expertly masked her thoughts and waited to see how this would play out without her interference.

    It didn’t take long to reap the rewards of that decision.

    Tamar stopped talking in the middle of the rundown of her and Abayomis’ encounter with Leviathan. She looked at the doors Kate had failed to enter.

    “Where’s Kate?”

    Yelena and Abayomi turned to Sonya expectantly and she offered a shrug. “Needed to walk out the trip.”

    Tamar frowned, concern lacing her voice.

    “Is she okay?”

    Yelena’s eyes narrowed infinitesimally as she registered the way Tamar spoke of Kate. Sonya wanted to laugh but she maintained her stoicism.

    “I believe so.”

    Tamar looked toward the door again and started to move toward it.

    “I can go check on her.” She paused and looked around. “ She hasn’t eaten anything and it looked like she was hungover this morning.”

    Yelena’s jaw clenched as she all but glared at Tamar now, the expression dissipating before anyone but Sonya could note it. Yelena shook her head.

    “You rest. I will go.”

    Tamar waved her off with a casual hand.

    “Oh I don’t mind. It was an easy flight.”

    “No.” Seeming to recognize the almost sharp bite to the word, Yelena’s tone softened. “No, it is okay. Please make yourselves at home. Sonya can show you both to an open room.” Tamar relented, shrugging, and Sonya watched Yelena journey to the kitchen, moving out of sight.

    Only then did she see a smug grin spread across Tamar’s face, her eyes dancing with mirth as they met Sonya’s. Impressed, Sonya let out a laugh.

    Abayomi looked between them and chuckled.

    “You are both meddlers.”

    Tamar walked over to the leather couch and tossed her bag down.

    “Somebody that stubborn needs to be meddled with.”

    Sonya nodded but refrained from a response as Yelena reappeared with a deeper frown than before, a bag in her hand that looked like it held an entire drawer of the fridge. “Are you going to have a picnic?”

    If Yelena wasn’t so talented Sonya expected her cheeks would have flushed at the comment. Instead, she shook her head.

    “Kate is very picky. I don’t want to give her a reason to complain. It is very annoying.” Yelena walked past them without further discussion or regard to any hosting duties. When the door closed behind her, Sonya offered the other two remaining a necessary bit of knowledge.

    “Two days ago I saw Kate eat an entire box of crackers that had been in the apartment before we arrived. She didn’t realize until she was scrounging for crumbs that they were expired.” Tamar devolved into giggles and Sonya decided to let them interpret the comment as they wished. “Come with me. We can settle you in and start on an actual meal.”

    Kate had made it about ten minutes in silence before she felt a presence trailing her. She didn’t need to turn around to know who it was. She slowed and tilted her head so that she could see her.

    “Yelena.”

    Yelena reached her and unceremoniously shoved a plastic grocery bag in Kate’s hands. “You should eat something.”

    Kate’s eyes widened at the gesture and she felt the anxiety that had been building since their landing begin to ease.

    “I wasn’t planning on camping out here.”

    Yelena didn’t seem embarrassed by the unwarranted amount of food stretching the plastic. “Why did you not come inside?”

    Kate picked through the offerings, eyes dancing over a yogurt cup, a can of cheese pringles, a banana, two apples, and a container of strawberries. Beneath them was a box of crackers and hummus.

    Kate pulled out an apple and resigned herself to carrying a hefty bag for the remainder of her walk with a half-smile.

    “I needed some air.”

    Yelena frowned at her.

    “The flight is very short.”

    Kate’s eyes cut to Yelena in challenge.

    “Why did you leave this morning?”

    Yelena huffed.

    “I had things to take care of.”

    They walked in silence for some time, Kate crunching down on the apple and almost moaning at the ability to ingest food without feeling ill.

    Yelena kept pace with ease, her stride feeling just as long as Kate’s despite their differing heights.

    Kate subtly sped up her stride, amused as Yelena was forced to do the same. For several minutes, banana peeled next and consumed, Kate incrementally increased her pace. When Yelena was about become visibly rushed, she pulled to an abrupt halt. Kate followed suit, offering an innocent tilt of her head.

    “Everything okay?”

    Yelena glared at her though there was no heat behind it.

    “You are so annoying.”

    Kate laughed and resumed the walk, her steps normal again.

    “Thank you.” She held up the bag. “And thank you for this.”

    Yelena shrugged.

    “Tamar said you didn’t eat.”

    Kate’s smile softened.

    “I like her.”

    Yelena’s expression cleared entirely and she looked away from Kate.

    “She is okay.”

    Kate bit back a grin and gently bumped her shoulder against Yelena’s.

    “She’s not my favorite Black Widow.”

    Yelena rolled her eyes even as her shoulders relaxed.

    “Obviously. I am the best one.”

    Kate’s answering chuckle brought a smile to Yelena’s face and they carried on for another fifteen minutes before the sun began to set.

    Kate stretched her neck and swung her arms a bit.

    “Alright. We can call it. I should reach out to Clint and tell him the good news.” She paused and added. “My arm is getting tired of carrying this pantry.”

    Yelena’s hand shot out and yanked the bag back, her eyes rolling.

    “Give it to me. You are so weak. It is not even that much food.”

    Chapter 12

    See the end of the chapter for more notes Kate’s eyes cracked open at the sound of incessant knocking on her door, her body tucked deeply within her covers. She was so perfectly warm and no part of her wanted to move. She called toward the interruption of her sleep even as she burrowed further, her eyes closing. “I don’t need a clock to tell it’s too early for this!”

    A laugh sounded on the other side of the door and Kate narrowed the culprit down to Tamar. Tamar knocked again.

    “Come spar with me, Kate.”

    Kate’s eyes shot open, a jolt of excitement flooding her while she tried to launch herself out of her tangle of sheets and comforter. She stumbled, knees almost hitting the ground as her legs woke up. She threw open the door to Tamar’s amused smile.

    “Just give me a second to change.”

    Tamar chuckled.

    “I heard you fall to the ground.”

    Kate held up a finger.

    “Almost. I almost fell to the ground.”

    Tamar shook her head and turned away, calling out behind her.

    “I’ll be in the backyard.”

    Kate stripped off her sleep shirt and threw on a pair of shorts, a sports bra, and a t-shirt before running down the steps of the large house. As she hit the landing she pulled up short, almost running directly into Sonya who had shifted her bowl of cereal out of Kate’s way. Sonya raised her brows in question and Kate grinned.

    “I’m sparring with Tamar.”

    Sonya chuckled and pointed to the kitchen.

    “Okay. Eat first.”

    Kate pouted.

    “But I’m not hungry.”

    Sonya rolled her eyes.

    “If you’re going to fight a Black Widow you need energy.” She waved Kate toward the kitchen. “Eat.”

    Yelena’s hair was pulled into a messy bun at the top of her head with strands spilling out and hanging around her neck and face. She had on a large, gray sweatshirt that hung past the tops of her thighs and Kate used all the strength in her body to keep her eyes from Yelena’s legs. Yelena glanced her way and lifted a brow.

    Yelena shrugged, taking another bite from the buttered toast in front of her. “He is old and grumpy.”

    Kate chuckled as she tugged open the fridge in search of a quick snack. She pulled out a perfectly ripened banana and looked for something to supplement it.

    “He isn’t that old nor that grumpy. And he lives on a farm.”

Kate ached. Yelena had done something to her; her words from the night Spain running through Kate’s mind, the ghost of her fingers against her mouth, on her skin.

Recognition passed between them, heavy and heated, and Kate kept her jaw tight so that the blazing need she was feeling didn’t pass through her lips like a whimper.

    “You’re grumpy but you don’t sleep in.”

    Yelena watched her with the same eyes as before, curious and dark and Kate wished she would look away if only to ease the staccato pounding of her heart.

    Yelena relented as Kate reached her breaking point. She took a bite of the toast in her hand. “But I am not old. You are not very good at putting two two together.” Kate’s chest warmed and she was certain she’d never tire of Yelena missing words and speaking with imperium anyway. Kate’s eyes fell to the plate that was slowly dwindling and back at Yelena, catching her eyes as they studied Kate carefully. She seemed to recognize Kate’s intentions before she even took a step, her hand settling next her meal, her lips curling up.

But Yelena hadn’t retreated, hadn’t stiffened at Kate’s attention, and Kate wasn’t certain she would be able to remove herself from Yelena’s orbit unless she was thrown.

She leaned down, lips so close to Yelena’s ear that she could tell the Widow was holding her breath. Kate felt a surge of power, her fingers at Yelena’s back pressing into the skin, unable to restrain her response to Yelena’s acquiescence.

    “It’s two and two.”

It wouldn’t do for Tamar to see her so debased, especially when Yelena hadn’t really done anything. She frowned, a flutter of uncertainty passing through her as she tried to parse apart their interaction. Maybe Kate was reading into things.

She walked over, hoping she looked normal enough.

    “Yeah, she was pretty adamant.”

    That’s right, Kate. Think about sexy things like physical therapy and getting shot in the back. That’ll get you in a better mindset.

Her confidence was serene, exuding from her like some kind of physical aura that could be touched and seen. Kate knew she was outmatched; with any Widow it was a given. But she could also see a difference between Tamar and the older Widows. Where they seemed to move like water, fluid and graceful to a point of absurdity that was hard to track or predict, Tamar was slower; deliberate.

She moved back in, closer, sweat beading at the crown of her head and readying its descent across her cheeks and temple.

It was often the path she took, liking the ability to take opponents off-guard and slam them to the ground with brute strength, but that approach did not work against a Widow. Tamar moved again– so fast that Kate barely tracked it, incapable of predicting it– swinging her feet up, hand on the ground as she kicked Kate’s guard out of place. Tamar’s arm purposefully collapsed under her and she spun, her leg a pendulum aimed for Kate’s head. It was all Kate could do to get her forearm in the way, the force slamming her own limb into her head and send her sideways. She grunted as she stumbled, feeling Tamar’s pursuit behind her this time. Kate feigned a couple extra steps of disorientation and dropped low, kicking out behind her toward Tamar’s legs. She twisted mid-attack and saw Tamar use her knee to block it. Kate swore and rolled to the side as Tamar brought a hammer strike down toward her head. She lurched into a standing position and backed away, trying to gain her bearings. Tamar allowed it and Kate felt a blossoming of frustration in her chest, her cheeks heating beyond her exertion. She fought back the urge to barrel in, the same internal voice from earlier demanding that she maintain control. Kate followed Tamar, mind buzzing with adrenaline and muscles vibrating with it.

    The dangerous thing about Widows, Kate was realizing, was their ability to fight from every position. They were just as dangerous on the ground as they were at their full height. They were just as lethal fighting close as they were at a distance. You couldn’t predict them because they had every option open to them and they were so much better at it than anyone else.

Kate sustained her footwork, body moving exactly as it should as she blocked a hook, knocked away a cross, and ducked under a rather nasty jab… right into Tamar’s driving knee.     Tamar laughed then.

    “Fine.”

She knew Tamar would have something waiting for her when her feet touched the ground– or before– and she acted without forethought, her arms reaching out and latching around Tamar’s neck as one of her legs hooked on her hip with the intention to throw her.

The impact knocked the wind out of her and bounced her head once on the dirt. She rolled to a stop and groaned in pain, her face pressed into the grass and her breath whistling in like a crimped hose. Sweat trickled into her eyes and she blinked rapidly to clear them, carefully using a hand to push herself onto her back.

    Bright sunlight pierced her eyes and she closed them, fighting the horrendous feeling of suffocation until it began to dissipate. Her chest rose and fell, lungs slowly filling with air until she was panting out ragged breaths. She heard approaching feet and the heat from the sun lessoned as a shadow covered her.

    “That will work with practice. You need a better grip on my neck. Your hand slipped off and it took your leverage with it.”

    “Вы так очевидны в своей зависти, Елена. Она заметит.”

    “Я не знаю, что вы имеете в виду.”

    Abayomi laughed and pulled Yelena in a hug even as Yelena tried to resist, her brow still furrowed.

    “Лжец. Я люблю тебя, сестра.”

She loved seeing Yelena let them. Kate’s mind carried back to the kitchen and she flushed, glad her body was already red with exertion. Yelena let her close as well, but it was nothing as soft as this.

What happened in the kitchen hadn’t been on her list of guesses, at least not so soon. Yelena pulled back and squeezed Abayomi’s forearm.

    Abayomi subtly shook her head and gave Kate a smile.

    “Let me know if you’re ever in Los Angeles. My family would love to have you.” Kate nodded, part of her mind still stuck on Abayomi’s first words. She continued to contemplate them as Sonya came to say her farewells and was only pulled from her thoughts when Tamar exited the safehouse with her bag in hand.

    Kate allowed the four Widows to speak amongst each other, comfortable watching until she was able to say her own goodbyes. Her heart ached as she watched them, the quiet comfort that radiated from them as they spoke to one another and grasped elbows and arms and shoulders.

    “I’m excited to abuse your fancy, rich-kid digs.”

    Kate took Tamar’s proffered hand and felt herself yanked into a hug.

    “Hey, the digs are adult-certified. I paid for them myself.”

    Tamar chuckled, their hands still clasped between them. She squeezed once. “Anything I need to look out for? Rotting take-out? Weird sex room?”

    Kate rolled her eyes.

    “The weird sex room is locked so I wouldn’t worry about it. And I had somebody go and clean the space once I realized I couldn’t go back.”

    Tamar’s brows raised as their hands dropped.

    “You let some random person into your home when you had a bounty on your head?” “It was facilitated by SWORD. I think it was safe.” Kate nodded toward where Abayomi was waiting by the cloaked Quinjet. “You better get a move on before she drags you there. I should be back in a few days if everything works out.”

    Tamar peaked behind herself at Abayomi and hummed. She slapped Kate’s arm a little harder than necessary and held up two fingers.

    “Looking forward to it, Kate. Have fun with the less-fun Widows.”

    Tamar bounced on her toes as she made her way to Abayomi before both disappeared into the aircraft.
