She felt an urge to keep Yelena there, worried if she let her go the Widow would revert to her past behavior. “I only got in like thirty minutes.”

    Yelena hummed, body turning to begin walking around Kate.

    “That is not a lot of practice.”

    Kate heard a door close and her eyes flicked toward the safehouse, noting Sonya’s departure. When she looked back to Yelena she saw the emotions roiling behind her eyes, dancing in and out of visibility as Yelena struggled to control them.

    Kate was thrilled that she felt suddenly more capable of reading Yelena. It was likely gradual, built from time spent and eager efforts, but it was like Yelena had handed her a key in Spain, a way to begin to understand her even she hadn’t truly wanted Kate to do so. Still, she didn’t know what Yelena was hiding, didn’t know what had gone through her mind in the kitchen or during her sparring with Tamar.

    “I found a way around your stupid leg swing.”

    Yelena’s lips curled in dismissal, kicking up something unpleasant in Kate’s chest. “It would not work on me. Tamar is inexperienced.”

    Kate settled back into a loose interpretation of her fighting stance, her body prepared for a surprise strike.

    “If she is inexperienced then I have the skills of a toddler.”

    Yelena shrugged.

    “You said it.”

    Irritation flared bright in Kate’s chest. Yelena looked unrepentant for her dig but Kate searched beyond the indifference toward whatever was putting the Widow in such a foul mood. She jabbed back.

    “She was a better teacher than you.”

    Kate wanted to smirk as the barb landed, resonating in Yelena’s body as the she stilled. Yelena’s eyes narrowed.

    “So like a child you want to be handheld into your power?”

    Kate grit her teeth.

    “Is that what I said?”

    A breeze across her back distracted Kate and she glanced to where the Quinjet should be. She waved once, offering a tight smile she wasn’t sure the Widows would see, and turned her face back as knuckles wrapped lightly across her jaw. The strength behind it was minimal, barely there, and Kate forced her feet to root into the ground as she glared at Yelena’s now- smug face.

    Yelena’s hand, the one used to feign her strike, moved to pat Kate condescendingly on the cheek.

    “See. I will pull my punches too. Maybe you will learn more when you are not tested, hmm?” Kate slapped Yelena’s hand out of her face.

    “You’re being a dick.”

    Yelena hardened, her voice veering toward caustic.

    “Do something about it, Kate Bishop.”

    Kate knew she was being goaded, knew that any attempt she could make to get the upper hand on Yelena would be punished. But her slow, simmering anger that seemed to come out to play whenever Yelena was in one of her moods had reached a capacity. It seems her fear that Yelena would regress was deeply founded and already happening.

    Kate frowned. Not if she could help it. She fully dropped into stance, mind replaying the way Yelena had held her against the island counter. Yelena hadn’t looked like this, hadn’t looked like she wanted to dig in and scrape against Kate’s nerves.

    Yelena wasn’t just hiding something. She was protecting it; herself maybe. She had wrapped her anger around whatever it was to shield it but Kate could almost reach in and touch it. She dropped out of her stance.

    Yelena noticed Kate’s change of demeanor and narrowed her eyes.

    Kate sighed.

    “Yelena, what’s wrong?”

    Yelena’s body tensed, taut as a bowstring.

    “Are we going to fight or talk?”

    Kate’s eyes never left Yelena’s. She would allow Yelena to run when she needed to, take her space and her time, but she wasn’t going to let Yelena spin out on her own. Not after realizing the toll it took on the Widow.

    “I’m not going to spar with you when you’re being like this.”

    Yelena’s lips pressed together in a thin line and she glared at Kate.

    “You have not had a problem before.”

    Kate took a step toward Yelena, slow and steady so as not to startle her. “You have to meet me in the middle, sometimes, Yelena. I can’t just be a punching bag until you feel safe enough to be vulnerable.”

    Kate saw panic spark behind Yelena’s eyes as she approached and her heart squeezed. Yelena stiffened and nodded her head.

    “Fine. If you do not want to spar this is a waste of time.”

    Her accent wrapped thickly around the words, whatever Yelena hid pressing at her teeth for escape. She turned to leave and Kate reacted, moving to attack knowing Yelena would block her. She drove her fist toward the back of Yelena ribs, fighting a smile when Yelena spun and knocked her back.

    Kate danced out of the way and circled.

    “Tell me why you’re upset.”

    Yelena growled and lunged, her attacks so precise and quick that Kate found herself continually backing away, grimacing as she managed to only block a few. Yelena drove her foot into Kate’s stomach and sent her sprawling onto her back. “I am not upset.”

    Kate braced herself, knees driving up as Yelena moved to jump on her. She kicked out, making enough space to roll to her feet.

    “Yelena.”

    Yelena ignored her, instead layering multiple blows to Kate’s torso with a final guard break and full hook to Kate’s jaw.

    Kate reeled backward, her already aching jaw protesting as she clenched it. She tasted iron in her teeth and licked the blood from her split lip.

    Yelena wasted no time following up with further strikes, the power behind them disorienting, impacting the hard lines of Kate’s abdomen.

    Kate grimaced through the attacks, her body seemingly more used to blows after her adventures with the Widows. It was relentless and Kate knew she needed to take some semblance of control.

    She waited for the right moment, catching strikes with extreme focus and getting Yelena into a rhythm. Then, as an uppercut drove into her side, she ate the strike, opening herself up as the hard blow cracked into her ribs.

    Kate groaned through the pain at her side as she grabbed the front of Yelena’s shirt and yanked, tugging the Widow into her body and wrapping her arms around her to throw them both to the ground.

    Kate had barely landed before Yelena was on her, slamming her hands into the dirt, her knees on either side of Kate’s hips.

    Yelena practically snarled.

    “Sacrificing your body for something that might not work is a fast way to get yourself killed. I have told you not to do this many times.”

    Kate attempted to dislodge Yelena, her hips rising futilely and her hands tugging against a steely grip.

    “It almost worked.”

    Yelena was breathing heavily, more so than Kate would expect after their short tussle. “Kate.”

    Kate couldn’t decipher whether the way her name left Yelena’s lips was a command or a plea but she could see Yelena cracking at the seams.

    “Let my hands go, please.”

    Yelena disregarded her request, seemingly frozen above her, her eyes unfocused and staring at Kate’s forehead.

    Kate pulled gently at her right hand, repeating her request and relaxing slightly as Yelena’s grip on her loosened. She carefully reached up, the tips of her fingers sliding strands of hair out of Yelena’s eyes.

    “Can you talk to me?”

    Yelena didn’t seem to have heard, her breath still quick and sharp.

    Concern flooded Kate and she wrapped her free hand around the back of Yelena’s neck to get her attention, her fingers pressing into the muscles there.

    “Yelena.”

    Yelena blinked back into reality and the mask she wore for a face slid into place and cracked into pieces.

    Kate’s heart broke. There was so much pain, so much fear, so much weight carried in the bright hazel and she wished nothing more than to take some of it onto herself. She wiggled her trapped hand.

    “Let me go?”

    Yelena yanked back her hand as if burned.

    Kate didn’t let her get far, her newly freed hand grasping Yelena’s shoulder to keep her from running as the other one remained at the back of her neck.

    “Wait. Just wait for a second.”

    Yelena swallowed heavily but said nothing, eyes averted.

    Kate tilted her head to find them and waited to speak until Yelena was looking at her. “It’s okay to let other people help you, Yelena.”

    Yelena tensed, on the verge of fleeing.

    “I do not need help.”

    Kate sighed, softening her grip and moving her hand from shoulder to jaw, rubbing softly into the muscle until Yelena unclenched it.

    “You were okay this morning. Can you tell me what changed?”

    Yelena subtly leaned into the press of Kate’s fingers as she struggled with herself. She looked to the side.

    “I had many thoughts.”

    Kate bit back a smile at the lack of information that came with Yelena’s efforts. “What kind of thoughts?”

    Her voice was low, quiet.

    Yelena frowned.

    “I do not know what I will do when this is over.”

    The words carried so much, so many questions and Kate heard them clearly. She couldn’t stop the way her thumb brushed along Yelena’s jaw, the skin under her fingers so soft and warm that she craved more.

    “You can be whatever you want to be.”

    Yelena frowned, brows furrowing in disagreement.

    “That is not true.”

    Kate huffed out a laugh at the petulance in Yelena’s voice.

    “You’re literally the most talented person I know. You will be good at whatever you try.” Yelena seemed to struggle through barriers to speak again, her accent heavy. “I will be alone.”

    Kate melted. She well and truly turned into a puddle in the grass, Yelena’s warm legs bracketing her own. She knew the answer to the question before she asked it. “Do you want to be alone?”

    Yelena offered the subtlest of head shakes.

    Kate almost whimpered at the sight but she refrained. She slid her fingers along Yelena’s brow, smoothing away the concern there.

    “Do you want to come back to New York with me?”

    Yelena took a deep breath, a flinch in her expression as she retreated slightly. Her words were hard, layered underneath with something softer.

    “You will already have one Widow on your hands.”

    Kate wanted to laugh.

    “Is this jealous Yelena that I’m seeing right now?”

    Yelena’s fractured mask began to draw back together.

    “Do not be stupid, Kate Bishop.”

    Kate couldn’t fight the grin that took over her face.

    “I already told you that you’re my favorite.”

    Yelena started to pull back, using her legs to try to stand.

    Kate’s hands fell but she quickly settled them on Yelena’s waist and pulled, tugging the Widow back down.

    Yelena’s eyes darkened, her hips slotting against Kate’s.

    “Would you prefer to stay like this or can I stand up now?”

    Kate shrugged, the movement awkward on the grass.

    “I’m okay with this. At least until you give me an answer.”

    Yelena hummed quietly, her weight distributing back against Kate.

    “You are very demanding.” She plucked at the loose material of Kate’s shirt and frowned. “I might get bored.”

    Kate didn’t remove her hands even after Yelena settled. She lightly pressed her thumbs into the muscles at Yelena’s hips.

    “I doubt it.”

    Yelena’s lips parted, a quiet intake that Kate would label as a gasp if she had the confidence for it.

    “Fine. I will come with you. For a small time. Tamar can find her own Hawkeye.” Kate laughed at that, her smile widening and her head pressing back against the ground. “Yelena, there’s enough space for both of you.”

    Yelena frowned and Kate had a fleeting thought that she had never been so endeared to another person.

    “We’ll see.”

    Kate smiled and forcefully pried her hands off of Yelena so the Widow could stand. To her surprise, Yelena didn’t move right away. She looked down at Kate, gaze searching, and Kate’s hands curled into fists in the grass.

    “Have you decided you like it here?”

    The innuendo was clear enough though it was meant to be a catalyzing word to get Yelena off of her. Kate was beginning to overheat, the press of Yelena against her too much. Yelena, to her surprise, offered a quiet hum.

    “Yes.”

    The news came three days later: Kate Bishop could go home. She was no longer a wanted woman. It almost felt too good to be true, the majority of her past year spent in hiding and on the run from people pretty set on her death.

    Yelena delivered the news with her own relief apparent in her eyes and Kate had been tempted to drag her into a hug. She’d refrained if only out of fear that any misstep would change Yelena’s mind about returning with her. She felt as if she was on the precipice of something huge; all of it dependent on whether or not she could keep Yelena in her life for just a bit longer.

    The days after the rather intense encounters they’d shared were easy in some respects. Yelena had grudgingly shared her plan with Sonya and Sonya had been excited but careful, seeming to understand the delicate nature of Yelena’s choice.

    Kate had quickly discovered that Sonya and her wife, Wren, lived about fifteen miles from her and Kate did not contain her excitement at the possibilities of their proximity. They packed their bags, Yelena with so many that Kate wondered whether or not the Widow had a genuine addiction, and loaded up two of the vehicles for the trip. As Kate climbed into the passenger seat, Yelena beside her, she felt at peace for the first time since she put on the Ronin suit.

    “You had us fooled for some time, Contessa.”

    The thick leather on her wrists chafed her skin, beads of blood coagulating as wounds scabbed and opened over the span of too many days.

    She ignored the taunting; it had been the same thing since her imprisonment. Playing to her intelligence, needling at her most recent mistake, crashing a baton across her body for information. She spat blood on the ground and closed her eyes, plans half-forming and dissipating as she realized they would never work.

    Too many guards, too many eyes on her.

    She sighed.

    Death would come soon. How disappointing.

    Chapter 13

    Chapter Notes Trigger Warning: Mentions of suicide and suicide ideation.

    Our girls get a little break from the chaos and the running. I played with the structure of this chapter a bit so hopefully it makes sense.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes “No.”

    Kate pouted. Full, turned down lips and imploring eyes. She almost went so far as to clasp her hands but she thought overdoing it might have the opposite effect on Yelena. “Please? Come on, I thought you guys buried the hatchet? Or the fists. Or whatever you were fighting with.”

    Yelena was strutting around Kate’s apartment like she belonged there, as was her way, and Tamar watched gleefully from the safety of the living room with her head craned. This was the third time Kate had brought this up– fourth if she counted the first attempt where Yelena had hardly let her get a word out before she was collecting far too many reusable bags from Kate’s kitchen and disappearing for groceries.

    She’d been gone for three hours and had returned with a bag of potatoes and four new shirts that she decidedly did not let Kate see.

    The second time Kate had managed to corner Yelena in the living room, practically standing over her so the Widow had nowhere to run. It had done nothing. One hint of the avoided subject and Yelena had used an ungodly amount of abdominal muscles to flip herself over the back of the couch, her knees almost colliding with Kate’s face in the process. The third attempt was somewhat effective if only because Yelena was in the process of teaching Kate how to properly incapacitate somebody.

    ”Can I try it?”

    Yelena frowned.

    “What do you think we are doing?”

    Kate rolled her eyes, skin dewy from over an hour of physical therapy and the additional exercises Yelena had decided she needed to teach her immediately. It had been two days since Kate’s last attempt and she’d instilled the Widow with a false sense of security. “No, like, can I try it on you? I feel like I’m not understanding the way my arm is supposed to lock in place.” Kate feigned idiocy. “Do I latch onto my own wrist or–” Yelena’s eyes widened.

    “That is what I have told you not to do.”

    Kate offered a helpless shrug and she did a stellar job of masking her victorious internal jig as Yelena sighed and relented.

    Yelena held out her hands.

    “Come here, Kate Bishop.”

    And if that hadn’t almost derailed Kate’s entire mission she would have focused a little more on the commanding tone of Yelena’s voice and the way it stirred her blood in her veins. Heart beating a bit too fast, breath shallow, Kate offered her hands to Yelena and felt the dichotomically soft and calloused palms slide against her own.

    Yelena turned into Kate’s arms, pulling one around her neck.

    “This arm goes here, use my shoulder if you need to find leverage to get into position until your other arm is in place.” She set Kate’s hand on her shoulder and reached for the other, drawing it up so that Kate’s elbow was beside her ear. “This is your strongest point. You are creating a very dangerous connection the moment you lock the choking arm onto your bicep. Not your wrist.”

    Kate nodded, her nose against the back of Yelena’s head, tickled by the damp strands of hair pulled up into a ponytail. She closed her eyes, listening; definitely listening. She could feel Yelena’s heartbeat through the warmth of her back and her ass was snug against Kate’s hips. “Got it. Not the wrist.”

    Yelena stilled.

    “Are you listening to me, Kate?”

    Kate hummed, her choking forearm light against Yelena’s throat and her fulcrum arm loose on her shoulder.

    “Yes. I’m just waiting for you to tell me when to go.”

    Yelena’s hands on Kate’s wrists tightened slightly.

    “You do like that, don’t you; me telling you what to do?”

    “No.”

    “Please? Come on, I thought you guys buried the hatchet? Or the fists. Or whatever you were fighting with.”

Maybe a bit harder than a touch, but Kate had noticed that Yelena seemed to struggle with the same lack of self- control when it came to keeping her hands to herself. Her touches had a tendency to be a bit more aggressive but they still managed to throw Kate off balance.

    “Yelenaaaaa.” If her voice pitched into a whine they both ignored it. “It’s just for a couple days. Laura wants to meet you too. And the kids.”

    Kate didn’t mention the rest of Laura’s words, the ones that had to do with Natasha and how much she’d meant to the Bartons. She didn’t want to manipulate Yelena into her agreement; not like that.

    “At least be useful and flip the bacon? You are in the way.”

    Kate looked around for a moment before grabbing a nearby fork– she had a whole set– and tried gingerly to turn each of the pieces in the pan. She managed two before the fork was removed from her hand and replaced with tongs.

    Yelena didn’t even have to express her condescension, it permeated the entire kitchen around them.

    Kate finished flipping the bacon and set the tongs haphazardly on the side. She turned to find Yelena chopping an onion.

    “Yelena.”

    “I am very busy, Kate Bishop.”

    Kate threw up her hands.

    “The book is literally called Hospitality Under the Influence. You don’t even know if it’s good. I don’t even know where that book came from!”

    Yelena shrugged.

    “Bacon is good. Potatoes and onions are good. What can be bad?”

    Kate slumped against the counter and watched Yelena flit across the kitchen, never idle and definitely avoiding Kate’s gaze.

    She looked at ease, perfectly so, but Kate knew the reason that Yelena was being so difficult was because she was uncomfortable. Maybe even a little scared.

    Kate sighed, her voice coming out softer.

    “Please, Yelena? I promise if it’s miserable I’ll pay for you to stay somewhere else for the duration.”

    Yelena moved a little stiffer, her shoulders tight and her head down toward the measuring she was completing.

    “I can pay for my own accommodations.”

    Hope tried to pry itself out of Kate’s chest and she attempted to remain unaffected. “So you’ll try?”

    Yelena was silent for a few moments but her hands had stilled. She relented. “Yes, Kate Bishop. On one condition.”

    Kate’s grin was megawatt and a smidge smug.

    “Anything.”

    Yelena turned, arms crossing over her body.

    “You will practice with a gun.” She held a hand up at Kate’s coming protest. “No, Kate. We do not live in a world of tights and steel. People have guns and your bow cannot be your only option.”

    Kate huffed and looked to Tamar for help, the Widow still watching silently from the living room. Tamar looked like she was about to proffer easy agreement to Yelena and Kate turned away.

    “Fine. They were called hose, just so you know.”

    Yelena stared blankly at her and Kate rolled her eyes.

    “You called them tights. They’re hose. I talk to a lot of larpers.”

    Yelena reached out and patted her arm.

    “Okay.”

    Kate frowned, her eyes narrowing as she turned to leave.

    “Oh. The Bartons will be here in two days. Just a head’s up.”

    She bit back a grin at the emotions she could feel rolling off of Yelena at her back.

    Kate stared at herself in her mirror, the space itself almost palatial enough that she would hear an echo if she spoke too loudly. Eucalyptus hung from the showerhead and the windows that lined the room had sills large enough to set plants. Still, she wished she could make it cozier, could somehow find a middle ground between the infinitesimal bathroom in Spain and her own here.

    She’d bought the place in a hurry after realizing the target on her back wouldn’t be going away; after the sixth attack at her old, slightly dingy, lovely little apartment over the pizza shop. It was a matter of practicality, of safety, but she’d still attempted to procure a space that she could make warm. She’d made some headway, done a decent job of it before her untimely stabbing and subsequent departure. Soft, whiskey leather furniture, cedar fixtures, and pale green accents bathed much of the first floor. The colors were wrapped in the exposed brick that had been a non-negotiable in her move.

    The living room and kitchen were her first projects and unfortunately the only parts of the penthouse touched by decor. Her room, in addition to the other two bedrooms– she had planned to make one her office but she wasn’t going to mention that to the Widows currently occupying them– were all quite bare. They had potential to be certain; her own master bedroom was spacious and bright, floor to ceiling windows with a connecting balcony she could waste her days on.

    For now, all she had to show for a personal touch was her bed and the multiple plants that lined the room. It had been a dreary sight on their arrival; every single living thing that had been in her home was a melange of cracked soil, withered foliage, and fallen petals. It wasn’t as if Kate was a green thumb, far from it, but she’d had more money than she knew what to do with and hiring caretakers for the things that weren’t Lucky had been far too easy. It was embarrassing to consider doing again with two incredibly competent and captious Black Widows living under the same roof.

Tamar had feigned surprise– Kate definitely mentioned Yelena’s impending arrival– and leveled Yelena with a look that spoke volumes. Kate had been worried at first. Yelena had seemed to prevaricate as soon as they’d dropped Sonya at her home with a promise to follow up after she’d properly greeted and ‘apologized’ to her wife. Kate had let Yelena stew in her thoughts, fearful that a wrong word might change the Widow’s mind. It had become a desperate need, rather than a hope, for Yelena to stay with her, to give her more time. She craved nothing more and the idea of losing it before she even really had it was enough to subdue her into silence beside the Widow. She hadn’t expected Tamar’s teasing to lighten the mood and yet Yelena had almost looked relieved, her shoulders softer than they had been in hours. Kate would have been offended if she wasn’t so happy to see Yelena settle. Instead, she did something she was starting to get far better about: she stepped away.

    She’d made her way to her room and set down her things in the middle of the floor, the harwoods warm against the socked soles of her feet. She’d spared it a cursory glance and moved to the only other room on this level, a bit down and across from her own. It was entirely empty besides a bookcase that hadn’t fit into her plans for her own room. It stood vacant against the brick. Like the other bedrooms, large windows took up much of the exterior wall. It was large and she’d figured it would be comfortable for Yelena for however long she stayed.

    The first week of her return home had been surreal. She’d expected both Tamar and Yelena to drag her through the city in their efforts to see everything. After all, Yelena had expressed her interest in seeing the sights when she’d broken into Kate’s old apartment for the first time with her indelible charm and dangerous promises.

    Yet, to her utter perplexity, they had spent the time shopping. So much shopping. And it wasn’t for clothes as Kate had come to expect. Yelena wouldn’t stop purchasing things for Kate’s apartment. Tamar seemed to tag along for the fun of the adventures through the city and the joy she felt at watching Kate chase after an excitable blonde assassin. Yelena had found Kate throwing away all of her dead plants their first morning back and she promptly picked her keys up from the counter and all but ordered Kate into the car. She’d taken them to a plant store. Yelena didn’t have a bed but she’d taken them to a plant store to purchase an ungodly amount of living green creatures that Kate knew in her heart of hearts she was going to murder. It would be negligence, nothing malicious, but those plants were going to die and she wasn’t sure what Yelena would do once it happened. Buy her even more plants?

They’d loaded up the car to the brim– Kate had sat with four plants pressed into her torso and legs, thick and tickling leaves poking her face and chest– and Yelena had spent three hours wandering around the apartment moving plants around.

Usually that something led to less calm outcomes, but this task had set Yelena on a level of relaxed that Kate had only seen glimpses of in the past. Tamar had grown bored of the wandering, of the few questions posed by Yelena when she thought one of them might have a better angle from where they were standing. She’d called that she was meeting a friend and had left Kate and Yelena standing in the middle of the living room with a collection of baby succulents that Yelena was trying to line up on the coffee table.

A Fiddle Leaf Fig—“It makes the air cleaner.”— was placed next to Kate’s living room couch– for now– and an Ivy plant was set by the banister that led up the staircase– “Kate, wait until it grows.” Kate had only managed a few trips up and down her steps– any extra plants shoved onto Kate’s balcony– before she asked a question that had been on her mind from the moment Yelena started to mumble facts at her.

    ”How do you know so much about plants?”

    Yelena gently set a full-fledged cactus against her own bedroom wall and smiled at it like a proud mother.

    “I used the Google search last night.”

    Kate raised her brows, lips curling into a soft grin.

    “Yeah? What’s the best thing you learned?”

    Yelena looked at her then, a guarded expression on her face for a few beats until she was able to confirm Kate’s sincere curiosity.

    “You can make many plants from one plant.”

    Kate shoved her hands in her pockets, realizing she’d followed Yelena into her room without much thought or question.

    “How?”

    Yelena moved then, aiming for a collection of unhomed plants in the corner. The crinkle of a bag sounded and soon Kate was watching Yelena pull out four separate books. Yelena shuffled through them and grabbed the third, holding it out toward Kate. “So this book will tell me but it is called propagating. Very cooll.”

    “Okay. Sounds good, Kate Bishop.”

    In the days after Plantgate, Kate couldn’t keep her eyes from Yelena for long. They’d gotten the Widow a bed and she’d already filled the closet with so many items of clothing that Kate wondered if Yelena had some shipped in when she wasn’t looking.

    In the midst of the shopping and the arranging and the training and the cooking, there were moments of stillness. Tamar seemed to seek it out; times to read or sit with music. She was easy to be around despite her proclivity toward poking and prodding people. She would disappear for hours at a time, sometimes leaving the apartment altogether. Yelena… Yelena was very, very bad with the stillness. Kate could read it in every line of her body when the Widow hadn’t had enough mental or physical stimulation.

Kate had tried to help, uncertain whether she should point it out or just provide distraction. Out of fear her help might be unwanted, she’d gone with the latter option. One that just happened to coincide with her already in-motion invitation for the Bartons to come visit. So she had started her attempts to let Yelena know and to convince her to stick around for it. That was accomplished now.

    Kate grinned.

    “You are joking with me.”

    “They sewed it into my thigh.”

    “You are just now telling me?”

    Kate flushed.

    “I didn’t want you to find it!”

    “I was so sore and fucked up after Cape Cod that I just needed to take some time.” “And after the time?”

    “You are right. It is. So embarrassing.” Yelena sighed, moving even closer and peering down at the scar. “You need to take it out.”

    Kate nodded, practically on fire and hoping that somehow she might get dragged into one of the many drains in the room and disappear.

    “With minimal mockery, if at all possible.”

    Yelena snorted.

    “No, it is not possible. I will get the medical kit.”

    Kate was starting to rethink her choice of asking for help. It couldn’t be at all healthy to be thinking about Yelena’s hands on her when she was literally holding a disposable scalpel. Not to mention the incessant comments.

    “Kate Bishop, did you think it would give you superpowers like the Iron Man if you sewed this inside you?” “Yelena, Tony didn’t have superpowers.” “How often did you look at your leg and not remember this was here?” “All the time.” “Maybe you are part robot. Try to do the dance.” “I’m not doing the robot, Yelena.”

She had already wrapped several pieces of gauze under Kate’s leg but instead of asking Kate to hold it, she had wrapped her own hand under the back of Kate’s thigh. It was close enough to Kate’s ass that she was ready to squirm after the first ten seconds.

Blood trickled down the sides of her legs to be caught by the gauze and Yelena set the scalpel down to pick up a pair of tweezers.     Yelena nodded, a veiled look of respect in her eyes.

    “Yes. It was a good hiding spot.”

    Yelena huffed.

    “Yes, those were not good.”

    Kate rolled her eyes, walking away and into her bedroom. She was tired, bone-deep and sore, but she could feel Yelena’s gaze on her back as she followed behind. She could feel the weight of an incoming question and waited for it.

    Yelena didn’t make her wait long.

    “Do you want to spar?”

    If Kate had a watch on her arm she would have looked at it pointedly. As it was, she simply turned around and raised her brow.

    “It’s late.”

    Yelena huffed, shrugging a shoulder dismissively.

    “It is not that late.”

    Weakness was becoming a rather consistent trait for Kate when it came to Yelena’s wants. Still, her body was ragged from the days of consistent training and she knew she needed a night to rest. She was standing close enough to Yelena that she saw the briefest flicker of disappointment when the Widow realized she was not going to agree. Her fingers brushed against the hook of Yelena’s elbow, catching it to get her attention and releasing just as quickly to keep from overstepping.

    Yelena’s expression was dubious.

    “What do you mean?”

    Kate grinned.

    “Follow me.”

    Kate glanced over at Yelena as she took in the view of New York from her little spot of heaven on the roof of her luxury apartments. It wasn’t much, not yet, but she’d purchased it with the intention of keeping anyone from throwing parties above her penthouse and for the possibility of what it might become. It had almost been as much as the apartment itself. Greer and Franny had laughed so hard that tears had streamed down their faces and Franny had collapsed to the ground in a heap, clinging to Greer in order to keep from faceplanting. Kate had used the purple shield– obtained through her larping friends– as a target until it had mysteriously disappeared. She suspected one of the two but both denied it vehemently.

    “Suddenly you change your mind at the thought of having to workout.”

    “You brought me here on the prospect of free labor?”

    “Yelena.”

    “What? I am very handy and talented. My skills are in high demand.”

What else was she supposed to do? It was becoming rather clear to her that Yelena had an unknowing grasp on her, one that she’d given up attempting to remove some odd weeks ago.     “Why?”

    Kate only briefly considered lying.

    “Because you can’t be still.”

    “You are very whiny.”

    “I can be.”

    Yelena frowned at her assent.

    “You are bad at thinking things through.”

    “Sometimes you just have to act on instinct.”

    “You are easily frustrated.”

    “I think it’s the people around me.”

    “You are too idealistic.”

    “I don’t think that’s a bad thing.”

    “You are a visual learner.”

    Kate let out a laugh.

    “That’s definitely not a bad thing.”

    “You are not cool”

    Kate grinned.

    “You know that’s not true. My muscles are bigger than yours.”

    Yelena’s mouth dropped open in offense and Kate couldn’t stop the laugh that burst from her chest, unbidden.

    Yelena frowned but she didn’t turn away; she didn’t leave.

    “Kate Bishop.”

    Kate’s eyes found hazel and she spoke softly enough that the words barely carried over the sound of the city below.

    Yelena stilled.

    “I am always breathing.”

    Kate nodded, consenting, but she pushed again.

    “You know that’s not what I mean. You can relax. You don’t need to be moving all the time.” Yelena frowned and looked down toward Kate’s foot pressed against her. She pulled her leg away.

    Kate tilted her head, body entirely relaxed into her chair to be as unthreatening as possible. “It causes you stress when you don’t immediately fix things?”

    “Before, I did not have such thoughts. My mind was filled only with what they wanted. They could not risk another failure so they removed the ability for us to отражать…” She trailed off as she searched for the word in English. “Think deeply.”

    “Reflect?”

    Yelena nodded.

    “We did not think beyond our actions. We did not consider the past or the future. Only what was now.”

    Kate didn’t offer apologies or pity.

    “When you reflect now, what do you think about?”

    Yelena’s expression shuttered slightly, her body still just like it had been that night on the couch in Madrid.

    “Feel like what?”

    Yelena leaned her head back against the chair and looked up at the muggy sky, stars barely visible. She closed her eyes.

    “Неполный.”

    “Incomplete.”

    Kate sucked in a sharp breath through her nose, her eyes burning with tears as she tried to blink them away. The sky above her became blurry and she felt the sting of salt on her skin. She wanted to say something but she knew if she opened her mouth she would probably sob and that was not what Yelena needed. She squeezed her eyes shut.

    “You are very sensitive, Kate Bishop.”

    Kate let out a laugh that veered into a sob anyway and she clenched her teeth together to keep from spewing out any more embarrassing sounds.

    “I’m just tired from all the sparring you’ve made me do.”

    Yelena’s hand shifted, her knuckles pulling the tears from Kate’s other cheek. “See, so whiny. You must have been a terrible protege for the old hawk.” Kate nodded, eyes still closed because she was certain she did not want to see the look on Yelena’s face.

Yelena looked picked apart, the wounds of her past and the fears of her present laid out like offerings for Kate to embrace or dismiss. Yelena’s body went rigid, arms frozen, suspended for several beats until they slowly, tightly, circled Kate’s waist. Her temple rested on the side of Kate’s jaw and her fingers curled into fists in the back of Kate’s t-shirt.

    Kate heard the waver as Yelena pulled in a breath and she held the Widow impossibly closer “I want to sit here and tell you how complete you feel to me but that would be unhelpful. What I can say is that I’m here. For anything you need. If you want to talk. If you don’t want to talk. I’m not going anywhere unless you want me to.”

    Yelena hummed, the vibration of the sound pulsing through Kate’s jaw.

    “You would not go anywhere even if I told you to.”

    Kate laughed.

    “That’s true.”

    Yelena’s grip on Kate loosened and Kate reluctantly took a step back.

    “Come on, Kate Bishop. You are going to sleep.”

    Kate trailed after Yelena, her eyes never once leaving her despite the fact that she knew Yelena could feel it. She wasn’t ready to part ways and the need to keep Yelena close was almost palpable.

    They descended the steps from the roof to the stairwell by Kate’s apartment, blocked off to anyone’s access but her own. Yelena stepped through the front door into a silent, comforting space, Tamar likely asleep as it was well past midnight.

    They bypassed the kitchen, quietly moved up the stairwell, and Yelena paused outside of the training room door. She glanced to where Kate had stopped beside her.

    “I will train.” Kate frowned, prepped to say something, and Yelena continued before she could. “And then I will sleep.”

    Kate struggled with her understanding of Yelena’s need and her own desire for the Widow to rest. She set herself aside.

    “Fine. Not too long?”

    Yelena huffed.

    “You are not the boss of me, Kate Bishop.”

    Kate grinned, eyes glinting in the dark hallway, the only light coming from the moonlight of their partially open rooms. The space around them felt sacred and Kate refrained from speaking the first thoughts that came to her mind. Instead, she reached out once more, unable to help it, and squeezed Yelena’s forearm.

    “Goodnight, Yelena.”

    Kate pushed her broom, gliding through the dirt and trash littered across the rooftop and piling it in a corner where any errant winds wouldn’t be able to undo all their hard work. To her left, Tamar was mimicking the steps of sweeping, her own motions lazy and her face pulled into a grimace.

    Tamar scooped a pathetic pile next to Kate and shot her a look.

    “You are rich, Kate. Unbelievably rich. Why am I cleaning shit on your roof?” Kate rolled her eyes, looking at the amount of work that remained.

    “To be fair, I did expect three of us to be doing this.” She looked at Tamar’s poor efforts. “And I didn’t think you’d be so bad at it.”

    Tamar shrugged, tapping her broom on the ground a few times before fully setting it against the rooftop’s short wall.

    “You are the one that let her go shopping instead of finishing her corner.” Kate sighed and dropped her own broom, her mind running through people she could call to get this finished before Yelena got back.

    “I didn’t let her do anything. She decided that it was not possible for her to wait another moment to start planning the garden.” The corner of Kate’s lips curled up in amusement. “She subscribed to the shop’s website a couple days ago and today she got an email about a sale on Bonsai trees. She’s decided we should have a corner just for Bonsais.” She chuckled, pulling out her phone. “She likes the fact that they can be tiny versions of full-sized trees.” Tamar didn’t respond right away and Kate glanced up to find the Widow watching her openly, a soft smile on her face.

    Kate shifted on her feet.

    “What?”

    Tamar’s smile widened.

    “You are very good for her. To her.”

    Kate felt the warmth that always invaded at word or sight or sound of Yelena pooling in her chest. She didn’t know what to say and she attempted to change the subject even though she could feel her cheeks burning.

    “I’m going to call a guy I know. He should be able to get people out here within the hour to take care of the cleaning.”

    Tamar offered Kate a knowing look and meandered to the greasy, purpled chair, groaning as she sat.

    Kate made the call. It was quick, expensive; a crew would arrive in thirty minutes. She pocketed her phone and wandered over to the empty chair.

    “That takes care of that. Do you think we can pass it off to Yelena as if we did it ourselves?” Tamar laughed.

    “Me, yes. You? She’d look at you and you’d melt into a puddle on the floor.” Kate’s eyes widened.

    “I would not!”

    Tamar offered her a disbelieving look.

    “Kate, you are putty in her hands and you like it.”

    The blush that had faded during her phone interaction resurfaced, searing across her cheeks and down her neck.

    “That’s so rude. I’m my own person.”

    Tamar grinned.

    “Yes, yes. You make all your own decisions.”

    “I do!”

    Tamar shrugged.

    “That’s what I said.”

    Kate huffed, her arms crossing over her chest.

    “You said it with a tone.”

    Tamar laughed, smugness evident as she made herself more comfortable. After her amusement petered out, she offered Kate a sincere smile.

    “It’s good, Kate. Yelena needs this. She needs you.”

    Kate shrugged.

    “She would be okay without me. I’m just glad she lets me be there sometimes.” Kate watched as a darker, pained look drew Tamar’s eyes down. It was the first time Kate had seen the Widow’s past so clearly in her expression.

    Tamar sighed.

    “Do not diminish your importance to her, Kate. Even a Widow needs to have their person.” Kate heard the ache in her words, the way they passed through her lips haunted and tired. “Do you? Have a person, I mean?”

    Tamar didn’t recoil at the question, didn’t stiffen or close off. But she did pull one leg up onto her chair, blocking off part of her body.

    “I did.” Tamar sighed. “For a short time.” Tamar offered her a contemplative look. “Kate… it’s really hard to wake up and realize that you were a puppet.”

    Kate didn’t say anything, her throat tight at the easy way Tamar offered her the words. At the trust that was extended without fear.

    A ghost of a smile passed over Tamar’s face.

    “When Abayomi saved me I was unraveling. I was angry and afraid and it all felt so unfair. What I’d done, what had been done to me…” Tamar jaw clenched. “I wanted to die.” Kate swallowed with effort, her hands tucked under her thighs and digging in harshly. Tamar continued.

    “Abayomi practically carried me to the temporary base of operations where they’d found me in Croatia and I just… I shut down. I laid on a shitty cot and closed myself from my new awareness. It was a lot. To feel so much after years of nothing was like being buried alive with nothing to protect your skin from the dirt and the rocks that pressed into you, getting so heavy you wonder if you might suffocate before the task is finished.” Tamar looked out over New York. “There was another Widow there; recently saved. She came into my room every day and sat on the floor to talk.”

    Tamar chuckled.

    “I ignored her for three days while she rambled about the comic book she’d found in her things; from whatever cover she’d had before she was freed. She tried to describe the artwork; something gruesome and spooky, she’d said. She’d explained the story too, or attempted it. Honestly I’d never met somebody so bad at staying on track. She’d meander through her explanations with so many segues and rhetorical questions about the physics of magic if it existed in our world that I finally broke my silence just to ask her to bring the book in.

    “When I think back I wonder if she did it on purpose, if she could tell how impatient I was.” Tamar shook her head. “She was smart; breathtakingly intelligent. And she came into my room every day for two weeks with her comic book and her ramblings and she made me feel like I could breathe. She was always talking and everything she said had a purpose even if it didn’t really matter.” Tamar sighed. “I thought it was for me, you know? I thought she just blathered on and on and on because she knew that my mind needed to be taken somewhere. I’m sure that was true to some extent.”

    Kate could feel the oncoming blow, something that would cave her chest and leave her rattled. She pressed her arms against her sides.

    Tamar offered an empty smile.

    “I adjusted. I found joy in the possibilities I had as I realized that I could become something beyond who I was in that moment. We talked about all the places we would go and visit now that we could make those choices.” Tamar’s voice grew taut. “The day before we moved– a Widow had been found in Romania and we were going to save her– she killed herself.” Kate drew in a sharp breath.

    “Tamar…”

    Tamar shook her head.

    “No, no, it’s been years, Kate. I’ve processed it. But I’m telling you this because I want you to know how much you mean to Yelena. I don’t know a single Widow that hasn’t thought about it; about dying. Your eyes open, really open, and it hurts so badly that you would do anything to make that hurt go away. Widows die from it. By their own hand or by the hands of others because they could not step away from the life they were forced into.” Kate nodded, tears pressing at her eyes for the Widow in front of her. For all the Widows who experienced this pain. For Yelena.

    “Thank you for telling me.”

    Tamar smiled, the effort strained but sincere.

    “I don’t mind talking about it now. You needed to understand.”

    Kate nodded, eyes tracking Tamar’s as they passed over the apartment roof. “What was her name?”

    Tamar chuckled.

    “Bernadette. She hated it. Asked me to call her Benny.”

    Chapter End Notes Thanks for reading!

    Chapter 14

    Chapter Notes OOOF. This chapter has been 2/3rds written for almost an entire month. I revised and scrapped and redid it so many times that I had to step away for awhile. That being said, while I finished today, one of the scenes is a little rough in the writing department. It's not the most important scene (to me, at least) but it's a kinda bleh. I'll go back and edit it tomorrow or a few days from now when I have energy to write again.

    Anyway, hope y'all are still reading!

    ALSO, PS, thank the reader/commenter SD for this chapter as their very lovely followups to past chapters reminded me to write and finally post this. See the end of the chapter for more notes They’d made a little headway on the rooftop.

    ”Kate Bishop, I know you did not clean it yourself.”

    “You’re right. Tamar helped.”

    “Ha. No. I saw many footprints. You do not wear such ugly shoes.”

    Yelena had ordered six separate Bonsai trees that were delicately maneuvered and shaped into artistic visions. She’d ordered a sapling for herself so that she could try her hand at sculpting it. She’d told Kate that they would look really good at the Northwest corner which meant she’d need to move her ugly chairs.

    It had been a day of hiding smiles and watching Yelena take herself very seriously as she planned out the entire space. Sometimes, when Yelena realized what she was doing, she would become quieter, contemplative, and she’d check with Kate.

    ”Is– do you think the greenhouse should be for people?”

    Kate had held her response longer than necessary, eyes on Yelena and her attempt to remember that this was Kate’s apartment.

    “I like what you are doing. The flowers will be perfect.”

    Yelena’s lips had curled up, pleased, and Kate’s stomach had dropped at the swooping feeling of falling she was experiencing so often now.

    Kate was almost desperate for a distraction herself, one that would help her put some distance between her and the assassin as the draw to be close, to speak her mind, to touch… became overwhelming.

    Clint had called her early that morning to update her on the Barton family’s progress. She’d heard chattering and a high-pitched laugh in the background and her grin had overtaken her face entirely. The ache, growing steadily over the weeks that she didn’t see them– the family that had taken her in, loved her when her mother couldn’t– was making itself known in her chest.

    It had been dormant, buried under the fear and stress of being targeted again; of her strained relationship with Yelena. Kate was glad. She wasn’t sure she could have endured this ache without knowing when it would end.

    They were going to stop by to drop off Lucky as their temporary housing wouldn’t allow for pets. Then they’d settle in, unpack, and they’d get lunch. She’d prepared as best she could, cleaning the first floor of her apartment that didn’t have enough in it to truly become messy. Tamar had fed off of Kate’s excitement and offered her own, asking questions about Clint and Laura and their kids. She’d asked Kate how they met, what it was like to work with Clint at the beginning, and Kate had shared the story with fondness and humor. Yelena… Yelena had spent the entire day in the kitchen with pots and pans and bowls and ingredients littering the counter around her. Kate hadn’t said anything about the mess, hadn’t tried to push or question, but she had connected her phone to the speakers in the kitchen and living room to play music and give Yelena something to hide behind.

    Kate wasn’t certain Yelena would last all that long once the space was invaded by people she didn’t trust. She almost regretted putting Yelena through it at all, knowing the Widow could have found herself a nice hotel room to sequester herself in until the Bartons left. But a part of her knew that it might help too; and that possibility made her endlessly glad Yelena had decided to try.

    It wasn’t like the Bartons would be staying with them; they had their own little AirBnB a few blocks away that would hopefully give Yelena the space she needed when she needed it. They were arriving soon, in search of Kate’s extortionately priced parking spaces that she’d known would come in handy. Tamar had stepped onto the porch outside the living room to make a call to Abayomi forty minutes ago and she was still speaking, her voice rising and falling with the cadence of their conversation.

    Kate had been watching her some moments when she had the willpower to turn from Yelena, but those moments were few and far between. Mostly, she’d been sitting on the couch, laptop on her thighs, feigning business in between her glances back at the kitchen. Yelena had been rather severe. Her expression was enduring frustration, almost anger, though Kate could read between those to find the slowly increasing agitation. She’d called out a warning– Bartons in ten– and had tried to give Yelena privacy for whatever she needed to feel before their arrival.

    Five minutes passed and Kate caved. She turned her head from her laptop and found Yelena once again. She set her computer to the side and stood, maybe too quickly, making her way to the kitchen where Yelena was standing, still as stone in front of the stove. Kate couldn’t see her face but she knew something was wrong.

    She made sure her footsteps were loud over the music even though she knew that Yelena was likely aware of her approach already. The itch in her palms to reach out and touch was so strong that Kate had to clench her hands into fists.

    “Yelena?”

    Yelena’s shoulders dropped slightly, releasing the heightened tension as her head turned. She wasn’t looking at Kate but she was acknowledging her.

    “I am fine.”

    Kate moved forward and leaned against the cabinet next to the stove so that she could see Yelena’s face. Her expression was exasperated but fond, and Kate’s heart jumped. “I know.” Kate reached over her to attempt to stick her finger in the sauce cooling in the pan. “I just wanted a taste.”

    Yelena’s hand easily caught her wrist.

    “The pigs on my mama’s farm have more manners than you.”

    Kate tugged lightly on her wrist, using the momentum when Yelena didn’t release her to move closer.

    “That is so rude.”

    Yelena turned toward her then, lips curling up slightly.

    “They are mind-controlled so maybe that is not a fair advantage.” Kate’s brow raised, concern and curiosity flooding her, and Yelena shook her head before Kate could ask. “Another time I will tell you.”

    They were close enough that Kate felt the air around them shift, settling in the fever and magnetism that seemed to manifest whenever they moved into each other's spaces. She wondered if Yelena felt it too.

    “Fine.” Kate looked around. “You made a mess.”

    Yelena’s grip on her wrist softened but she didn’t let go.

    “I made you a better host. Did you plan on feeding the Bartons the fruit snacks you have in your cupboards?”

    Kate feigned offense.

    “Take out is perfectly acceptable.”

    Yelena rolled her eyes, dropping Kate’s hand so that she could pour the sauce into ceramicware that Kate had never seen before.

    “Okay.”

    Kate’s eyes roved from the ceramic cruet to the other dishes lining the counter. Some was the stoneware she’d purchased before she’d had to disappear. Interspersed throughout were curious little pieces of differing material and design. She couldn’t hide the amusement in her voice.

    “Where did all of this come from?”

    Yelena started moving dishes to the fridge. She offered Kate a look.

    “The store, Kate Bishop.”

    Kate rolled her eyes, ready to press for more information, when there was a buzz from the living room. She watched Yelena transform in front of her, her once relaxed posture on edge and her face expressionless. It felt like a slap even though Kate knew it wasn’t directed at her. It had only been a week of Yelena’s easing vulnerability yet having it yanked away was painful.

    Kate swallowed and turned away to let the Bartons up.

    She felt anxious; nerves buzzing through her as she waited for what felt like an eternity. She could feel Yelena at her back and the door to the porch slid open signaling Tamar’s return. Kate’s jaw clenched and she wondered if this was about to be a trainwreck. The echo of paws and a familiar whine sounded from the other side of the door and any concerns or nerves Kate was feeling dissipated in moments, a smile taking over her whole face as she tugged the front door open and got an armful of fluffy, golden retriever. She stumbled back several steps, a tongue laving around her face, sticking her hair to her skin. She laughed freely, her heart so full of joy as Lucky wiggled and whined in her arms. She knelt down and he pressed his entire body into hers with force. Over his fluffy body, she took in a beautiful sight.

    Nate and Lila were inching into the apartment looking eager and thrilled and Kate opened her arms to them despite the handful of Lucky in them.

    Nate didn’t hesitate. He squealed in his eight-year-old pitch and fell into Kate’s arms, practically draped over Lucky’s back. Lila waited patiently, as Cooper stepped in behind her, offering Kate a grin.

    Kate felt warmth. So much so that she thought she might cry if she didn’t get a handle on herself. She looked over Nate’s shoulder, past Cooper and Lila, and saw Clint and Laura. She finally stood as Lucky disappeared from her side, sprinting behind her, and Kate hefted Nate into the air despite his newfound height. He giggled in her arms. “It’s been forever! It’s been a hundred years!”

    Kate laughed and squeezed once more before putting him down.

    “That is quite the exaggeration, Nathaniel.” She ruffled his hair and he scrunched up his nose. She looked closely, eyes comically wide. “Wait. No. You do look one hundred years old.” She picked up a strand of his hair. “Is this gray?” She poked his chin. “And a beard?!” Nate’s laughter continued to pick up until he dropped his head back and wobbled and Kate’s chest was so warm with love that she thought she might burst.

    “I don’t have a beard, Aunt Katie.”

    Kate narrowed her eyes, peering closer.

    “Oh… you’re right. I’m seeing things.”

    Nate looked over her shoulder and without fear stepped behind to meet Tamar. Kate saw him reach out his tiny hand before she had her arms full again.

    Her embrace with Lila had less squealing but she hugged Kate tightly before speaking, excitement in her voice.

    “I brought you something.”

    Kate’s brows raised and she watched Lila tug a photo frame from the bag on her back. Kate’s laugh burst freely from her chest as she stared at the blurry, grainy image of her falling down a stairwell. You could just make out her face, blood smeared all over her lips and chin. She hugged Lila again.

    “This is the best present I’ve ever gotten.” There was a disapproving grunt from Laura and Kate shot her a grin. She held her hands on Lila’s shoulders. “It’s not fair that you’re going to be taller than me. Your dad is so short.”

    She heard a grunt– from Clint this time– and matched her mischievous grin to Lila’s. Lila looked down at the frame again, but her expression turned more serious when she glanced back up.

    “I’m really glad you’re okay.”

    Kate gently took the frame out of her hands with one hand, the other squeezing her shoulder. “I had somebody looking out for me the whole time, Lila. Nothing was going to happen to me.”

    Lila glanced at where Kate knew Yelena was standing and she fought the pull to look back and see the Widow for herself. She’d been looking at nothing but Yelena for days. Surely she could give it a rest for a few the time it took to say her hellos.

    Cooper stepped a little closer. Kate could hear Nate chattering away behind them and Lila moved to join him.

    Cooper smiled, his jaw a little sharper than the last time Kate had seen him. The baby fat was slowly fading away and Kate wondered how his parents felt about that. She pulled him into a quick hug.

    “I hear you’ve got a few stories for me. New girlfriend?”

    Cooper blushed, his changing voice cracking slightly as he tried to explain. “Dad likes to exaggerate.” Cooper shrugged. “She’s really cool though. I think she’d like you a lot.”

    Kate squeezed his shoulders, broader now than she’d last seen them.

    “Of course she would. I’m the only cool person your dad knows.”

    Another sigh from Clint and Cooper laughed, his reserved nature receding a bit as he moved beyond her for further introductions.

    And finally, she was faced with the adult Bartons. Laura was quick to step forward, her face bright and soft as she tugged Kate into such a motherly hug that she lost herself for long moments, her heart aching for her own mother and a connection they’d never really had. Laura rubbed her back.

    “I’m glad you’re safe, Kate. We’ve been so worried about you.”

    Kate tucked her chin over Laura’s shoulder.

    “I’m sorry.”

    Laura pulled back and her hands cupped Kate’s cheeks.

    “No, no. None of that. We’re proud of you too.”

    Kate nodded, struggling to meet Laura’s eyes for all the love that shone from them. She whispered conspiratorially.

    “Was Clint super emotional? Tears? Late-night discussions of his fears and revelations about my innate badassery?”

    Laura nodded sagely.

    “Sobbing. I had to send the kids away.”

    “Alright.” Clint stepped forward and pulled Laura back with feigned protectiveness. “No more slander.” He shot Kate a glare but she could see the relief in his eyes as he looked at her. “All my hard work and patience to train you and this is how you repay me? Turning my own family against me?”

    Laura stepped to the side, pressing a kiss to Clint’s cheek as he replaced her. He offered Kate a smile, his gruff exterior only half in place.

    “Hey, Hawkeye. It’s been awhile.”

    Kate grinned hard enough that her cheeks hurt.

    “Hey, Clint.”

    She didn’t have to wait for the hug, it wasn’t as long or as tight as Laura’s or the kids’ but Kate felt it in her soul.

    Clint grumbled in her ear.

    “You almost gave me a heart attack, kid.”

    Kate pulled back and nodded.

    “Yeah, me too. But I’m here and things are–” Kate thought of Yelena’s trust and Tamar’s friendship. “Things are really good.”

    Clint knocked her shoulder back with his fist.

    “We’ll have to see what you can still do after such a long vacation from your bow.” Kate rolled her eyes.

    “Right. With all the gunfire and people punching me I forgot I was supposed to be relaxing.” Nate’s voice rose higher than the murmur of chatter and Kate remembered the situation happening behind her.

    She turned quickly to take stock of the Widows and the remainder of the family. Her heart melted to a puddle at her feet.

    Tamar was speaking easily with Cooper and Lila though Kate couldn’t make out what they were talking about. It was sweet but expected; Tamar didn’t seem like she’d have a difficult time with this.

    No, Kate’s eyes were on Yelena and the little boy standing in front of her with all the confidence that comes with naivety. He looked like he’d simply followed Lucky, his feet carrying him to stand before the blonde assassin with the utmost confidence. Laura was standing several feet back, not wanting to intrude but ensuring that Nate didn’t make Yelena uncomfortable.

    For her part, Yelena was crouched beside Lucky, his face pressed into her chest and his tail wagging from side to side. Kate was glad he remembered her.

    Yelena’s eyes were on Nate as he spoke animatedly with his hands, his voice pitched and carrying loudly through the room.

    “...last week I worked at the bank and I got to spend my money on whatever I wanted…” Nate spoke with emphasis and excitement as he patted the back of Lucky’s head. Yelena’s eyes glanced up to find Kate’s, a measured amount of panic flickering in and out of visibility before she met Nate’s eyes again. He continued speaking, the exchange over his head unseen.

    “...Nabil was pretty sad because he wanted to be a police officer but I said that they weren’t even that cool…”

    Yelena seemed to realize that she wouldn’t have to have a large part in the conversation and she nodded along far more seriously than the story warranted. Kate couldn’t stop her grin and Yelena caught it, her expression twitching in annoyance at Kate before settling back into passivity.

    “...gave me a ticket and I wasn’t even doing anything–”

    Kate was happy to watch Yelena endure this for as long as possible but Laura had mercy. She swooped in with a smile.

    “Alright, Nate, let’s let everybody else meet Yelena.”

    Nate huffed a little but stepped to the side, almost pressed against Yelena’s leg so that he could keep petting Lucky.

    Yelena’s eyes found Kate’s again and this time they were minutely wider. Kate snickered, reading the request for aid and ignoring it.

    She watched as Laura stepped forward, offering Yelena a smile.

    “It’s nice to meet you, Yelena.”

    Introductions happened around her but Kate kept her eyes on the Widow, waiting for the moment Clint had to make his way over.

    Laura had been an easy meeting, her presence a balm on any situation. Cooper and Lila were awkward but manageable. They didn’t know how to ignore Yelena’s lack of response beyond her greeting. No smile or shift in expression meant they could not rely on normal social cues. Everyone stilled when Clint stepped from Tamar toward Yelena.

    Kate couldn’t help the way she moved forward a step as Yelena and Clints’ eyes met. The tension in the room ratcheted up as neither said anything for a moment. It was a battle of wills and Kate knew that Clint would lose.

    Clint seemed to know that too.

    “Yelena.”

    “Clint.”

    Yelena’s voice was clipped, history lining the single syllable.

    Clint smiled.

    “Thank you.”

    Yelena’s frosty exterior faded. She didn’t show much but Kate could tell she was confused.. Clint didn’t elaborate and Laura, as always, stepped in.

    “Let’s go ahead and drop our things at the AirBnB and then we can all grab lunch.” Laura turned to Kate, offering her own brow-raise, and Kate shrugged. She wasn’t sure what Clint was getting at either but at least nobody had started slinging insults. Or fists. “We’ll give you a call once we’re a bit settled. You know the city far better than us so we’ll let you pick the place.”

    Kate nodded as Laura passed, squeezing Kate’s arm and ushering the remaining Bartons toward the door.

    Nate had turned back to Yelena, his eyes curious but voice reverent as he spoke. It was likely he understood at least some of the gravity of what left his mouth. If not for Yelena, for Clint. “Antie Nat used to do her hair like that.”

    Kate’s heart dropped to her feet and she felt Clint freeze beside her. Yelena, to her credit, simply looked at the small boy in front of her and offered a nod. “She was the one that taught me how to do it.”

    Kate wanted to intervene. She saw Yelena pull her arms behind her back, her shoulders taut, likely from clenching her hands.

    Nate nodded solemnly.

    “She taught me how to tie my shoes.”

    Kate saw Yelena’s jaw tighten and she worried it would crack at the force behind it. Clint stepped back.

    “Come on, Nathaniel. Let’s catch up to mom.”

    Nate offered Yelena a wave, his smile bright. He waved at Tamar next. “Bye! See you later!”

    Kate didn’t look away from Yelena’s face. Not as Clint passed with Nate. Not when the door closed behind them.

    Yelena was absolutely still. Her eyes were vacant.

    Kate took a step toward her and Yelena blinked.

    She didn’t meet Kate’s gaze as she turned toward the stairs.

    Kate let out a strained breath, feet rooted in place as she recognized that this was a time to give Yelena space. Still, she watched her ascend the steps until she was out of sight. Tamar offered a low hum.

    “Almost unscathed.”

    Kate sighed.

    “Yeah. Little kids, man.”

    Tamar smiled softly.

    “She’ll be okay. She needs to process this and she’s avoided it for a long while it seems.” Kate tried to push down the anxiety that radiated in her body.

    “Yeah.” She shuffled in place for a moment. “I know. It’s just hard to watch it happen.” Tamar moved closer and slung an arm around Kate’s shoulders.

    “Come on, Aunt Katie. Let’s pick a place for lunch.” She led Kate to the couch, Lucky trailing happily beside them. “You didn’t tell me Laura was so hot.”

    Kate groaned.

    Kate glanced back toward the stairs for the third time in as many minutes and Tamar shot her a look.

    “Kate, you’re not going to summon Yelena by looking for her. If she’s going to come she’ll be down here when the car arrives.”

    Kate glanced down at her phone; the car was a few minutes out and she’d sent a text to Yelena ten minutes previous to let her know they would be departing. She ran a hand through Lucky’s fur, his head resting on the couch cushion beside her as he watched her movements with his one eye.

    “Yes. Totally.” Kate mimed writing. “Does…not…have…magical…summoning…powers.” She frowned. “Yet.”

    Tamar rolled her eyes and reached out a foot to kick Kate’s knee from where she sat in the armchair.

    Two minutes. The car was rounding the corner by her building.

    Kate counted the minutes and finally stood with a barely concealed pout as she slid her phone into the pocket of her jeans. They were loose on her legs, holes along the thighs and knees that hung open. She tilted her head to the door.

    “Car’s here.”

    Day One: Success. Mostly.

    The Bartons– mostly the kids– devoured everything, offering easy praises to the chef. Yelena’s surprise at each compliment may only have been noticeable to Kate. It was in the little lift of her brow and the tiny shifts of her hands or posture. Kate could tell she liked them, appreciated them.

Still, she tried to find ways to talk Yelena up wherever she could because she wanted her to know that other people could see her and care for her too.

Sometimes it just took a brief interlude with Lucky and his lolling tongue and soft fur and other times Yelena would step into the kitchen or onto the patio. And family, the loss of them, people forgetting… everyone in the room above the age of twenty felt the message a little differently.

    There was silence for several moments.

    ”It is no matter, if we cannot be surgical, we shall be crude. Ready Beta-Order and prepare to clean up the mess.”

    “Yes ma’am. The weapon will be ready at 21:00 hours.”

    ”Excellent.”

    Yelena, Kate, and Tamar had spent the rest of the day exploring New York, making a special stop at the Rockefeller Center. Kate was relieved to see that it had been rebuilt in its entirety; all the previous damage reversed.

    They walked with Lucky as Yelena and Kate told Tamar about their encounter on Christmas Eve.

    Yelena’s hand was running through the fur on Lucky’s head as they walked, Kate holding the leash. He always seemed better-behaved for Yelena and Kate found it fitting to their dynamic– if a bit annoying.

    “Kate pushed all of the elevator buttons thinking it was going to stop me from killing Barton.”

    Tamar laughed, her pace in line with Kate’s.

    “That seems like a very Kate move.”

    Kate huffed out a laugh, her eyes on Yelena despite the eventful afternoon happening around her.

    “I resent that.”

    Yelena’s head turned, her eyes catching Kate’s.

    “You cannot resent what is true.”

    Kate rolled her eyes, a smile pulling at her lips.

    “Untrue. I resent that Lucky listens to you more than me. I resent the fact that Nate is going to be taller than me. I resent–”

    Tamar groaned.

    “We get it, Kate.” She turned her attention to Yelena. “You’re telling me that you, on your revenge mission to kill Barton, allowed Kate to sidetrack you for that long without killing her?”

    Kate offered a smug smile.

    “She didn’t kill me at least twice before that too.”

    Yelena glowered, falling back in line with them.

    “It is not too late to accomplish now.”

    Kate pursed her lips, hiding her laugh as she traded the hand Lucky’s leash was in. She reached out, the knuckles of her hand brushing against Yelena’s.

    “Have fun telling the Widows. They like me more than they like you.”

    Yelena let out a dismissive laugh but her eyes spoke of warmth as she let two of her fingers link with Kate’s.

    “So funny, Kate Bishop.”

    Lucky’s head tilted back to find Yelena, his tongue lolling out of his mouth. He stopped without warning, tripping Kate up enough that she had to dodge around her own dog. She saw Yelena’s pleased grin when she found her balance and a thrum of heat curled in anticipation around her chest.

    It was interrupted by the quiet buzzing of Yelena’s phone. Her eyes softened as she read the name on her screen and she answered quickly.

    “Я думал, ты забыл обо мне.”

    Kate wrangled her curiosity, deciding at that moment that she would begin her language training in earnest.

    The voice that responded was muffled but familiar enough that Kate relaxed. Yelena let out a dry laugh.

    “Now that you have had your fill of one another you have remembered we exist?” Sonya’s voice on the other end let out an amused sound and she responded quickly. Yelena rolled her eyes. “We will meet you there.”

    Yelena hung up the phone and tucked it away.

    “Sonya said that she and Wren would like to meet us tonight at Squire Lounge. She said it was a dive, which I do not understand but does not sound like a place I would like to be.” Kate grinned, excited at the opportunity to see Sonya and meet her other half. “Dives are fun, Yelena. Grungy, old furniture, weird smells, poor lighting, sticky wooden bar tops: the list goes on.”

    Yelena’s lips curled down and her nose scrunched in disgust.

    Tamar laughed beside her and clapped her on the shoulder.

    “Sometimes they let people pay to control the music too so you never know what you’ll be forced to listen to.”

    Kate nudged Yelena before she could think too long on it.

    “It’ll be fun. Promise.”

    —

    Kate watched a bearded man stumble down the steps. His eyes lingered a bit too long on their group and Kate grimaced.

    “Are we certain this is a good idea?”

    Kate looked up at the shitty, partially glowing sign that read ‘Squire Lounge,’ the hazy, red glow lighting the street.

    “I know, I know. I’m just saying: three Widows and a bunch of grimy drunks that may or may not invade your personal spaces and say bad things? Seems like a recipe for disaster.” Tamar threw an arm around Kate’s shoulder.

    “It’s hard to picture you thinking things through even when it’s happening in front of my eyes.”

    Kate rolled her eyes but glanced to Yelena, searching her face for signs of discomfort. As expected, there was no outward emotion showing on Yelena’s face, but she met Kate’s eyes with a lift of her brow.

    “You are worried for our delicate sensibilities?

    Kate absently hooked her arm around Tamar as she responded, the closeness easing an ache that she felt with all members of her family dead or distant.

    “I’m worried for the fragile bones and egos that will break tonight.”

    Tamar scoffed, dragging Kate toward the door.

    “Egos should be broken if they are so weak. Let’s go. We’ll leave if somebody ends up in an ambulance.”

    Tamar revealed her ID to the bouncer, somehow retrieving it during the conversation and leaving Kate to scramble for her own as Yelena stepped in front of her. Sometimes Kate wondered if the universal discomforts experienced by most of the population ever applied to the Widows or if the capability of such awkwardness had simply been removed from them.

    Kate felt warm, the way Yelena watched her, the safety she felt under her gaze. She tried not to look Yelena over too obviously as she moved toward her. The Widow had done nothing particularly special: a straight-leg jean with a couple well-placed tears, a black, mock neck tank top, and an oversized colorblock jacket that looked like it belonged just as much in the eighties as it did now. Yet Kate could feel her eyes drawn to the way the soft fabric circled close to Yelena’s throat and she could picture the toned arms under the jacket. Her eyes trailed down to the thin strip of skin across Yelena’s abdomen. Clearing her throat, she met Yelena’s eyes and tried to play off her obvious staring. It was nothing different than she was receiving in return.

    Kate felt warm, the way Yelena watched her, the safety she felt under her gaze. She was pulled to her, shades of want and hope coloring her face. It was lucky for her that the lights of the bar were low and the mugginess that came from too many bodies and alcohol could bring red to any cheek.
