They giggled, clinging to one another like they’d been drinking long enough that the inhibitions that kept people’s hands to themselves were long gone. One of them, a blonde with a denim jacket and bangles up her arm, bumped Kate slightly as she passed.

    Kate saw Yelena’s eyes shoot to the contact, her brows furrowing. She’d taken a single step toward them when the blonde whipped around, free hand coming up to grip Kate’s shoulder. “Girl, I’m so sorry.” She wiped off a bit of liquid from Kate’s bare shoulder, knocking off drops of what smelled like tequila soda. “There you go.” Pale & hazy blue eyes met Kate’s and slightly smudged lips quirked up. She turned away but squeezed once on Kate’s shoulder. “Enjoy your night.”

    Kate reached Yelena and found her still staring after the drunk woman. Kate touched her arm. “Let’s go find Tamar.”

    Yelena’s stiff posture didn’t relent as she turned, eyes quick to land across the room where Tamar had managed to snag them a booth.

    Kate didn’t bother wondering how she’d done that in such a crowded bar. Yelena slid into the booth and Kate followed, eyes wandering the bar in its entirety now that she didn’t have to maneuver around the populace.

    It was definitely a dive; red, pleather booths and dark wooden tables that had scars and stains covering every inch of them. The cushion she sat on was lumpy and ripped. The lighting was as much to set a smokey tone as it was to hide the bar’s more blatant flaws. It was a single room, divided slightly by the circular bar in the center. On one side were the booths and tables sat so close together that private conversation was only possible due to the clamor of the crowd and the droning music. On the other were three pool tables with fading green felt and tarnished metal pockets.

    Kate loved it.

    “I’m assuming both of you excel at pool.” Both Widows smirked and Kate waved off their answers. “Never mind. I’m going to get a drink. What do you want?”

    Tamar was quick to respond.

    “Guess, Kate.”

    Kate rolled her eyes, pushing herself from the booth and feeling the pull of the idling crowd. “Vodka. Got it. I’ll keep an eye out for Sonya.”

    ——

    Sonya and Wren arrived twenty minutes later. The rest of them were a shot and a beer in. Getting a spot at the bar had been its own adventure; the amount of stools packed against the scuffed wood made easy access near impossible. Bodies crowded around it and Kate ended up using a well-placed shoulder to make room.

    She’d gone ahead and ordered ten shots and a beer for herself, returning to the table to a whistle from Tamar and a lifted brow from Yelena.

    Kate shrugged, pushing it to the side.

    “I don’t want to go up for every drink and the way you put back alcohol leads me to believe this is just the start of the night.”

    Tamar lifted her first shot and grinned.

    “I’ll drink to that.”

    The three of them clinked their shot glasses. Yelena’s eyes narrowed as she took hers, the judgment written across her face.

    “Kate Bishop, what is this?”

    Kate took a sip of her beer, the malty, lighter flavor a decent enough chaser. “They didn’t have Russian Vodka.”

    “And you chose this?”

    Kate feigned outrage.

    “Don’t you dare say that about Tito’s! He wears a cute sweater.”

    “I do not know what you are saying but the clothing of a Vodka does not mean it is good.” Sonya’s voice carried over the voices around them.

    “Good to see you two are still having such invigorating arguments.”

    Kate turned with a bright smile, taking in Sonya’s warm and rested appearance. Beside her was a woman with red hair held from her face in a French braid that made it down to the middle of her back. Freckles laid across her cheeks and nose and her eyes were a muddy green that held warmth and sharpness in equal measure.

    Kate stood and pulled Sonya into a hug, her eyes moving to Wren. She held out a hand. “It’s nice to meet you. I’ve heard stories.”

    Wren smiled, her hand leaving Sonya’s back to find Kate’s.

    “Ditto to both, Kate.” She nodded to the table. “I see we’re expected to catch up.” Tamar scooted around to make room, shoving and annoyed Yelena closer to the edge where Kate was sitting.

    “That’s right; get to it. We’ve got a wealthy patron tonight buying all of our drinks.” Kate huffed and sat back down, this time far closer to Yelena. Their thighs pressed together under the table and their shoulders bumped.

    Yelena didn’t seem nearly as distracted by their new contact as Kate, her eyes genuine as she took in her friend.

    “You look well, Sonya.”

    Sonya smiled, looking to her wife.

    “It’s been very good to be home.”

    Wren’s cheeks flushed slightly but she leaned more fully into Sonya as she turned her attention to Tamar and finished introductions. The next round of shots followed shortly after. ——

    Kate was drunk.

    Not the plastered kind of drunk that made you sloppy and careless; but her body buzzed with warmth and levity and her mind was very diligently begging her to put her hands on the woman beside her.

    For her part, Yelena seemed rather unaffected. They’d each taken their fourth shot and Kate was still nursing the warm dredges of her beer. The only signs Kate could see that Yelena was impaired at all was a slight tint to her cheeks and the way her body had relaxed against hers, their skin brushing with every movement.

    Wren and Tamar were currently discussing the potential risks of advancement in artificial intelligence and Kate and Yelena had stopped trying to participate five minutes prior. Sonya held out a bit longer but had finally gotten up to grab drinks for the table. Kate leaned in close to Yelena, playing off the proximity as something needed to be unheard. “Who knew Tamar was such a nerd.”

    Yelena scoffed.”

    “You are calling the kettle black.”

    Kate opened her mouth to correct her and then offered a thoughtful frown. “Actually that works.”

    Yelena turned to her in question and Kate didn’t hear the words that followed. Instead, Kate watched her perfect lips curl around words and the sharp and soft lines of her face reflected in the dim lights of the bar. Red tones highlighted Yelena’s jaw and her undeniably cute nose and the softness of her hair as it framed her face.

    Kate stopped breathing entirely.

    Sometimes she could look at Yelena and manage the way the Widow broke her open; the way her body ignited and ached. Up until she knew exactly what Yelena tasted like and the way her skin felt against her own, Kate was in control.

    Now?

    Kate took a shuddering breath and blinked, trying to reboot her brain because she knew that Yelena had said something and she knew she needed to respond before she was irreparably embarrassed.

    “What?”

    Good. Solid response, Kate. Stunning. Idiot.

    Yelena’s expression— bemused— shifted into smugness and Kate felt her cheeks heat. Her voice was deep and knowing and Kate looked away.

    “Kate.”

    Kate finished the worst part of the beer with a grimace.

    “Hm?”

    When Kate didn’t look back, she felt Yelena shift against her. Kate’s jaw went slack as a warm hand settled on her knee, fingers pressing into the denim there.

    Yelena offered an innocent smile.

    “You can look. I do not mind.”

    The hand on her knee squeezed and Kate wasn’t sure if she’d ever gone from zero to turned on and useless so quickly. Her heart pounded in her ears and her breathing stuttered out like it had been forcibly pushed from her lungs.

    She tried to adjust to relieve the pressure and Yelena’s hand tightened further, fingers curling around the inside of Kate’s thigh.

    Kate let out a laugh that sounded far more breathy and strained than she’d have liked. She forced her gaze back to Yelena and was lost. She managed to find her voice in the midst of green and blonde and so much smugness.

    “You’re an asshole.”

    Yelena feigned offense but her eyes danced with delight.

    “That is so rude. I am just giving you permission to do what you are already doing so often.” Kate found it infuriatingly attractive and she tried to think about anything besides Yelena pressed against her looking so aware.

    “Don’t pretend like you don’t know what you’re doing to me, Yelena.”

    Yelena’s pleased expression became dangerous. Her lips turned up and the hand on Kate’s knee slid to the middle of her thigh as Yelena leaned in.

    “What am I doing to you, Kate?”

    Tamar’s voice crashed over Kate like frigid ocean water.

    “Contain yourselves, children. We’re in public.”

    Kate blinked and turned her attention to the rest of the table. Sonya had returned and by the surprised and pleased look on her face, she’d been there long enough.

    Five more shots sat on a small tray in front of her.

    Beside Sonya, Wren was watching with open curiosity and amusement and Tamar had a shit- eating grin on her face.

    Kate cleared her throat and the heat filling her became overwhelming.

    “I have to… pee.”

    She slid from under Yelena’s hand and the table’s gaze. She didn’t look back as she weaved through the crowd a little less gracefully than she knew she was capable. By the time she’d gone through the motions of her excuse, she was still feeling unprepared to return. Peering down the hallway, she saw the pale exit sign glowing above a metal door. She decided to try to regain her bearings by stepping outside.

    Fresh air flooded her lungs and she felt the foggy blanket wrapped around her slide off. There were a few other people gathered in the alley, a couple of them smoking, two pressed against each other outside the reach of the single light that revealed the alleyway. Kate stepped to the side and leaned against the wall just as the door slammed open beside her. Two people stumbled out, arms wrapped around each other’s waists as they both groaned. Kate recognized the blonde with the bangles and moved so they’d have room to find their own patch of alley.

    The blonde dropped her arm and searched her pockets until she found a loose cigarette in the jacket she’d tied to her waist. She slapped her friend lightly.

    “Do you have a lighter?”

    The friend, a man with black, shaggy hair and scruff long enough that it was starting to become a beard, straightened up and frowned, pressing his hands against his chest like he might find one in his see-through white t-shirt.

    Bangles turned to the closest body in the alley, which happened to be Kate, and her eyes widened. A grin lit up her face.

    “Hey there. I poured a drink on you earlier.” She motioned with the cigarette. “Got a light?” Kate chuckled.

    “You did. And no, but I’m sure they do.”

    She gestured at a pair smoking next to them; one of the men was already pulling a BIC out of his pocket. He tossed it over easily and Bangles caught it with a pleased coo. She moved to lean against the wall beside Kate, their shoulders pressing together as she took her first drag.

    “I’m Jeanie, that’s Porter. We’re not dating.”

    Kate huffed.

    “So succinct, thank you.”

    Porter’s face grew red and he looked close to darting back inside.

    Jeanie took no mercy on him.

    “I was instructed to tell you.”

    Porter reached forward and yanked the cigarette from her hands to his lips, mumbling grumpily as he walked several feet down the alley.

    Jeanie called after him.

    “P, that’s so fucking rude. You gotta ask for what you want.”

    Kate chuckled.

    “I’m not looking at the moment.”

    Jeanie turned, a little too close but incredibly non-threatening.

    “I know. That hottie in there with the eyes had them all over you.”

    Kate flushed.

    “Yeah.. no, well—“ Jeanie snorted and stepped back toward her friend.

    “God you’re cute. I’ll keep my friends away from your table as best I can.” She motioned to her friend. “He gets a little bolder a few drinks in but he’ll listen if you say it to his face.” Kate grimaced.

    “Thanks for the head’s up.”

    Jeanie winked and waved, turning back to her friend and taking the cigarette from his hand. Kate decided she’d been gone long enough.

    As she pulled open the door, she came face to face with Yelena.

    Yelena’s brows furrowed slightly and Kate wanted to kiss her.

    “Sorry I needed some fresh air.”

    Yelena moved closer.

    “Your fresh air smells like smoke.”

    Kate felt like any clarity she’d found away from Yelena was doused in favor of breathing her in.

    “It was.” Kate allowed her alcohol-fueled mind to guide her and she reached forward in the gap between them, fingers landing on Yelena’s ribs. “Were you worried about me?” Yelena frowned.

    “Yes. You were gone for very long to be in the bathroom.”

    Kate smiled.

    “Thank you for coming to look for me then.”

    Yelena’s frown softened, her eyes darting from Kate’s eyes to her lips before she looked away.

    “Sonya says we are playing pool.”

    Kate allowed Yelena’s retreat, hand leaving her ribs to lock with her fingers. She squeezed once and released.

    “Lead the way.”

    Kate sank the 12– last stripe on the table— and tried to contain her smug satisfaction. Wren stood to the side with a wide grin on her face and both Sonya and Yelena glared at her. Tamar snorted out a laugh and their gazes shifted to her. She held up her hands. “Hey, I’m not the one kicking your asses. You should have known something was up when Kate picked Wren for her team.”

    Wren looked affronted, pool cue in her hands raising to point at Tamar. “Asshole.”

    Tamar pointed at Sonya, divesting the blame from herself.

    “She said you were average on a good day!”

    Sonya’s eyes widened and her glare turned deadly on Tamar before offering a pleading look to her wife.

    “You know you’re not…” she trailed off at Wren’s raised eyebrow. “Right. Okay. She shoved Yelena’s shoulder and pointed at Kate. “How about we focus on the real problem. Hustling your friends is rude.”

    It spoke to Yelena’s inebriation that she stepped with Sonya’s shove, her eyes hazy on Kate’s. “It is rude.”

    Kate was thrilled; already knowing she was about to make things significantly worse. She walked around the table, eyes never leaving Yelena as she found her spot and paused. “You know…” She leaned over the table further than necessary, “it’s really not hustling,” Kate lined up her shot before returning her gaze to Yelena, “if you just underestimated me.” She grinned. “8-ball, corner pocket.”

    Without looking, Kate released her shot, the cue ball spinning off the 8-ball to keep it from scratching.

    She heard the gratifying ‘thunk’ and slowly returned to vertical, maybe arching her back a little in the process.

    Yelena’s eyes were dark and greedy and Kate felt goosebumps on her skin. She never imagined being prey would fit so well on her.

    A throat clearing behind her brought her attention to the last person she wanted to speak to in that moment.

    Porter stood with a confident smile, only betrayed by his shifting from foot to foot. “Hey… I was wondering if I could buy you a drink for your victory. Maybe get the next game.”

    Kate could see over his shoulder enough to catch the apologetic but amused smile from Jeanie. Three others sat with her and each had varying levels of grimace on their faces as Jeanie spoke to the table, motioning toward Kate and Porter.

    Kate returned her focus to the problem in front of her as she felt the heavy eyes of Yelena on her back.

    “Thank you for the offer but I’m not interested.”

    Porter’s smile dimmed slightly but he cleared his throat.

    “It doesn’t have to be like that. You could come have a drink with the table too. It might make you more comfortable.”

    Kate opened her mouth to respond but Yelena stepped in front of her, body pressing far closer than necessary and settling with her back to Kate’s chest.

    “No.”

    Porter took a step back to recollect the space Yelena had taken. He swallowed, eyes flickinger between Yelena and Kate before settling on the lesser of the threats. Bad idea. Kate tried to warn him to shut his mouth but he was clearly drunk enough to miss the flashing ‘danger’ sign in front of his face.

    He limply motioned toward Kate. He was far less confident; bordering on petulant. “I’m sure she can answer for herself.”

    Kate felt the way Yelena’s body went taut against hers. She slid her arms around Yelena’s stomach and kept her in place.

    “I did. My answer was no.”

    He hesitated and Kate tightened her grip on Yelena. Heat simmered between them in every place they touched.

She offered Kate an apologetic smile. “Sorry for that. We’ll make sure he feels like shit in the morning.”

    She turned before either could respond and caught up to Porter, hand slapping the back of his head as he slumped into their booth.

    Yelena stiffened in Kate’s grip and disentangled them, stepping away. She looked resolutely at anything but Kate and found her pool cue.

    “We will play again.”

    Kate watched her for several seconds trying to parse out where Yelena’s emotions were taking her. She glanced at the others and found less concern than she expected. Sonya looked mildly irritated but it faded as she turned her gaze to where Yelena was close to shattering her pool stick.

    “Yelena, let’s take a quick walk.”

    Yelena frowned, only resisting for a moment before Sonya tugged the pool cue from her hands.

    Sonya handed the two cues to Wren and kissed the corner of her mouth.

    “We’ll be back, love.”

    Yelena didn’t look at Kate as she headed for the back.

    Kate felt the urge to follow and squashed it. She turned back to Tamar and Wren and found them already looking.

    Tamar walked forward and slung an arm around Kate’s shoulders.

    “You are a font of drama, Kate.”

    Kate chuckled, rolling her eyes.

    “Just rack up, Tamar.”

    Yelena felt untethered in the worst of ways.

    She stalked away from the building with tight shoulders, jaw clenching hard into itself. She was too loose from the alcohol to maintain control of her mind and it wandered aimlessly from fear to fear like a wounded street cat.

    Sonya was quiet beside her, waiting out her silence patiently.

    They walked one block. Then two. It was only as they turned to circle back that Yelena slowed to a stop. She took a deep breath, lungs tight.

    “It is stupid.”

    Sonya huffed.

    “Tell me anyway.”

    Yelena didn’t know how to start. She looked at an old man down the street, sitting against the curb and yelling meaningless words into the sea of people that walked around him. “Kate will always do as she sees fit.”

    “As she should.”

    There was reprimand in Sonya’s tone and Yelena let out a frustrated sigh. “You are not understanding.”

    Sonya didn’t reply.

    Yelena waited until she thought she might have the right words.

    “Kate can do what she wants.” Yelena almost choked on her next sentence. She was not used to insecurity. “What if she does not want me?”

    It felt acrid pooling out of her throat and she refused to look at Sonya in case she saw pity there.

    Sonya touched Yelena’s elbow.

    “She already does, Yelena.”

    “Minds can change. I am difficult.”

    Sonya stepped in front of her.

    “She knows that and she wants you anyway.” Sonya shook her head in disbelief. “Yelena, Kate is never going to want some drunk guy at a bar.”

    Yelena’s breath shook in her chest.

    “It would be better for her.”

    Sonya conveyed sincere disbelief with the way her brow lifted.

    “You’re wrong, Yelena. Kate deserves more than a simpleton in a bar with greasy hair and no understanding of boundaries.”

    Yelena bristled.

    “I know she does. She also deserves more than me.”

    Sonya squeezed both of her shoulders, eyes softer than she often showed Yelena. “You’re wrong, Yelena. I know you’re scared—“ she gripped harder when Yelena tried to pull away. “I know you hate how vulnerable this makes you. But you have to give yourself a chance. You have to give Kate a chance.”

    Yelena blinked back tears, her cheeks warm.

    “I do not like it.”

    Sonya laughed.

    “Yeah you do. You’ll like it even more when you guys finally fu—“ Yelena knocked Sonya’s hands off and started walking.

    “No more talking.”

    Sonya’s laugh hitched up and Yelena heard steps following behind her.

    Despite the rawness she felt at being so seen by so many, she ached to be back with Kate. They’d walked a block back when the sound of an alarm pierced the air. Yelena’s steps picked up, eyes searching for a source. As they neared the block where the bar sat nestled beside an alley, panicked shouts and voices could be heard.

    Yelena’s heart dropped to her stomach, fear curling violently around it. Bodies were pouring into the street, stumbling over one another and squeezing through the door.

    Yelena forgot about Sonya as she began shoving her way toward the door, tossing bodies aside and using far more force than she should.

    She couldn’t breathe and her heart was either stopped or beating too fast for her to feel anything at all.

    “Wren!”

    Sonya’s voice was terrified and it drew Yelena’s eyes to her right. Sonya was keeping pace with her.

    They were still across the street when a foreboding whistle sounded above the roar and the alarm. Yelena’s vision went red, her movements brutal as she launched terrified pedestrians out of her way. Even moving at speed, her progress was slow. Her shoulder slammed into the chest of a man twice her size and she felt him give, falling to the ground at her feet. The whistle became louder and Yelena saw the line of white and the gray of a small projectile.

    Her scream was carried away as the blast ripped through the building first, then the bodies.

    Chapter 16

    Chapter Notes I’ve gotten to a point where I feel like I could mess up their characterization if I’m not careful, so that is why these updates are taking so long. This chapter has been written like five times.

    It’s shorter than my normal chapters which I apologize for, but it was either this or you wait six more weeks. I thought posting it would give me motivation to write more as we are close to the completion of this story.

    As usual, this is unedited.

    See the end of the chapter for more notes It was deafening. She was thrown backward by the force of the blast as scorching heat seared her skin.

    She hit the ground hard, her lungs expelling all oxygen as her back scraped the concrete of the street. Her head bounced once and she only stopped when somebody collapsed on top of her.

    Yelena choked on blood and clawed the body off of her as her ears rang and her vision swam. She could hear nothing but the pounding in her own skull and she struggled to her feet, almost tripping on the people around her trying to stand.

    Yelena coughed, her eyes falling to the carnage that was left. One wall remained though flames licked up the warped rebar and plaster. The rest was a mess of rubble and hot metal and fire.

    Bodies were strewn across the sidewalk and street and cars were tipped on their sides; some on fire and others flashing their lights.

    Yelena vaguely felt a hand on her shoulder, pulling at her. She continued to walk toward the decimation, cold and empty and afraid.

    The ringing began to fade as she moved and sounds of roaring flames and car alarms seeped into her ears. People groaned and cried at her feet and she stepped over them. The hand returned to her arm.

    “Yelena.”

    She knew that voice. Probably. It seemed like she should know it.

    “Yelena!”

    Sonya swam into her blurry vision and she had tear stains on her soot-covered face. Her hair was a mess on her head and there were visible burns on her skin.

    Yelena couldn’t stop moving. She stepped over another body and almost fell as this one tried to stand. Yelena shifted to the left and carried forward.

    She stepped into something slick, foot sliding a few inches before she righted herself. Yelena drew to a stop close enough to the heat that it hurt, her eyes searching the rubble and fire.

    Her chest felt cavernous, cratered out just like this building. It bled into her ribs and stomach, weighing her down until she could no longer lift her feet.

    “Sonya!”

    Wren. Yelena turned but somehow she knew before she saw that Kate wasn’t with her. Sonya and Wren embraced; Wren sobbing into Sonya’s shoulder.

    Yelena looked past, around her. She wanted to scream, shake Wren and ask about Kate. But fear closed her throat, her mind fractured around the possible answers to the question she couldn’t speak.

    “Yelena, Sonya.”

    Tamar. She was wounded. She limped toward them with blood coating her thigh and her hands blistered and angry red. Her eyes trailed across the three of them and settled on Yelena. There was grief there; remorse.

    Yelena looked back to the fire.

    Tamar stopped next to her, voice warbled.

    “She went back in to help.”

    The voice that left Yelena didn’t seem like it belonged to her. It was hollow and dead. “You let her.”

    Tamar flinched, her eyes casting away. She said nothing because there was nothing to say. Sirens wailed around them and sobs carried through the street.

    Yelena couldn’t take her eyes from the fire.

    Kate tried not to let her mind wander to what Yelena and Sonya might be talking about. She absently sunk the 6 in the middle pocket and moved to her next shot.

    A buzzing in her pocket drew her from her daze.

    Clint.

    She smiled, warmth circling her chest as she picked up the call.

    “Hey, Hawkster, how’s it—“ ”Kate, you need to run.”

    Kate’s body stilled, her blood turning cold.

    “What?”

    ”I don’t have time to explain, Kate. You have to get out of there. Now. Kate looked up to find Tamar and Wren watching her with concern. She clenched her teeth and set her pool cue down.

    “We’re leaving. What’s happening?”

    She motioned for Wren and Tamar to follow and headed for the door.

    ”Valentina was made. She was wrong. Leviathan has not forgotten you. There was a breath of a pause as Kate reached the door. ”They’re locking onto your location as we speak. It was the way he said it; like he wasn’t telling her everything. She drew to a halt. “Locking on? With what?” Silence. Kate’s adrenaline kicked in violently, bringing goosebumps to her skin. “With what, Clint.”

    ”A precision strike missile.”

    Kate’s turned back to the packed bar.

    “All these people, Clint.”

    There was hesitation, then: ”Kate, they won’t attack if you’re not there.”

    “You’re lying.”

    “Kate—“ Kate hung up the phone and gripped Tamar’s arm.

    “Get Wren out.”

    Tamar frowned.

    “What’s happening?”

    Kate shook her head.

    “There’s no time. Get outside, I’ll be out right after you. Look out for Yelena and Sonya. Don’t let them come back in.”

    Kate didn’t wait for an answer as she sprinted to the back hallway of the bar where she’d seen the fire alarm. She yanked hard, grimacing at the shrill pierce of it through her ears. “FIRE! GET OUT!”

    People panicked, turning towards exits and pouring out in swarms. She forced herself through them toward the middle of the room, screaming as she went.

    “FIRE! FIRE! LET’S GO!”

    Some people were slow to move, their minds addled by alcohol and their bodies loose. She yanked arms and shoved backs as she sent people toward the exits. Kate looked around, heart pounding in her chest as she saw everyone moving at last. She was about to follow, ready, when she caught sight of the kitchen.

    Kate sprinted to it, eyes searching, and found a boy, tawny skin and shaggy hair, sitting frozen in the back corner of the kitchen by the dishwashers.

    He had glazed eyes and his fingers shook. Kate gripped his shoulders, shaking. “Let’s go! Move!”

    She yanked him toward the back door and flung him after the stragglers as they escaped the building.

    Over the alarm and the clamoring, Kate heard something that set her bones brittle and fear clutching her chest. She sprinted out of the building and made to exit the alley as the world around her exploded. She felt her body slam forcefully into the building beside the bar, her cheek splitting open against the brick as dust sprayed into her eyes and heat encompassed her whole body. Her ears rang, pitched and terrifying as she hit the ground. Her ribs protested as they landed on searing chunks of brick. She tried to roll off and found glass piercing her palm. The smoke was so oppressive she couldn’t see anything. A voice cried out a few feet in front of her and Kate hoped to anything listening that it was the boy and he was in the direction of the alley exit.

    She groaned as she pushed herself to her feet, her lungs protesting every movement as they sought air that wasn’t poison. Kate stumbled forward and listened for the sound of the voice. The cry sounded again but the voice was hoarser. Kate reached out, seeking with her eyes closed and watering profusely. She tripped over something soft and stumbled down, her hand finding skin and hair and blood. Kate felt blindly for a pulse and grit her teeth as she found none.

    Sobbing came from her right this time and she stepped carefully over the body beneath her, reaching until her hand nudged into another body.

    Two hands immediately clung to her, the body weighing as they collapsed. She swayed but managed to steady herself. The person was light, likely the boy she’d thrown out the door. Through her warbled hearing she could still make out his sobs though they were slowly fading. They needed to get out of the smoke fast before he fell unconscious. The woozy shift of her mind reminded her she wasn’t immune to such consequences either. She tightened her arm around the boy’s shoulder and started walking blindly forward. She thought she could hear the sound of a siren ahead.

    Kate had to ignore the heat at her feet and against her skin. Everything burned. She tried not to think too hard about what they were stepping on as they hurried forward. The smoke was beginning to clear the further they went. She pulled her shirt up to her nose but it hardly helped as she sucked in another lungful of smoke. She coughed weakly and kept driving them forward.

    Another person called out from the alley but Kate focused on getting the teen out first. The smoke thinned enough that opening her eyes let her see black sky and flashing lights. Hands grabbed onto her and the boy as people called for medics.

    Kate doubled over, coughing violently until her vision blackened at the edges. She felt hands on her again as soft plastic was pressed to her nose and mouth, fresh oxygen filling her lungs. Kate took several mouthfuls before she shoved the figure away.

    “There’s somebody else in there.”

    Hands clasped to her arms.

    “The firefighters will go in when they arrive. They were three minutes behind us.” She easily broke the hold and stumbled back, beelining for the alley and calling out. “Shout so I can find you!”

    There was silence for several moments until a weak voice reached her ears. The words hardly made sense but Kate could tell their direction. They weren’t far.

    “KATE.”

    Kate whirled, the sound of Yelena’s voice releasing the vice around her chest. She wanted to sob in relief but knew the person in the alley didn’t have much time. Yelena was pushing through the crowd twenty feet away, eyes set on Kate. Kate tried to express her apology with her eyes before moving back into the toxic smog. She moved toward the voice, slow and steady, trying to limit her breathing. Seconds turned into a minute and she heard nothing. She began to worry that she’d lost them when a whimper sounded just in front of her.

    Kate knelt down, wincing as her knees pressed into glass. She felt forward until she had arms in her hands and used all her strength to pull them over her shoulders. She didn’t have time to coax them into a standing position.

    “Wait—“ the woman coughed. “My friend. He’s there. Please.”

    Kate couldn’t see where the woman pointed but she reached out and felt further. Her hands hit skin, then the slick slide of blood, then the sharp break of bone. Kate yanked her hand back and stood, ignoring the weak protests of the person on her back. She was slipping into delirium by the time she felt hands on her, these ones familiar and sure. The weight was pulled from her shoulders and Kate didn’t have the energy to make sure the person was okay before she slumped into Yelena’s body.

    Oxygen was pressed to her face again as Yelena spoke harried Russian words into her hair. She caught “глупая“ which she was pretty certain meant stupid.

    It took minutes before she felt semi-coherent. She was still coughing. As she tried to speak, to ask about the others, Kate choked and her legs gave out. Bile gathered in her mouth and she turned away from Yelena. She barely shoved the mask from her face before she was expelling little to nothing from her stomach. She dry heaved again and she knew she was only upright due to Yelena’s iron tight grip on her hip and waist.

    “Breathe, Kate.”

    Kate sucked in a breath and rested her head against Yelena’s shoulder. Yelena the mask against her face again.

    “That’s it. вот и все. хорошая девочка.”

    The commotion around them only seemed to increase and soon paramedics were urging Kate toward a sea of ambulances.

    Yelena didn’t leave her side and only let go when the paramedic suggested Kate might have further injuries.

    Kate let herself be inspected and winced as pieces of glass were removed from her hands and knees. Her burns were treated next and Kate was too tired to even wince at the pain. Yelena watched over the paramedics shoulder; likely too close for comfort. But the woman seemed unbothered, working around Yelena’s stoic form. There were cuts on her face and a bruise mottled her jaw on the right side. Her clothes were torn and burnt and her hair was singed. There was blood at the back of her head and Kate wanted to ask if she was okay but she knew it wouldn’t be appreciated.

    Kate turned her focus to another burning question.

    “The others?”

    Yelena glanced to the side and nodded.

    “They are here; safe. They were moved beyond the perimeter.”

    Kate didn’t ask how Yelena got back inside but instead reached a bandaged hand out toward her. She could see the way Yelena ground her teeth together and the frustration and fear that were very poorly hidden in her eyes. She seemed to be walking a very fine line of panic and control and Kate hated that she had been the cause.

    Yelena looked down at Kate’s hand and then at Kate as she took it carefully. Kate tried to squeeze.

    “I’m sorry I scared you.”

    Yelena’s throat tightened and she took a sharp breath. Anger seeped into her eyes even as they flooded with unshed tears.

    “Do not say what you do not mean.”

    Kate frowned, wanting to argue and press and wipe off the haunted look that permeated Yelena’s expression but somebody called her name. She turned her attention to Clint as he approached looking more like a worried father than she’d ever seen him. Laura was at his side with her eyes searching the crowd. She looked like an agent assessing a threat and Kate remembered Clint’s revelation about their history.

    Clint slowed to a halt and looked he’d over like he didn’t trust the paramedic to do her job. “Kate—“ he closed his eyes for a moment and then met hers. “I’m so glad you’re okay.” He looked at Yelena. “You too, Yelena.”

    Kate wanted to throw her arms around him but the paramedic was still bandaging her knees. Clint gripped her shoulder and looked like he might cry.

    Finally, the paramedic stepped back.

    “You’re set for now, hon. But you should go to the hospital. You inhaled a lot of smoke.” Clint shook his head.

    “We’ll take care of her. We have the means.”

    The paramedic looked ready to argue but caught Yelena’s expression and sighed. “I can’t force you to go. But smoke inhalation is incredibly dangerous and symptoms can take time to appear.”

    Kate just wanted her to leave but she nodded and offered a grateful smile. “I’ll make sure to keep them updated. Thank you.”

    The paramedic nodded and went to help other victims of the explosion. Clint pulled Kate into a tight hug that hurt every part of her. She hugged him back just as tightly.

    “You scared the hell out of me, kid.”

    Kate huffed into his jacket and closed her eyes.

    “I know, I know. I’m sorry. I’m okay.”

    When Clint finally let her go, Laura was there to take his place.

    Kate swallowed back a sob and let herself be loved by two people that were practically surrogate parents.

    When she stepped back, she saw that Yelena was still watching her, her face entirely impassive.

    It set Kate on edge; she didn’t look like her Yelena. She looked like the Yelena that had kicked sand in her mouth so many months ago.

    “Yelena?”

    Yelen blinked once and her expression softened. She stepped closer toward Kate and glanced around them. Her eyes settled on Clint.

    “Do you have a safe house for us?”

    Clint nodded, expression turning more serious.

    “Yes. Fury has something prepared.”

    Yelena bristled.

    “No.”

    Clint offered her a somewhat apologetic shake of the head.

    “I know. But it’s our best option right now. It can’t be a target for attack.” He turned to Kate. “Can you stand? We’ll have you checked over again once we get there.” Kate slowly rose, her body protesting. Breathing was difficult without the oxygen. “Why can’t it be a target for attack?”

    Her voice came out strained and she pressed a hand to her chest as if it would relieve the ache.

    Clint looked ready to step in if she wobbled at all.

    “I misspoke. It doesn’t matter if it’s a target. It won’t be hit by an attack. Pepper said we could stay at Stark’s.”

    Kate’s brows raised as they made their way to the barricade where hundreds were gathered and watching the tragedy.

    A few seemed to take note of Kate, watching her, some motioning her direction. She was too tired to consider what that might mean.

    As they reached the tape a police officer halted them with a lifted hand. “You’ll need a witness interview before you leave the premises.”

    Laura pulled out what looked like a badge and kept walking. The officer cleared his throat and turned, lifting the police tape for them to walk under.

    Kate leaned down slightly and her head spun, nausea rising in her throat. She paused, swayed, then felt a sturdy arm around her back.

    She shifted some of her weight to Yelena, arm over her shoulders as they walked into the crowd. People moved and shifted out of their way with Clint and Lauras’ aid. Ahead, Kate saw Wren watching with relief painted across her face. It was jarring to see such open emotion after being surrounded by so many self-contained operatives. She moved to them quickly.

    “Kate, I’m so glad you’re alright.”

    Kate tried for a grin.

    “I wonder how many lives I’d have left if I were a cat.”

    Yelena tensed beside her and Kate made a note to apologize and make sure Yelena knew she meant it. Kate searched the two Windows and Wren caught her gaze.

    “They’re securing the area; making sure it’s safe to leave. I think Tamar is ‘borrowing’ a vehicle to follow behind us.”

    Kate couldn’t tell if Wren was displeased with that or just unused to common theft. It made Kate wonder how she’d received Sonya’s past.

    Clint stepped off to answer his phone and a minute later two cars were pulling around. Sonya stepped out of the passenger side of one and nodded at Kate. She was just as battered as the rest of them. But her expression was severe and focused as she approached. “We need to get out of here. There were multiple Leviathan agents positioned in range. Tamar is handling the last of them.”

    Yelena guided Kate to one of the cars, helping her into the back before sliding in beside her. Yelena was rigid beside her but Kate was fading fast and couldn’t find the energy to check in. Her head lolled as Wren and Sonya climbed into the car with them.

    Yelena turned to her, her worry poorly masked as she brushed Kate’s hair from her face. “Stay awake, Kate. We will be there soon.”

    The car ride was a blur of pain, a sickly twisting in her stomach, and oppressive silence from the passengers. Kate’s head fell onto Yelena’s shoulder and didn’t move again until the car was rolling to a stop on the smoothest road Kate had ever felt. It was an odd thing to note in her delirium and she tried to turn her head to look.

    She felt dizzy, her breathing was strained, and she really, really wanted to lay down. Yelena helped her out of the car and leaned Kate against the side. She held Kate’s face, studying her carefully.

    “They are going to bring you a gurney. There is a medical team inside.” Kate turned her cheek into Yelena’s palm. She could see the austere look clearly despite the blurriness of her vision.

    “Yelena—“ She curled away as she coughed, her throat and lungs feeling shredded. She dropped her head back on the car, sucking in air with tears in her eyes from the strain. Yelena’s thumb brushed under her eye and Kate felt a tremor. She blinked and tried to meet Yelena’s eyes.

    “I’m okay. I promise.”

    Yelena drew in a breath, her head turning away for a moment as her jaw clenched. Voices sounded to their right. She didn’t recognize the faces as two people came into view but they looked kind.

    A young woman with shock-white hair shaved into a Mohawk smiled at Kate. The other was a man in his fifties that wore round glasses and had a wild beard falling down to his chest. There was no gurney between them but each held two cuff-like machines in their hands. The woman smiled.

    “Hey, Kate, we’re going to put these on your wrists and ankles, okay? They’ll make it easier for you to get inside.”

    Yelena stepped back but watched closely as the two took her place.

    Kate felt soft material, seemingly weightless, resting on her wrists. There was a quiet whirring before something slipped between her back and the vehicle. Her feet lifted from the ground first but she didn’t slip or jolt. Gently, her body moved until she was horizontal. It felt like she was resting on air but the support was secure. The man placed a half-mask on her face. It wasn’t connected to anything but oxygen began pouring into her lungs. She could tell they were moving by the sky above but could feel nothing. Kate closed her eyes for a moment, basking in the relief to her lungs and body.

    Kate woke to the quiet murmuring of a television. She felt… perfect. There wasn’t a single point of pain she could call out and her body seemed relaxed into some kind of cloud. She blinked to adjust to the dim light in the room, peering around for Yelena. Tamar was sitting in a corner, her head slumped against the back of the most modern rocking chair Katd had ever seen.

    A sense of discontent seeped up Kate’s spine. Something felt wrong.

    “Tamar?”

    Her voice was croaky still but the pain was non-existent.

    Tamar’s head shot up but instead of relieved, she looked distraught for a moment before it vanished behind a smile.

    “Kate, you lived. Idiot.”

    Kate laughed softly but the sense of discontent was shifting into dread and she still didn’t know why. She knew it had to be Yelena.

    Something about the way Tamar held back, the way she sat here instead of the woman that Kate wanted…

    “Where is she?”

    Tamar sighed and Kate closed her eyes.

    “She left. I’m sorry. She…“ Tamar didn’t seem to know what to say and her voice trailed off.

    Kate’s throat tightened to a point where she should have felt pain but there was nothing. She couldn’t feel the ache that was consuming her chest or the way her lungs expanded like she might scream for her.

    Yelena stood in the extravagance of a living room made for a man with too much money, her eyes on the family gathered around a massive television. Clint and Laura stood with their arms around one another, their kids in front of them as live footage from the scene of the attack played on repeat.

    The caption read “Terrorist attack: no claim staked” And reporters were currently running through the list of witness testimony and interviews. Their information was useless and incorrect, but Yelena couldn’t stop watching the smoking corpse of a building that almost took everything from her. Again.

    Wren and Sonya had retreated to a guest room to recuperate and Tamar was sitting on a couch by herself, eyes on the screen but entirely unseeing.

    Yelena felt entirely consumed; out of control of the catastrophes that crowded through her thoughts. There were so many possible ways Kate should have died today that Yelena was still fighting to believe she hadn’t.

    Kate was tucked away in a luxurious room with monitors tracking every vital system in her body. She’d looked peaceful when Yelena had stepped out at suggestion from one of the doctor’s Fury had on site.

    They told her they’d let her know the moment Kate woke and she hadn’t had the words to disagree.

    “It’s Kate, look.”

    Yelena’s eyes refocused at Lila’s voice, the girl moving closer to the television as shaky footage from some bystander’s cellular device played without sound, the reporter speaking over it.

    “Reports and eyewitness accounts corroborate the actions of an unlikely hero that saved not one, but two victims from the still burning building.”

    Yelena rewatches Kate stumble from the flames, accept oxygen, and shove away to return to the danger.

    Yelena sees again, from a harsher light, the moment that Kate hears her call her name. She can see herself blurred on the screen, the malware that protects her identity easily accessing and warping the studio copies of the footage. And she sees Kate turn away, no hesitation, to sacrifice her life for a stranger that means nothing.

    Yelena’s throat tightened sharply, a sense of finality in her mind as she turned away. She was not capable of surviving Kate Bishop. She was too much like Natasha. One day, Kate’s miraculous tenacity, her victories, would come to a devastating, violent end. Yelena would not be there to see it. She could not give herself to a woman destined for tragedy by the weight of her own righteousness.

    Yelena’s hands pushed through the doors to the garage, stalking through rows of vehicles. She could see that the vehicles were on platforms, interchangeable with ones tucked into the building’s infrastructure.

    A disembodied voice sounded around her.

    ”Hello, how can I assist you?”

    Yelena didn’t have time to hash words with a robot.

    “Motorcycle.” She pointed at the Triumph Scrambler. “That one.”

    ”Clearance accepted: delivering.”

    The motorcycle sat on a platform that lowered at speed and Yelena moved to it, not bothering to question why she had been provided any sort of clearance.

    She swung her leg over the motorcycle as one of the smaller garage doors began to lift. A mechanical arm dropped down, revealing a helmet that Yelena quickly donned. She knew Clint’s holier-than-thou attitude would send him after her in minutes, and she did not think she would be able to refrain from running him over if he tried to stop her. Sonya would be furious. Kate would be hurt. But Yelena was going to put an end to the threat on Kate’s life without endangering anyone else. After that, if she survived, she would disappear.
